# PART I: GREAT AND HOLY PASCHA AND BRIGHT WEEK (PASCHAL MATINS, HOMILY OF ST. JOHN CHRYSOSTOM, PASCHAL HOURS, AND BRIGHT MONDAY THROUGH SATURDAY) [^16]

*[Engraving: The Resurrection of Holy and Righteous Lazarus the Four-Days-Dead. Our Lord Jesus Christ, with His right hand raised in divine blessing before the sepulchral cave, commands Lazarus to come forth; Lazarus, bound hand and foot in graveclothes, emerges from the tomb; Martha and Mary kneel reverently at the feet of the Savior, surrounded by the holy Apostles and witnessing crowds]*

## ON FRIDAY OF PALM WEEK, AT VESPERS

*At the Lucernarium [Lord, I have cried], the customary chanting of the Psalter.*

*At Lord, I have cried, we set 10 verses. And we chant the Idiomelon of the day twice, and the Martyrikon; and 5 Idiomela of Righteous Lazarus, repeating them to make 7.*

*Tone 8. Idiomelon of the Day:*
Having completed the forty days that bring profit to our souls, we entreat Thee, O Lover of mankind: enable us also to see the Holy Week of Thy Passion, that we may glorify therein Thy marvelous deeds and Thine ineffable dispensation for our sake, as with one mind we chant: Lord, glory to Thee! *(Twice)*

*Martyrikon:*
O martyrs of the Lord, pray unto our God, and entreat for our souls a multitude of compassions and the cleansing of our many offenses, we beseech you.

*Then we chant 5 Idiomela of Saint Lazarus. The composition of the pious emperor, lord Leo the Wise.*[^17] *Tone 6:*
Lord, desiring to see the tomb of Lazarus, though of Thine own will Thou wast about to dwell in a tomb, Thou didst ask: Where have ye laid him? And having learned that which was not unknown to Thee, Thou didst call aloud unto him whom Thou lovedst: Lazarus, come forth! And the lifeless one gave ear unto the breath of Him Who gives breath unto him, Thee, the Savior of our souls. *(Twice) [Running header: p. 2 / On Friday of Palm Week]*

*Sticheron 2. Tone 6:*
Lord, Thou camest unto the tomb of the four-days-dead Lazarus, and having shed tears over the tomb, Thou didst raise the dead man four days in the grave, O Bread of Life. Therefore was death bound by Thy voice, and the graveclothes were loosed by hands. Then was the multitude of Thy disciples filled with joy, and one agreement of service sounded from all: Blessed art Thou, O Savior, have mercy upon us! *(Twice)*

*Sticheron 3. Tone 6:*
Lord, Thy voice destroyed the realm of Hades, and the word of Thy power raised from the tomb him that was dead four days; and Lazarus became a saving prefiguration of regeneration. All things are possible unto Thee, O Master, King of all: grant unto Thy servants cleansing and great mercy. *(Once)*

*Sticheron 4. Tone 6:*
Lord, desiring to assure Thy disciples of Thy Resurrection from the dead, Thou camest unto the tomb of Lazarus; and when Thou didst call him, Hades was despoiled and released the four-days-dead man, who cried unto Thee: Blessed Lord, glory to Thee! *(Once)*

*Sticheron 5. Tone 6:*
Lord, taking Thy disciples with Thee, Thou didst come unto Bethany that Thou mightest raise Lazarus; and having wept over him according to the law of human nature, yet as God Thou didst raise him up four days in the grave, and he cried unto Thee, O Savior: Blessed Lord, glory to Thee!

*Glory... Tone 8:*
Standing before the tomb of Lazarus, O our Savior, and calling unto the dead man, Thou didst awaken him as from sleep. He shook off corruption at the nod of Thine incorruption, and at Thy word he came forth bound with graveclothes. Thou art able to do all things, all things serve Thee, O Lover of mankind, all things obey Thee, O our Savior, glory to Thee!

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Tone 8:*
Having completed the forty days that bring profit to our souls, let us cry aloud: Rejoice, city of Bethany, home of Lazarus! Rejoice, Martha and Mary, his sisters! For tomorrow Christ comes to quicken your dead brother by His voice. And bitter and insatiable Hades, hearing His voice, shall tremble in fear and groan aloud, releasing Lazarus bound in graveclothes. And the assembly of the Hebrews, marveling at His miracle, shall meet Him with palms and branches; and the children shall appear praising Him, while the fathers envy Him: Blessed is He that comes in the name of the Lord, the King of Israel!

*Entrance with the censer.*
*O Joyful Light:*
*Prokeimenon, Psalm 123, Tone 6:*
Our help is in the name of the Lord, Who made heaven and earth.
*Verse:* If it had not been that the Lord was with us, let Israel now say.

*The Reading from Genesis (LXX Gen. 49:33–50:26):*[^18]
Jacob ceased commanding his sons; and Jacob drew up his feet into the bed, and expired, and was gathered unto his people. And Joseph fell upon his father's face, and wept bitterly over him, and kissed him. And Joseph commanded his servants the undertakers to embalm his father; and the undertakers embalmed Israel. And forty days were fulfilled for him, for so are the days of embalming numbered; and Egypt mourned for him seventy days. And when the days of mourning were past, Joseph spoke unto the princes of Pharaoh, saying: If I have found grace before you, speak concerning me in the ears of Pharaoh, saying: My father bound me by an oath before he died, saying: In the tomb which I dug for myself in the land of Canaan, there shalt thou bury me. Now therefore I will go up and bury my father, and I will return.

*[Running header: p. 3 / Vespers]*

and I will return. And they spoke unto Pharaoh according to the word of Joseph. And Pharaoh said unto Joseph: Go up, bury thy father, according as he made thee swear. And Joseph went up to bury his father: and with him went up all the servants of Pharaoh, and the elders of his house, and all the elders of the land of Egypt; and all the house of Joseph, and his brethren, and all the house of his father, and his kindred: but the sheep and the oxen they left behind in the land of Goshen. And there went up with him also chariots and horsemen, and it was an exceedingly great camp. And they came unto the threshing-floor of Atad, which is beyond Jordan, and they wailed for him with a great and exceedingly grievous lamentation: and he made mourning for his father seven days. And the inhabitants of the land of Canaan saw the mourning at the threshing-floor of Atad, and they said: This is a great mourning unto the Egyptians: therefore the name of that place was called The Mourning of Egypt, which is beyond Jordan. And his sons did so for him, according as he had commanded them. And his sons carried him into the land of Canaan, and buried him in the double cave, which cave Abraham acquired for a possession of a burying-place from Ephron the Hittite, opposite Mamre. And Joseph returned into Egypt, himself and his brethren, and all who had gone up with him to bury his father.

And when the brethren of Joseph saw that their father was dead, they said: Lest perhaps Joseph remember our wickedness, and with a recompense repay unto us all the evils which we showed unto him. And coming unto Joseph, they said: Thy father charged us before his death, saying: Thus shall ye say unto Joseph: Forgive them their injustice and their sin, for they showed evil unto thee; and now receive the injustice of the servants of the God of thy father. And Joseph wept while they spoke unto him. And coming unto him, they said: Behold, we are thy servants. And Joseph said unto them: Fear not, for I am God's. Ye devised evil against me, but God devised for me good, that it should be as this day, and that many people might be nourished. And he said unto them: Fear not, I will nourish you and your houses. And he comforted them, and spoke unto their heart. And Joseph dwelt in Egypt, himself and his brethren, and all the house of his father: and Joseph lived an hundred and ten years. And Joseph saw the children of Ephraim unto the third generation; and the sons of Machir, the son of Manasseh, were born upon the knees of Joseph. And Joseph said unto his brethren: I die; and with visitation God will visit you, and will bring you out from this land into the land which God swore unto our fathers, to Abraham, Isaac, and Jacob. And Joseph adjured the sons of Israel, saying: In the visitation wherewith God will visit you, ye shall carry up my bones from hence with you. And Joseph died, being an hundred and ten years old; and they buried him, and laid him in a chest in Egypt.

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 124, Tone 6:*
They that trust in the Lord shall be as Mount Sion: he that dwells in Jerusalem shall never be moved.
*Verse:* For the Lord will not leave the rod of sinners upon the lot of the righteous.

*The Reading from Proverbs (LXX Prov. 31:8–31):*[^19]
Son, open thy mouth for the word of God, and judge all things soundly. Open thy mouth, and judge righteously, and plead the cause of the poor and weak.

Who shall find a valiant woman? For more precious than costly stones is such an one. The heart of her husband trusts in her; such an one shall not lack good spoils. For she works good for her husband all her life. Finding wool and flax, she hath made that which is well-pleasing with her hands. She hath become like a merchant ship, bringing together her wealth from afar. And she rises up while it is yet night, and hath given food unto her household, and tasks unto her handmaids. Having viewed an estate, she bought it: and from the fruits of her hands she hath planted a vineyard. She hath girded her loins with strength, and hath made strong her arms for work.

*[Running header: p. 4 / On Friday of Palm Week, at Vespers]*

And she hath tasted that it is good to labor, and her lamp goes not out all the night. She stretches forth her arms unto profitable things, and she fixes her hands unto the spindle. And she opens her hands unto the poor, and she hath reached forth her palm unto the needy. Her husband is not anxious concerning those in the house when he delays anywhere, for all that are with her are clothed. Double garments hath she made for her husband, and garments of fine linen and purple for herself. Her husband becomes renowned in the gates, whensoever he sits in council with the elder inhabitants of the land. Fine linen sheets hath she made, and sold them unto the Phoenicians, and girdles unto the Canaanites. She hath clothed herself with strength and beauty, and hath rejoiced in the latter days. Strict are the pathways of her house; and she hath not eaten the bread of slothfulness. She hath opened her mouth wisely and according to law, and her mercy hath raised up her children, and they are grown rich; and her husband hath praised her: Many daughters have acquired wealth, many have wrought power; but thou hast excelled and surpassed all. False favor and vain beauty of woman are not in thee; for an understanding woman is blessed, and let her praise the fear of the Lord. Give unto her of the fruits of her lips; and let her husband be praised in the gates.

*And the remainder of the Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts as usual.*

*Be it known that from this day forward neither the Martyrikon, nor the Theotokion, nor the Octoechos is chanted, until the Saturday of Saint Thomas.*[^20]

*[Ornamental decorative headpiece with cross]*

## ON THE SAME FRIDAY OF PALM WEEK, AT COMPLINE

*we say Great Compline (but according to the Typikon of the Synod of Lviv, Small Compline).*[^21]

*And after Compline we chant the present Canon, the composition of the holy father, lord Andrew of Crete, the Hierosolymite.*[^22]

*Tone 1. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Let us all chant a song of victory unto God, Who hath wrought marvelous wonders with an high arm, and saved Israel, for He is glorified.

*Refrain: Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*

*Troparion:*
Having raised Lazarus four days dead, O my Savior, Thou didst deliver him from corruption with an high arm, and didst show forth Thy power as the Mighty One.

*Troparion:*
Having called Lazarus from the tomb, Thou didst straightway raise him up; but Hades below groaned bitterly, and the wall trembled, O Savior, before Thy power.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst weep over Lazarus, O Lord, showing forth the incarnation of Thy dispensation, and that being God by nature, Thou wast by nature man for our sake.

*[Running header: p. 5 / On Friday of Palm Week, at Compline]*

*Troparion:*
Thou didst assuage the tears of Martha and Mary, O Lord, raising Lazarus from the dead, O Savior, and showing forth the dead man animated by Thy power.

*Troparion:*
By the law of human nature Thou didst inquire, O Master: Where hath Lazarus been laid? showing unto all, O Savior, Thy true dispensation toward us.

*Troparion:*
Then didst Thou shatter the bars of Hades, having called Lazarus; and Thou didst shake the hostile dominion, and didst subdue it before the Cross, that it should tremble before Thee, O only Savior.

*Troparion:*
Master, as God Thou didst anticipate Lazarus bound and held by Hades, and didst loose him from bonds; for all things, O Mighty One, go forth at Thy command.

*Glory...*
*Troparion:*
Let us glorify the Father, the Son, and the Spirit, the Undivided Trinity in unity of nature; and with the Angels let us praise Her as the one uncreated God.

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Theotokion:*
Without mutation, O Virgin Mother, hast Thou given birth unto the Creator of nature, Who through the Holy Spirit according to the Father's good pleasure became that which we are, without alteration or confusion.

*Ode 2. Heirmos:*
Give ear, O heaven, and I will speak, and will praise Christ, the only Lover of mankind.

*Troparion:*
Glory to Thee Who only didst call aloud, and didst raise up Thy four-days-dead friend Lazarus from the tomb.

*Troparion:*
The breathless one heard Thy voice, and animated from the dead straightway arose; glory to Thee, O Lord.

*Troparion:*
Receiving the command of Thy life-bearing voice, Lazarus, though stinking, arose from the tomb, O my Savior.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst shed tears over Thy friend, O my Savior, confirming our nature which Thou didst bear, and him didst Thou raise.

*Troparion:*
Hades trembled when it beheld him running again, straightway bound with burial wrappings, called by Thy voice to this present life.

*Glory...*
*Troparion:*
The Jewish multitudes were struck with terror, when calling aloud, O Savior, Thou didst raise by Thy word stinking Lazarus.

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Theotokion:*
The storehouses of Hades were shaken, as Lazarus below was straightway animated at that time by the voice of Him Who gave him life.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
The stone which the builders rejected hath become the head of the corner; this is the stone upon which Christ hath established the Church, which He redeemed from the nations.

*Troparion:*
A strange and most marvelous wonder: how the Creator of all, as though not knowing that which was not unknown to Him, inquired: Where lies he over whom ye weep? Where hath Lazarus been buried, whom in a little while I will raise alive from the dead unto you?

*[Running header: p. 6 / On Friday of Palm Week]*

*Troparion:*
Commanding the stone to be taken away which they that buried thee rolled against thee, Jesus straightway raised thee, crying aloud unto thee: Lazarus, arise, come forth unto Me, that Hades may be frightened at thy voice!

*Troparion:*
Martha and Mary, O Lord, cried out with weeping: Behold, he whom Thou lovedst, being four days dead, stinks; if Thou hadst been here, Lazarus had not died; yet as Thou art indivisibly everywhere, Thou didst straightway raise him up, calling aloud unto him.

*Troparion:*
Having shed tears over Thy friend for the sake of the dispensation, Thou didst show forth the flesh taken from us, united unto Thee in substance and not in fantasy, O Savior; and as the God Who loves mankind, Thou didst straightway raise him up, calling aloud unto him.

*Troparion:*
Woe is me in truth! now am I destroyed! cried Hades aloud, and thus cried he unto death, saying: Behold, the Nazarene shakes the nether regions, and cutting through my belly, calling aloud, He hath raised up the breathless dead man.

*Troparion:*
Where is the Jewish folly? Where is their unbelief? How long, ye aliens, how long, ye deceivers, seeing the dead man coming forth at His voice, do ye not believe in Christ? Truly ye are all sons of darkness!

*Glory...*
*Troparion:*
Though Thou wast incarnate, I know Thee as One of the Trinity, and as One I glorify the incarnate Son Who sprang forth without seed from the Mother of God, and is glorified as the One Son with the Father and the Spirit.

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Theotokion:*
A strange and fearful vision of the dispensation, which the unerring prophets foresaw: the Virgin Mother of God, who conceived without seed, and without corruption gave birth unto God, yet remained pure after childbirth.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
The sun was taken up, and the moon stood in its order; Thou wast lifted up upon the Tree, O Long-suffering One, and upon it hast Thou established Thy Church.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst weep over Lazarus, O Lord, showing that Thou art man; and Thou didst raise up the dead man, O Master, and showedst unto the people that Thou art the Son of God.

*Troparion:*
The breathless one heard Thy command: Come forth, Lazarus! and running, he arose with his graveclothes, and leaped, O God, showing forth Thy dominion.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst assuage the tears of Martha and Mary, O Christ God, calling Lazarus with sovereign power; Thou didst raise him by Thy voice, and he bowed down before Thee.

*Troparion:*
Having wept as man over Lazarus, Thou didst raise him up as God; Thou didst inquire: Where hath the four-days-dead man been buried? confirming, O God, Thy becoming man.

*Troparion:*
Desiring, O God, to confirm the signs of the Passion and of Thy Cross, Thou didst tear open the insatiable belly of Hades, and didst raise up as God him that was dead four days.

*Troparion:*
Who hath seen, who hath heard, that a dead man stinking arose? Elijah indeed raised the dead, and Elisha likewise; yet not from the tomb, nor him that was four days dead.

*Troparion:*
We chant Thy might, O Lord, and we chant Thy Passion, O Christ: for in the former Thou didst work wonders as One full of compassions, and in the latter Thou didst accept suffering according to Thy dispensation as man.

*Troparion:*
Thou art God and man, truly in deed and in name; Thou didst stand before the tomb in the flesh, O Word, and didst raise up as God him that was four days dead.

*[Running header: p. 7 / At Compline]*

*Troparion:*
The Jewish multitudes were struck with terror, O Master, when they saw the dead Lazarus arise from the tomb at Thy voice; yet they remained disobedient unto Thy wonders.

*Glory...*
*Troparion:*
Without beginning hast Thou shone forth from Thy Father, as One of the Trinity, O Savior; and in time Thou didst come forth from the virginal blood in the Virgin, taking flesh, O Transcendent in essence.

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Theotokion:*
Seedless was the conception of the Sovereign Lady, and birthgiving apart from corrupt passion: for God, working wonders of His own, emptied Himself, that He might be united unto us.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Give us Thy peace, O Son of God, for other God beside Thee we know not, and Thy name do we name, for Thou art the God of the living and of the dead.

*Troparion:*
Being Life, O Lord, and the true Light, calling aloud unto Lazarus, Thou didst raise him; as the Mighty One in all things Thou didst show forth that Thou art the God of the living and of the dead.

*Troparion:*
Hades, that receives many, could not endure Thine intolerable command, O Jesus; it was afraid, and released Lazarus who was four days dead, alive at Thy voice, and no longer dead.

*Troparion:*
Having united dust unto spirit, O Word, Who of old didst animate clay by the breath of life by Thy word: now also by Thy word hast Thou raised Thy friend from corruption out of the nether regions.

*Troparion:*
None resisted Thy nod, O Lord: for when Thou didst call the dead Lazarus, straightway the breathless one arose, and bearing bonds, walked with his feet.

*Troparion:*
O Jewish folly! O blindness of enemies! Who hath seen a dead man arise from the tomb? Elijah of old raised the dead, yet not from the tomb, nor him that was four days dead.

*Troparion:*
O Invincible and Long-suffering One, Who for our sake dost all things as God, and sufferest as man: make us all partakers of Thy Kingdom, by the prayers of Lazarus.

*Glory...*
*Troparion:*
O Trinity pre-unoriginate, co-beginningless, equal in honor: Father Almighty, and Son, and Holy Spirit; Holy Unity in Three Persons: save those descended from Adam who faithfully hymn Thee.

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Theotokion:*
He Who is before all essence, taking flesh from it, entered into thy most pure womb and sanctified it, O Pure One: the Word from the Father, worshipped in the Trinity, and God with the Spirit.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Thou didst cast me into the depths of the heart of the sea, and didst save me, O Savior, from the bondage of death, and hast loosed the bonds of mine iniquities.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst inquire: Where is he? knowing all things; and Thou didst shed tears, O Savior, as man by nature; and Thou didst raise me who was dead by Thy command.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst call me, O Savior, from nethermost Hades, cries Lazarus unto Thee, the Destroyer of Hades; and Thou didst raise me who was dead by Thy command.

*[Running header: p. 8 / On Friday of Palm Week]*

*Troparion:*
Thou didst clothe me, O Savior, in an earthly body, and didst breathe life into me, and I beheld Thy light; and Thou didst raise me who was dead by Thy command.

*Troparion:*
Thou, O Savior, didst animate the breathless form of my flesh; Thou didst bind me together with bones and sinews, and Thou didst raise me who was dead by Thy command.

*Troparion:*
Having rent asunder the all-devouring belly of Hades, Thou didst snatch me away, O Savior, by Thy power, and didst raise me who was dead by Thy command.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst bear my human composite nature entirely, O Savior; and Thou didst preserve pure the most pure womb, from which Thou didst come forth incarnate, being One of the Trinity.

*Glory... Triadikon:*
Holy Trinity, I glorify Thy tender compassion, and with the Angels I chant the Thrice-Holy song: Have mercy upon the souls of us who hymn Thee.

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*
Into thy most pure womb the Word entered, and preserved it pure again after childbirth, O Mother of God, a wonder truly most glorious.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The fire touched not nor troubled Thy youths in the furnace, O Savior; then the three, as with one mouth, chanted and blessed, saying: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst shed tears over the dead man, O Savior, Lover of mankind: that Thou mightest show unto all people that, being God, for our sake Thou appearedst as man; and Thou didst shed tears willingly, offering us models of heartfelt love.

*Troparion:*
When Lazarus, four days dead, heard Thy voice below, O Savior, he arose, and hymned Thee and rejoicing cried out thus: Thou art my God and Creator; unto Thee do I bow down, and I hymn Him Who hath raised me up.

*Troparion:*
Though I be encompassed with bonds, Lazarus cried below unto the Deliverer: yet in nowise shall I remain in the belly of Hades, if only Thou wilt call unto me: Lazarus, come forth! For Thou art my Light and my Life.

*Troparion:*
I pray thee, Lazarus, said Hades: arise, depart quickly from my bars, go forth indeed; for it is better for me to bewail the bitter loss of one, than of all whom I swallowed voraciously aforetime.

*Troparion:*
O why dost thou delay, Lazarus? He saith: Come forth! Thy Friend, standing outside, calls thee; go forth therefore, that I also may receive relief: for since I devoured thee, food hath become turned to vomiting for me.

*Troparion:*
Why dost thou not arise quickly, Lazarus? cried Hades from below with weeping: Why dost thou not straightway run hence having been raised? Lest Christ, having raised thee, take captive the others also from me.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast admired, O Master Christ, having wrought many wonders at that time: for by Thy word Thou didst open light unto the blind, and ears unto the deaf; and as God Thou didst raise up Thy friend Lazarus from the dead, having called aloud unto him.

*Glory... Triadikon:*
Let us chant a Trinitarian song, glorifying the unoriginate Father, and the Son, and the upright Spirit, the single and one Essence, which we hymn in a Trinitarian manner: Holy, Holy, Holy art Thou, O Trinity!

*[Running header: p. 9 / At Compline]*

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*
As One of the Trinity do we glorify Thee, O Christ, for having become incarnate of the Virgin, without change Thou didst endure all things as man, not departing from Thy Father's nature, O Jesus, even though Thou wast united unto us.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Ye heavens of heavens, and the waters that are above the heavens, bless, chant the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
The Creator and Sustainer of all, out of tender mercy, came unto Bethany to raise Lazarus.

*Troparion:*
Lazarus, four days dead and stinking, bound with swaddling bands, the breathless one was changed by breath when Thou didst call aloud unto him, O Lord.

*Troparion:*
When the Jewish people beheld the dead man arise by Thy voice, O Christ, they were puffed up with rage.

*Troparion:*
Ye Jews darkened concerning the light, why do ye not believe in the rising of Lazarus, the beginning of the deeds of Christ?

*Troparion:*
Let Sion rejoice, and let it chant unto the Giver of life, Who by His word hath raised Lazarus from the tomb.

*Troparion:*
The heavenly hosts and the race of earth-born hymned Thee, O my Savior, when Thou didst raise Lazarus.

*Glory...*
*Troparion:*
With the Father and the Son I glorify the Spirit, and with unceasing voice I cry aloud: Thrice-Holy, glory to Thee!

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*
Thee do I bless, and I bow down before Him Who was born of the Virgin, Who departed not from the throne of Thy holy glory.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
He hath wrought strength with His arm: for He hath put down the mighty from their seats, and exalted the humble, the God of Israel, in which the Dayspring from on high hath visited us, and hath guided us into the way of peace.

*Troparion:*
Let Bethany chant the wonder with us: for in it the Creator shed tears according to the law of the nature of the flesh, raising Lazarus; and turning the tears of Martha and the weeping of Mary into joy, He hath raised the dead man.

*Troparion:*
Confirming Thy Resurrection, O Word, Thou didst call Lazarus from the tomb, and didst raise him up as God, that Thou mightest show unto the people that God and man are truly together, and that Thou hast raised the temple of Thy Body.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst shake the gates and the iron bars, Thou didst strike terror into Hades with Thy voice, and straightway death also was terrified with it, when they saw Lazarus, O Savior, who was a prisoner among them, animated and risen by Thy voice.

*Troparion:*
All were astonished when they saw Thee weeping, O Savior, over dead Lazarus; and the multitudes said: Behold, how He loved him! Then straightway Thou didst call aloud unto him, and the breathless one, arising, cast off corruption at Thy command.

*[Running header: p. 10 / On Friday of Palm Week, at Compline]*

*Troparion:*
The gates were shaken, the bars were shattered, the bonds of the dead man were loosed by the voice of Christ's power; and Hades groaned bitterly, weeping and crying aloud unto its own: Woe is me! what and whence is this voice that quickens the dead?

*Troparion:*
Arise and depart hence, having listened to the voice; for thy Friend calls thee outside: This is He that raised the dead aforetime; Elijah indeed raised the dead, and Elisha likewise; but it was He that was speaking and acting through them!

*Troparion:*
We chant Thine invincible might, O Word: for with bones and sinews hast Thou raised up him that was dead by Thy word, as Creator of all; and him hast Thou raised, O Savior, from the nether regions, even as the widow's son who was upon the bier.

*Glory...*
*Troparion:*
All-Holy Trinity: Father unoriginate God, co-unoriginate Son, and divine Word, good Comforter, Holy Spirit of God: one and triple-radiant Light, consubstantial Essence, one God and Lord, have mercy upon the world!

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Theotokion:*
O Jesus, Who in wisdom didst make all things, and wast clothed in me entirely from the Virgin, and abidest ever wholly in the bosom of the Father: having sent down Thy Holy Spirit upon Thy flock as God, O Christ, enlighten us!

*Instead of "It is truly right", the Heirmos: He hath wrought strength with His arm:*

*[Ornamental decorative headpiece with angelic medallion]*

## SATURDAY OF HOLY AND RIGHTEOUS LAZARUS[^23]

*At Midnight Office:*

*At the first Trisagion, after Our Father: Troparion: Confirming the common resurrection: At the last Trisagion, after Our Father, Kontakion: Christ, the joy of all: Lord, have mercy (12 times). And the dismissal. But the prayers: Remember, O Lord, we do not say.*[^24]

*At Matins:*

*At "God is the Lord", the Troparion, Tone 1:*
Confirming the common resurrection before Thy Passion, Thou didst raise Lazarus from the dead, O Christ God; therefore we also, like the children bearing the emblems of victory, cry aloud unto Thee, the Conqueror of death: Hosanna in the highest! Blessed is He that comes in the name of the Lord! *(Thrice) [Running header: p. 11 / On the Saturday of Palms, at Matins]*

*Then, the chanting of the Psalter: The Lord said unto my Lord:*

*After the 1st chanting of the Psalter, the Sessional Hymn, Tone 1, Podoben: "When the stone had been sealed" (Ка́мени запеча́танꙋ):*
Having had compassion upon the tears of Martha and Mary, Thou didst command the stone to be rolled away from the tomb, O Christ God; and calling aloud, Thou didst raise the dead man, confirming thereby the resurrection of the world, O Giver of life: Glory to Thy power, O Savior; glory to Thy dominion; glory to Him Who by a word hath composed all things!

*Glory... both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. The same.*

*Then we chant the Kathisma: The Blameless [Psalm 118]. And we chant the Resurrection Troparia: Blessed art Thou, O Lord: The angelic council: and the rest, as is customary on Sunday; then the Sessional Hymn.*[^25]

*Tone 5, Podoben: "The Word co-unoriginate" (Собезнача́льное Сло́во):*
Coming unto Bethany, O Fountain of wisdom and foreknowledge, Thou didst inquire of those who were with Martha: Where have ye laid friend Lazarus? For whose sake having shed tears, O Merciful One, Thou didst raise by Thy voice him that was four days dead, O Lover of mankind and Compassionate One, as Giver of life and Lord!

*Glory... both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. The same.*

*Then we say: Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ: and Psalm 50.*

*The Gospel is not read, except in a temple of Saint Lazarus. And we make the Reading, the commentary on the Gospel according to John for that day, chapters 33 and 34.*[^26]

*Two Canons, the composition of lord Theophanes up to the sixth ode; and from thence we begin the Four-Ode Canon. The first Canon with the Heirmos to 8; the second to 6.*[^27]

*Tone 8. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Let us chant unto the Lord, Who led His people through the Red Sea, for He alone is gloriously glorified.

*Troparion:*
By divine nod Thou didst raise dead Lazarus: as Thou art the Creator, and the Treasury of life, O Lover of mankind.

*Troparion:*
By a word, O Immortal One, Thou didst call aloud unto Lazarus who was four days dead, having shattered the dark realm of Hades by Thy power.

*Troparion:*
Unto all hast Thou shown the knowledge of Thine essence-transcending Godhead, raising from the dead Lazarus who was four days dead, O Master.

*Troparion:*
Today Bethany foretells the Resurrection of Christ the Giver of life, keeping festival at the rising of Lazarus.

*The Second Canon, the composition of John the Monk.*[^28]

*Tone 8. Heirmos: Having passed through the water:*

*Troparion:*
Thou Who aforetime didst bring all creation from non-existence, and knowest the hidden things of hearts, dost foretell as Master unto the disciples the falling asleep of Lazarus.

*Troparion:*
Having become substantial man by nature from the Virgin, O Christ, Thou didst inquire as man to learn the burial of Lazarus, yet not unknowing as God where he was lying.

*[Running header: p. 12 / On the Saturday of Palms]*

*Glory...*
*Troparion:*
Confirming truly Thy Resurrection, O Word, as from sleep Thou didst raise from the tomb Thy beloved who was already stinking, a dead man four days in the grave.

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Theotokion:*
The ranks of angels and men, O unwedded Mother, praise thee unceasingly: for Thou didst bear in thine arms their Creator as an Infant.

*Katavasia:*
Having passed through the water as dry land, and escaped from the evil of Egypt, the Israelite cried aloud: Let us chant unto our Deliverer and God!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Thou art the establishment of them that run unto Thee, O Lord; Thou art the Light of the darkened, and my spirit chants unto Thee.

*Troparion:*
Setting forth Thy two energies, O Savior, Thou hast shown the duality of Thy natures: for Thou art God and man.

*Troparion:*
Being the Abyss of understanding, Thou dost inquire: Where hath the dead man been laid? desiring, O Giver of life, to raise him that lies there.

*Troparion:*
Passing from place to place as having become man, Thou appearedst circumscribed, yet fulfilling all things as uncircumscribed God.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst raise Lazarus by Thy divine word, O Christ; and raise me also, I pray Thee, who am dead through many transgressions.

*Another. Heirmos: Of the celestial vault:*

*Troparion:*
Standing before the tomb, O wonder-working Lord, in Bethany Thou didst shed tears over Lazarus according to the law of nature, confirming Thy flesh, O Jesus my God, which Thou didst take upon Thee.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst straightway assuage the weeping of Mary, O Savior, and didst show Thy sovereign power unto Martha; for Thou art the Resurrection, Thou art also Life, even as Thou saidst: for Thou art the Truth, and Lord of all.

*Glory...*
*Troparion:*
Bound with swaddling bands, Thy beloved Lazarus, O Lord, Thou didst snatch from dark Hades by Thy voice all-powerful, shattering the bars and realms of death.

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Theotokion:*
Thou didst dwell bodily in the Virgin, O Lord, and appearedst unto men as was fitting for Thee to be seen; and her hast Thou revealed as the true Mother of God and helper of the faithful, O only Lover of mankind.

*Katavasia:*
Supreme Creator of the celestial vault, O Lord, and Builder of the Church: do Thou establish me in Thy love, O Summit of desire, Confirmation of the faithful, O only Lover of mankind.

*[Running header: p. 13 / Matins]*

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 1, Podoben: "Joseph was amazed" (Оу҆диви́ся І҆ѡ́сифъ):*
Together the sisters of Lazarus stood before Christ, and weeping bitterly and lamenting for him, they said unto Him: Lord, Lazarus is dead. But He, as God not unknowing the burial, inquired as man: Where have ye laid him? And approaching unto the tomb, He called forth Lazarus of four days; and he straightway arose, and bowed down before Him Who raised him.

*Glory... Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Tone 8, Podoben: "The Wisdom..." (Премꙋ́дрости):*[^29]
Foreknowing all things as Creator, in Bethany Thou didst foretell unto Thy disciples: Our friend Lazarus hath fallen asleep this day; and knowing, Thou didst inquire, saying: Where have ye laid him? And Thou didst pray unto the Father, shedding tears as man; therefore also, calling him whom Thou lovedst, Thou didst raise from Hades, O Lord, Lazarus of four days. For this cause do we cry aloud unto Thee: Receive, O Christ God, those who dare to offer praise unto Thee, and make all worthy of Thy glory.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
I have heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation; I have understood Thy works, and I have glorified Thy Divinity.

*Troparion:*
Needing no helper, but accomplishing the ineffable dispensation, in prayer Thou didst raise the dead man, four days in the tomb, O Almighty One.

*Troparion:*
The Word, co-eternal with the Father, and God previously visible, now doth pray as man, while receiving the prayers of all.

*Troparion:*
Thy voice, O Savior, destroyed all the power of death, and the foundations of Hades were shaken by Thy divine power.

*Theotokion:*
Let us hymn the Virgin, who remained a Virgin after childbirth, and gave birth unto Christ God, Who delivered the world from delusion.

*Another Canon. Heirmos: Thou art my strength, O Lord:*

*Troparion:*
As a Shepherd, Thou didst snatch away the man, O Christ, who was already four days corrupted, O Creator, from the ravenous and all-devouring wolf in truth, as Almighty and Lord, thereby manifesting the universal glory of Thy three-day Resurrection now.

*Troparion:*
Beholding Thee, the Life, those with Martha cried aloud, O Christ: If Thou hadst been here, O Lord, the Enlightenment and Life of all, by no means would Lazarus have died, nor would he have appeared dead; but being the Life of the departed, O Lover of mankind, Thou turnest their weeping into joy.

*Glory...*
*Troparion:*
The abysses fear Thee, O Lord, as the Fountain; all waters serve Thee; the bars of Hades tremble before Thee, O Christ, and the bolts are shattered by Thy dominion, when Lazarus rose from the dead at Thy voice, O almighty Savior, Lover of mankind.

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Theotokion:*
Thou art the boast of the faithful, O unwedded Maiden; thou art the intercession, thou art also the refuge of Christians, a wall and a haven: for unto thy Son thou bringest supplications, O all-blameless one, and deliverest from tribulations those who in faith and love know thee to be the pure Mother of God.

*[Running header: p. 13v / On the Saturday of Palms]*

*Katavasia:*
Thou art my strength, O Lord, Thou art also my power, Thou art my God, Thou art my rejoicing, Who hast not left the bosom of the Father, and hast visited our poverty; therefore with the Prophet Habakkuk I cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind.

*Ode 5. Heirmos: Why hast Thou cast me off:*

*Troparion:*
Standing before the tomb of Lazarus, O Lover of mankind, Thou didst summon him and give him life, being immortal Life unto all men, as God clearly prefiguring the resurrection to come.

*Troparion:*
Bound by the feet, Lazarus walked, a marvel among marvels: for Christ appeared greater than the one who restrained him, strengthening him; unto Whose word all things subserviently minister, laboring as unto God and Master.

*Troparion:*
Having raised up the dead, stinking Lazarus of four days, O Christ, raise me also up who am now dead through my sins, and laid in the pit and dark shadow of death; and as Compassionate, deliver and save me.

*To the same Heirmos:*
*Troparion:*
Giving glory unto the Father, in that Thou wast not opposing God while praying, assuring the standing crowd, O Long-suffering One, and offering thanksgiving unto Thy Father, by command Thou dost raise Lazarus.

*Troparion:*
O voice of good tidings and divine power of Thy dominion, O Savior! Whereby Thou didst shatter the gates of Hades of all-devouring death; yet snatch me from my passions, even as formerly Thy friend Lazarus of four days.

*Glory...*
*Troparion:*
Through the prayers of Lazarus, and of Martha and Mary, make us worthy to be beholders of the Cross and of Thy Passions, O Lord, and light-bearers of the days and queen of Thy Resurrection, O Lover of mankind.

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Theotokion:*
Having maternal boldness toward thy Son, O Most Pure One, despise not the kindred providence for us, we pray thee, for we Christians offer thee alone as an acceptable reconciliation unto the Master.

*Katavasia:*
Why hast Thou cast me off from Thy presence, O Light never setting, and alien darkness hath covered me, the wretched one? But turn me back, and guide my paths unto the light of Thy commandments, I pray Thee.

*From here begins the four-ode canon of Lord Cosmas. Heirmoi twice; troparia to 12:*[^30]

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Thou didst dwell with Jonah alone in the sea monster, O Lord: do Thou save me who am bound with the nets of the enemy, even as Thou didst save him from corruption.

*Troparion:*
Love led Thee, O Lord, unto Bethany unto Lazarus, and him who was already stinking Thou didst raise as God, and delivered him from the bonds of Hades.

*Troparion:*
Martha indeed despaired of Lazarus when she saw him four days dead; but Christ as God raised him that was corrupted, and restored him unto life by His word.

*[Running header: p. 14 / Matins]*

*Another canon, the composition of John the Monk. The same Tone. Heirmos: Cleanse me, O Savior:*

*Troparion:*
Being true God, Thou knewest the falling asleep of Lazarus, and this Thou didst proclaim unto Thy disciples, confirming, O Master, the boundless operation of Thy Divinity.

*Troparion:*
Being circumscribed in the flesh, Thou Who art uncircumscribed, having come to Bethany as man, O Master, dost weep over Lazarus, yet as God Thou art willing to raise him who was four days dead.

*Katavasia:*
Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are my transgressions, and lead me up from the depth of evils, I pray Thee: for unto Thee have I cried, and do Thou give ear unto me, O God of my salvation.

*Kontakion, Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the things on high" (Вы́шнихъ и҆щѧ̀):*[^31]
The Joy of all, Christ, the Truth, the Light, the Life, and the Resurrection of the world, hath appeared through His goodness unto those upon earth, and hath become the image of the resurrection, granting divine forgiveness unto all.

*Ikos:*
Unto the disciples the Creator of all foretold, saying: Brethren and acquaintances, our friend hath fallen asleep; by these words foretelling and teaching that Thou knowest all things, as Creator of all: Let us go therefore, and behold a strange burial, and the lamentation of Mary, and let us see the tomb of Lazarus; for there do I desire to work a miracle, accomplishing the beginning of the Cross, and granting divine forgiveness unto all.

*Synaxarion:*[^32]
*Verses:*
*Thou weepest, O Jesus, this belongs unto mortal nature;*
*Thou givest life unto Thy friend, this belongs unto divine might.*

On this same day we celebrate the resurrection of the holy and righteous friend of Christ, Lazarus of four days. This man was by descent a Hebrew, of the sect of the Pharisees, and the son, as is found somewhere, of the Pharisee Simon, originating from the village of Bethany. When Our Lord Jesus Christ was walking upon earth accomplishing the economy for the salvation of our race, He thus united Himself in friendship with them. For because Christ often conversed with Simon, as also he himself had promised to believe in the resurrection of the dead, and often came unto his house, Lazarus was joined in friendship with Him: not he alone, but also his two sisters, Martha and Mary.

When the saving Passion was already drawing nigh, wherein it was fitting for the mystery of the resurrection to be more firmly confirmed, Jesus was abiding beyond the Jordan, having previously raised the daughter of Jairus and the son of the widow from the dead. And His friend Lazarus, being seized with a grievous disease, died. Jesus therefore, not being there, said unto the disciples: Our friend Lazarus hath fallen asleep; and after a little, standing before them again, He said: Lazarus is dead. He comes therefore unto Bethany, having left the Jordan, being summoned by his sisters. Now Bethany was distant from Jerusalem about fifteen stadia. And the sisters of Lazarus met Him, saying: Lord, if Thou hadst been here, our brother had not died; but even now, if Thou desirest, raising him, Thou art able. Jesus asked the people: Where have ye laid him? And straightway all went forward unto the tomb. And when they removed the stone, Martha said: Lord, by this time he stinks, for he hath been four days in the tomb. Jesus prayed, and shed tears over him that was lying there, and cried with a loud voice: Lazarus, come forth! And straightway the dead man came forth; and being loosed, he departed unto his house.

Beholding this strange wonder, the Hebrew people were moved with envy against Christ, and they were incensed against Him; but Jesus departed again. And the chief priests took counsel also to put Lazarus to death, because many beholding him joined themselves unto Christ. But he, knowing their thoughts, fled unto the island of Cyprus, and abiding there, was afterward manifested by the Apostles as Bishop of the city of Kition, and having lived well and in a manner pleasing to God, thirty years of time having passed after his resurrection, he fell asleep again, and is buried there, having wrought many wonders. It is said that he ate nothing without sweetening. And the Most Pure Mother of God, having fashioned his omophorion with her own hands, gave it unto him.

His precious and holy relics the most pious Emperor Leo the Wise, having seen in a divine vision, transferred them from thence unto Constantinople, unto the church built in the name of the same saint, having honorably and at great cost built it, on the right side of the church as one goes toward the facing holy altar table: where even now his relics lie, working ineffable fragrant grace.

This resurrection was appointed by our holy and God-bearing Fathers to be celebrated on this present day, because after the fasting of the forty days for cleansing, desiring to behold the Holy Passions of Our Lord Jesus Christ, since this wonder was found to be the beginning and cause especially of the Jewish madness against Christ, for this reason they placed this supernatural wonder here. For the Evangelist John alone hath recorded this, as the other Evangelists omitted it: since Lazarus was yet living and visible. It is said, indeed...

*[Running header: p. 14v / On the Saturday of Palms]*

...that also for this very cause the rest of the Gospel was written: and that nothing concerning the unoriginate Nativity of Christ did the other Evangelists mention. For this was the thing sought to be confirmed: that Christ was the Son of God and God, and that He arose, and that there shall be a resurrection of the dead: which by Lazarus is above all confirmed. Lazarus said nothing of what was in Hades: either because it was not permitted him to see what was there, or having seen, he was commanded to be silent concerning these things. From that time forth, every man who hath died is now called Lazarus, and the funeral shroud is likewise named "Lazaritsa", hinting at the word that we should come to the remembrance of the first Lazarus. For if he by the word of Christ arose, and lived again, so also this man, though he die, having arisen at the last trumpet, shall live for ever. Through the prayers of Thy friend Lazarus, O Christ God, have mercy on us. Amen.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The Hebrew youths boldly trampled the flame in the furnace, and changed the fire into dew, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst shed tears as man, O Compassionate One, and didst raise as God him that was in the tomb; and being loosed from Hades, Lazarus cried aloud: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages.

*Troparion:*
Bound with grave-bands, Lazarus came forth, having fled the abyss of Hades and darkness by the Master's word, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages.

*Another Heirmos: Having arrived from Judea:*

*Troparion:*
Having wept over Thy friend, O Compassionate One, Thou didst assuage the tears of Martha, and by Thy voluntary Passion Thou hast taken away every tear from the face of Thy people: Blessed art Thou, the God of our fathers.

*Troparion:*
O Treasure of life, Thou didst raise the dead man as from sleep, O Savior, and tearing open the womb of Hades by Thy word, Thou didst raise him chanting: Blessed art Thou, the God of our fathers.

*Troparion:*
The dead man, stinking and bound with grave-bands, O Master, Thou didst raise; and me who am bound with the cords of transgressions do Thou raise, who chant: Blessed art Thou, the God of our fathers.

*Katavasia:*
Having arrived from Judea in Babylon of old, the youths trampled the flame of the furnace by faith in the Trinity, chanting: Blessed art Thou, the God of our fathers.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
When musical instruments sounded in harmony, and countless people bowed down unto the image in Deira, the three youths, refusing to obey, hymned the Lord and praised Him unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
As a Shepherd Thou didst seek the lamb, snatching it from the fierce destroying wolf; and him that was corrupted Thou didst renew, who chants unto Thee: Hymn and supremely exalt Him unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
As man Thou didst seek the tomb; the dead man as Creator Thou didst raise by Thy Master's command; at Whom Hades was affrighted, as he cried unto Thee: Hymn and supremely exalt Him unto all ages.

*Another Heirmos: The King of heaven:*

*Troparion:*
Thou inquirest indeed as man, yet as God Thou dost raise by Thy word him that was four days dead; therefore do we hymn Thee and supremely exalt Thee unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
Unto Thee wisely, O Master, as was due, Mary offers myrrh, chanting unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
Thou callest upon the Father indeed as man, yet as God Thou dost raise Lazarus; therefore do we hymn Thee, O Christ, unto the ages.

*[Running header: p. 15 / Matins]*

*We praise, we bless, and we worship the Lord, chanting and supremely exalting Him unto all ages.*

*Katavasia:*
The King of heaven, Whom the angelic hosts hymn, praise and supremely exalt unto all ages.

*The Magnificat ("More honorable than the Cherubim") is not sung.*

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Let us, O peoples, gloriously honor the pure Mother of God, who received the Fire of the Godhead in her womb without being consumed, magnifying her with hymns.

*Troparion:*
Beholding the dead man walking, four days in the tomb, the people were amazed at the miracle, and cried aloud unto the Deliverer, magnifying Thee as God in hymns.

*Troparion:*
Confirming beforehand Thy glorious Resurrection, O my Savior, Thou deliverest from Hades the dead man of four days, Lazarus, who magnifies Thee with hymns.

*Another Heirmos: Truly the Mother of God:*

*Troparion:*
Honoring Thy Father, and showing that Thou art not an adversary of God, Thou makest prayer, O Christ, having raised him of four days by Thine own sovereign power.

*Troparion:*
Thou dost raise Lazarus from the tomb of four days, showing unto all a most true witness of Thy three-day Resurrection, O Christ.

*Troparion:*
Thou walkest and weepest, and Thou speakest, O my Savior, showing Thy human operation, yet manifesting the divine, Thou dost raise Lazarus.

*Troparion:*
Thou hast ineffably wrought my salvation, O Master, my Savior, according to both of Thy two natures, by Thy sovereign will.

*Katavasia:*
Thee, the true Mother of God, we confess, who have been saved through thee, O pure Virgin, magnifying thee with the bodiless choirs.

*Then, in Tone 1:*
*Holy is the Lord our God. (Thrice)*

*Exapostilarion, Idiomelon (Twice):*
By Thy word, O Word of God, Lazarus now leaps up, having run again unto life, and with branches the people honor Thee, O Mighty One, for that Thou wilt utterly destroy Hades by Thy death.

*Glory... Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Another Exapostilarion, Podoben (Once):*
Through Lazarus Christ already destroys thee, O death; and where is thy victory, O Hades? The weeping of Bethany is now transferred unto thee: let us all bring branches of victory unto Him.

*At the Praises, we set 8 stichoi, and chant the idiomelic stichera. Tone 1:*

*Stichos: To execute upon them the judgment that is written: this glory shall be to all His saints.* (LXX Ps. 149:9)
*Sticheron:*
Being the Resurrection and the Life of men, O Christ, Thou didst stand before the tomb of Lazarus, confirming unto us Thy two essences, O Long-suffering One, that Thou camest as God and man from the Pure Virgin, *[Running header: p. 15v / On the Saturday of Palms]*

for as man Thou didst inquire where he was buried, and as God Thou didst raise by a life-bearing beckoning him that was four days dead.

*Sticheron:*
Lazarus who was four days dead Thou didst raise from Hades, O Christ, before Thy death, shattering the dominion of death, and by one beloved friend proclaiming the deliverance of all men from corruption; therefore, bowing down before Thine almighty power, we cry aloud: Blessed art Thou, O Savior, have mercy on us.

*Sticheron:*
Martha and Mary said unto the Savior: If Thou hadst been here, O Lord, Lazarus had not died. But Christ, the Resurrection of the fallen asleep, raised him that was already four days dead from the dead: come, all ye faithful, let us bow down before Him Who comes in glory to save our souls.

*Sticheron:*
Giving unto Thy disciples an image of Thy Divinity, Thou didst humble Thyself among the multitudes, desiring to be hidden; therefore unto the Apostles, as God foreknowing, Thou didst foretell the death of Lazarus; and having approached the people in Bethany, not unknowing the tomb of Thy friend, Thou didst inquire to learn as man; but he who was four days dead, being raised by Thee, showed forth Thy divine dominion: O Almighty Lord, glory to Thee.

*Tone 4:*
*Sticheron:*
Thou didst raise Thy friend of four days, O Christ, and didst assuage the weeping of Martha and Mary, showing unto all that Thou art He that accomplishes all things by divine power, by Thy sovereign will, unto Whom the Cherubim cry unceasingly: Hosanna in the highest! Blessed art Thou, O God over all, glory to Thee.

*Sticheron:*
Martha cried aloud unto Mary: The Teacher is present, and calls thee, approach! And she, having come quickly where the Lord was standing, and seeing Him, cried aloud, falling down and bowing before Him, kissing Thy most pure feet, saying: Lord, if Thou hadst been here, our brother would not have died.

*Tone 8:*
*Stichos: Arise, O Lord my God, let Thine hand be lifted up: forget not Thy poor for ever.* (LXX Ps. 9:33)
*Sticheron:*
Lazarus who was dead in Bethany Thou didst raise, of four days: for only didst Thou stand before the tomb, and Thy voice became life unto the dead man; and Hades, groaning, released him through fear: a great marvel, O greatly merciful Lord, glory to Thee.

*Stichos: I will confess Thee, O Lord, with my whole heart; I will tell of all Thy marvelous works.* (LXX Ps. 9:2)
*Sticheron:*
Even as Thou saidst, O Lord, unto Martha: I am the Resurrection, in deed hast Thou fulfilled the word, calling forth Lazarus from Hades; and me, dead through passions, as Compassionate, do Thou raise, I pray Thee, O Lover of mankind.

*Glory... Tone 2:*
*Doxastikon:*
A great and marvelous wonder hath been accomplished this day: for Christ, having called forth the dead man of four days from the tomb, raised him and summoned His friend; let us glorify Him as most glorious, that through the prayers of righteous Lazarus He may save our souls.

*[Running header: p. 16 / Matins]*

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Theotokion:*
Most blessed art thou, O Virgin Theotokos... *Then:*

*The Great Doxology, and the Dismissal.*

*At the Divine Liturgy, the Beatitudes: Ode 3 and Ode 6, to 8.*

*After the Little Entrance, the Troparion of the feast. Glory... Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. The Kontakion.*

*Instead of the Trisagion, we chant:*
As many of you as have been baptized into Christ have put on Christ. Alleluia.[^33]

*Prokeimenon, Tone 3:*
The Lord is my light and my Savior: whom shall I fear? (LXX Ps. 26:1)
*Verse:* The Lord is the defender of my life: of whom shall I be afraid? (LXX Ps. 26:1)

*The Epistle to the Hebrews, Pericope 333 from the midpoint (Heb. 12:28–13:8):*
Brethren: Therefore we receiving a kingdom which cannot be moved, let us have grace, whereby we may serve God acceptably with reverence and godly fear: for our God is a consuming fire. Let brotherly love continue. Be not forgetful to entertain strangers: for thereby some have entertained angels unawares. Remember them that are in bonds, as bound with them; and them which suffer adversity, as being yourselves also in the body. Marriage is honorable in all, and the bed undefiled: but whoremongers and adulterers God will judge. Let your conversation be without covetousness; and be content with such things as ye have: for He hath said, I will never leave thee, nor forsake thee. So that we may boldly say, The Lord is my helper, and I will not fear what man shall do unto me. Remember them which have the rule over you, who have spoken unto you the word of God: whose faith follow, considering the end of their conversation. Jesus Christ the same yesterday, and today, and unto ages of ages.

*Alleluia, Tone 5:* The Lord is King, He hath clothed Himself with majesty. (LXX Ps. 92:1)
*Verse:* For He hath established the world, which shall not be moved. (LXX Ps. 92:1)

*The Gospel according to Saint John, Pericope 39 (John 11:1–45):*
At that time, a certain man was sick, named Lazarus, of Bethany, the town of Mary and her sister Martha... *Ending:* And having seen the things which Jesus did, they believed in Him.

*Communion Verse:* Out of the mouth of babes and sucklings hast Thou perfected praise. (LXX Ps. 8:3) *Alleluia. (Thrice)*

*At the table we are refreshed with cooked food with oil; and if we have caviar, let there be given unto each brother three ounces; and we drink wine.*[^34]

*Be it known: If the temple is dedicated to Righteous Lazarus: after the customary kathisma, the Polyeleos; the Hierarchal Magnification and the Psalm: Hear this, all ye nations; then the Sessional Hymn following Ode 3; and the Life of Saint Lazarus written by Nikephoros Xanthopoulos.[^35] The Hymns of Ascent, the first Antiphon of Tone 4. Prokeimenon: Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His saints. Verse: What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He hath rendered unto me? Let every breath praise the Lord. The Gospel according to Saint John, Pericope 36 (John 10:9–16): I am the door... Psalm 50. Sticheron, Tone 6: O Lord, Thy voice destroyed... The two Canons to 14, and the Katavasia of the second Canon. After Ode 3, the Sessional Hymn of the feast. After Ode 6, the Kontakion: The Joy of all; and the Ikos. And the rest of the order as indicated.*

*[Running header: p. 16v / On the Saturday of Palms]*

*
[Ornamental decorative woodcut headpiece]
*

## ON THE SATURDAY OF PALMS, AT SMALL VESPERS[^36]

*At Small Vespers, at "Lord, I have cried," we set 4 stichoi, and chant the following stichera:*

*Tone 6, Podoben: "Having risen on the third day" (Тридне́внѡ):*

*Sticheron:*
From simple babes, O Christ, sitting upon a foal, Thou didst receive a song of victory, coming unto Thy Passion, hymned by angels with the thrice-holy hymn.

*Sticheron:*
Behold, thy King, O Sion, meek and saving, comes sitting upon a foal, seeking to smite enemies in power: rejoice and be exceedingly glad, celebrating with palms.

*Sticheron:*
Let us sing together in harmony, ye faithful, branches of virtues and we like the youths now offering unto Christ; and spreading garments of divine deeds before Him, let us mystically receive Him.

*Sticheron:*
Offering the palms of virtues, O brethren, unto Christ God Who comes to suffer in the flesh for our sake of His own will, let us grant impassibility unto all by the power of the Godhead.

*Glory... Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Tone 6, Podoben: "Having risen on the third day" (Тридне́внѡ):*
*Doxastikon:*
Borne upon the shoulders of Cherubim as God, He sits upon a foal, coming unto the seat of His own will for our sake: come, let us eagerly hymn Him with palms.

*At the Aposticha, Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (До́ме є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*

*Sticheron:*
Be radiant, O Sion; * bearing new palms, * chant with the youths: * behold, thy King, saving, * comes unto the Passion.

*Stichos: Out of the mouth of babes and sucklings hast Thou perfected praise.* (LXX Ps. 8:3)
*Sticheron:*
Be glad, * O Adam and Eve, * with the prophets: * behold, the Lord comes * to call you by His Passion.

*Stichos: O Lord, our Lord, how wonderful is Thy name in all the earth!* (LXX Ps. 8:2)
*Sticheron:*
On high with the Father * and the Spirit * receiving angelic song, * strangely below * He receives children's praise.

*Glory... Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (До́ме є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*
*Doxastikon:*
I hymn Thy fearful * dispensation with fear, * therefore I cry aloud unto Thee Hosanna: * Thou comest to save me, * O blessed Lord.

*[Running header: p. 17 / At Vespers]*

*Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart in peace: The Trisagion.*

*And the rest as customary. And the Troparia, Tone 1:*

*Troparion, Tone 1:*
Confirming the common resurrection before Thy Passion, Thou didst raise Lazarus from the dead, O Christ God; therefore we also, like the youths, bearing the symbols of victory, cry aloud unto Thee, the Vanquisher of death: Hosanna in the highest! Blessed is He that comes in the name of the Lord.

*Another Troparion, Tone 4:*
Having been buried with Thee through baptism, O Christ our God, we have been made worthy of immortal life through Thy Resurrection; and chanting we cry aloud: Hosanna in the highest! Blessed is He that comes in the name of the Lord.

*And the Dismissal is made.*

*And we enter into the refectory.*

*

 [Running header: p. 17v / On the Saturday of Palms]*
