## ON THE SATURDAY OF PALMS, AT GREAT VESPERS (EVE OF PALM SUNDAY)[^37]

*After the Initial Psalm, the customary psalmody: "Blessed is the man..." The entire Kathisma is chanted.*

*At "Lord, I have cried," we set 10 stichoi, and chant 5 idiomelic stichera, repeating them:*

*Tone 6:*

*Sticheron 1:*
Today the grace of the Holy Spirit hath gathered us together, and we all, having taken up Thy Cross, say: Blessed is He that comes in the name of the Lord; Hosanna in the highest!

*Sticheron 2:*
He Who hath heaven for His throne and the earth for His footstool, the Word and co-eternal Son of God the Father, humbled Himself this day upon an irrational foal, having come unto Bethany; therefore the Hebrew children, holding branches in their hands, praised Him with the word: Hosanna in the highest! Blessed is He that comes, the King of Israel!

*Sticheron 3:*
Come, let us also today, the entire new Israel, the Church called from the gentiles, cry aloud with the Prophet Zechariah: Rejoice greatly, O daughter of Sion; proclaim aloud, O daughter of Jerusalem: for behold, thy King comes unto thee, meek and bringing salvation, sitting upon a colt, the foal of an ass; celebrate as the children, and holding branches in thy hands, praise Him: Hosanna in the highest! Blessed is He that comes, the King of Israel!

*Sticheron 4:*
Prefiguring unto us Thy precious Resurrection, O Good One, by Thy command Thou didst raise from the tomb the breathless dead man, Thy friend Lazarus, who was four days dead and stinking; therefore also, mounting upon a foal figuratively as though borne upon a chariot, Thou tamest the gentiles, O Savior; for this cause also beloved Israel offers praise out of the mouth of sucklings and innocent infants, beholding Thee, O Christ, entering into the Holy City six days before Pascha.

*Sticheron 5:*
Six days before Pascha, Jesus came unto Bethany, and His disciples came unto Him, saying unto Him: Lord, where wilt Thou that we prepare for Thee to eat the Passover? And He sent them, saying: Go ye into the village opposite, and ye shall find a man carrying an earthen pitcher of water; follow him, and say unto the master of the house: The Teacher saith: With thee will I keep the Passover with My disciples.

*[Running header: On the Saturday of Palms, at Vespers / p. 23]*

*Glory... the 1st sticheron:* Today the grace of the Holy Spirit...

*Both now... the same again.*

*Entrance: "O Joyful Light."*

*Prokeimenon: The Lord is King, He is robed in majesty [Ps. 92:1 LXX].*

*Reading from Genesis (Chapter 49):*[^38]

Jacob called his sons, and said unto them: Assemble yourselves, that I may tell you what shall befall you in the last days. Assemble yourselves, and hear me, ye sons of Jacob; hear Israel, hear your father. Judah, thee shall thy brethren praise; thy hands shall be upon the shoulders of thine enemies; the sons of thy father shall bow down before thee. Judah is a lion's whelp; from the tender plant, my son, thou art gone up; having couched, thou didst sleep as a lion and as a whelp; who shall rouse him? A prince shall not fail from Judah, nor a leader from his loins, until the things stored up for him shall come; and he is the expectation of the nations; binding his foal unto the vine, and the colt of his ass unto the tendril of the vine; he shall wash his robe in wine, and his garment in the blood of the grape; his eyes are cheerful beyond wine, and his teeth are whiter than milk.

*Reading from the Prophecy of Zephaniah (Chapter 3):*

Thus saith the Lord: Rejoice greatly, O daughter of Zion; proclaim aloud, O daughter of Jerusalem; be glad and make merry with all thine heart, O daughter of Jerusalem! The Lord hath taken away thine iniquities; He hath delivered thee from the hand of thine enemies. The Lord, the King, is in the midst of thee; thou shalt see evil no more. In that day the Lord shall say to Jerusalem: Be of good courage, O Zion, let not thine hands be slack. The Lord thy God is in thee; the Mighty One shall save thee; He shall bring gladness upon thee, and renew thee in His love, and He shall rejoice over thee with delight as in a day of feast. And I will gather thy crushed ones: woe unto him that receives a reproach against him! Behold, I will work in thee for thy sake, saith the Lord, at that time; and I will save her that is oppressed, and receive her that is cast out; and I will make them a praise and renowned throughout the whole earth.

*Reading from the Prophecy of Zechariah (Chapter 9):*

Thus saith the Lord: Rejoice greatly, O daughter of Zion; proclaim aloud, O daughter of Jerusalem: behold, thy King comes unto thee, righteous and saving; He is meek, and riding upon a beast of burden and a young foal. And He shall utterly destroy the chariots out of Ephraim, and the horses out of Jerusalem, and the battle bow shall be utterly destroyed, and an abundance and peace from the nations; and He shall rule over waters from sea to sea, and from the rivers unto the ends of the earth. And thou, by the blood of thy covenant, hast sent forth thy prisoners out of the pit wherein is no water. Sit ye in strongholds, ye prisoners of the congregation; and for one day of thy pilgrimage I will render thee double. For I have bent thee for Myself, O Judah, as a bow; I have filled Ephraim, and I will raise up thy children, O Zion, against the children of Greece, and I will handle thee as the sword of a warrior. And the Lord shall appear over them, and His arrow shall go forth as lightning; and the Lord God Almighty shall blow with the trumpet, and shall go forth in the whirlwind of His threat. The Lord Almighty shall defend them.

*[Running header: On the Saturday of Palms / p. 24]*

*At the Litiya, idiomelic stichera, Tone 1:*

*Sticheron 1:*
The All-Holy Spirit, Who taught the Apostles to speak in other strange tongues, the same commands the innocent Hebrew children to cry aloud: Hosanna in the highest! Blessed is He that comes, the King of Israel!

*Sticheron 2:*
The co-unoriginate and co-eternal Son and Word of the Father, sitting upon an irrational foal, came this day unto the city of Jerusalem; He Whom the Cherubim cannot gaze upon with fear, the children praised with palms and branches, mystically singing praise: Hosanna in the highest! Hosanna to the Son of David, Who art come to save our entire race from deception!

*Sticheron 3:*
Six days before Pascha, Thy voice was heard, O Lord, in the depths of hades; therefore also Thou didst raise Lazarus who was four days dead; and the Hebrew children cried aloud: Hosanna, O our God, glory to Thee!

*Tone 2:*

*Sticheron 4:*
As Thou didst enter into the holy city, O Lord, sitting upon a foal, Thou didst hasten to come unto the Passion, that Thou mightest fulfill the Law and the Prophets; and the Hebrew children, foretelling the victory of the Resurrection, met Thee with branches and palms, saying: Blessed art Thou, O Savior, have mercy on us!

*Sticheron 5:*
Glory to Thee, O Christ, Who sittest on high upon the throne, and art now awaited with Thy precious Cross; therefore the daughter of Zion is glad, the nations of the earth rejoice, the children hold branches, the disciples spread garments, and the whole universe is taught to cry aloud unto Thee: Blessed art Thou, O Savior, have mercy on us!

*Glory... Both now... Tone 3:*
Six days before the existence of Pascha, Jesus came unto Bethany to recall the dead Lazarus who was four days in the tomb, and to proclaim the Resurrection; and the women, Martha and Mary the sisters of Lazarus, met Him, crying aloud unto Him: Lord, if Thou hadst been here, our brother had not died. Then He said unto them: Did I not tell you beforehand: He that believs in Me, though he die, yet shall he live? Show Me where ye have laid him. And the Creator of all cried unto him: Lazarus, come forth!

*And the customary prayers of the Litiya in the narthex.*

*At the Aposticha, idiomelic stichera, Tone 8:*

*Sticheron 1:*
Rejoice and be glad, O city of Zion! Exult and rejoice, O Church of God! For behold, thy King is come in righteousness, sitting upon a foal, hymned by the children: Hosanna in the highest! Blessed art Thou Who hast an abundance of tender mercies, have mercy on us!

*Verse: Out of the mouth of babes and sucklings hast Thou perfected praise [Ps. 8:3 LXX].*

*Sticheron 2:*
The Savior came this day unto the city of Jerusalem to fulfill the Scripture; and all took branches in their hands, and spread garments before Him, knowing that He is our God, unto Whom the Cherubim cry unceasingly: Hosanna in the highest! Blessed art Thou Who hast an abundance of tender mercies, have mercy on us!

*Verse: O Lord our Lord, how wonderful is Thy name in all the earth [Ps. 8:2 LXX]!*

*[Running header: At Vespers / p. 25]*

*Sticheron 3:*
Borne upon the Cherubim and hymned by the Seraphim, Thou didst mount upon a foal in the manner of David, O Good One; and the children praised Thee in a manner befitting God, while the Jews blasphemed lawlessly; the sitting upon the foal prefigured the stubbornness of the Gentiles transformed from unbelief into faith; glory to Thee, O Christ, Who alone art merciful and the Lover of mankind!

*Glory... Tone 6:*
Today the grace of the Holy Spirit hath gathered us together...

*Both now... the same again.*

*Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart; the Trisagion, and the rest according to custom. And we say the dismissal troparion, Tone 1: "Giving assurance of the general resurrection..." twice.[^39] Another troparion, Tone 4: "Having been buried with Thee through baptism..." once. And "Blessed be the name of the Lord;" and the Psalm: "I will bless the Lord at all times."*

*And a reading is set forth: the Festal Homily of the feast.*

*
[Ornamental decorative woodcut headpiece]
*

## ON THE SUNDAY OF PALMS

*At Matins,*

*at "God is the Lord," two troparia: "Giving assurance of the general resurrection..." twice. Glory... Both now...: "Having been buried with Thee through baptism..." (see fol. 21).[^40]*

*After the 1st Kathisma Psalmody, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Joseph was amazed" (ᲊди́висѧ І҆ѡ́сифъ):*
With branches, spiritually purified in our souls, let us as the children faithfully praise Christ, crying aloud with a loud voice unto the Master: Blessed art Thou, O Savior, Who art come into the world to save Adam from the ancient curse, having spiritually become, O Lover of mankind, the New Adam, even as Thou wast pleased; O Word, Who hast ordered all things for our benefit, glory to Thee!

*Glory... Both now... Another Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ):*
Thou Who didst raise Lazarus four days dead from the tomb, O Lord, didst teach all to cry aloud unto Thee with palms and branches: Blessed art Thou that comest!

*And a reading in the Commentary on the Gospel by John Chrysostom.*

*After the 2nd Kathisma Psalmody, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Joseph was amazed" (ᲊди́висѧ):*
Over Thy friend, O Christ, Thou sheddest tears mystically, and Thou raisest from the dead Lazarus lying dead, wherein Thou didst show forth compassion as the Lover of mankind; and having learned of Thy coming, O Jesus, the multitude of infants went forth this day, holding palms in their hands, crying aloud Hosanna unto Thee: Blessed art Thou, Who art come to save the world!

*[Running header: On the Sunday of Palms / p. 26]*

*Glory... Both now... Another Sessional Hymn, Tone 1, Podoben: "When the stone had been sealed" (Ка́мени запеча́танꙋ):*
Praise with one accord, ye peoples and nations! For the King of angels hath mounted now upon a foal, and comes desiring upon the Cross to strike down the enemies as the Mighty One; therefore also the children with palms cry aloud a song: Glory to Thee Who art come as Conqueror! Glory to Thee, O Savior Christ! Glory to Thee, the Blessed One, our only God!

*And a reading of the same homily.*

*Then the Polyeleos; and after the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "That which was commanded mystically" (Повелѣ́нное):*
Sitting upon the Cherubic throne and mounting upon a foal for our sake, and having arrived at the voluntary Passion, this day He hears the children proclaiming: Hosanna! and the multitudes crying aloud: O Son of David, hasten to save those whom Thou hast created, O blessed Jesus; for unto this art Thou come, that we might perceive Thy glory!

*Glory... Both now... the same again.*

*And we make a reading on the feast: the Homily of lord Andrew of Crete. Then the Hymns of Ascent: the first antiphon of Tone 4.*

*Prokeimenon, Tone 4:*
Out of the mouth of babes and sucklings hast Thou perfected praise [Ps. 8:3 LXX].  
*Verse: O Lord our Lord, how wonderful is Thy name in all the earth [Ps. 8:2 LXX]!*

*"Let every breath praise the Lord." The Gospel from Matthew, pericope 83 [Matthew 21:1-11, 15-17]. But "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ" we do not say; but immediately Psalm 50.*

*Then the Priest, taking the censer, censes cruciformly, saying the Prayer for the Blessing of Palms:[^41]*

**Deacon:** Let us pray to the Lord.

**Choir:** Lord, have mercy.

**Priest:** O Lord our God, Who sittest upon the Cherubim, Who didst stir up the power of Thy Son, our Lord Jesus Christ, that He might save the world through His Cross, and burial, and Resurrection; Whom also now having come unto Jerusalem for the voluntary Passion, the people that sat in darkness and the shadow of death, taking the tokens of the Resurrection, the branches of trees and palms of date-trees, met Him, signifying the Resurrection: do Thou Thyself, O Master, also preserve and keep us who, in imitation of them, carry palms and branches of trees in our hands on this pre-festal day; that, even as those multitudes and children offered Hosanna unto Thee, we likewise in songs and spiritual hymns may attain unto the life-creating and third-day Resurrection, in the same Christ Jesus our Lord; with Whom Thou art blessed, together with Thine all-holy, good, and life-creating Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.

*And while the brethren kiss the Gospel, the Superior distributes the palms.*

*Glory... Tone 2:*
This day Christ enters into the town of Bethany, sitting upon a foal, destroying the most evil irrationality of the Gentiles which of old raged fiercely.

*Both now... the same again.*

*[Running header: At Matins / p. 27]*

*Then: "Have mercy on me, O God." Sticheron, Tone 6: "Today the grace of the Holy Spirit hath gathered us together." Thereafter the prayer: "O God, save Thy people;" "Lord, have mercy," 12 times; and after the exclamation.*

*The Canon, the heirmoi twice; the troparia unto 12. Katavasia: the heirmos by both choirs together:*

*The composition of lord Cosmas, the acrostic whereof is: Hosanna, Christ is blessed God (Ὡσαννὰ Χριστὸς εὐλογημένος Θεός):*[^42]

*Ode 1, Tone 4. Heirmos:*
The fountains of the deep were seen, devoid of moisture, and the tempest-tossed foundations of the surging sea were uncovered; for by Thy nod Thou didst rebuke it, and didst save the chosen people who sing a song of victory unto Thee, O Lord!

*Troparion [Acrostic: (*Ѡ*)]:*
Out of the mouth of innocent babes and sucklings Thou hast perfected praise for Thy servants, to destroy the adversary, and by the Passion of the Cross to avenge the fall of ancient Adam, and by the Tree to raise him up who sing a song of victory unto Thee, O Lord!

*Troparion [Acrostic: (*С*)]:*
The Church of the righteous offers praise unto Thee, O Christ, Who dwellest in Zion; and in Thee, his Creator, Israel rejoices; and the mountains, the stone-hearted Gentile nations standing opposite, were made glad before Thy face, singing a song of victory unto Thee, O Lord!

*Katavasia: "The fountains of the deep were seen."*

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
The people of Israel, by Thy command, sucked water flowing from the precipitous sharp rock; but Thou art the Rock, O Christ, and the Life, whereon the Church is established, crying aloud: Hosanna! Blessed art Thou that comest!

*Troparion [Acrostic: (*А*)]:*
By Thy command hades in trembling released from the dead Lazarus who was four days dead; for Thou art the Resurrection, O Christ, and the Life, wherein the Church is established, crying aloud: Hosanna! Blessed art Thou that comest!

*Troparion [Acrostic: (*Н*)]:*
Sing, ye peoples, in a manner befitting God in Zion, and render prayer unto Christ in Jerusalem; He Himself comes in glory with dominion, wherein the Church is established, crying aloud: Hosanna! Blessed art Thou that comest!

*Hypakoe, Tone 6:*
Having sung praises first with branches, the ungrateful Jews afterwards took hold of Christ God with staves; but we, ever honoring Him with unwavering faith as Benefactor, continually cry unto Him: Blessed art Thou that comest to call back Adam!

*And a reading of the feast.*

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Christ Who comes is manifestly our God; He shall come and shall not delay, from the mountain overshadowed and densely wooded, from the Maiden who gave birth without knowing a man, as the Prophet said of old; therefore we all cry aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion [Acrostic: (*Н*)]:*
Let the mountains drip a strong, rich gladness, and all the hills, and let the trees of the forest leap for joy! Praise Christ, ye Gentiles, and unto Him, all ye peoples, crying out with rejoicing, shout: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion [Acrostic: (*А*)]:*
The Lord, Who by His might reigns over the ages, shall come clothed with majesty; the beauty and glory of His majesty in Zion is incomparable; therefore we all cry aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*[Running header: On the Sunday of Palms / p. 28]*

*Troparion [Acrostic: (*Х*)]:*
He Who hath measured the heaven with a span and the earth with the palm of His hand, the Lord, is come; for He hath chosen Zion, wherein He hath been pleased to dwell and reign, and He hath loved the people who faithfully cry aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Get thee up into the high mountain of Zion, thou that bringest good tidings, and thou that preachest to Jerusalem, lift up thy voice with strength: Glorious things are spoken of thee, O city of God! Peace be upon Israel, and salvation unto the Gentiles!

*Troparion [Acrostic: (*Р*)]:*
God, Who sittest on high upon the Cherubim and lookest down upon the humble, He comes in glory with dominion, and all things shall be filled with His divine praise: Peace be upon Israel, and salvation unto the Gentiles!

*Troparion [Acrostic: (*І*)]:*
O Zion of God, holy mountain, and Jerusalem, lift up thine eyes round about, and see thy children gathered together within thee; for behold, they are come from afar to worship thy King: Peace be upon Israel, and salvation unto the Gentiles!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
The souls of the righteous cried aloud with gladness: Now is a New Covenant ordained for the world, and let the people be renewed by the sprinkling of divine Blood!

*Troparion [Acrostic: (*С*)]:*
Receive, O Israel, the Kingdom of God; and let him that abides in darkness behold a great light, and let the people be renewed by the sprinkling of divine Blood!

*Troparion [Acrostic: (*Т*)]:*
Release thy prisoners loosed from bonds, O Zion, and lead them out from the waterless pit of ignorance, and let the people be renewed by the sprinkling of divine Blood!

*Kontakion, idiomelon, Tone 6:*
Borne upon the throne in heaven and upon a foal on earth, O Christ God, Thou didst accept the praise of angels and the hymnody of the children who cried unto Thee: Blessed art Thou that comest to call back Adam!

*Ikos:*[^43]
Because Thou hast bound hades, O Immortal One, and hast put death to death, and hast raised the world, with palms the children praised Thee, O Christ, as Conqueror, crying unto Thee this day: Hosanna to the Son of David! For no longer, He saith, shall infants be slain because of Mary's Infant; but for all infants and elders, Thou alone art crucified; no longer shall the sword be brandished against us, for Thy side shall be pierced with a spear; therefore rejoicing we say: Blessed art Thou that comest to call back Adam!

*Synaxarion:*[^44]

*Verses:*
He Who by His word stretched out the heaven sat upon a foal,  
Seeking to deliver mankind from irrationality.  

On this day, the glorious and all-radiant feast of the celebration of palms is celebrated for the following cause: After the resurrection of Lazarus from the dead, many having seen what had come to pass believed in Christ; and so the council of the Jewish assembly determined to put to death both Christ and Lazarus himself. Jesus therefore fled, giving place to malice; and the Jews took counsel to kill Him on the feast of the Passover. And a long interval of time having elapsed, six days before the Passover, He saith, Jesus came unto Bethany, where Lazarus was who had died. And dinner having been made there, Lazarus also ate with them; and his sister Mary poured myrrh upon the feet of Christ. And on the morrow He sent His disciples to bring the ass and the foal. And He Who hath heaven for His throne, sitting upon the foal, entered into Jerusalem; and the Hebrew children themselves spread their garments before Him, while others, cutting branches of palm trees, carried them in their hands, crying aloud before Him: Hosanna to the Son of David! Blessed is He that comes in the name of the Lord, the King of Israel! And this came to pass as the All-Holy Spirit moved the tongues of the holy ones for the glory and praise of Christ. The palms, that is, the branches, prefigured Christ's victory over death...

*[Running header: At Matins / p. 29]*

...victory by palms, that is, branches; for among the Hebrews a tender branch is called *vaia*. For it was the custom to honor those who achieved victories or certain kings with branches of evergreen trees in triumphal processions, and they surrounded the assembly. More enigmatically, the foal prefigured us, the people from among the Gentiles, upon whom Christ mounted and, having sojourned, became Victor and Conqueror, and was proclaimed King of the whole earth. Concerning this feast the Prophet Zechariah also spoke: Rejoice greatly, O daughter of Zion; behold, thy King comes unto thee, meek, and sitting upon a beast of burden and a young colt, the foal of an ass. And David again concerning the children: Out of the mouth of babes and sucklings hast Thou perfected praise. But when Christ entered, He saith, all Jerusalem was shaken; and the multitudes in turmoil, having been incited by the chief priests, sought to kill Him; but He concealed Himself, hiding and appearing, and spoke unto them in parables. By Thine ineffable compassion, O Christ our God, make us victors over irrational passions, and make us worthy to behold Thy radiant victory over death and Thine illuminated and life-bearing Resurrection, and have mercy on us, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
Thou Who didst save in the fire Thy children of Abraham, and didst slay the Chaldeans whom justice righteously ensnared: O all-hymned Lord, the God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion [Acrostic: (*О*)]:*
Worshiping, the peoples and rejoicing with the disciples, with palms cried aloud: Hosanna to the Son of David! O all-hymned Lord, God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion [Acrostic: (*С*)]:*
The innocent multitude, nature yet in infancy, hymned Thee in a manner befitting God, O King of Israel and of the angels: O all-hymned Lord, God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion [Acrostic: (*Е*)]:*
With palms and branches the multitudes praised Thee, O Christ, crying aloud: Blessed is He that is come, the King of the ages! O all-hymned Lord, God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Be glad, O Jerusalem! Keep feast, ye that love Zion! For the Lord of hosts, Who reigns unto the ages, is come; let all the earth stand in awe before His face, and cry aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion [Acrostic: (*Ѵ*)]:*
Mounted upon a young foal, thy King, O Zion, Christ, hath appeared; for He is come to destroy the irrational deception of idols and to stay the unrestrained rush of all the Gentiles, that they may sing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion [Acrostic: (*Л*)]:*
Thy God, rejoice greatly, O Zion, Christ, reigns unto the ages! He, as it is written, meek and saving, our righteous Deliverer, is come upon a foal, to put to death the furious horse-rage of the enemies who cry not: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion [Acrostic: (*О*)]:*
The law-transgressing congregation of the disobedient is deprived of divine ramparts, since they made the house of prayer of God a den of thieves, casting off from their heart the Deliverer, unto Whom we cry aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*"More honorable than the Cherubim" is not sung.*[^45]

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
God is the Lord, and hath appeared unto us; assemble the feast, and coming with gladness let us magnify Christ, crying with palms and branches in hymns: Blessed is He that comes in the name of the Lord, our Savior!

*Troparion [Acrostic: (*Г*)]:*
Why do ye rage, ye Gentiles? Scribes and priests, why have ye meditated on vain things, saying: Who is this, unto Whom the children with palms and branches cry aloud in hymns: Blessed is He that comes in the name of the Lord, our Savior!

*Troparion [Acrostic: (*Н*)]:*
This is our God, unto Whom none is like; having found out every righteous path, He gave it unto beloved Israel; and after these things He was seen upon earth and lived among men: Blessed is He that comes in the name of the Lord, our Savior!

*[Running header: On the Sunday of Palms / p. 30]*

*Troparion [Acrostic: (*М*)]:*
Why have ye laid stumbling-blocks in the paths close to us, ye disobedient? Your feet are swift to shed the Master's blood; but He shall surely rise to save them that cry: Blessed is He that comes in the name of the Lord, our Savior!

*The Exapostilarion we do not say, but immediately:*

*"Holy is the Lord our God," thrice, in Tone 4.*

*At the Praises,*

*we set 6 stichoi, and chant 4 idiomelic stichera, repeating two:*

*Tone 4:*

*Sticheron 1:*
A multitude of the people, O Lord, spread their garments in the way; while others cut branches from the trees, and carried them; and they that went before and they that followed after cried aloud, saying: Hosanna to the Son of David! Blessed art Thou Who art come, and art coming again in the name of the Lord!

*Sticheron 2:*
When Thou wast pleased to enter into the holy city, O Lord, the people carried branches of gardens, singing unto Thee, the Master of all; and beholding Thee sitting upon a foal even as upon the Cherubim they beheld Thee, for this cause they cried aloud thus: Hosanna in the highest! Blessed art Thou Who art come, and art coming again in the name of the Lord!

*Sticheron 3:*
Come forth, ye Gentiles! Come forth also, ye peoples, and behold this day the Heavenly King, as upon a lofty throne, entering into Jerusalem upon a lowly foal! O faithless and adulterous Jewish generation, come and see Him Whom Isaiah beheld about to come in the flesh for our sake, how He betroths unto Himself the chaste New Zion, and casts off the condemned synagogue; for as unto an open and undefiled bridal banquet, the undefiled and innocent children ran together with praised acclaim, with whom we also singing cry aloud the angelic hymn: Hosanna in the highest to Him that hath great mercy!

*Sticheron 4:*
Giving assurance of the general resurrection before Thy voluntary Passion for the strengthening of all, O Christ God, Thou didst raise dead Lazarus who was four days in the tomb in Bethany by Thy sovereign power; and unto the blind Thou didst grant sight as the Giver of light, O Savior; and Thou didst enter into the holy city with Thy disciples, sitting upon the foal of an ass, fulfilling the prophetic preachings, even as One borne upon the Cherubim; and the Hebrew children went out to meet Thee with branches and palms; therefore we also, carrying branches of olives and palms, cry unto Thee with thanksgiving: Hosanna in the highest! Blessed is He that comes in the name of the Lord!

*Glory... Both now... Tone 6:*
Six days before Pascha, Jesus came unto Bethany, and His disciples came unto Him, saying unto Him: Lord, where wilt Thou that we prepare for Thee to eat the Passover? And He sent them: Go ye into the village opposite, and ye shall find a man bearing an earthen jar of water; follow him, and say unto the master of the house: The Teacher saith: With thee will I keep the Passover with My disciples.

*Great Doxology. And the troparion: "Giving assurance of the general resurrection."*

*Then the Litanies, and the First Hour, and the Catechesis, and the customary Dismissal, and a Litiya outside the monastery.*

*At the Liturgy,*

*Antiphon 1, Tone 2, Psalm 114 [LXX]:*[^46]

*Verse 1:* I have loved, because the Lord will hear the voice of my supplication [Ps. 114:1 LXX].  
*Refrain:* Through the prayers of the Theotokos, O Savior, save us.

*Verse 2:*

*[Running header: At the Liturgy / p. 31]*

*Verse 2:* For He hath inclined His ear unto me, and in my days will I call upon Him [Ps. 114:2 LXX].  
*Refrain:* Through the prayers of the Theotokos, O Savior, save us.

*Verse 3:* The sorrows of death compassed me, the perils of hades found me [Ps. 114:3 LXX].  
*Refrain:* Through the prayers of the Theotokos, O Savior, save us.

*Verse 4:* Affliction and sorrow did I find, and I called upon the name of the Lord [Ps. 114:4 LXX].  
*Refrain:* Through the prayers of the Theotokos, O Savior, save us.

*Glory... Both now...*  
*Refrain:* Through the prayers of the Theotokos, O Savior, save us.

*Antiphon 2, Psalm 115 [LXX]:*

*Verse 1:* I believed, therefore have I spoken; but I was humbled exceedingly [Ps. 115:1 LXX].  
*Refrain:* Save us, O Son of God, Who didst sit upon a foal, who sing to Thee: Alleluia!

*Verse 2:* What shall I render unto the Lord for all the things that He hath rendered unto me [Ps. 115:3 LXX]?  
*Refrain:* Save us, O Son of God...

*Verse 3:* I will take the cup of salvation, and call upon the name of the Lord [Ps. 115:4 LXX].  
*Refrain:* Save us, O Son of God...

*Verse 4:* I will pay my vows unto the Lord in the presence of all His people [Ps. 115:5 LXX].  
*Refrain:* Save us, O Son of God...

*Glory... Both now...*  
Only-begotten Son and immortal Word of God...

*Antiphon 3, Tone 1, Psalm 117 [LXX]:*

*Verse 1:* Give thanks unto the Lord, for He is good, for His mercy endures unto the ages [Ps. 117:1 LXX]!  
*Troparion:* Giving assurance of the general resurrection before Thy Passion, Thou didst raise Lazarus from the dead, O Christ our God...

*Verse 2:* Let the house of Israel now say: For He is good, for His mercy endures unto the ages [Ps. 117:2 LXX]!  
*Troparion:* Giving assurance of the general resurrection...

*Verse 3:* Let the house of Aaron now say: For He is good, for His mercy endures unto the ages [Ps. 117:3 LXX]!  
*Troparion:* Giving assurance of the general resurrection...

*Verse 4:* Let all that fear the Lord now say: For He is good, for His mercy endures unto the ages [Ps. 117:4 LXX]!

*[Running header: On the Sunday of Palms, at the Liturgy / p. 32]*

*Troparion:* Giving assurance of the general resurrection...

*Entrance Verse:*[^47]  
Blessed is He that comes in the name of the Lord; we have blessed you out of the house of the Lord; God is the Lord, and hath appeared unto us [Ps. 117:26-27 LXX].

*Troparion: "Giving assurance of the general resurrection;" Glory... "Having been buried with Thee through baptism, O Christ;" Both now... Kontakion: "Borne upon the throne in heaven."*

*Then the Trisagion. Prokeimenon of the Epistle, Tone 4:*  
Blessed is He that comes in the name of the Lord; God is the Lord, and hath appeared unto us [Ps. 117:26-27 LXX].  
*Verse: Give thanks unto the Lord, for He is good, for His mercy endures unto the ages [Ps. 117:1 LXX]!*

*The Epistle to the Philippians, pericope 247 [Philippians 4:4-9].*  
*Alleluia, Tone 1: Sing unto the Lord a new song, for the Lord hath done marvelous things [Ps. 97:1 LXX].*  
*Verse: All the ends of the earth have seen the salvation of our God [Ps. 97:3 LXX].*

*The Gospel from John, pericope 41 [John 12:1-18]. And the Liturgy of John Chrysostom is celebrated.*

*Communion Verse: Blessed is He that comes in the name of the Lord; God is the Lord, and hath appeared unto us [Ps. 117:26-27 LXX]. Alleluia, thrice.*

*At the refectory there is consolation: we eat fish and drink wine, giving thanks unto God.*[^48]

*
[Ornamental decorative woodcut headpiece]
*

## ON THE SUNDAY OF PALMS, AT VESPERS[^49]

*At "Lord, I have cried," we set 6 stichoi, and chant three idiomelic stichera, repeating them, Tone 8:*

*Sticheron 1:*
Rejoice and be glad, O city of Zion! Exult and rejoice, O Church of God! For behold, thy King is come in righteousness, sitting upon a foal, hymned by the children: Hosanna in the highest! Blessed art Thou Who hast an abundance of tender mercies, have mercy on us!

*Sticheron 2:*
The Savior came this day unto the city of Jerusalem to fulfill the Scripture; and all took branches in their hands, and spread garments before Him, knowing that He is our God, unto Whom the Cherubim cry unceasingly: Hosanna in the highest! Blessed art Thou Who hast an abundance of tender mercies, have mercy on us!

*Sticheron 3:*
Borne upon the Cherubim and hymned by the Seraphim, Thou didst mount upon a foal in the manner of David, O Good One; and the children praised Thee in a manner befitting God, while the Jews blasphemed lawlessly; the sitting upon the foal prefigured the stubbornness of the Gentiles transformed from unbelief into faith; glory to Thee, O Christ, Who alone art merciful and the Lover of mankind!

*Glory... "Rejoice and be glad."*

*Both now... "Borne upon the Cherubim."*

*Entrance. Customary Prokeimenon: "Behold now, bless ye the Lord [Ps. 133:1 LXX]."*

*At the Aposticha, idiomelic stichera, Tone 8:*

*Sticheron 1:*
From branches and palms, as from a divine feast having passed unto a divine feast, unto the precious and saving mystery of Christ's Sufferings, let us faithful hasten together, and let us behold Him...

*[Running header: On the Sunday of Palms, at Vespers / p. 33]*

...enduring His voluntary Passion for us; and unto Him let us with thanksgiving sing a fitting song, crying out with contrition: O Fountain of tender mercy and Haven of salvation, O Lord, glory to Thee!

*Verse: Sing unto the Lord a new song [Ps. 97:1 LXX].*

*Tone 3:*

*Sticheron 2:*
A fearful thing it is to fall into the hands of the living God [Heb. 10:31]. He is the Judge of the thoughts and intents of the heart [Heb. 4:12]; let no one enter tempting the undefiled faith; but in meekness and fear let us draw near unto Christ, that we may receive mercy and find grace to help in time of need [Heb. 4:16].

*Verse: All the ends of the earth have seen the salvation of our God [Ps. 97:3 LXX].*

*Tone 7:*

*Sticheron 3:*
O wicked and adulterous synagogue, that hast not preserved faith unto thy Husband, why holdest thou the covenant whereof thou wast not the heiress? Why boastest thou of the Father, having rejected the Son? Thou didst not receive the Prophets who foretold the Son; be ashamed at least before thine own children, who cry out thus: Hosanna to the Son of David! Blessed is He that comes in the name of the Lord!

*Glory... Tone 2:*
From branches and palms...

*Both now... Tone 3:*
A fearful thing it is to fall into the hands of the living God...

*Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart;*

*And after "Our Father," the troparion: "Rejoice, O Virgin Theotokos;"*

*And the rest according to custom, and the customary bows.*

*Be it known that "Rejoice, O Virgin Theotokos," and the rest, are chanted with melodious chanting on the Sundays of the Holy Forty Days; see likewise for the present Sunday.*[^50]

*On the other days of Great Week, they are said quickly.*

*

 [Running header: On the Sunday of Palms / p. 34]*

*

## ON THE SUNDAY OF PALMS, AT COMPLINE

*We say Small Compline, whereon we sing the tri-ode of lord Andrew of Crete. The heirmos twice. The troparia, as many as there be. And the final heirmos by both choirs together. In like manner do we also on the remaining evenings of Great Week, at Compline.*[^51]

*Ode 1, Tone 8. Heirmos:*
Unto Him Who broke wars with His arm, and led Israel through the Red Sea, let us sing unto Him, as unto our Redeemer God, for He is glorified!

*Troparion:*
Having imitated the chastity of Joseph, ye faithful, let us with a pure mind honor the rational human nature, having lived in all watchfulness by active virtue.

*Troparion:*
The non-working of good deeds is likened unto the fig tree; therefore let us turn away from this, lest we wither away as it did then, prefiguring the assembly that covered itself with leaves.

*Troparion:*
Bearing the Master's image, Joseph is cast into a pit, is sold by his kinsmen, and all things the ever-memorable one endures, truly as a type of Christ.

*Troparion:*
Fearing the barrenness of the fig tree, brethren, let us understand the figure, lest we wither away as it did then, when returning the Lover of mankind came unto it hungering.

*Troparion:*
Jesus, striving to suffer voluntarily for the world, goes with His disciples unto the city of Jerusalem unto the voluntary Passion, which He is come to suffer.

*Troparion:*
As all things of the Lord draw near which He is eager to suffer, let us be ready for spitting, for reproaches, and for humiliations, that by His all-pure Sufferings we may be glorified together with Him, ye faithful.

*Troparion:*
By His Sufferings He Who suffered for us heals our passions; for willingly He directs His life-creating sufferings upon our human nature, that we may be saved.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*
The Trinity unoriginate I glorify, three holies I sing, three co-ever-existing in One Essence I proclaim; for as One in Father, Son, and Spirit, God is glorified.

*Both now... Theotokion:*
The rod of Moses and the rod of Aaron showed forth a strange transformation and a dispensation beyond understanding; but thy womb, O Mother of God, renews a new Birth.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ):*
Let not the condemnation of the fig tree anticipate thee; but strive, O my soul, to make known good fruits with the clusters of the heart unto Christ thy Creator, offering them unto Him in repentance.

*[Running header: At Compline / p. 35]*

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Unto Him Who was glorified on the holy mountain, and by fire in the bush revealed unto Moses the mystery of the Ever-Virgin, sing unto the Lord, and exalt Him above all for ever!

*Troparion:*
Having adorned our life with chastity, and having preserved the faith with wisdom, let us seek uprightness of righteousness, that following after courage, we may be crucified together with Christ.

*Troparion:*
Finding a second Eve in the Egyptian woman, the patriarch Joseph was not overcome unto an unfitting deed, but stood like an adamant, not seized by sinful passions.

*Troparion:*
Passing through the journeys of this life, O my Savior, Thou didst hunger voluntarily, desiring the salvation of all; for of this didst Thou hunger: the conversion of those distanced from Thee.

*Troparion:*
The forefather, having tasted of the tree, when he knew himself naked and put to shame, took fig leaves and girded himself; for the stripped synagogue prefigured Christ.

*Troparion:*
Prepare thyself, O my soul, before the departure; order thyself unto that other life; and unto Christ, Who for thy sake is eager to suffer that He may glorify thee, strive to suffer together, and to be crucified together, and to die.

*Troparion:*
How shall death not be terrified, O my Savior? How shall hades not fear when meeting Thee, eager in good pleasure unto the Passion, and beholding Thee, the Righteous One, coming to suffer on behalf of the unrighteous?

*Troparion:*
Having seen the resurrection of Lazarus, the Jews, the priests and the Levites, having prepared a conspiratorial agreement through envy, by the deceit of betrayal delivered Christ unto Pilate unto death.

*Troparion:*
Thy Lamb, and handmaid, and Virgin, seeing Thee hastening unto the Passion, and laying down Thy life for us, the Good Shepherd, wept after Thee with a mother's inner heart.

*We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit: the Lord.*

*Trinity:*
As a Monad in Essence I sing of Thee, as a Trinity in Persons I honor Thee, O Father, and Son, and All-Holy Spirit; the unoriginate dominion of Thy Kingdom I glorify unto the ages.

*Both now... Theotokion:*
The Mother of God prays Thee, O Christ; the assembly of Thine Apostles prays Thee: grant Thy peace unto Thy world, and lavishly bestow Thy tender mercies upon us unto the ages.

*We praise, we bless, and we worship the Lord.*

*Heirmos: "Unto Him Who was glorified on the holy mountain..."*

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Virginity is alien to mothers, and childbearing is strange to virgins; but in thee, O Mother of God, both were accomplished; therefore all the families of the earth unceasingly magnify thee.

*Troparion:*
Chastity is alien to the ungodly, and transgression of the law is strange to the righteous; but great Joseph turned away from sin, and became an image of chastity, and truly a likeness of Christ.

*Troparion:*
Good order is alien to lawbreakers, and good understanding is strange to the faithless; and the Jews rejected these things through lawlessness, therefore they also alone inherited the curse, even as the fig tree.

*[Running header: On the Sunday of Palms / p. 36]*

*Troparion:*
Christ, my God, Who is the Bread of life, hungered for the salvation of mankind; therefore, having anticipated the fig tree—the barren synagogue adorned legally with leaves—when He beheld it, He cursed it.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst curse the barrenness of the Law, as leaves budding the letter's meaning, yet having no fruits of deeds because of lawlessness; but bless us all, the sons of grace, O Savior.

*Troparion:*
The rod of Moses of old was changed into a serpent, and the rod of Aaron was changed into foliage and budded leaves; but the law-transgressing, dried-up, barren assembly was changed into the barren fig tree.

*Troparion:*
Prepare thy priests, O Judah, and set thine hands to the slaying of God; for behold, the meek and quiet One comes unto the Passion, Who is the Lamb and our Shepherd, Christ, King of Israel.

*Troparion:*
Receive thy King, O Judah; for behold, He comes voluntarily unto the Passion, that He may suffer and save those who cry out unceasingly: Blessed is He that comes to save man by the Cross!

*Troparion:*
The River hath turned thy feasts into mourning, O Judah, according to the prophecy; for thou hast shown thyself a slayer of God, of Him Who of old turned the rock and the flinty stone into water springs and lakes, even as David sings [Ps. 113:8 LXX].

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*
It is alien to the lawless to glorify Thee, the unoriginate Essence, Father, and Son, and Holy Spirit, uncreated Almighty power, whereby the whole world is upheld by the beckoning of Thy divine majesty.

*Both now... Theotokion:*
We draw near unto prayer, O Mother of God: by thy prayers and those of Thine Apostles, make us partakers, O Master, of Thy good things, and count us worthy, O Savior, of the brightness of Thy Resurrection.

*And again the same heirmos: "Virginity is alien to mothers."*

*Then also the rest according to custom, and the Dismissal.*

*Be it known that in this holy Great Week we complete the Psalter once, and we say three kathismata at Matins, and at the Hours, the Third and the Sixth, two. And they are completed on Wednesday.*[^52]

*On Great Saturday the Blameless [Psalm 118 LXX / Kathisma 17] are chanted; and at Vespers we say according to custom: "Unto the Lord, when I was afflicted" [Psalm 119 LXX / 18th Kathisma].*[^53]

*[Running header: On Great and Holy Monday, at Matins / p. 37]*

*
[Ornamental decorative woodcut headpiece]
*

## ON GREAT AND HOLY MONDAY

*At Matins,*[^54]

*After the Six Psalms, we chant "Alleluia," in Tone 8, in a loud voice, and with sweet singing. After "Alleluia," we chant the troparion slowly and in a loud voice, and likewise with sweet singing.*

*Tone 8:*

*Troparion:*
Behold, the Bridegroom comes at midnight, and blessed is the servant whom He shall find watching; but unworthy is he again whom He shall find heedless; beware therefore, O my soul, lest thou be weighed down with sleep, lest thou be given over unto death, and be shut out from the Kingdom; but rouse thyself, crying aloud: Holy, Holy, Holy art Thou, O God, through the Theotokos have mercy on us! *Thrice.*

*Then the customary psalmody with bows.*

*After the 1st Kathisma Psalmody, Sessional Hymn, Tone 1, Podoben: "Thy tomb, O Savior" (Гро́бъ тво́й):*
The present day causes the venerable Sufferings to shine forth upon the world like perfect luminaries; for Christ comes to suffer in goodness, He Who holds all things in the palm of His hand, and is pleased to be stretched upon the Tree that He may save man.

*Glory... Both now... the same again.*

*After the 2nd Kathisma Psalmody, Sessional Hymn, Tone 1, Podoben: "Thy tomb, O Savior" (Гро́бъ тво́й):*
O Invisible Judge, how wast Thou seen in the flesh, and goest to be slain by lawless men, condemning our condemnation by Thy Passion? Therefore sending up praise, majesty, and glory unto Thy dominion, O Word, we offer it with one accord.

*Glory... Both now... the same again.*

*After the 3rd Kathisma Psalmody, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "The Wisdom and Word of God" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
The present day brings light as the beginning of the Lord's Sufferings; come, therefore, ye lovers of feasts, let us go out to meet them with hymns; for the Creator comes to receive the Cross, interrogations, and wounds, judged by Pilate; therefore being struck on the head by a servant, He endures all things, that He may save man; for this cause let us cry aloud unto Him: O Lover of mankind, Christ God, grant remission of transgressions unto them that worship with faith Thine all-pure Sufferings!

*Glory... Both now... the same again.*

*[Running header: On Great and Holy Monday / p. 38]*

*And immediately we read a reading in the Commentary on the Gospel according to Matthew.*[^55]

*And the Homily of John Chrysostom on the withering of the fig tree, the beginning whereof is: "Even as the appearance..."*[^56]

*And the Homily of Damascene on the withering of the fig tree, and on the parable of the vineyard.*[^57]

*Then: "And that we may be accounted worthy;" the Gospel according to Matthew, pericope 84, without skipping: "At that time, Jesus returning into the city, hungered;" ending in pericope 88: "And it shall be given to a nation producing the fruits thereof" [Matthew 21:18-43].*

*After this, Psalm 50. Then: "O God, save Thy people;" "Lord, have mercy," 12 times.*

*The Tri-ode, the composition of lord Cosmas, the acrostic whereof is: Τῇ δευτέρᾳ, that is: "On the second day," which is Monday. The heirmos twice, the troparia unto 12.*[^58]

*Refrain: Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee. (This throughout the whole week). The final heirmos by both choirs together:*

*Tone 2. Ode 1. Heirmos [Acrostic: T]:*
Unto Him Who by His divine command dried up the impassable surging sea, and guided the pedestrian people to march on foot through it, let us sing unto the Lord, for He is gloriously glorified!

*Troparion [Acrostic: H / Slavonic (*И*)]:*
The ineffable condescension of God the Word—that the same Christ is God and man, not deeming it robbery to be God [Phil. 2:6], when taking the form of a servant—He shows unto the disciples, for He is gloriously glorified!

*Troparion [Acrostic: Δ]:*
I Myself came to minister, Whose form the Creator was pleased to put on, unto impoverished Adam, though rich in Divinity, desiring to lay down My life as a ransom for him, Who am impassible in Divinity.

*Kontakion, Tone 8, Podoben: "As the first-fruits of nature" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ нача́тки):*
Jacob lamented the loss of Joseph, while the noble one sat upon a chariot, honored as a king; for having not been enslaved then unto the pleasures of the Egyptian woman, he was glorified by Him Who knows the hearts of men, and Who sends an incorruptible crown.

*Ikos:*
Unto lamentation let us now add lamentation, and let us pour forth tears with Jacob, mourning for the ever-memorable and chaste Joseph, who was indeed enslaved in body, but kept his soul unenslaved, and ruled over all Egypt; for God bestows an incorruptible crown upon His servants.

*Synaxarion:*[^59]

*Verses on Joseph the All-Comely:*
Chaste Joseph showed himself a righteous ruler,  
And a provider of corn: O what noble possessions!  

*Verses on the Withered Fig Tree:*
Likening the Jewish synagogue unto a fig tree  
Void of spiritual fruits,  
Christ dries it up with a curse; let us flee its fate!  

On Great and Holy Monday, we commemorate the blessed Joseph the All-Comely, and the withered fig tree; because from this point the holy Sufferings of our Lord Jesus Christ take their beginning...

*[Running header: At Matins / p. 39]*

...And Joseph is first taken as a type of this. For he was the youngest son of the patriarch Jacob, born to him of Rachel, envied by his own brethren on account of certain dreams. First he was hidden in an excavated pit, and stolen from his father by his children with a deceitfully blood-stained coat, as though he had been devoured by beasts. Then he was sold for thirty pieces of silver unto the Ishmaelites, who in turn sold him unto Potiphar, chief of the eunuchs of Pharaoh, king of Egypt. And when his mistress raged against the chastity of the youth, because he would not commit lawlessness, he left his garment and fled; but she slandered him unto her master, and bonds and prison received him. Then by the interpretation of dreams he was brought out, and presented unto the king, and appointed ruler over all the land of Egypt. And again by the distribution of corn he was made known unto his brethren, and having spent all his life well, he died in Egypt, known as great in chastity alongside his other virtues. And he is a type of Christ: for Christ also is envied by the Jews of His own tribe, and sold by His disciple for thirty pieces of silver, and shut up in the dark and gloomy pit of the tomb; and bursting forth thence by His own sovereign power, He reigns over Egypt—that is, over every sin, and vanquishes it unto the end—and rules over the whole world, and as the Lover of mankind redeems us by the mystical distribution of corn, since He gave Himself for us, and feeds us with heavenly bread, His life-bearing Flesh. In accordance with this discourse, therefore, Joseph the All-Comely is commemorated this day.

Here also we make commemoration of the withered fig tree: for the divine Evangelists, namely Matthew and Mark, record after the narrative concerning the palms: "On the morrow, when they were come from Bethany, He hungered" [Mark 11:12]. And the other says: "Now in the morning, as He returned into the city, He hungered; and seeing a fig tree having only leaves, for the time of figs was not yet, He came unto it, and when He found no fruit thereon, He said: Let no fruit grow on thee henceforward for ever; and presently the fig tree withered away" [Matt. 21:18-19]. The fig tree is the Jewish synagogue, wherein the Savior found not fitting fruit, but only the shadow of the Law; and this He took away from them, making it utterly void. But if anyone say: For what reason did an inanimate tree become dry, taking a curse having not sinned? Let him know that the Jews, seeing Christ ever doing good unto all and causing no one any grief, objected that He had power only to do good, but not to do evil. And the Master, being the Lover of mankind, was not pleased to show this upon a man, lest it so happen; therefore, that He might convince the ungrateful people that He hath also sufficient power for vengeance, yet as the Good One desires it not, upon an inanimate and insensate nature He works vengeance. Together with this, there is also a certain ineffable saying that hath come down unto us from the most wise elders, even as Isidore of Pelusium says:[^60] that this was the tree of transgression, whose leaves the transgressors used for a covering; therefore also it was cursed by Christ as the Lover of mankind, since it did not suffer then, that it might no longer bear fruit that was guilty of sin. And that sin is likened unto a fig is praiseworthy: for it hath that which sweetens with pleasure, the stickiness of sin, and which hardens afterwards and torments the conscience. Nevertheless, from these things the narrative of the fig tree is set forth here for moral instruction, even as for Joseph because he bore the type of Christ. For every soul is a fig tree that is destitute of every spiritual fruit; unto which in the morning, immediately after the present life, the Lord finding no rest therein, dries it up with a curse, and sends it into eternal fire; and it stands as a withered pillar, terrifying them that bring not forth the fitting fruit of virtues.

By the prayers of Joseph the All-Comely, O Christ God, have mercy on us. Amen.

*Ode 8. Heirmos [Acrostic: E]:*
The unwearied fire, nourished in boundless fuel, was terrified by the devout youths, whose undefiled body was conformed unto the soul, and drew back; and as the flame dried up before the ever-living One, an everlasting hymn was sung: All ye works of the Lord, sing unto the Lord, and exalt Him above all for ever!

*Troparion [Acrostic: Y / Slavonic (*В*)]:*
"Then shall all know that ye are My disciples, if ye keep My commandments," says the Savior unto His friends, going unto the Passion: "Have peace in yourselves, and among all, and minding humble things ye shall be exalted; and knowing Me to be the Lord, sing unto Me, and exalt Me above all for ever!"

*Troparion [Acrostic: T / Slavonic (*Ч*)]:*
"Let the dominion of your kindred be of an order opposite to the Gentiles; for not My portion, but tyranny is self-chosen understanding; he among you therefore who desires to be preferred above others, let him be the last of all; and knowing Me to be the Lord, sing unto Me, and exalt Me above all for ever!"

*We praise, we bless, and we worship the Lord.*

*And again the Heirmos: "The unwearied fire..."*

*"More honorable than the Cherubim" is not sung.*

*Ode 9. Heirmos [Acrostic: E]:*
Thou hast magnified, O Christ, the Theotokos who bare Thee, from whom, O our Creator, Thou didst clothe Thyself in a body of like passions with us, for the loosed forgiveness of our transgressions; blessing her, all generations, we magnify Thee!

*Troparion [Acrostic: R / Slavonic (*С*)]:*
"Casting off all passionate defilement, let us receive a goodly-minded understanding worthy of the divine Kingdom," Thou didst foretell unto Thine Apostles, O Wisdom of all, "wherein ye shall be glorified, shining more radiantly than the sun."

*[Running header: On Great and Holy Monday / p. 40]*

*Troparion [Acrostic: A]:*
"Looking upon Me," Thou didst say, O Lord, unto Thy disciples: "Mind not high things, but condescend to men of low estate [Rom. 12:16]; drink ye the cup which I drink, that in the Kingdom of the Father ye may be glorified together with Me."

*Then the heirmos: "Thou hast magnified, O Christ..." And prostrations unto the earth.*

*The Exapostilarion [Tone 3], thrice, slowly and with sweet singing.*

*This is said until Great Thursday; it is chanted in the midst of the church by the chanter, and is echoed back by us:*[^61]

Thy bridal chamber I behold adorned, O my Savior, and I have no garment that I may enter therein; enlighten the vesture of my soul, O Giver of light, and save me!

*At the Praises, idiomelic stichera, on 4:*

*Tone 1:*

*Sticheron 1:*
The Lord, going unto the voluntary Passion, said unto the Apostles on the way: Behold, we go up to Jerusalem, and the Son of Man shall be betrayed, even as it is written concerning Him [Matt. 20:18]. Come therefore, we also, with cleansed thoughts, let us journey with Him, and let us be crucified together, and let us put to death for His sake the pleasures of this life, that we may also live with Him, and hear Him crying aloud: No longer unto the earthly Jerusalem, in order to suffer; but I ascend unto My Father, and your Father, and unto My God, and your God [John 20:17], and I will raise you up together into the Jerusalem above, into the Heavenly Kingdom! *Twice.*

*Tone 5:*

*Sticheron 2:*
Having attained unto the saving Passion of Christ God, ye faithful, let us glorify His ineffable long-suffering; that by His tender mercy He may raise up together also us who were deadened by sin, as the Good One and Lover of mankind! *Twice.*

*Glory... Both now... the same Tone:*
O Lord, going unto the Passion, strengthening Thy disciples, Thou didst say, having taken them aside: How do ye not remember My words which I told you before: that it is not written for any prophet to be slain except in Jerusalem? Now therefore the time is come which I told you: for behold, I am betrayed to be mocked by the hands of sinners, who having both nailed Me to the Cross and delivered Me to burial, shall reckon Me abhorred as dead; nevertheless, take courage, for on the third day I will arise unto the joy of the faithful and life everlasting!

*At the Aposticha, idiomelon, Tone 5:*

*Sticheron 1:*
O Lord, not attaining unto the mystery of Thine ineffable dispensation, the mother of the sons of Zebedee asked of Thee that the honors of a temporal kingdom might be bestowed upon her children; but instead of this, Thou didst promise Thy friends that they should drink the cup of death, which cup Thou didst say that Thou Thyself wouldst drink before these for the cleansing of sins; therefore we cry unto Thee: O Salvation of our souls, glory to Thee!

*Verse: We were filled in the morning with Thy mercy, O Lord [Ps. 89:14 LXX].*

*Sticheron 2:*
O Lord, teaching Thy disciples to mind the most perfect things, Thou didst tell them not to be conformed unto the Gentiles in ruling over the lesser; for it shall not be so among you, My disciples, for I am willingly poor; let the first among you therefore be the servant of all; and he that rules, as he that is ruled; and he that is preferred, as the last; for I Myself came to impoverished...

*[Running header: At Matins / p. 41]*

...to impoverished Adam to minister, and to give My life as a ransom for many who cry unto Me: Glory to Thee!

*Verse: And let the brightness of the Lord our God be upon us [Ps. 89:17 LXX].*

*Tone 8:*

*Sticheron 3:*
Fearing the rebuke of the fig tree withered for barrenness, brethren, let us bring forth worthy fruits of repentance unto Christ, Who bestows great mercy upon us.

*Glory... Both now... the same Tone:*
Finding the Egyptian woman a second Eve, the serpent tried by flatteries to trip up Joseph; but he, leaving his garment, fled from sin; and he was naked and not ashamed, even as the first-formed before the disobedience; by his prayers, O Christ, have mercy on us!

*Then: "It is good to give thanks;" and the rest according to custom, with bows.*

*Then the Dismissal:*
"The Lord, going unto His voluntary Passion for our salvation, Christ our true God..."

*And this Dismissal is said on Tuesday and on Wednesday.*[^62]

*Be it known that at the First Hour and the Ninth, we do not say the Psalter in Great Week, but only the three Psalms are said according to custom.*

*The Third Hour and the Sixth we sing with the Psalter. There is read also*

*The Four Gospels,*[^63]

*in Monday, in Tuesday, and in Wednesday it is completed.*

*
[Ornamental decorative woodcut dividing band]
*

*At the 3rd hour of the day, the paraecclesiarch strikes the semantron, as is the custom. And when we have assembled in the church,*

*We sing the Third Hour,*

*with a kathisma, and making the customary bows.*

*Then the troparion: "O Lord, Who at the third hour didst send down Thine All-Holy Spirit;" and "O Theotokos, thou art the vine;" 3 bows.*

*And immediately, an analogion having been adorned, they place upon it the Holy*

*Book of the Four Gospels,*

*and two lit lamps. All standing with uncovered heads. The Priest censes the whole temple and the brethren. And at the 6th hour he censes only the Gospel. At the 9th hour he censes the Gospel, and the whole temple, and the brethren. And thus the Gospel is read. And after the completion of the reading of the Gospel we say: "Blessed be the Lord God;" and the rest of the sequence. The kontakion of the day is also said: "Jacob lamented;" and the customary bows. And immediately we begin*

*The Sixth Hour.*

*"O come, let us worship," thrice, and the customary Psalms.*

*The regular kathisma. Then the troparion: "Thou Who on the sixth day and hour;" and the Theotokion: "Seeing that we have not boldness;" and 3 bows.*

*Then the Troparion of the Prophecy, Tone 2:*
With a contrite soul we fall down before Thee, and we pray Thee, O Savior of the world; for Thou art the God of the penitent.

*[Running header: On Great and Holy Monday / p. 42]*

*Glory... Both now... the same again.*

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 125 [LXX], Tone 4:*
When the Lord turned again the captivity of Zion [Ps. 125:1 LXX].  
*Verse: Then was our mouth filled with joy [Ps. 125:2 LXX].*

*Reading from the Prophecy of Ezekiel (Chapter 1) [Ezekiel 1:1-20]:*[^64]

Now it came to pass in the thirtieth year, in the fourth month, on the fifth day of the month, as I was in the midst of the captivity by the river Chebar, that the heavens were opened, and I saw visions of God. On the fifth day of the month, this was the fifth year of the captivity of king Jehoiachin, the word of the Lord came expressly unto Ezekiel the priest, the son of Buzi, in the land of the Chaldeans by the river Chebar; and the hand of the Lord was upon me. And I looked, and behold, a whirlwind came out of the north, a great cloud, and a fire infolding itself, and a brightness was round about it, and out of the midst thereof as the color of amber, out of the midst of the fire, and brightness was in it. Also out of the midst thereof came the likeness of four living creatures; and this was their appearance: they had the likeness of a man. And every one had four faces, and every one had four wings. And their legs were straight, and their feet were winged, and sparkled like the color of burnished brass, and their wings were swift. And they had the hands of a man under their wings on their four sides; and their faces and their wings were joined one to another across the four; their faces turned not when they went; they went every one straight forward. As for the likeness of their faces, they four had the face of a man, and the face of a lion on the right side; and they four had the face of an ox on the left side; they four also had the face of an eagle. And their faces and their wings were stretched upward on the four; two wings of every one were joined one to another, and two covered their bodies above; and they went every one straight forward: whither the spirit was to go, they went; and they turned not as they went. And in the midst of the living creatures was an appearance like burning coals of fire, and like the appearance of torches turning back and forth in the midst of the living creatures; and the fire was bright, and out of the fire went forth lightning. And the living creatures ran and returned as the appearance of a flash of lightning. Now as I beheld, behold, one wheel upon the earth by the four living creatures. The appearance of the wheels and their workmanship was like unto the appearance of beryl, and they four had one likeness; and their work was as it were a wheel in the middle of a wheel. When they went, they went upon their four sides; and they turned not when they went. As for their rings, they had both height; and I beheld them, and the rings of all four were full of eyes round about. And when the living creatures went, the wheels went by them; and when the living creatures were lifted up from the earth, the wheels were lifted up. Whithersoever the cloud was to go, there was also the spirit to go; the living creatures went, and the wheels were lifted up with them; for the spirit of life was in the wheels.

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 126 [LXX], Tone 4:*
Except the Lord build the house, they labor in vain that build it [Ps. 126:1 LXX].  
*Verse: Except the Lord keep the city, the watchman wakes but in vain [Ps. 126:1 LXX].*

*And the Gospel is read according to the prescribed order. And after the reading:*
"Let Thy tender mercies speedily anticipate us, O Lord [Ps. 78:8 LXX]."  

*Trisagion, with bows.*  

*After "Our Father:" Kontakion of the day. "Lord, have mercy," 40 times.*  

*Behold, be it known that the Gospel according to Matthew is read in its entirety, likewise also according to Mark, and according to Luke; but according to John it is read until the beginning of the Holy Sufferings, even as far as: "Now is the Son of Man glorified" [John 13:31]. And it is read thus in three days:*[^65]

*days, that is to say, on Monday, on Tuesday, and on Wednesday; and it is completed on Wednesday at the Ninth Hour. There are three readings from Luke, and two each from the others: in all there are nine readings, and none at Compline.*[^66]

*The Ninth Hour of these three days has no chanting of the Psalter, as has been said. But after the three Psalms, the appointed monk sings: "O Thou Who at the Ninth Hour;" "Glory... Both now... Thou Who for our sake wast born of the Virgin." And the reading from the Four Gospels takes place. After which we say: "Deliver us not up utterly unto the end;" the Trisagion with prostrations. After "Our Father:" the Kontakion of the day; "In Thy Kingdom," and the rest of the sequence, as is customary. We say also the Kontakion of the day; and we prepare also for Vespers.*[^67]

*[Broad decorative headpiece frame with intricate floral strapwork and central cross]*

## ON HOLY AND GREAT MONDAY, IN THE EVENING

*After the Introductory Psalm, the customary chanting of the Psalter: "Unto the Lord, when I was in affliction, I cried" [Ps. 119 LXX].*

*On "Lord, I have cried," we set 10 verses. And we sing 4 idiomel stichera of the day: twice each, and two once each:*[^68]

*Tone 1:*
The Lord, going unto His voluntary Passion, said unto the Apostles on the way: "Behold, we go up to Jerusalem, and the Son of Man shall be delivered up, as it is written concerning Him." Come therefore, and let us also, with minds purified, travel with Him, and be crucified with Him, and be mortified for His sake unto the pleasures of this life, that we may also live with Him, and hear Him crying aloud: "No longer do I ascend unto the earthly Jerusalem to suffer, but I ascend unto My Father and your Father, and unto My God and your God; and I will exalt you together with Myself into the Jerusalem on high, into the Heavenly Kingdom." *(Twice)*

*Tone 5:*
Having attained the saving Passion of Christ God, O ye faithful, let us glorify His ineffable longsuffering, that by His tender mercy He may raise up together also us who have been slain by sin, for He is good and the Lover of mankind. *(Twice)*

*Tone 5:*
O Lord, going unto Thy suffering, Thou didst establish Thy disciples, and taking them aside, didst say: "How do you not remember My words, which I spoke unto you before: that it is written of every prophet that he should be slain nowhere except in Jerusalem? Now therefore the time is at hand, concerning which I told you; for behold, I am delivered up into the hands of sinners to be mocked, who also having nailed Me to the Cross and given Me over to burial, shall account Me an abomination as one dead. Nevertheless, take courage; for on the third day I shall rise, unto the joy of the faithful and life everlasting." *(Twice)*

*Tone 5:*
O Lord, the mother of the sons of Zebedee, not comprehending the ineffable mystery of Thy dispensation, asked of Thee that the honors of a temporal kingdom might be granted unto her children. But instead of this, Thou didst promise Thy friends that they should drink the cup of death, which cup Thou didst say that Thou Thyself wouldest drink before them, for the cleansing of sins. Therefore we cry unto Thee: O Salvation of our souls, glory be to Thee! *(Twice)*

*Tone 5:*
O Lord, instructing Thy disciples to mind things more perfect, Thou didst say that they should not be like unto the Gentiles in lording it over the lesser; "For it shall not be so with you, My disciples, for I am willing to be poor; the first among you, therefore, let him be the servant of all; and he that rules, as he that is ruled; and the chief, as the last. For I Myself came to serve the impoverished Adam, and to give My life as a ransom for many who cry unto Me: Glory be to Thee!"

*Tone 8:*
Fearing the rebuke of the fig tree withered for its fruitlessness, brethren, let us offer worthy fruits of repentance unto Christ, Who grants us great mercy.

*Glory... Both now... in the same Tone:*
The serpent, finding in the Egyptian woman a second Eve, sought through flattery to supplant Joseph; but he, leaving his garment behind, fled from sin, and being naked was not ashamed, even as the first-formed man before the transgression. By his intercessions, O Christ, have mercy on us!

*Then the Entrance with the Gospel. "O Joyful Light."*

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 127 [LXX], Tone 6:*
The Lord bless thee out of Sion, and mayest thou see the good things of Jerusalem [Ps. 127:5 LXX].  
*Verse: Blessed are all they that fear the Lord, that walk in His ways [Ps. 127:1 LXX].*

*The Reading from Exodus [Exodus 1:1–20]:*[^69]
Now these are the names of the children of Israel, who came into Egypt together with Jacob their father; every one entered with all his house: Reuben, Simeon, Levi, Judah, Issachar, Zebulun, and Benjamin, Dan, and Naphtali, Gad, and Asher. But Joseph was in Egypt. And all the souls that came out of Jacob were seventy and five. And Joseph died, and all his brethren, and all that generation. And the children of Israel increased, and multiplied, and became numerous, and grew exceedingly strong; and the land multiplied them. Now there arose another king in Egypt, who knew not Joseph. And he said unto his people: "Behold, the race of the children of Israel is a great multitude, and is stronger than we. Come therefore, let us deal craftily with them, lest they multiply; and if war should happen to fall upon us, they also will join themselves unto our adversaries, and having overcome us, they will go forth out of our land." And he set over them taskmasters, that they might afflict them in their works. And they built strong cities for Pharaoh: Pithom, and Raamses, and On, which is Heliopolis.[^70] But the more they afflicted them, so much the more numerous they became, and grew exceedingly strong; and the Egyptians abhorred the children of Israel. And the Egyptians compelled the children of Israel with violence. And they made their life bitter with hard labors, in mortar, and in brick-making, and in all labors in the fields, in all their works wherein they enslaved them with violence. And the king of Egypt spoke to the Hebrew midwives, of whom the name of the one was Shiphrah, and the name of the second Puah; and he said unto them: "When you act as midwives to the Hebrew women, and they are at the birth, if it be a male child, kill him; but if it be a female, preserve her." But the midwives feared God, and did not as the king of Egypt commanded them, but preserved the male children alive. And the king of Egypt called the midwives, and said unto them: "Why is it that you have done this thing, and preserved the male children alive?" And the midwives said unto Pharaoh: "Because the Hebrew women are not as the Egyptian women; for they give birth before the midwives come in unto them, and they were delivered." And God dealt well with the midwives; and the people multiplied, and grew exceedingly strong.

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 128 [LXX], Tone 6:*
We have blessed you in the name of the Lord [Ps. 128:8 LXX].  
*Verse: Many a time have they fought against me from my youth [Ps. 128:1 LXX].*

*The Reading from Job [Job 1:1–12]:*[^71]
There was a certain man in the land of Ausitis [Uz], whose name was Job; and that man was true, blameless, righteous, and godly, departing from every evil matter. And there were born unto him seven sons, and three daughters. And his cattle were seven thousand sheep, three thousand camels, five hundred yoke of oxen, and five hundred she-asses feeding, and exceedingly many servants; and great works were his upon the earth; and that man was the noblest of all that were in the east of the sun. And his sons went and feasted one with another every day, taking with them also their three sisters to eat and drink with them. And when the days of the feast were finished, Job sent and purified them, rising up early in the morning; and he offered sacrifices for them according to their number, and one calf for sin for their souls; for Job said: "Lest perhaps my sons have sinned, and thought evil against God in their mind." Thus therefore did Job all the days. And it came to pass on this day, that behold, the Angels of God came to present themselves before the Lord, and the devil came also with them. And the Lord said unto the devil: "Whence comest thou?" And the devil answered the Lord, and said: "Having gone round about the earth, and walked under heaven, here I am." And the Lord said unto him: "Hast thou considered in thy mind My servant Job? For there is none like him upon the earth, a man blameless, true, godly, departing from every evil matter." And the devil answered and said before the Lord: "Doth Job honor the Lord for nought? Hast not Thou hedged about his outward things, and the inward parts of his house, and all that he hath on every side round about? The works of his hands Thou hast blessed, and his cattle Thou hast multiplied upon the earth. But put forth Thine hand, and touch all that he hath: surely he will bless Thee to Thy face!" Then said the Lord unto the devil: "Behold, all that he hath I give into thy hand, but upon himself touch thou not." And the devil went forth from the presence of the Lord.

*Then: "Let my prayer be set forth."*

*And after this, the Gospel according to Matthew, section 158 [Matthew 24:3–35]: "At that time, as Jesus sat..."*

*And the rest of the Liturgy of the Presanctified, according to the customary order.*

*Thus must it also be done on Holy and Great Tuesday, and on Holy and Great Wednesday.*

*[Ornamental tailpiece vignette]*

*If there is no Presanctified Liturgy:*
*We sing at "Lord, I have cried," having set 10 verses, on 6. Sticheron, Tone 1: "The Lord, going unto His voluntary Passion" (Twice); "Having attained, O ye faithful" (Twice); "O Lord, unto the ineffable mystery" (Twice). Glory... Both now... "O Lord, going unto Thy suffering." After these, the Entrance with the Gospel, and "O Joyful Light." Then the Prokeimenon, the Readings, and the Gospel. Then: "Vouchsafe, O Lord," and the Litany: "Let us complete our evening prayer."*

*At the Aposticha, from the same stichera, Sticheron, Tone 5: "O Lord, instructing Thy disciples to mind things more perfect." Verse: "Unto Thee have I lifted up mine eyes" [Ps. 122:1 LXX], and again the same sticheron.*

*Verse: "Have mercy on us, O Lord" [Ps. 122:3 LXX]. Tone 8: "Fearing the rebuke of the fig tree." Glory... Both now... in the same Tone: "The serpent, finding in the Egyptian woman."*

*Then: "Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart," and the rest of the customary order with prostrations. And after the final Trisagion and after "Our Father:" "Lord, have mercy," 12 times. Then the prayer: "All-Holy Trinity," and "Blessed be the name of the Lord," and the Dismissal.*

*Thus therefore do we perform on Tuesday and on Wednesday.*

*At the table, we eat dry food, and with this we are satisfied. And even as in the first week of this holy fast, so also in these days, on Great Monday, on Tuesday, and on Wednesday, it is fitting to fast.*[^72]

*For it is fitting to know that the Sixth Great Council commanded the faithful to pass the days of the saving Passion in fasting, prayer, and contrition of heart; and to cease the fast towards midnight of Great Saturday, the divine Evangelists Matthew and Luke describing the night that delays us, the one speaking of late on the Sabbath, and the other of the deep dawn.*[^73]

*[Broad ornamental headpiece frame]*

## ON HOLY AND GREAT MONDAY, AT COMPLINE

*We say Great Compline; at Compline, the Tri-ode of the Lord Andrew of Crete.*[^74]

*Ode 2, Tone 8. Heirmos:*
Attend, O heaven, and I will speak, and will hymn Christ, Who was born of the Virgin for our salvation.

*Troparia:*
Let us go with Christ unto the Mount of Olives, and let us mystically take up our abode with the Apostles beside Him.

Understand, O my humble heart, what is the parable of the millstones, concerning which Christ spoke before, and henceforth become sober.[^75]

Prepare thyself, O my soul, for the departure: the coming of the inexorable Judge draws near.

*Theotokion:*
O All-Pure Virgin Theotokos, alone all-immaculate, entreat Thy Son on behalf of Thy servants.

*Another Heirmos, in the same Tone:*
See, see that I am God, Who of old led Israel through the Red Sea, and fed, and saved him, and delivered him from the bitter bondage of Pharaoh.

*Troparia:*
See, see that I am God, Who before the existence of all things, and before the creation of earth and heaven, knows all things; and Who am entirely in the Father, and bear all things within Myself.

By the Word I established the heaven together with the earth: being with the Father unto the ages, and by the Word I uphold all things, as the Word, Wisdom, and Power, and Image, co-effective and of equal operation.

Who appointed the times? Who preserves the ages? Who foreknew and raises up all things together, save the Unoriginate One Who is ever with the Father, like the radiance with the light?

O the measureless loving-kindness of Thee, O Jesus! For Thou didst tell us from on high the time of the consummation, while hiding the hour, yet brightly revealing the images of its terrors.

All things Thou knowest, all things Thou discernest, O Jesus, as possessing in Thyself the entire Fatherly subsistence divinely, and bearing in Thyself the whole Spirit mutually, Who is co-eternal with the Father.

O Master and Lord, Creator of the ages, account also us worthy then to hear that sacred voice, calling the chosen of the Father into the Heavenly Kingdom.

*Glory:*
O Unoriginate, Uncreated Trinity, Undivided Unity, existing threefold and one, O Father, Son, and Spirit, the one God: receive the hymnody from clay tongues, even as from flaming lips.

*Both now... Theotokion:*
A holy habitation hast thou shown thyself to be, O Virgin of God: for in thee the Heavenly King dwelt in the flesh, and came forth all-fair, having divinely fashioned man in Himself.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 2, Podoben: "Being the wellspring of loving-kindness" (Милосе́рдїя сꙋ́щи):*
Moved by compassion, O Christ our Benefactor, Thou goest forth willingly to suffer, desiring to deliver us from the passions and from condemnation in hades; therefore we hymn Thy venerable Sufferings, and we glorify, O Savior, Thy supreme condescension unto all.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Ye Angels and heavens, bless, hymn, and exalt Him above all for ever, Who sits upon the throne of glory, and is unceasingly glorified as God.

*Troparia:*
In every way, O soul, hast thou heard how Christ proclaimed unto His divine disciples, telling of the consummation; understanding therefore thine end, make ready henceforth: the time of departure is at hand.
