Thou hast known, O barren soul, the parable of the wicked servant; fear, and neglect not the gift which thou hast received, that thou hide it not in the earth, but trade therewith.

Let the lamp be made bright, and let this oil overflow, even as compassion was then for the virgins, that thou, O my soul, mayest adorn the bridal chamber of Christ, which shall then be opened.

The Teacher spoke of flight on the Sabbath and in winter, prefiguring, I believe, the seventh age of this present world, in which the consummation shall come upon us like winter.

As the passing swiftness of lightning, so shall that dread coming of thy Master then be, O my soul: thou hast heard it; hasten henceforth to be prepared.

When the Judge shall come with thousands then and ten thousands of angelic ranks and powers, what fear, O my soul, what trembling! Woe is me, when all shall stand naked!

*Let us bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord.*  
God is one, even the Trinity: neither hath the Father lost His proper characteristic, nor hath the Son been changed into procession, but possessing Their proper attributes, and Their own, as Light and God, Three do I glorify unto the ages.

*Both now... Theotokion:*
By the intercessions of the Mother of God, O God, receive our prayer, and send down Thy mercies richly upon all, and grant Thy peace unto Thy people.

*We praise, we bless, and we worship the Lord, hymning and exalting Him above all unto all ages.*  
*Then the Heirmos: "Ye Angels and heavens upon the throne..."*

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
The Nativity of the Ever-Virgin, prefigured on the mountain unto the Lawgiver in fire and the bush, for the salvation of us the faithful, with unceasing hymns do we magnify.

*Troparia:*
Thou hast heard, O my soul, the Judge foretelling and teaching thee the time of the consummation: prepare the works that pertain unto departure, lest as one untested thou be rejected of God.

From the fig tree, O my soul, learn thou the consummation: when its foliage is tender and its branches put forth shoots, the hour of harvest is at hand; and thou, when thou seest these things, understand that it is nigh, even at the doors.

Who else beside Thee knows Thy Father? Or who save Thee knows the hour or the day? For in Thee are all the treasures of wisdom contained within, O Christ God.

The books shall then be opened, the thrones being set, and all deeds shall be laid bare when all stand naked, with neither witnesses nor accusers coming forward: for all things are naked unto God.

The Judge of all comes to be led unto judgment—He Who sits upon the Cherubic throne—to stand before Pilate as one guilty, and to suffer all things, that Adam may be saved.

Our great and divine Pascha draws nigh; for after two days Christ foreordains it, having brought forward the day of suffering, wherein the Sacrifice shall be offered unto the Father.

Standing by Thy Cross, O Savior, the Mother, beholding Thy wrongful slaughter, cried aloud: "Woe is me, My Child! Woe is me, O Light that knows no setting! Shine forth upon all, O Sun of glory, Thy light!"

*Glory:*
O Holy Unity, Trinity one Godhead, and Trinity Unity God, three-hypostatic Nature, of equal honor and undivided glory: deliver our souls from afflictions!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Receive Thine own Mother, O Christ, in prayer, that by her intercessions Thou mayest give peace unto the world, and establish the scepters of kingdoms, and gather Thy Churches into one.

*Heirmos of the 9th Ode: "The Nativity of the Ever-Virgin..." instead of "It is truly meet." And a prostration to the ground.*

*Trisagion. And after "Our Father:" the Kontakion of the day. Then: "O Lord of hosts, be with us;" and the rest as customary. And the Dismissal.*

*[Broad decorative woodcut headpiece frame with floral strapwork]*

## ON HOLY AND GREAT TUESDAY, AT MATINS[^76]

*Alleluia, in Tone 8.*

*Troparion, thrice: "Behold, the Bridegroom comes;" written on Holy Monday (see fol. 37)[^77]. And the customary chanting of the Psalter.*

*After the 1st chanting of the Psalter, the Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up upon the Cross" (Вознесы́йся на крⷭ҇тъ):*
Brethren, let us love the Bridegroom, let us adorn our lamps, shining with virtues and right faith, that like the wise virgins of the Lord we may be ready to enter with Him into the marriage banquet; for the Bridegroom, as God, grants unto all an incorruptible crown as His gift.

*Glory... Both now... the same.*

*After the 2nd chanting of the Psalter, the Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Joseph was amazed" (Оу҆диви́ся І҆ѡ́сифъ):*
O Savior, the priests and scribes, having maliciously gathered an unlawful council against Thee through envy, moved Judas unto betrayal; therefore shamelessly he went forth, saying against Thee unto the lawless people: "What will you give me," says he, "and I will deliver Him into your hands?" From this condemnation deliver our souls, O Lord!

*Glory... Both now... the same.*

*After the 3rd chanting of the Psalter, the Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "The Wisdom and Word of God" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
Judas in his mind becomes a lover of money; he rises up hostilely against the Master; he takes counsel and studies betrayal; he falls from the light, receiving darkness; he agrees upon the price, and sells Him Who is Priceless; therefore also hanging, the recompense for what he wrought, does the wretch find, and a bitter death, having betrayed Thee, the Lord. From his portion deliver us, O Christ God, granting forgiveness of transgressions unto those who celebrate Thy most pure Passion in faith.

*Glory... Both now... the same.*

*Then: "And that we may be accounted worthy..." The Gospel according to Matthew, section 90 without stepping over [Matthew 22:15–23:39]: "At that time, all the Pharisees went and took counsel against Jesus, how they might entangle Him in His talk..." Ending in section 96: "Blessed is He that comes in the name of the Lord."*

*We sing Psalm 50. Then:*

* **Priest:** *Save, O God, Thy people...*  
* **Choir:** *Lord, have mercy (12 times).*  
*After this, the Little Litany.*

*Then the Kontakion, Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the highest" (Вы́шнихъ и҆щѧ̀):*
Having considered the hour of the end, O my soul, and having feared the cutting down of the fig tree, diligently cultivate the talent that was given unto thee, O wretched one, being watchful and crying aloud: "Let us not remain outside the bridal chamber of Christ!"

*Ikos:*
Why dost thou despond, O my wretched soul? Why dost thou imagine unseasonably unprofitable anxieties? Why art thou occupied with passing things? The final hour is at hand from henceforth, and we are to be parted from the things that are here. While thou hast time, arise and cry aloud: "I have sinned against Thee, O my Savior; cut me not down like the barren fig tree, but as Thou art tender-hearted, O Lord, have compassion on me who cry with fear: Let us not remain outside the bridal chamber of Christ!"

*Synaxarion.*[^78]

*Verses:*
*Tuesday, crowned, brings ten virgins,*  
*Bearing victory for the timeless Master.*

On Holy and Great Tuesday, we make commemoration of the Parable of the Ten Virgins. For such parables the Lord, going up to Jerusalem and journeying unto His Passion, spoke unto His disciples, and there are parables which He extended also unto the Jews. The Parable of the Ten Virgins, turning it unto almsgiving, He spoke, at the same time teaching that all should be prepared before the end. For He told them much concerning virginity, and concerning eunuchs; and virginity hath great glory, for it is truly great. But lest anyone, fulfilling this work, should neglect other virtues, and especially almsgiving, whereby the lamp of virginity is enlightened, the Holy Gospel introduces this parable. And five it calls wise, as having joined with virginity also the abundant and rich oil of almsgiving; but five it calls foolish, as they also indeed possessed virginity, but had an incomparable lack of almsgiving. On this account they are called foolish, because having accomplished the greater, they neglected the lesser, and differed in nothing from harlots: for the latter indeed were overcome by the flesh, but the former by possessions. And the night of this present life passing away, all the virgins slumbered, that is to say, they died: for sleep is called death. And while they were sleeping, a cry arose at midnight; and those indeed who had brought oil in abundance, the doors being opened, entered with the Bridegroom; but the foolish, having an insufficient supply of oil, sought it after their sleep. But the wise, though wishing to give, were unable, and before entering answered, saying: "Lest perhaps there should not be enough for us and you, go you to them that sell, that is to say, to the poor, and buy; but with difficulty, for after death this is impossible." Which also Abraham shows concerning the rich man and Lazarus, saying: "There is an impassable gulf, so that they cannot cross over from hence thither." But the foolish, having come unenlightened, cry aloud thus, knocking at the gate: "Lord, Lord, open unto us!" But the Lord Himself through Himself gives that dread answer: "Depart," saying, "I know you not; for how will you see the Bridegroom, not having the bridal gift, even almsgiving itself?" On account of this figure, therefore, the Parable of the Ten Virgins was appointed by the God-bearing Fathers to be placed here, instructing us ever to watch and to be prepared for the meeting of the true Bridegroom, by good works, and especially by almsgiving; because the day and hour of the end are unknown: even as through Joseph we acquire chastity, and through the fig tree we ever bring forth spiritual fruit. For he who accomplishes one single and greatest deed, but neglects others, and especially almsgiving, shall not enter with Christ into eternal rest, but returns back put to shame. For nothing is more mournful, and more full of disgrace, than virginity overcome by possessions.[^79] But, O Christ the Bridegroom, unite us with the wise virgins, and number us with Thy chosen flock, and have mercy on us. Amen.

*Then the Di-ode, whose acrostic is: "And on the Third Day." The composition of the Lord Cosmas.*[^80]  
*The Heirmos twice, and the troparia on 12.*  
*The last Heirmos by both choirs together.*

*Tone 2. Ode 8. Heirmos:*
The venerable Three Youths, not having obeyed the command of the tyrant, cast into the furnace, confessed God, singing: "Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!"

*Troparia:*
Let us cast slothfulness far from us, and with bright lamps let us meet the Immortal Bridegroom, Christ, with hymns, crying aloud: "Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!"

May the abundant oil of our soul suffice in the vessels, so that not spending the time of rewards upon buying, we may sing: "Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!"

As many of you as have received a talent from God, an equally powerful grace, by the help of Christ Who gave it, increase it, singing: "Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!"

*We praise, we bless, and we worship the Lord. And again the Heirmos: "The venerable Three Youths..."*

*We do not sing the Magnificat.*

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Thou who didst contain in thy womb God Whom nothing can contain, and didst bear Joy unto the world: thee do we hymn, O Virgin Theotokos!

*Troparia:*
Unto the disciples, O Good One, Thou didst say: "Watch, for in what hour the Lord shall come, you know not, to render unto each."

In Thy second and dread coming, O Master, number me with the sheep on Thy right hand, overlooking the multitude of my transgressions.

*And again instead of "It is truly meet," the Heirmos: "Thou who didst contain in thy womb..."*

*Exapostilarion, thrice:*[^81]  
Thy bridal chamber I behold adorned, O my Savior, and I have no wedding garment that I may enter therein; enlighten the vesture of my soul, O Giver of light, and save me.

*At the Praises, we set 4 verses, and we sing the idiomel stichera, repeating them:*

*Tone 1:*
In the splendor of Thy saints, how shall I enter, unworthy as I am? For if I dare to enter into the bridal chamber, my garment accuses me, for it is not a wedding garment, and being bound I shall be cast out by the Angels. Cleanse, O Lord, the pollution of my soul, and save me, for Thou art the Lover of mankind.

*Tone 2:*
Through spiritual slothfulness I slumbered, and I acquired not a burning lamp of virtues, O Christ the Bridegroom; and I was made like unto the foolish virgins, wandering about in the time of labor. Close not against me the bowels of Thy compassions, O Master; but having shaken off my darkened sleep, raise me up, and lead me with the wise virgins into Thy bridal chamber, where there is the pure voice of those that celebrate, and who cry aloud unceasingly: O Lord, glory be to Thee!

*Glory... Both now... Tone 4:*
Having heard the condemnation of him that hid the talent, O soul, hide not the word of God; proclaim His wonders, that multiplying the gift, thou mayest enter into the joy of thy Lord.

*At the Aposticha, the idiomel stichera, Tone 6:*
Come, O ye faithful, let us work diligently for the Master; for He distributes riches unto His servants, and according to proportion let each of us multiply the talent of grace: let one indeed offer wisdom through good deeds, and let another accomplish the ministry of splendor; let a believer communicate the word of mystery unto him that is untaught, and let another disperse his wealth unto the poor: for thus shall we multiply that which was lent, and as faithful stewards of grace we shall be accounted worthy of the Master's joy. Account us worthy thereof, O Christ God, for Thou art the Lover of mankind.

*Verse: We were filled in the morning with Thy mercy, O Lord [Ps. 89:14 LXX].*  
When Thou shalt come in glory with the angelic hosts, and shalt sit upon the throne of judgment, O Jesus, separate me not, O Good Shepherd; for Thou knowest the ways on the right hand, but perverse are those on the left. Destroy me not therefore with the goats, coarsened as I am by sin; but joining me unto the sheep on Thy right hand, save me, for Thou art the Lover of mankind.

*Verse: And let the brightness of the Lord our God be upon us [Ps. 89:17 LXX].*  
O Bridegroom, surpassing in beauty beyond all sons of men, Who hast called us unto the spiritual banquet of Thy bridal chamber: strip off from me the unsightly form of my transgressions by communion in Thy Sufferings, and having adorned me with the garment of glory of Thy beauty, show me forth as a radiant guest of Thy Kingdom, for Thou art compassionate.

*Glory... Both now... Tone 7:*
Behold, the Master entrusts thee with the talent, O my soul: receive the gift with fear; lend unto Him Who gave it; distribute unto the poor, and acquire the Lord as a friend, that thou mayest stand on His right hand when He shall come in glory, and mayest hear that blessed voice: "Enter, good servant, into the joy of thy Lord!" Account me worthy thereof, O Savior, wanderer that I am, for the sake of Thy great mercy.

*And the rest of the sequence, as is customary.*

*[Ornamental divider]*

*The Hours with the customary chanting of the Psalter, and the reading of the Gospel.*

*At the Sixth Hour, Troparion of the Prophecy, Tone 1:*  
Unto us who sin beyond measure, richly forgive, O Savior, and account us worthy without condemnation to worship Thy holy Resurrection, by the intercessions of Thine all-pure Mother, O Thou Who alone art Plenteous in mercy!

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 129 [LXX], Tone 6:*  
For with the Lord there is mercy, and with Him is plenteous redemption [Ps. 129:7 LXX].  
*Verse: Out of the depths have I cried unto Thee, O Lord; Lord, hear my voice [Ps. 129:1 LXX].*

*The Reading from the Prophecy of Ezekiel [Ezekiel 1:21–28]:*[^82]  
When the living creatures went, the wheels went; and when they stood, the wheels stood with them; and when they were lifted up from the earth, the wheels were lifted up with them, for the Spirit of life was in the wheels. And the likeness over the head of the living creatures was as a firmament, as the appearance of crystal, stretched forth over their wings above. And under the firmament their wings were stretched out, flying one toward another; to each were two wings joined, covering their bodies. And I heard the voice of their wings when they flew, as the voice of many waters, as the voice of God Shaddai; and when they walked, the voice of speech was as the voice of a host; and when they stood, ...and when they stood, their wings rested. And lo, there was a voice from above the firmament that was over their head; when they stood, their wings dropped down. And above the firmament that was over their head was the likeness of a sapphire stone, the similitude of a throne upon it; and upon the similitude of the throne was the likeness as the appearance of a man above upon it. And I saw as it were the appearance of electrum, as the appearance of fire within it round about, from the appearance of the loins and upward; and from the appearance of the loins even unto downward, I saw the appearance of fire, and His brightness was round about. As the appearance of the rainbow when it is in the clouds in the day of rain, so was the appearance of the brightness round about. This was the vision of the similitude of the glory of the Lord.[^83]

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 130 [LXX], Tone 6:*  
Let Israel hope in the Lord, from henceforth and for ever [Ps. 130:3 LXX].  
*Verse: Lord, my heart is not exalted, nor are mine eyes lifted up [Ps. 130:1 LXX].*  
*And the rest.*

*Together we chant also the Ninth Hour, without the kathisma, and we read the Gospel.*[^84]  
*And we chant the Beatitudes, and: Remember us, O Lord, with prostrations.*  
*And all the rest, even as was indicated on Monday.*

* * *

## ON THE SAME HOLY AND GREAT TUESDAY, IN THE EVENING

*At the Lucernarium, the customary chanting of the Psalter.*

*At Lord, I have cried, we set 10 verses, and we chant the idiomel stichera:*

*Tone 1:*  
In the splendor of Thy saints, how shall I enter, unworthy as I am? For if I dare to enter into the bridal chamber, my garment accuses me, for it is not a wedding garment, and being bound I shall be cast out by the Angels. Cleanse, O Lord, the pollution of my soul, and save me, for Thou art the Lover of mankind.

*Tone 2:*  
Through spiritual slothfulness I slumbered, and I acquired not a burning lamp of virtues, O Christ the Bridegroom; and I was made like unto the foolish virgins, wandering about in the time of labor. Close not against me the bowels of Thy compassions, O Master; but having shaken off my darkened sleep, raise me up, and lead me with the wise virgins into Thy bridal chamber, where there is the pure voice of those that celebrate, and who cry aloud unceasingly: O Lord, glory be to Thee!

*Tone 4:*  
Having heard the condemnation of him that hid the talent, O soul, hide not the word of God; proclaim His wonders, that multiplying the gift, thou mayest enter into the joy of thy Lord.

*Tone 6:*  
Come, O ye faithful, let us work diligently for the Master; for He distributes riches unto His servants, and according to proportion let each of us multiply the talent of grace: let one indeed offer wisdom through good deeds, and let another accomplish the ministry of splendor; let a believer communicate the word of mystery unto him that is untaught, and let another disperse his wealth unto the poor; for thus shall we multiply that which was lent, and as faithful stewards of grace we shall be accounted worthy of the Master's joy. Account us worthy thereof, O Christ God, for Thou art the Lover of mankind.

When Thou shalt come in glory with the angelic hosts, and shalt sit upon the throne of judgment, O Jesus, separate me not, O Good Shepherd; for Thou knowest the ways on the right hand, but perverse are those on the left. Destroy me not therefore with the goats, coarsened as I am by sin; but joining me unto the sheep on Thy right hand, save me, for Thou art the Lover of mankind.

O Bridegroom, surpassing in beauty beyond all sons of men, Who hast called us unto the spiritual banquet of Thy bridal chamber: strip off from me the unsightly form of my transgressions by communion in Thy Sufferings, and having adorned me with the garment of glory of Thy beauty, show me forth as a radiant guest of Thy Kingdom, for Thou art compassionate.

*Glory... Both now... Tone 7:*  
Behold, the Master entrusts thee with the talent, O my soul: receive the gift with fear; lend unto Him Who gave it; distribute unto the poor, and acquire the Lord as a friend, that thou mayest stand on His right hand when He shall come in glory, and mayest hear that blessed voice: "Enter, good servant, into the joy of thy Lord!" Account me worthy thereof, O Savior, wanderer that I am, for the sake of Thy great mercy.

*Entrance with the Gospel. O Gladsome Light.*

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 131 [LXX], Tone 6:*  
Arise, O Lord, into Thy rest, Thou and the ark of Thy holiness [Ps. 131:8 LXX].  
*Verse: Lord, remember David, and all his meekness [Ps. 131:1 LXX].*

*The Reading from Exodus [Exodus 2:5–10]:*[^85]  
The daughter of Pharaoh came down to wash herself at the river; and her maidens walked by the river's side; and when she saw the ark in the flags, she sent her maid to fetch it. And when she had opened it, she saw the child weeping in the ark; and the daughter of Pharaoh had compassion on him, and said: "This is one of the Hebrews' children." Then said his sister to Pharaoh's daughter: "Wilt thou that I go and call to thee a nurse of the Hebrew women, that she may nurse the child for thee?" And Pharaoh's daughter said unto her: "Go." And the maid went and called the child's mother. And Pharaoh's daughter said unto her: "Take this child away, and nurse it for me, and I will give thee thy wages." And the woman took the child, and nursed it. And when the child was grown, she brought him unto Pharaoh's daughter, and he became her son; and she called his name Moses, saying: "Because I drew him out of the water."

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 132 [LXX], Tone 6:*  
Behold now, what is good or what is pleasant, but for brethren to dwell together in unity? [Ps. 132:1 LXX].  
*Verse: It is like the ointment upon the head, that ran down upon the beard, the beard of Aaron [Ps. 132:2 LXX].*

*The Reading from Job [Job 1:13–22]:*[^86]  
It was as on this day, that the sons and daughters of Job were drinking wine in their eldest brother's house. And lo, there came a messenger unto Job, and said unto him: "The oxen were plowing, and the she-asses were feeding beside them; and the spoilers came and took them away, and slew the servants with the sword; and I only am escaped alone to tell thee." While he was yet speaking, there came another messenger, and said unto Job: "Fire fell from heaven, and burned up the sheep, and in like manner consumed the shepherds; and I only am escaped alone to tell thee." While he was yet speaking, there came another messenger, and said unto Job: "The horsemen formed three companies, and surrounded the camels, and took them away, and slew the servants with the sword; and I only am escaped alone to tell thee." While he was yet speaking, there came another messenger, saying unto Job: "While thy sons and thy daughters were eating and drinking wine in their eldest brother's house, suddenly a great wind came from the wilderness, and touched the four corners of the house, and the house fell upon thy children, and they are dead; and I only am escaped alone to tell thee." Then Job arose, and rent his garments, and shaved his head, and fell down upon the ground, and worshiped the Lord, and said: "Naked came I out of my mother's womb, and naked shall I return thither; the Lord gave, and the Lord hath taken away; as it pleased the Lord, so is it done; blessed be the name of the Lord for ever." In all these things that befell him, Job sinned not before the Lord, and imputed not folly unto God.

*And straightway: Let my prayer arise...*  
*The Gospel according to Matthew, section 120 [Matthew 26:6–16].*[^87]  
*And the rest of the Presanctified.*

*[Woodcut Engraving: The Sinful Woman Anointing the Head and Feet of the Lord in the House of Simon the Leper]*

## ON HOLY AND GREAT TUESDAY, AT GREAT COMPLINE

*Whereon we chant the Tri-ode, the composition of the lord Andrew of Crete.*[^88]

*Tone 2. Ode 3. Heirmos:*  
My barren mind, show Thou forth fruitful, O God, Cultivator of the good, Planter of blessings, by Thy tender-hearted compassion!

*Troparia:*  
The time of the end is at hand, let us turn back henceforth, Christ teaches; for He shall come in a moment, He shall come and shall not tarry, to judge the whole world.

Manifesting the suddenness of His coming, Christ spoke, saying: Even as of old was the unexpected destruction that was about to come upon the earth.

The bridal chamber is opened, the divine banquet is adorned therewith; the Bridegroom is near, calling us; let us prepare ourselves henceforth.

The house of Simon contained Thee, O Jesus the King, Whom nothing can contain anywhere; and the sinful woman anointed Thee with myrrh.

Filled with mystical fragrance, the woman was delivered from the former stench of her many sins, O Savior; for Thou dost pour forth the myrrh of life.

O Bread of Life, Life of them that hunger, Thou Thyself didst eat with men, O Christ, making manifest Thy condescension.

The ungrateful disciple rejected Thee, O Christ; he incited the council of the all-lawless people against Thee; he turns himself unto betrayal.

*Glory:*  
With the Father I worship and sing praise unto Thee, the Son, and unto the right Spirit, one Nature, dividing the Persons, and uniting the Essence.

*Both now, Theotokion:*  
The Ewe-lamb that bare Thee, the Shepherd and the Lamb, ever prays, O Jesus God, for all the people that believe in Thee.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 1, Podoben: "The angelic choir" (Ли́къ а҆́гг҃льскїй):*[^89]  
Lo, the crafty council in truth gathered furiously, to condemn as a convict Him Who sits on high, as the Judge of all and the Lord; now Herod and Annas, together also with Caiaphas, gather with Pilate to examine the One Long-suffering Lord.

*Tone 2. Ode 8. Heirmos:*  
Him Who in the bush to Moses prefigured the wonder of the Virgin of old on Mount Sinai: praise, bless, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Troparia:*  
Not ignorant of the time of our end, He Who upholds the ages foretold that that day should not be known; but He set boundaries for all unto humility of mind.

When Thou shalt sit as Judge, separating, as Thou hast said, the goats from the sheep, O Savior: deliver us from that left-hand standing, and deprive us not of Thy divine right hand.

Thou art our Pascha, sacrificed for all, as the Lamb and the Offering, and the Cleansing of transgressions; and Thy divine Sufferings do we exalt above all, O Christ, unto all ages.

Unto a tree, and a field, and a dwelling is all my life likened, O my soul; therefore acquire a heart prepared toward God, lest thou leave anything behind unto corruption of the flesh.

Not unto the Pharisees, O Savior, nor unto Simon alone didst Thou deign to enter into the house for food; but now both publicans and harlots together draw forth Thy compassion.

Judas, the betrayer, courting avarice, was grieved over the poured-out myrrh; thereafter, coming unto the lawless concerning the betrayal of the Master, he agreed upon the price.

O blessed hands! O hair and lips of the chaste harlot! wherewith she poured out myrrh upon Thy feet, O Savior, wiping them and kissing them often.

While Thou wast reclining at table, O Word, the woman, having stood by and weeping at Thy feet, poured out an alabaster vial of myrrh upon Thy head, O Savior, the immortal Myrrh!

*Let us bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord.*  
With the Father, the Son, and the Holy Spirit, we glorify the Holy Trinity in one Godhead, crying aloud: Holy, Holy, Holy art Thou unto ages!

*Both now, Theotokion:*  
Through the prayers, O Savior, of Thine all-pure Mother and of Thine Apostles, send down Thy mercies richly upon us, and grant Thy peace unto Thy people.

*We praise, we bless, and we worship the Lord.*  
*And again the Heirmos: Him Who in the bush to Moses...*

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*  
The Word Who pre-shone timelessly from the Father, supernaturally conceived in the womb according to the flesh, in unceasing hymns do we, the faithful, magnify!

*Troparia:*  
The time of labor, the secure harbor of salvation, having taken the talent as an ancient figure, O my soul, purchase eternal life.

Having adorned our souls as radiant lamps, as the Bridegroom approaches unto the incorruptible bridal banquet, before the shutting of the doors let us enter in with Him.

Wishing to show forth the supernatural depth of Thy humility unto all, O Jesus, Thou didst eat, supping in the house of Simon, O Food of them that hunger!

Being the Life-creating Bread, Thou didst eat, O Jesus, with Simon the Pharisee, that the harlot might obtain Thine invaluable grace by the pouring out of the myrrh.

"My hands are defiled, my lips are the lips of a harlot, my life is unclean, my limbs are corrupt; but remit and forgive me," cries the harlot unto Christ.

Drawing near unto Thy feet, O Savior, the woman poured out myrrh, filling the house with fragrance, and being filled with the cleansing of deeds of peace.

"I am rich in sweet spices, but I am impoverished in virtues; that which I have, I offer unto Thee; give Thou Thyself that which Thou hast, and remit and forgive me," cries the harlot unto Christ.

"Myrrh with me is corruptible, but with Thee is the myrrh of life; for Myrrh is Thine own Name poured forth upon the worthy; but remit and forgive me," cries the harlot unto Christ.

*Glory:*  
Without beginning art Thou, O Father; uncreated art Thou, O Son; co-enthroned art Thou, O Spirit; Three in One by nature, and Three in Persons, the One True God.

*Both now, Theotokion:*  
O Theotokos, Hope of them that ever honor thee, cease not to pray unto Him Who was born of thee, to deliver me from afflictions and all temptations.

* * *

## ON HOLY AND GREAT WEDNESDAY, AT MATINS[^90]

*After the Six Psalms, Alleluia, in Tone 8, and the Troparion, thrice: Behold, the Bridegroom comes... (see fol. 37).*[^91] *And the chanting of the Psalter.*

*After the 1st chanting of the Psalter, the Sessional Hymn, Tone 3, Podoben: "Beholding the beauty of thy virginity" (Красотѣ̀ дѣ́вства):*  
The harlot drew near unto Thee, O Lover of mankind, pouring out myrrh with tears upon Thy feet, and by Thy command she is delivered from the stench of evil deeds; but the ungrateful disciple, though breathing Thy grace, casts it off and clothes himself in stench, selling Thee through love of money. Glory to Thy tender-hearted compassion, O Christ!

*Glory... Both now... the same.*

*After the 2nd chanting of the Psalter, the Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Joseph was amazed" (Оу҆диви́ся І҆ѡ́сифъ):*  
Judas the deceiver, courting love of money, craftily plotted to betray Thee, the Lord, the Treasure of Life; therefore having become drunk therewith, he hastens to the Jews and says unto the lawless: "What will you give me, and I will deliver Him unto you, to crucify Him?"

*Glory... Both now... the same.*

*After the 3rd chanting of the Psalter, the Sessional Hymn, Tone 1, Podoben: "While the soldiers were guarding Thy tomb" (Гро́бъ тво́й):*  
The harlot cried aloud in weeping, O Compassionate One, wiping Thy most pure feet warmly with the hair of her head, and groaning from the depth: "Cast me not away, nor abhor me, O my God, but receive me in repentance and save me, for Thou alone art the Lover of mankind!"

*Glory... Both now... the same.*

*The readings are all from the homily on the Holy Gospel according to Matthew for the day.*

*Then the Gospel according to John, section 41 near the end, without stepping over [John 12:17–50]:*[^92]  
At that time, the people that was with Jesus bore witness when He called Lazarus out of the grave...  
*Ending in section 43:*  
Even as the Father said unto Me, so I speak.

*Psalm 50. And the Tri-ode, the composition of the lord Cosmas, whose acrostic is: "On Wednesday I sing" [Τετράδι ψάλλω / (*(*Тетра́ди ѱалῶ*)*)].*[^93]  
*That is: On Wednesday I sing. The Heirmoi twice, and the troparia on 12. The last Heirmos by both choirs together.*

*Tone 2. Ode 3. Heirmos:*  
Having established me upon the rock of faith, Thou hast enlarged my mouth against mine enemies; for my spirit rejoiced when singing: There is none holy as our God, and there is none righteous beside Thee, O Lord!

*Troparia:*  
In vain does the council of the lawless gather together with an evil intention, to pronounce Thee, the Redeemer, condemned, O Christ, unto Whom we sing: Thou art our God, and there is none holy beside Thee, O Lord!

The cruel council of the lawless, possessing a God-fighting soul, schemes, as an evil thing, to slay Christ the Righteous One, unto Whom we sing: Thou art our God, and there is none holy beside Thee, O Lord!

*Kontakion, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up upon the Cross" (Вознесы́йсѧ):*[^94]  
More than the harlot have I transgressed, O Good One, yet I have brought no shower of tears unto Thee; but praying in silence I fall down before Thee, kissing Thy most pure feet with love, that as Master Thou mayest grant me the remission of debts, as I cry unto Thee, O Savior: Deliver me from my defiled works!

*Ikos:*  
She that was formerly a harlot suddenly appeared chaste, hating the deeds of shameful sin and bodily pleasures, remembering the great shame and the judgment of torment which harlots and the defiled shall suffer, of whom I am first, and I fear, yet I abide foolishly in evil custom; but the sinful woman, having feared and made haste, came quickly, crying aloud unto the Redeemer: O Lover of mankind and Compassionate One, deliver me from my defiled works!

*Synaxarion.*[^95]

*Verses:*  
*The woman, pouring myrrh upon the body of Christ,*  
*Anticipates the myrrh and aloes of Nicodemus.*

On Holy and Great Wednesday, the most divine Fathers commanded that commemoration be made of the woman, a harlot, who anointed the Lord with myrrh, because this took place shortly before the saving Passion. For when Jesus came to Jerusalem and was in the house of Simon the leper, the woman approached Him, and poured that costly myrrh upon His head. It was arranged to be placed here for this reason: that according to the Savior's word, that most fervent deed of hers should be proclaimed everywhere and unto all. From what motive then did she come? Because she saw Christ's compassion and His approachability toward all, and now especially when she saw Him enter the house of that leper, whom the Law commanded to be unclean and cast out of fellowship. The woman therefore considered that even as He healed the other's leprosy, so also would He heal her spiritual sickness. And while He was reclining at supper, she pours myrrh upon the crown of His head, being of the value of three hundred denarii, which is sixty assaria, ten unciae, or three pieces of silver. And the disciples rebuked her, and especially Judas Iscariot; but Christ defended her, that they should not cut short her good intention. Then He also recalls His burial, turning Judas away from betrayal and vouchsafing the woman the honor that her good deed should be preached throughout the whole inhabited world. Be it known, however, that this woman seems to some to be one and the same among all the Evangelists, yet it is not so; but among three of them, as the divine Chrysostom says (Homily on Matthew [Homily 80]), she is one and the same who was also called a harlot; but in John she is no longer the same, but another marvelous woman possessing an honorable life, Mary the sister of Lazarus, who, not being as a harlot, was beloved of Christ.[^96] Which Mary, indeed, six days before Pascha, in her own house in Bethany, while He was reclining at supper, performed the anointing with myrrh, pouring it upon the extremities of His feet, and wiping them with the hair of her head, rendering double honor unto Him and offering myrrh as unto God. For she knew well that oil was offered unto God in sacrifices, and priests were anointed with myrrh (Exodus, chap. 30), and Jacob of old anointed a pillar unto God (Genesis 28 and 35). And she brought this, offering it as a gift unto Him as Teacher and as God, for the sake of the revivification of her brother. Therefore no reward is promised unto her, when Judas himself murmurs as a lover of money. But the other woman, being a harlot, two days before Pascha, while Christ was still in the same Bethany, and having been at the house of Simon the leper at supper, and then reclining, pours that costly myrrh upon His head, even as holy Matthew and Mark related. And concerning this harlot the disciples also were indignant, clearly foreseeing Christ's zealous care for almsgiving; and unto her also was the reward given, that her good deed should be glorified everywhere throughout the world. Some therefore say she is one; but the Golden-mouthed says that there are two women. There are also some who say that there are three: two indeed aforementioned, when the Passion of Christ was drawing near to the doors, and a third, another before these, or rather the first, who did this near the middle of the Gospel preaching, who was both a harlot and a sinner, inside the house not of the leper, but of Simon the Pharisee, pouring myrrh on the feet of Christ Himself and alone; when also one Pharisee was scandalized, unto whom the Savior gives the reward of the remission of sins. And concerning this woman alone does the divine Luke relate in his Gospel (section 33), near the middle, as has been said. And after the account concerning this harlot, he straightway adds these words: "And it came to pass afterward, that He went throughout every city and village, preaching and showing the glad tidings of the Kingdom of God," from which it is evident that this was not at the time of the Passion. It seems, therefore, from the time, and from those who received Him, and from the place, and persons, and houses, and moreover from the manner of the anointing with myrrh, that these women were three: two indeed were harlots, but the third was Mary the sister of Lazarus, celebrated for an honorable life. And there was one house of Simon the Pharisee, and another of Simon the leper in Bethany, and yet another house of Mary and Martha the sisters of Lazarus, in the same Bethany, even as from these things it is to be understood: that there were also two suppers for Christ, and both in Bethany: one indeed six days before Pascha in the house of Lazarus, when Lazarus also ate with Him, as the son of thunder relates, saying thus: (John, section 31): "Then Jesus six days before the Passover came to Bethany, where Lazarus was which had been dead, whom He raised from the dead. There they made Him a supper, and Martha served; but Lazarus was one of them that sat at the table with Him. Then took Mary a pound of ointment of spikenard, very costly, and anointed the feet of Jesus, and wiped His feet with her hair." But the other supper was for Him two days before Pascha, while Christ was still in Bethany in the house of Simon the leper, when the harlot also came unto Him, pouring out the costly myrrh, even as sacred Matthew relates, saying as from Christ to the disciples: (Matthew, section 108): "Ye know that after two days is the feast of the Passover." And shortly thereafter he again added: "Now when Jesus was in Bethany, in the house of Simon the leper, there came unto Him a woman having an alabaster box of very precious ointment, and poured it on His head, as He sat at meat." Wherewith Mark also agrees, saying: (Mark, section 63): "After two days was the feast of the Passover, and of unleavened bread; and being in Bethany in the house of Simon the leper, as He sat at meat, there came a woman..." and the rest thereafter. But those who contend and say that the woman who anointed the Lord with myrrh was one and the same among the four Evangelists, consider Simon the Pharisee and the leper to be one and the same; whom some also gave out to be the father of Lazarus and of his sisters Mary and Martha; and they think, not well, that there was one and the same supper, and the same one house of his in Bethany, in which also the furnished upper room was prepared, and that it was the Mystical Supper. For these two suppers were outside Jerusalem in Bethany, six days and two days [as has been said] before the legal Passover, when the women brought myrrh unto Christ in different manners. But the Mystical Supper and the furnished upper room were adorned inside the city of Jerusalem, one day before the legal Passover and the day of Christ's Passion. Some say it was in the house of an unknown man, but others say [as they assert] in the house of the beloved disciple John on holy Zion, where also the disciples were hidden for fear of the Jews, and where the touching of Thomas took place after the Resurrection, and the coming of the Holy Spirit at Pentecost, and certain other ineffable and mystical things were accomplished.[^98] Therefore this indeed seems to me, and in truth, to be more certain according to the Golden-tongued: that these two women are accounted: one indeed, as has been said, among three Evangelists, being a harlot and a sinner, who poured myrrh upon Christ's head; and the other in John, Mary the sister of Lazarus, who brought and poured it upon the divine feet of Christ themselves. Other suppers were those in Bethany, and another was the Mystical Supper. And clearly from those things after the account concerning this harlot, the Savior sends the disciples to the city to prepare the Passover: "Go," saying, "into the city to such a man, and say: The Master says, With thee I will keep the Passover with My disciples." And again: "And there shall meet you a man bearing a pitcher of water, and he will show you a large upper room furnished; there make ready for us." And they having gone, he says, found even as He had commanded them, and made ready the Passover, as the legal Passover was near at hand, which He also came and accomplished with the disciples, as the divine Chrysostom says. Thereafter, when the supper was taking place, that is, the Mystical Supper, and the divine washing had been accomplished in the midst, having reclined again, He delivers our Pascha upon the table itself, even as John the Golden-mouthed relates; and these things are thus. But the divine John, and with him Mark, the divine Evangelists, also added the kind of myrrh, naming it genuine [pistic] and costly. For they thought to call it pistic, that is, genuine, unadulterated and unmingled, and trusted unto purity; or perhaps it was a certain name of the most excellent and first myrrh. Mark also adds that from haste the woman broke the vessel, as being narrow-mouthed, which he also calls an alabaster box; and this vessel is of glass, as sacred Epiphanius says, made without a handle, which is also called a bikion.[^97] And that myrrh was composed of many kinds, but especially of these: myrrh, fragrant cinnamon flower, that is, aromatic reed, and oil. But do Thou, Who wast anointed with spiritual myrrh, O Christ God, free us from the passions that assault us, and have mercy on us, for Thou alone art holy and the Lover of mankind. Amen.

*Tone 2. Ode 8. Heirmos:*  
When the tyrant's command prevailed, the furnace was heated sevenfold of old, wherein the Youths were not consumed, having trampled down the king's decree, but they cried aloud: Praise the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Troparia:*  
Pouring out costly myrrh upon Thy Master's and fearful divine head, O Christ, the woman touched Thy most pure feet with defiled hands, and cried aloud: Praise the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

With tears she that was guilty of sins washes the feet of the Creator, and wipes them with her hair; therefore in her life she missed not the redemption of what she had wrought, but cried aloud: Praise the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

Redemption is sacredly accomplished for the woman of good character, from the saving bowels of compassion and the fountain of tears, wherein having washed herself through confession, she was not ashamed, but cried aloud: Praise the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*We praise, we bless, and we worship the Lord.*  
*And again the Heirmos: When the tyrant's command prevailed...*

*We do not sing the Magnificat.*

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*  
With pure souls and undefiled lips, come, let us magnify the undefiled and most pure Mother of Emmanuel, through whom we bring prayer unto Him that was born of her: Spare our souls, O Christ God, and save us!

*Troparia:*  
Senseless and crafty did the companion show himself: he sells a worthy, divine gift for a word, whereby the debt of sins was loosed; the cruel Judas steals the divine love of grace: Spare our souls, O Christ God, and save us!

Going unto the lawless rulers, he says: "What will you give me? and I will deliver Christ unto you, Whom you seek"; Judas, having cast away the adoption of Christ for gold: Spare our souls, O Christ God, and save us!

O ungodly one of blinding avarice, whence thou didst obtain forgetfulness that the world is in nowise equal in worth unto a soul, even as thou wast taught; for through despair thou didst hang thyself, O betrayer of the Godhead: Spare our souls, O Christ God, and save us!

*And again the Heirmos: With pure souls and undefiled lips...*

*Exapostilarion, thrice, as is customary:*  
Thy bridal chamber I behold adorned, O my Savior, and I have no wedding garment that I may enter therein; enlighten the vesture of my soul, O Giver of light, and save me.

*At the Praises, we set 4 verses, and we sing these idiomel stichera, Tone 1:*[^99]  
Knowing Thee, the Son of the Virgin, to be God, the harlot said, praying in weeping as one who had wrought deeds worthy of tears: "Loose my debt, even as I have loosed my hair; love her that loves Thee, who am justly hated, and near the publicans will I proclaim Thee, O Benefactor and Lover of mankind!"

The harlot mingled costly myrrh with tears, and poured it out upon Thy most pure feet, kissing them; straightway Thou didst justify her, and unto us grant forgiveness, O Thou Who didst suffer for us, and save us!

When the sinful woman was offering the myrrh, then was the disciple agreeing with the all-lawless; the one indeed rejoiced, pouring out the costly myrrh, but the other made haste to sell the Priceless One; she recognized the Master, but he parted from the Master; she was set free, but Judas became a slave of the enemy. A grievous thing is slothfulness, but great is repentance, which grant Thou unto me, O Savior Who didst suffer for us, and save us!

O the misery of Judas! He beheld the harlot kissing the footsteps, and he craftily plotted the kiss of betrayal; she loosed her hair, but he was revealed in fury, bearing evil-smelling malice instead of myrrh; for envy knows not how to prefer what is profitable. O the misery of Judas! from which deliver our souls, O God!

*Glory, Tone 2:*  
The sinful woman ran to buy the costly myrrh, wherewith to anoint the Benefactor, and she cried unto the seller of myrrh: "Give me the myrrh, that I also may anoint Him Who cleansed all my sins!"

*Both now, Tone 6:*  
She that was sunken in sin found Thee as a harbor of salvation; pouring out myrrh with tears, she cried unto Thee: "Behold, Thou that awaitest the repentance of those that sin! But, O Master, save me from the wave of sin, for the sake of Thy great mercy!"

*At the Aposticha, the idiomel stichera, Tone 6:*[^100]  
Today Christ comes into the house of the Pharisee, and the sinful woman, drawing near unto His feet, prostrated herself, crying aloud: "Behold her that is sunken in sin, despaired of because of her deeds, yet not abhorred by Thy goodness; and grant me, O Lord, remission of evils, and save me!"

*Verse: We were filled in the morning with Thy mercy, O Lord [Ps. 89:14 LXX].*  
The harlot spread out her hair unto Thee, the Master; Judas spread out his hands unto the lawless: the one indeed to receive remission, but the other to take silver pieces. Therefore we cry aloud unto Thee Who wast sold, and Who hast freed us: O Lord, glory be to Thee!

*Verse: And let the brightness of the Lord our God be upon us [Ps. 89:17 LXX].*

The woman of foul smell and defiled drew near, pouring out tears, at Thy feet, O Savior, confessing her passion: "How shall I gaze upon Thee, O Master? For Thou Thyself art come to save the harlot; raise me up who am dead from the depth, O Thou Who didst raise Lazarus from the tomb on the fourth day; receive me, a wretched woman, O Lord, and save me."

*Verse: I will give thanks unto Thee, O Lord, with my whole heart [Ps. 137:1 LXX].*

She that was despaired of because of her life, and notorious because of her ways, bearing myrrh, drew near unto Thee, crying aloud: "Cast me not away, the harlot, O Thou Who wast born of the Virgin; despise not my tears, O Joy of the angels; but receive me that repent, whom Thou didst not thrust away when sinning, O Lord, for the sake of Thy great mercy."

*Glory, both now, Idiomel, Tone 8. The Work of Kassiani the Nun:*[^101]  
Lord, the woman who had fallen into many sins, perceiving Thy Divinity, taking the rank of a myrrh-bearer, lamenting brings myrrh unto Thee before Thy burial, saying: "Woe is me! for night is unto me the frenzy of intemperance, a dark and moonless love of sin! Receive the fountains of my tears, O Thou Who by clouds dost bring forth the water of the sea; incline Thyself unto the sighings of my heart, O Thou Who didst bow down the heavens by Thine ineffable condescension, that I may kiss Thy most pure feet, and wipe them again with the tresses of my head, which Eve in Paradise at midday, having her ears filled with the sound, hid herself in fear. The multitude of my sins and the abysses of Thy judgments, who shall search out? O Savior of souls, my Savior, despise not me Thy servant, O Thou Who hast immeasurable mercy!"

*And immediately: It is good to give thanks unto the Lord [Ps. 91:2 LXX]. The Trisagion with prostrations, and the First Hour without kathisma psalmody. Prostrations are made according to their rank. And the Litiya, and the Dismissal.*

*And the rest as was indicated before on Great Monday.*[^102]

*At the Sixth Hour, the Troparion of the Prophecy, Tone 8:*  
Today the wicked council is gathered together, and against Thee have they meditated vain things; today from the covenant Judas finds hanging, and Caiaphas against his will confesses: that Thou, One for all, dost willingly accept death; O our Redeemer, Christ God, glory be to Thee!

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 133, Tone 4:*  
The Lord bless thee out of Sion, Who made heaven and earth [Ps. 133:3 LXX].  
*Verse: Behold now, bless the Lord, all ye servants of the Lord [Ps. 133:1 LXX].*

*The Reading from the Prophecy of Ezekiel [Ezek. 2:1–3:3]:*[^103]  
The Lord said unto me: "Son of man, I send thee unto the house of Israel, unto them that embitter Me, who have embittered Me; they and their fathers have rejected Me unto this very day. And the sons are hard-faced and stiff-hearted; I send thee unto them, and thou shalt say unto them: Thus saith Adonai the Lord. If then they will hear, or if they will fear—for it is an embittering house—they shall know that thou art a prophet in the midst of them. And thou, son of man, be not afraid of them, nor be dismayed at their face; for they will rage and surround thee on every side, for thou dwellest in the midst of scorpions; be not afraid of their words, nor be dismayed at their face, for it is an embittering house. And thou shalt speak My words unto them, if then they will hear, or if they will fear, for it is a provoking house. And thou, son of man, hear Him that speaks unto thee: Be not thou embittering, as the most embittering house; open thy mouth, and eat that which I give thee." And I looked, and behold, a hand was stretched out unto me, and in it was a roll of a book. And He unrolled it before me, and therein were written things within and without; and there was written therein lamentation, and mourning, and woe. And He said unto me: "Son of man, eat this roll, and go, and speak unto the sons of Israel." And I opened my mouth, and He fed me with this roll. And He said unto me: "Son of man, thy mouth shall eat, and thy belly shall be filled with this roll given unto thee." And I ate it, and it was in my mouth as sweet honey.

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 134, Tone 6:*  
Ye that fear the Lord, bless the Lord [Ps. 134:20 LXX].  
*Verse: Praise the name of the Lord, praise the Lord, ye His servants [Ps. 134:1 LXX].*

*The Reading of the Holy Gospel.*[^104]  
*And according to order the Trisagion with prostrations, and we complete the kathisma psalmody of the Hours.*

*And the Ninth Hour we begin from the Trisagion, wherein also takes place the final reading from the Holy Gospel.*[^105] *Then we say: Forsake us not unto the end... The Trisagion with prostrations. Likewise also the Beatitudes, making also the customary prostrations. And we say the Kontakion of Great Wednesday, and of the Saint of the temple. Glory: With the saints give rest... Both now, of the temple of the Mother of God; but if not, we say: Steadfast Protectress of Christians... Lord, have mercy, 40 times, and 3 great prostrations, and 12 small prostrations. Trisagion, and 3 prostrations. Lord, have mercy, 12 times, and the Prayer: O All-Holy Trinity... Then: It is truly meet... Glory... both now... And instead of the Dismissal, the Superior says the Prayer:*

**Superior:** O Master of great mercy...  
*all the way to the end.*[^106] *While this is being said, we fall prostrate upon the earth, praying.*

*And upon the completion thereof, after we have risen, he himself bows down to the brethren, asking forgiveness, saying:*  
**Superior:** Bless, holy fathers, and forgive me a sinner whatever I have sinned in all my life, and in all the Holy Forty Days, by word, by deed, by thought, and by all my senses.  
*And the brethren answering:*  
**Choir:** May God forgive thee, venerable father.

*Then the brethren come two by two, and likewise they ask forgiveness, saying:*  
**Brethren:** Forgive me, holy father...  
*and the rest as was written above.*[^107]  
*And having received forgiveness, we go unto our cells, and we keep silence until the hour of Vespers. In cathedral and parish temples, however, there is no dispersal; but after the mutual forgiveness there is a ringing of bells for the two services.*[^108]

## HOLY AND GREAT WEDNESDAY AT VESPERS
*For the Lucernarium, the customary psalmody begins from the Trisagion without prostrations. We also recite the Kathisma: In my distress I cried unto the Lord [Ps. 119 LXX], without prostrations.*

*At "Lord, I have cried," we set 10 verses, and we sing these idiomel stichera, Tone 1:*[^109]  
Knowing Thee, the Son of the Virgin, to be God, the harlot said, praying in weeping as one who had wrought deeds worthy of tears: "Loose my debt, even as I have loosed my hair; love her that loves Thee, who am justly hated, and near the publicans will I proclaim Thee, O Benefactor and Lover of mankind!"

The harlot mingled costly myrrh with tears, and poured it out upon Thy most pure feet, kissing them; straightway Thou didst justify her, and unto us grant forgiveness, O Thou Who didst suffer for us, and save us!

When the sinful woman was offering the myrrh, then was the disciple agreeing with the all-lawless; the one indeed rejoiced, pouring out the costly myrrh, but the other made haste to sell the Priceless One; she recognized the Master, but he parted from the Master; she was set free, but Judas became a slave of the enemy. A grievous thing is slothfulness, but great is repentance, which grant Thou unto me, O Savior Who didst suffer for us, and save us!

O the misery of Judas! He beheld the harlot kissing the footsteps, and he craftily plotted the kiss of betrayal; she loosed her hair, but he was revealed in fury, bearing evil-smelling malice instead of myrrh; for envy knows not how to prefer what is profitable. O the misery of Judas! from which deliver our souls, O God!

*Tone 2:*  
The sinful woman ran to buy the costly myrrh, wherewith to anoint the Benefactor, and she cried unto the seller of myrrh: "Give me the myrrh, that I also may anoint Him Who cleansed all my sins!"

*Tone 6:*  
She that was sunken in sin found Thee as a harbor of salvation; pouring out myrrh with tears, she cried unto Thee: "Behold, Thou that awaitest the repentance of those that sin! But, O Master, save me from the wave of sin, for the sake of Thy great mercy!"

Today Christ comes into the house of the Pharisee, and the sinful woman, drawing near unto His feet, prostrated herself, crying aloud: "Behold her that is sunken in sin, despaired of because of her deeds, yet not abhorred by Thy goodness; and grant me, O Lord, remission of evils, and save me!"

The harlot spread out her hair unto Thee, the Master; Judas spread out his hands unto the lawless: the one indeed to receive remission, but the other to take silver pieces. Therefore we cry aloud unto Thee Who wast sold, and Who hast freed us: O Lord, glory be to Thee!

The woman of foul smell and defiled drew near, pouring out tears, at Thy feet, O Savior, confessing her passion: "How shall I gaze upon Thee, O Master? For Thou Thyself art come to save the harlot; raise me up who am dead from the depth, O Thou Who didst raise Lazarus from the tomb on the fourth day; receive me, a wretched woman, O Lord, and save me."

She that was despaired of because of her life, and notorious because of her ways, bearing myrrh, drew near unto Thee, crying aloud: "Cast me not away, the harlot, O Thou Who wast born of the Virgin; despise not my tears, O Joy of the angels; but receive me that repent, whom Thou didst not thrust away when sinning, O Lord, for the sake of Thy great mercy."

*Glory, both now, Tone 8. The Work of Kassiani the Nun:*[^110]  
Lord, the woman who had fallen into many sins, perceiving Thy Divinity, taking the rank of a myrrh-bearer, lamenting brings myrrh unto Thee before Thy burial, saying: "Woe is me! for night is unto me the frenzy of intemperance, a dark and moonless love of sin! Receive the fountains of my tears, O Thou Who by clouds dost bring forth the water of the sea; incline Thyself unto the sighings of my heart, O Thou Who didst bow down the heavens by Thine ineffable condescension, that I may kiss Thy most pure feet, and wipe them again with the tresses of my head, which Eve in Paradise at midday, having her ears filled with the sound, hid herself in fear. The multitude of my sins and the abysses of Thy judgments, who shall search out? O Savior of souls, my Savior, despise not me Thy servant, O Thou Who hast immeasurable mercy!"

*Entrance with the Gospel.*[^111]  
*O Gladsome Light:*

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 135, Tone 4:*  
O give thanks unto the God of heaven, for He is good, for His mercy endures for ever [Ps. 135:26 LXX].  
*Verse: O give thanks unto the God of gods, for His mercy endures for ever [Ps. 135:2 LXX].*

*The Reading from Exodus [Exod. 2:11–22]:*[^112]  
It came to pass in those many days, when Moses was grown great, that he went out unto his brethren the sons of Israel; and having looked upon their affliction, he saw an Egyptian striking a certain Hebrew of his brethren the sons of Israel. And looking this way and that way, he saw no one; and he struck the Egyptian, and hid him in the sand. And going out on the second day, he saw two Hebrew men striving together, and he said unto him that did the wrong: "Why dost thou strike thy neighbor?" But he said: "Who appointed thee a ruler and a judge over us? Wilt thou kill me, in the same manner as thou killedst the Egyptian yesterday?" And Moses feared, and said: "If this word is made known in this manner!" And Pharaoh heard this word, and sought to kill Moses. But Moses departed from the face of Pharaoh, and dwelt in the land of Midian; and having come into the land of Midian, he sat down by a well. Now the priest of Midian had seven daughters, who fed the sheep of their father Jethro; and they came and drew water, until they had filled the troughs, to water the sheep of their father Jethro. But the shepherds came and drove them away; and Moses arose and delivered them, and drew water for them, and watered their sheep. And they came unto Reuel their father; and he said unto them: "Why have ye made haste to come today?" And they said: "An Egyptian man delivered us from the shepherds, and drew water for us, and watered our sheep." And he said unto his daughters: "And where is he? and why have ye left the man in this manner? Call him therefore, that he may eat bread." And Moses went to dwell with the man; and he gave Zipporah his daughter unto Moses to wife. And the woman conceiving in her womb bare a son, and Moses called his name Gershom, saying: "For I am a stranger in a strange land." And conceiving again she bare a second son, and called his name Eliezer, saying: "For the God of my father is my helper, and hath delivered me from the hand of Pharaoh."

*Prokeimenon, Psalm 137, Tone 4:*  
O Lord, Thy mercy endures for ever; despise not the works of Thy hands [Ps. 137:8 LXX].  
*Verse: I will give thanks unto Thee, O Lord, with my whole heart, and before the angels will I sing unto Thee [Ps. 137:1 LXX].*

*The Reading from Job [Job 2:1–5]:*[^113]  
It came to pass on a certain day, that the angels of God came to present themselves before the Lord, and the devil came in their midst to present himself before the Lord. And the Lord said unto the devil: "Whence comest thou?" Then the devil said before the Lord: "Having traversed the earth under heaven, and walked about the whole earth, I am come." And the Lord said unto the devil: "Hast thou then attended with thy mind unto My servant Job? For there is none like him of those upon the earth: a man blameless, true, spotless, godly, abstaining from every evil thing, and still he holds fast to innocence; but thou didst speak to destroy his possessions in vain." And the devil answered the Lord, and said: "Skin for skin, and all whatever a man hath will he give for his life. Howbeit put forth Thine hand, and touch..."

"...his bones and his flesh, surely he will bless Thee to Thy face!" And the Lord said unto the devil: "Behold, I deliver him unto thee, only preserve his life." And the devil went out from the presence of the Lord, and struck Job with sore boils from his feet unto his head. And Job took a potsherd that he might scrape away his sores, and he sat upon a dung-heap outside the city. And when much time had passed, his wife said unto him, saying: "How long wilt thou endure, saying: 'Behold, I will wait yet a little while, awaiting the hope of my salvation'? For behold, thy memory is wiped out from the earth, the sons and daughters of the pains and labors of my womb, for whom I labored in vain with travails; and thou thyself sittest in the putrefaction of worms, spending the night outside without shelter; and I go about wandering and serving, moving from place to place and from house to house, awaiting when the sun will set, that I may rest from my labors and from the sorrows that now encompass me; but speak some word against the Lord, and die!" But Job looked upon her and said unto her: "Why hast thou spoken as one of the foolish women? If we have received good things from the hand of the Lord, shall we not endure evil things?" In all these things that befell him, Job sinned in nothing with his lips before God, [and he imputed no folly unto God].[^114]

*Then: Let my prayer be set forth... [Ps. 140:2 LXX]*  
*The Holy Gospel according to Matthew, section 150 [Matt. 26:6–16].*[^115]  
*And the rest of the Order of the Presanctified Gifts.*  
*And after the bringing in of the Precious Gifts, 3 prostrations.*  
*And immediately prostrations made in church cease completely.*[^116]  
*In cells, however, they are performed even unto Great Friday.*

[Engraving: The Angel of the Lord holding a crown and cross in celestial glory.]

*On Holy and Great Wednesday at Compline, we say Small Compline, whereat we sing the Tri-ode of Master Andrew, of Great Thursday.*[^117]  
*Tone 6. Ode 4. Heirmos:*  
The prophet heard of Thy coming, O Lord, and was afraid, that Thou wast about to be born of a Virgin and appear unto men, and he said: "I heard the report of Thee, and was afraid; glory to Thy power, O Lord!" [Hab. 3:2 LXX].

*Troparia:*  
The furnished upper room received Thee, the Creator, and the Initiates of the mysteries; and there Thou didst accomplish the Pascha, and there Thou didst work the mysteries; for there, by the two disciples now sent by Thee, the Pascha was prepared for Thee.

"Go ye into the sheep-quarter," saith He that knows all things beforehand unto the Apostles, "and there is a man met, who can faithfully receive the Lord; having prepared the upper room of his heart, and the supper of piety."

The disposition of avarice and the manner of thy madness is full, O senseless Judas! For being entrusted with the coffer alone, thou didst in nowise incline unto mercy, but didst close the compassions of thy hard heart, betraying the One Compassionate.

The disposition of the God-killers mingled with the betrayal of the lover of money: the former indeed were sharpened for murder, but the latter hastened after the pieces of silver; for having then wickedly anticipated repentance by hanging, he was deprived of life.

A kiss full of deceit, the greeting "Hail!" with a weapon, O deceitful Judas! For with the tongue indeed thou speakest things of union, but in disposition thou beckonest toward separation; for thou didst deceitfully make haste to betray the Benefactor unto the lawless.

Thou kissest and betrayest, Judas; thou embracest and art not ashamed, having run with deceit; who kissing hates, O thrice-wretched one? who loving sells for a price? Thy kiss exposes the disposition of thy shameless evil counsel.

*Glory:*  
Indivisible in Essence, unconfused in Persons, I glorify Thee, O single Triune Godhead, as consubstantial and co-enthroned; I cry aloud unto Thee the great hymn, thrice-chanted in the highest.

*Both now, Theotokion:*  
A conception beyond word, Thy childbearing beyond nature, O Mother of God: the one indeed from the Spirit without seed, and the other concealed from the laws of nature, as not secret, and beyond the nature of every childbirth: for He that is born is God.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лся є҆сѝ дне́сь):*[^118]  
Eating with Thy disciples, O Master, Thou didst mystically reveal Thine all-holy slaying, whereby we were delivered from corruption, who honor Thy venerable Sufferings.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*  
Him Whom the heavenly hosts glorify, and Cherubim and Seraphim tremble before, let every breath and creation sing, bless, and exalt throughout all ages!

*Troparia:*  
Having fulfilled the legal command, He that wrote the tablets of the Law on Sinai ate the ancient and shadow-like Passover; and He became the mystical and life-offering Pascha.

Initiating into the wisdom hidden from the ages, O Christ, Thou didst show it forth together unto all the Apostles, O Savior, at the Supper, which the faithful handed down unto the Churches.

"One of you will deceitfully betray Me, having sold Me unto the Hebrews in this night," the Savior Christ cried aloud, troubling His friends; then one toward another they hesitated in perplexity.

Having humbled Thyself for our sake, O Rich One, having risen from the Supper and taken a towel, Thou didst gird Thyself therewith, and having bowed Thy neck, Thou didst wash the feet of the disciples and of the betrayer.

Beyond mind and ineffable knowledge is Thy height, O Jesus! who will not marvel that Thou didst stand forth as a servant, O Fashioner of mankind, washing the feet and wiping them with the towel!

The disciple whom the Lord loved, leaning on His breast, said unto Him: "Who is he that betrays Thee?" And Christ said unto him: "He that hath now put his hand into the dish."

Having received the honorable Bread, after the Bread he is separated, meditating betrayal; he runs unto the Jews, and saith unto the lawless: "What will you give me, and I will deliver Him unto you?"

*Let us bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord.*  
One God according to Essence I honor, three Hypostases I sing distinct, other, yet not of another nature; since the Godhead is One in three Persons: as Father, and Son, and the Divine Spirit.

*Both now, Theotokion:*  
Deliver us, O Jesus our Savior, from the deceit of the crafty one and from temptation; receive the Mother of God who intercedes unceasingly; for She is Thy Mother, and is able to entreat Thee.

*We praise, we bless, and we worship the Lord.*  
*And again the Heirmos: Him Whom the heavenly hosts glorify... And a prostration.*

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*  
Inconceivable is the birth of the Seedless Conception, incorrupt the Fruit of the unwedded Mother; for the birth of God renews natures; therefore all generations orthodoxly magnify thee as the Bride of God and Mother.

*Troparia:*  
Revealing the great mystery of Thine incarnation, as Thou didst recline at Supper with Thy disciples, O Lover of mankind, Thou didst say: "Eat with faith the Living Bread, and drink the Blood poured forth from the piercing of My side in sacrifice."

A heavenly tabernacle appeared the upper room, wherein Christ celebrated the Pascha: the unbloody Supper and the rational service, and the table of perfected mysteries, an intelligible altar.

Pascha is Christ, great and most venerable, eaten as Bread, yet slaughtered as a Sheep; for He was offered as a sacrifice for us; of His Body piously and of His Blood do we all mystically partake.

Having blessed the Bread, being Himself the Heavenly Bread, having given thanks unto the Father Who begat Him, having taken also the Cup, Thou didst give it unto the disciples, crying aloud: "Take, eat, this is My Body, and the Blood of incorrupt life."

"Amen," said the Vine unto the branches, Christ the Truth unto the Apostles: "Henceforth I will not drink of the fruit of the vine, until I drink it new in the glory of My Father, with you My heirs."

For thirty pieces of silver thou sellest the Lord, and considerest not, O lawless Judas, the mystery of the Supper, nor the honorable washing; oh, how thou hast slipped into the fall, thou art come unto the ends of darkness, having embraced hanging!

Thou hast stretched out the hands wherewith thou didst receive the Bread of incorruption, to take pieces of silver; deceitfully offering for a kiss the mouth wherewith thou didst receive the Body and Blood of Christ; but woe unto thee, even as Christ said!

Christ hath banqueted the world, the Heavenly and Divine Bread; come therefore, ye lovers of Christ, with mortal lips but with pure hearts, let us faithfully receive the Pascha that is slain, sacerdotally ministered within us.

*Glory:*  
Let us glorify the Father, let us exalt the Son, let us faithfully worship the Divine Spirit: the Undivided Trinity, the Unity according to Essence; as Light and Lights, and Life and Lives, giving life and enlightening the ends of the world.

*Both now, Theotokion:*  
A heavenly bridal chamber and Bride, Ever-Virgin, thou alone wast revealed, having carried God, and having given birth unto Him unchangeably incarnate of thee; therefore all generations orthodoxly magnify thee as the Bride of God and Mother.

*And again the Heirmos: Inconceivable is the birth of the Seedless Conception...*  
*And the rest of Small Compline according to custom, and the Dismissal; and we make 3 prostrations, all equally together.*  
*And the prayer having been said by the Superior, we depart unto our cells.*  
*Be it known that from this holy day, the Midnight Office is not sung in church until the Week of Thomas.*[^119]

[Engraving: The Washing of the Feet at the Mystical Supper in the Upper Room of Sion. Christ, girded with a towel and kneeling, washes the feet of Saint Peter, while the holy Apostles recline around the table in solemn contemplation.][^120]

## HOLY AND GREAT THURSDAY AT MATINS
*The paramonarios strikes the semantron in the seventh hour of the night; and when we have assembled in church, and the priest has made the beginning:*[^121]  
**Priest:** Blessed is our God...  
*we begin immediately according to custom. Then the Six Psalms; and upon the completion of the Six Psalms:*  
*Alleluia, in Tone 8.*  
*And the Troparion, Tone 8, thrice:*[^122]  
When the glorious disciples were enlightened at the washing of the Supper, then Judas the ungodly was darkened, ailing with avarice, and unto lawless judges he delivers Thee, the righteous Judge. Behold, O lover of possessions, him that because of them made use of the halter! Flee from the insatiable soul that dared such things against the Master! O Thou Who art good toward all, Lord, glory be to Thee!

*Then the Holy Gospel according to Luke, section 108 [Luke 22:1–39], without interruption:*[^123]  
At that time, the feast of unleavened bread drew near...  
*The end is in section 109 [Luke 22:39]:*  
*And Psalm 50.*  
*The prayer: O God, save Thy people (*Спасѝ Бж҃е лю́ди твоѧ̑*) we do not say.*  
*And we begin the Canon, the work of Master Cosmas, Tone 6: the heirmoi twice, and the troparia unto 6. The Canon whose acrostic is: Τῇ μακρᾷ πέμπτῃ μακρὸν ὕμνον ᾄδω [Tē makra pemptē makron hymnon adō], that is: "On Great Thursday I sing a long hymn."*[^124]

*Matins*

*Ode 1, Tone 6. Heirmos:*
The Red Sea is severed by the stroke of the rod, and the wave-nourished deep is dried up; the same path became passable for the unarmed, and a grave for those in full armor, while a God-pleasing hymn was sung: Gloriously hath Christ our God been glorified.[^125]

*Troparion:*
The all-originating Cause and Giver of life, the boundless Wisdom of God, built Herself a house from the pure, unwedded Mother;[^126] for having clothed Himself bodily as in a temple, gloriously hath Christ our God been glorified.

*Troparion:*
Initiating Her friends into the mysteries, the Wisdom of God prepares the soul-nourishing Table, and mingles the Cup of immortality in truth for the faithful; let us approach with piety and cry aloud: Gloriously hath Christ our God been glorified.

*Troparion:*
Let all us faithful give ear unto the uncreated and natural Wisdom of God summoning us with a lofty proclamation, for She cries: Taste, and understanding that I am Christ, cry aloud: Gloriously hath Christ our God been glorified.

*After this, both choirs chant the Heirmos again together.*

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Being the Lord of all and Creator God, the Passionless One, having impoverished Himself, united creation unto Himself; and being Himself the Passover for those for whom He was about to die, He offered Himself beforehand as a sacrifice, crying: Eat My Body, and be established in faith.[^127]

*Troparion:*
With Thy Cup, which is redemption for the entire human race, Thou didst fill Thy disciples with the Cup of gladness, filling it full; for Thou Thyself dost minister Thyself as a holy sacrifice, crying: Drink My Blood, and be established in faith.

*Troparion:*
The mindless man, who among you is the betrayer, Thou didst foretell unto Thy disciples, O Guileless One, and he shall not understand these things; and being senseless, he will not comprehend; nevertheless abide in Me, and be established in faith.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 1, Podoben: "While the soldiers were guarding Thy tomb" (Гро́бъ тво́й):*
He Who created lakes and fountains and seas, instructing us in surpassing humility, girded Himself with a towel and washed the feet of the disciples, humbling Himself through the abundance of His tender mercy, and lifting us up from the abysses of evil, the only Lover of mankind.

*Glory... Tone 3, Podoben: "Of the divine faith" (Бж҃ественныя вѣ́ры):*
Humbling Thyself in Thy tender mercy, Thou didst wash the feet of Thy disciples, directing them toward the divine course; and Peter, who would not submit to be washed, straightway obeyed the divine command when washed, earnestly entreating Thee to grant us great mercy.

*Both now... Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лся є҆сѝ дне́сь):*
Eating with Thy disciples, O Master, Thou didst mystically reveal Thine all-holy slaughter, whereby we are delivered from corruption, who honor Thy venerable Passions.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Foreseeing Thy mystery ineffable, O Christ, the prophet proclaimed: Thou hast established the steadfast love of Thy strength, O Bountiful Father; for, O Good One, Thou hast sent Thine Only-begotten Son into the world as an atonement.[^128]

*On Holy and Great Thursday*

*Troparion:*
Going forth unto the Passion which pours out dispassion upon all who are descended from Adam, O Christ, Thou didst say unto Thy friends: With you I have desired to partake of this Passover; for as the Only-begotten the Father hath sent Me into the world as an atonement.

*Troparion:*
Partaking of the Cup, Thou didst cry unto the disciples, O Immortal One: I shall no longer drink of the fruit of the vine henceforth while living with you; for as the Only-begotten the Father hath sent Me into the world as an atonement.

*Troparion:*
I say that I drink a new drink passing all utterance in My Kingdom, Christ said unto His friends; for as God I shall be with you who are gods; for as the Only-begotten the Father hath sent Me into the world as an atonement.[^129]

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Bound together by the bond of love, the Apostles, having dedicated themselves to Christ Who rules over all, had their beautiful feet cleansed, proclaiming peace unto all.[^130]

*Troparion:*
Holding the unrestrainable and lofty water in the air, bridling the abysses and restraining the seas, the Wisdom of God pours water into the basin, and the Master washes the feet of His servants.

*Troparion:*
The Master shows His disciples an example of humility; He Who clothes the heaven with clouds girds Himself with a towel and bows His knees to wash the feet of His servants, in Whose hand is the breath of all that exist.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
The lowest abyss of sins hath encompassed me, and no longer enduring the tempest, like Jonah unto the Master I cry unto Thee: Lead me up from corruption.[^131]

*Troparion:*
Ye call Me Lord and Teacher, O disciples, for so I am, didst Thou cry, O Savior; therefore imitate the example which ye have seen in Me.

*Troparion:*
He that hath no stain hath no need to wash his feet; and ye, O disciples, are clean, but not all; for by a mad impulse one of you rages.

*Kontakion, Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the highest" (Вы́шнихъ и҆щя̀):*
Having taken the Bread into his hands, the betrayer secretly reaches them forth, and receives the price of Him Who fashioned man with His own hands; and Judas remained incorrigible, a slave and a deceiver.[^132]

*Ikos:*
Approaching the Mystical Table in fear, let us all receive the Bread with pure souls, remaining with the Master, that we may see how He washes the feet of the disciples, and do as we see: submitting one to another and washing one another's feet; for Christ thus commanded His disciples, foretelling them to do so; but Judas, a slave and a deceiver, would not listen.

*Matins*

*Synaxarion. Verses on the Sacred Washing:*
At eventide God washes the feet of the disciples,
Whose foot in Eden of old trampled upon delusion.[^133]
