*Verses on the Mystical Supper:*
A twofold supper: for it brings the Passover of the Law,
And the new Passover, the Blood and Body of the Master.

*Verses on the Transcendent Prayer:*
Prayer and terrifying dread, the sweat of drops of blood:
O Christ, praying openly before the Father's face,
Deceiving the enemy through these things by death.[^134]

*Verses on the Betrayal:*
Why need ye knives? Why staves, O slayers of men,
Against Him Who is willing to die for the redemption of the world?

On Holy and Great Thursday, the divine Fathers, who have ordered all things well, receiving them in succession from the divine Apostles and the sacred and divine Gospels, handed down unto us to celebrate four distinct mysteries: the Sacred Washing of the feet; the Mystical Supper, that is, the transmission of the dread Mysteries for our sake; the Transcendent Prayer; and the Betrayal itself.[^135] For the Jewish Passover was to be sacrificed on Friday. It was therefore fitting that the figure should be followed by the truth, and furthermore that Christ our Passover should be sacrificed for our sake. Our Lord Jesus Christ therefore, anticipating it, as the divine Fathers say, celebrated it with His disciples on Thursday evening; for that evening and the whole of Friday are reckoned as a single day among the Jews, for in this manner they reckon the day following the evening. He therefore celebrated this Passover then [as some have said] with the disciples according to the Law, among whom is also the divine Chrysostom: first standing simply, girded, shod with sandals, leaning upon staves, and performing all other things whatsoever the Law commands, lest He should be deemed a transgressor of the Law. For Zebedee prepared these things, for he was the one carrying the earthen vessel of water, as the great Athanasius says, although others have spoken differently concerning this.[^136] Then, showing His disciples the more perfect things, He delivered the mystery of our Passover in the Upper Room, night having already fallen. For when evening was come, it says, He reclined at table with the twelve. And before beginning to sup [for thus says the divine Chrysostom], He rises from supper, and lays aside His garments, and pours water into the basin, ministering all things Himself with His own hands, both to put Judas to shame and to remind the other disciples not to seek preeminence, even as He instructed them after the washing, saying: "Whosoever desires to be first, let him be the last of all," setting Himself forth as an example.[^137] And it appears that Christ washed Judas before all the rest, who had brazenly seated himself in the foremost place; and last of all He comes to Peter. But he, being more fervent than the others, forbids the Teacher, yet straightway submits to Him again. Having therefore washed their feet and shown forth surpassing exaltation through humility, taking His garments again and reclining, He instructs them to love one another and not to seek preeminence. And while they were eating, He introduces the matter of the betrayal. And while the disciples were troubled at this saying, Jesus says to John alone in secret: "He it is to whom I shall give a piece of bread when I have dipped it, that same is he that betrays Me." For if Peter had known the saying, being more fervent than the others, he would have slain Judas.[^138] And again He says: "He that dipps his hand with Me in the dish"; which both took place. Then, after waiting a little, taking bread, He says: "Take, eat"; and likewise the cup: "Drink of it, all of you," He said; "this is My Blood of the New Testament; this do in remembrance of Me"; yet He Himself also did this, eating and drinking with them. And after the bread Satan entered into Judas: who had tempted him before, but now took up his abode in him utterly. And going forth, it says, he made an agreement with the chief priests, that he should betray Him unto them for thirty pieces of silver. And after the supper the disciples went out unto the Mount of Olives, unto a certain place called Gethsemane. And after a long time Jesus says unto them: "All ye shall be offended because of Me this night." But Peter said: "Even though all shall be offended, yet will I never deny Thee." And it was late, that is, deep night. But He said: "Before the cock crow twice, thou shalt deny Me thrice." Which also came to pass, an immeasurable dread having manifested itself in Peter; for God showed the weakness of our nature, and together with this, since He was to commit the world unto him, that knowing the slipperiness of his own nature, he might be forgiving toward those who sin. Nevertheless, Peter's threefold denial prefigured the sin of all mankind against God: first, the commandment which Adam transgressed; second, the transgression of the written Law; and third, the Incarnation of the Word Itself; which afterwards the Savior healed again by a threefold repentance, saying thrice: "Peter, lovest thou Me?" After these things He says unto the disciples: [showing what is human, that death is fearful unto all:] "My soul is exceeding sorrowful, even unto death." And going a little forward, about a stone's cast, He prayed thrice, saying: "O My Father, if this cup cannot pass away from Me, except I drink it, Thy will be done." And again: "O Father, if it be possible, let this cup pass from Me." And returning and finding the disciples plunged in sleep, He turns to Peter, speaking thus: "Could ye not watch with Me one hour?" that is to say: Thou that sayest thou wilt struggle even unto death, dost thou thus sleep with the rest? And having passed over to the other side of the brook of Cedron, where there was a garden, He took up His abode there with His disciples. And He was accustomed often to come thither, therefore Judas also knew the place; who, taking some of the soldiers and an accompanying multitude, came upon Jesus, having given them a sign of a kiss: and he said this because many times when He was seized He had passed invisibly through the midst of them; even as here also Christ Himself first goes forth unto them, saying: "Whom seek ye?" But they did not yet recognize Him, for night was no impediment, seeing there were lanterns, it says, and burning torches; and falling down with fear they retreated; and when they came again He answered them Himself. And when Judas had accomplished the agreement, Christ says: "Friend, for what art thou come?" that is to say, That for which thou hast come, Judas, is in due season. And again He says: "Are ye come out as against a robber with...

*On Holy and Great Thursday*

...swords and staves to take Me? And they came by night, lest there should be some tumult among the people. Then Peter, being more fervent, drew a sword [for they were prepared for such things at the supper], and striking the servant of the high priest, Malchus by name, cut off his right ear. But Jesus, knowing that the chief priests would say: "He did not hear or teach the Law well," rebukes Peter, because it is not good for a disciple of a spiritual man to use the sword; and He heals Malchus's ear.[^139] Having seized Jesus, therefore, they lead Him bound to the courtyard of Annas the high priest, who was the father-in-law of Caiaphas; for all those who spoke against Christ, the Pharisees and scribes, were gathered there. Here took place the denial of Him by Peter before the maidservant; and as the middle of the night approached, the cock crew for the third time; and he, remembering, wept bitterly. And when the time had already drawn near unto morning, they lead Christ from Annas to the high priest Caiaphas, where He also received spitting and false witnesses were summoned; and as the day began to dawn, Caiaphas sends Him unto Pilate. And they who led Him, it says, did not enter into the praetorium, lest they should be defiled, but that they might eat the passover. It is considered, therefore, that the chief priests and Pharisees acted unlawfully at that time in postponing the passover, as the divine Chrysostom says: for on this night they ought to have eaten it, but because of the murder of Christ they postponed it. And that they ought to have eaten it then, Christ demonstrated by eating it before supper during the night, and then initiating the perfected into the mysteries; or else because it was fitting that the legal type, as has been said, should give place to the truth: for John says, "before the feast of the Passover." For all these things that came to pass on Thursday and during its night, we also celebrate the commemoration of those dread and ineffable deeds and acts, performing it with fear.[^140]
By Thine ineffable tender mercy, O Christ our God, have mercy on us. Amen.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The Children in Babylon feared not the fiery furnace flame, but cast into the midst of the flame and bedewed with moisture, they sang: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers.[^141]

*Troparion:*
Nodding his head, Judas meditated evil, seeking a favorable opportunity to deliver over unto condemnation the Judge, Who is the Lord of all and the God of our fathers.

*Troparion:*
Christ cried unto you, His friends: One shall betray Me! Forgetting their gladness, they were seized with sorrow and dread, saying: Tell us, who is this, O God of our fathers?

*Troparion:*
He that with boldness puts his hand into the dish with Me, for him indeed it were good never to have passed through the gates of life; him who was the betrayer did the God of our fathers make known.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Enduring danger for the sake of the laws of their fathers, the blessed youths in Babylon spat upon the senseless command of the king; and joined together, not consumed by the fire, they sang a worthy hymn unto the Ruler of all: Praise the Lord, all ye works, and exalt Him above all unto all ages.[^142]

*Troparion:*
Blessed companions at table, having endured with the Word in Sion, the Apostles followed the Shepherd like lambs; and joined together, not separated from Christ, nourished by divine words, they cried aloud in thanksgiving: Praise the Lord, all ye works, and exalt Him above all unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
Willingly forgetting the lawful friendship, the ill-named Iscariot prepared for betrayal those feet which He had washed; and eating Thy Bread, the divine Body, he lifted up a snare against Thee, O Christ, and understood not to cry: Praise the Lord, all ye works, and exalt Him above all unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
The conscience-less one received the Body which looses sin, and the divine Blood shed for the world; yet he was not ashamed when drinking that which he was selling for a price, nor was he indignant at his malice, and understood not to cry: Praise the Lord, all ye works, and exalt Him above all unto all ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Come, ye faithful, let us enjoy the Master's hospitality and the immortal Table in the Upper Room with elevated minds, having learned from the Word concerning the ascended Word, Whom we magnify.[^143]

*Troparion:*
Go, the Word said unto the disciples, prepare the Passover in the Upper Room, whereby the mind is established, and prepare it mystically with the unleavened word of truth; and magnify the steadfastness of grace.

*Matins*

*Troparion:*
The Father begat Me, the Fashioning Wisdom, before the ages, as the beginning of His ways, and created Me for works now mystically accomplished; for being by nature the uncreated Word, I appropriate the utterances of that nature which I have now assumed.[^144]

*Troparion:*
Even as I am man in essence and not by phantom appearance, so am I God by manner of exchange, the nature being united unto Me, the one Christ; therefore know Me from the things whereby and whereunto I exist, saving the same.[^145]

*Exapostilarion, thrice:*
I see Thy bridal chamber adorned, O my Savior, and I have no wedding garment wherewith to enter therein; enlighten the vesture of my soul, O Giver of light, and save me.[^146]

*At the Praises, we set 4 verses, and we chant Idiomel Stichera, Tone 2:*

*Sticheron 1, Tone 2:*
The Jewish council runs together now, that it may deliver over unto Pilate the Fashioner and Creator of all things. O lawless ones! O faithless ones! For they prepare for judgment Him Who is coming to judge the living and the dead; they prepare for sufferings Him Who heals passions. O long-suffering Lord, great is Thy mercy, glory to Thee.[^147]

*Sticheron 2, Tone 2:*
Judas the lawless, O Lord, who at the Supper dipped his hand into the dish with Thee, stretched forth his hands unto the lawless to receive the silver pieces; and having calculated the price of the myrrh, he feared not to sell Thee, the Priceless One; extending his feet to be washed, he deceitfully kissed the Master, that he might deliver Him over unto the lawless; and being cast out from the choir of the Apostles, and having cast down the thirty pieces of silver, he knew not of Thy third-day Resurrection, whereby have mercy on us.

*Sticheron 3, Tone 2:*
Judas the betrayer, being deceitful, with a deceitful kiss betrayed the Savior and Lord, and sold the Master of all unto the Jews as a slave; like a sheep unto the slaughter, so followed the Lamb of God, the Son of the Father, the only greatly merciful.

*Sticheron 4, Tone 2:*
Judas was revealed by his deeds to be a slave and a deceiver, a disciple and a plotter, a friend and a devil; for he followed the Teacher, and meditated on His betrayal, saying within himself: I will deliver Him up and acquire the gathered possessions; and he sought that the myrrh also should be sold, and Jesus seized by craft; he gave a kiss, he betrayed Christ, and like a sheep unto the slaughter, thus followed [the Lamb of God], the only tenderhearted Lover of mankind.

*Glory... Both now... Tone 2:*
He Whom Isaiah proclaimed as a Lamb comes unto His voluntary slaughter, and gives His back unto stripes, and His cheeks unto buffetings, and turned not away His face from the shame of spittings, and is condemned unto a shameful death; all these things the Sinless One voluntarily accepts, that He may grant unto all the resurrection from the dead.[^148]

*At the Aposticha, Idiomel Stichera, Tone 8:*

*Sticheron 1, Tone 8:*
Today the evil assembly is gathered against Christ, and meditated vain things against Him, to deliver over the Innocent One unto Pilate for death; today Judas puts upon himself the noose for money, and is deprived of both temporal and divine life; today Caiaphas prophesies unwillingly, saying: It is expedient that one man should die for the people; for He came to suffer for our sins, that He might set us free from the bondage of the enemy, as good and the Lover of mankind.[^149]

*Verse: He that eats my bread hath lifted up his heel against me.* [LXX Ps. 40:10] *On Holy and Great Thursday*

*Sticheron 2, Tone 8:*
Today Judas hides the face of love for the poor, and uncovers the appearance of covetousness; no longer is he concerned for the poor, no longer does he sell the myrrh of the sinful woman, but the Heavenly Myrrh, and from Him he seeks silver pieces; he runs unto the Jews, saying unto the lawless: What will ye give me, and I will deliver Him unto you? O the covetousness of the betrayer! He makes a bargain easily surrendered to the will of the buyers; he makes a sale of the Priceless One, and shows himself not stingy regarding the price, but sells Him as a runaway slave; for it is the custom of thieves to cast away precious things, and now the disciple cast holy things unto dogs; for the madness of covetousness made him rage against his own Master; whose temptation let us flee, crying: O long-suffering Lord, glory to Thee.[^150]

*Verse: He went forth, and spake together.* [LXX Ps. 40:7]

*Sticheron 3, Tone 8:*
Thy disposition is filled with guile, O lawless Judas; for ailing with covetousness, thou didst acquire hatred of mankind; for if thou didst love wealth, why didst thou come unto Him Who taught concerning poverty? And if thou didst love it, why didst thou sell the Priceless One, delivering Him unto murder? Be astonished, O sun; groan, O earth, and quaking cry aloud: O Guileless Lord, glory to Thee.

*Verse: They laid a lawless word against me.* [LXX Ps. 40:9]

*Sticheron 4, Tone 8:*
Let no one, O ye faithful, uninitiated into the mysteries of the Master's Supper, let no one at all approach the Table deceitfully like Judas; for he, having received the morsel, turned against the Bread, in appearance being a disciple, but in deed being a murderer, rejoicing with the Jews, yet abiding with the Apostles; hating while kissing, and kissing while selling Him Who redeemed us from the curse, the God and Savior of our souls.[^151]

*Glory... Tone 8:*
Thy disposition is filled with guile... *(see above)*

*Both now... Tone 5:*
Instructing Thy disciples in the mysteries, O Lord, Thou didst teach them, saying: O friends, see that no fear separate you from Me; for though I suffer, it is for the world; be not offended therefore in Me; for I came not to be ministered unto, but to minister, and to lay down My life as a ransom for the world. If then ye are My friends, imitate Me: whosoever desires to be first, let him be the last; the Master is as the servant; abide in Me, that ye may bear fruit; for I am the living Vine.[^152]

*It is good to give praise unto the Lord...*

*We join also the First Hour, in which we say also the Troparion of the Prophecy, Tone 3:*
Thou Who for the sake of mankind wast smitten on the cheek and wast not provoked to wrath, deliver our life from corruption, O Lord, and save us.[^153]

*Glory... Both now... the same.*

*Prokeimenon, Tone 1: Let the nations understand that the Lord is Thy name.*
*Verse: O God, who shall be like unto Thee?* [LXX Ps. 82:19, 2] *At Vespers*

*Reading of the Prophecy of Jeremiah:* [Jer. 11:18–12:15 LXX][^154]
O Lord, make it known unto me, and I shall know; then I beheld their devices. But I was like an innocent lamb led to the slaughter, and I knew not that they had devised an evil counsel against me, saying: Come and let us put wood into his bread, and let us utterly blot him out from the land of the living, and let his name be remembered no more.[^155] O Lord of hosts, Who judgest righteously, Who triest the hearts and the reins, let me see Thy vengeance upon them, for unto Thee have I revealed my plea. Therefore thus saith the Lord against the men of Anathoth that seek my life, that say: Prophesy not in the name of the Lord, or else thou shalt die by our hands; therefore thus saith the Lord of hosts: Behold, I will visit them: their young men shall die by the sword, and their sons and their daughters shall die by famine; and there shall be no remnant of them, for I will bring evil upon them that dwell in Anathoth, in the year of their visitation. Righteous art Thou, O Lord, that I should plead with Thee; yet will I speak of judgments unto Thee: Why doth the way of the ungodly prosper? Why are all they abundant that deal very treacherously? Thou hast planted them, and they have taken root; they have begotten children, and brought forth fruit; Thou art near in their mouth, and far from their reins. But Thou, O Lord, knowest me, Thou hast seen me, and proved mine heart before Thee; gather them together like sheep for the slaughter, and purify them in the day of their slaughter. How long shall the land mourn, and all the herb of the field wither, for the malice of them that dwell therein? The beasts and the birds are consumed, because they said: God shall not see our ways. Thy feet run, and they make thee weary. Go, gather together all the wild beasts of the field, and let them come to devour her. Many pastors have destroyed My vineyard, they have defiled My portion, they have made My pleasant portion an impassable wilderness; they have made it a desolate waste. For thus saith the Lord against all the evil neighbors that touch Mine inheritance, which I have divided unto My people Israel: Behold, I will pluck them up from their land, and I will cast out the house of Judah from the midst of them; and it shall come to pass, after that I have plucked them out, I will return, and have compassion on them, and will settle them, every man in his heritage, and every man in his own land.

*Prokeimenon, Tone 8: Make your vows and pay them unto the Lord our God.*
*Verse: Known in Judea is God, in Israel great is His name.* [LXX Ps. 75:12, 2]

*It should be known that the litany for the reposed in the narthex does not take place until Thomas Sunday.*[^156]

*The remaining Hours are chanted as simple three-psalmed hours.*[^157]

*On Holy and Great Thursday*

*[Woodcut Engraving: The Savior blessing the bread and the cup at the Mystical Supper]*

## Great and Holy Thursday at Vespers

*At the lighting of the lamps, the semantron is struck in the eighth hour; and when the hierarch or priest has given the blessing, we begin Vespers: "Come, let us worship," and the Introductory Psalm. The Litany is said by the priest.*[^158]

*Then, on "Lord, I have cried," in Tone 2, we set 10 verses.*

*The priest, changing his priestly vestments, comes forth and censes, and after returning, performs the Proskomedia. And we chant the Idiomel Stichera of the day, Tone 2, repeating them:*[^159]

*Sticheron 1, Tone 2:*
The Jewish council runs together now, that it may deliver over unto Pilate the Fashioner and Creator of all things. O lawless ones! O faithless ones! For they prepare for judgment Him Who is coming to judge the living and the dead; they prepare for sufferings Him Who heals passions. O long-suffering Lord, great is Thy mercy, glory to Thee.

*Sticheron 2, Tone 2:*
Judas the lawless, O Lord, who at the Supper dipped his hand into the dish with Thee, stretched forth his hands unto the lawless to receive the silver pieces; and having calculated the price of the myrrh, he feared not to sell Thee, the Priceless One; extending his feet to be washed, he deceitfully kissed the Master, that he might deliver Him over unto the lawless; and being cast out from the choir of the Apostles, and having cast down the thirty pieces of silver, he knew not of Thy third-day Resurrection, whereby have mercy on us.

*Sticheron 3, Tone 2:*
Judas the betrayer, being deceitful, with a deceitful kiss betrayed the Savior and Lord, and sold the Master of all unto the Jews as a slave; like a sheep unto the slaughter, so followed the Lamb of God, the Son of the Father, the only greatly merciful.

*Sticheron 4, Tone 2:*
Judas was revealed by his deeds to be a slave and a deceiver, a disciple and a plotter, a friend and a devil; for he followed the Teacher, and meditated on His betrayal, saying within himself: I will deliver Him up...

*At Vespers*

...and acquire the gathered possessions; and he sought that the myrrh also should be sold, and Jesus seized by craft; he gave a kiss, he betrayed Christ, and like a sheep unto the slaughter, thus followed [the Lamb of God], the only tenderhearted Lover of mankind.

*Sticheron 5, Tone 2:*
He Whom Isaiah proclaimed as a Lamb comes unto His voluntary slaughter, and gives His back unto stripes, and His cheeks unto buffetings, and turned not away His face from the shame of spittings, and is condemned unto a shameful death; all these things the Sinless One voluntarily accepts, that He may grant unto all the resurrection from the dead.

*Glory... Both now... Tone 6:*
Truly is Judas an offspring of vipers, who ate the manna in the wilderness and murmured against the Nourisher; for while the meat was yet in their mouths, the ungrateful ones spoke slander against God; and this wicked man, bearing the Heavenly Bread in his mouth, wrought the betrayal against the Savior. O insatiable disposition, and inhuman daring! He sells Him Who nourishes him, and the Master Whom he loved he delivered over unto death; truly is he an iniquitous son of those men, and with them he inherited perdition; but spare our souls, O Lord, from such inhumanity, O Thou Who alone art ineffable in long-suffering.[^160]

*Entrance with the Gospel.*
*O Joyful Light:*

*Prokeimenon, Tone 1: Deliver me, O Lord, from the evil man; from the unrighteous man rescue me.*
*Verse: Who have imagined unrighteousness in their heart all the day.* [LXX Ps. 139:2, 3]

*Reading from Exodus:* [Ex. 19:10–19 LXX][^161]
The Lord said unto Moses: Go down and testify unto the people, and sanctify them today and tomorrow, and let them wash their clothes. And let them be ready against the third day; for on the third day the Lord will come down upon Mount Sinai in the sight of all the people. And thou shalt set bounds unto the people round about, saying: Take heed to yourselves, that ye go not up into the mountain, or touch any part of it; whosoever touches the mountain shall surely die. There shall not a hand touch it, for he shall be stoned with stones, or shot through with a dart; whether it be beast or man, it shall not live; when the voices and the trumpets and the cloud depart from the mount, they shall come up to the mountain. And Moses went down from the mountain unto the people, and sanctified them; and they washed their clothes. And he said unto the people: Be ready; for three days come not near unto your wives. And it came to pass on the third day in the morning, that there were voices and lightnings, and a dark cloud upon Mount Sinai, and the voice of the trumpet sounded exceeding loud, and all the people that was in the camp trembled. And Moses brought forth the people out of the camp to meet God, and they stood under the mountain. And Mount Sinai was altogether on a smoke, because God descended upon it in fire; and the smoke thereof ascended as the smoke of a furnace, and all the people were greatly amazed. And the voices of the trumpet sounded louder and louder; Moses spake, and God answered him by a voice.

*Prokeimenon, Tone 7: Deliver me from mine enemies, O God, and from them that rise up against me rescue me.*
*Verse: Rescue me from them that work iniquity.* [LXX Ps. 58:2, 3]

*Reading from Job:* [Job 38:1–23; 42:1–5 LXX][^162]
The Lord said unto Job through whirlwind and clouds: Who is this that hids counsel from Me, and keeps words in his heart, and thinks to conceal them from Me? Gird up now thy loins like a man; I will question thee, and do thou answer Me. Where wast thou when I laid the foundations of the earth? Declare unto Me, if thou hast understanding: Who determined the measures thereof, if thou knowest? Or...

*On Holy and Great Thursday*

...who stretched the line upon it? Whereupon are the foundations thereof fastened? Or who is he that laid the cornerstone thereof, when the stars were made, and all My angels praised Me with a loud voice? And I shut up the sea with gates, when it burst forth, issuing out of its mother's womb; and I placed a cloud for the garment thereof, and wrapped it in mist; and I set bounds unto it, and encompassed it with bars and doors, and said unto it: Hitherto shalt thou come, and shalt not pass, but here shall thy waves be broken within thee! Or in thy time did I order the morning light, and did the morning star know his appointed place, to take hold of the wings of the earth, to shake the wicked out of it? Or didst thou, taking clay from the earth, fashion a living creature, and place this speaking being upon the earth? Hast thou withheld the light from the wicked, and crushed the arm of the proud? Hast thou come unto the springs of the sea, or walked in the traces of the deep? Are the gates of death opened unto thee in fear, and did the gatekeepers of Hades tremble when they saw thee? Hast thou been instructed in the breadth of that which is under heaven? Tell Me now, how great is it? In what land doth the light dwell, and what is the place of darkness? If thou canst bring Me unto their borders, and if thou knowest their paths: I know then that thou wast born at that time, and the number of thy years is great! Hast thou entered into the treasuries of snow, and hast thou seen the treasuries of hail, which are reserved for thee against the time of enemies, against the day of wars and battle?

Then Job answered and said unto the Lord: I know that Thou canst do all things, and that nothing is impossible with Thee. Who is he that hids counsel from Thee, sparing words, and thinks to conceal them from Thee? And who shall declare unto me things which I knew not, great and marvelous things which I understood not? Hear me, O Lord, that I also may speak; I will ask of Thee, and do Thou teach me. By the hearing of the ear I heard of Thee before, but now mine eye hath seen Thee.

*Reading of the Prophecy of Isaiah:* [Isa. 50:4–11 LXX][^163]
The Lord gives me the tongue of instruction, that I may know when it is seasonable to speak a word; He appointed me morning by morning, He added unto me an ear to hear, and the instruction of the Lord opens mine ears; and I resist not, nor do I gainsay. I gave My back unto stripes, and My cheeks unto buffetings; and I turned not away My face from the shame of spittings. And the Lord, the Lord was My helper; therefore was I not ashamed, but I set My face as a steadfast stone, and I knew that I should not be put to shame. For He that justifies Me draws near; who will contend with Me? Let him oppose Me together: and who will judge Me? Let him draw near unto Me. Behold, the Lord, the Lord will help Me: who will harm Me? Behold, all of you shall grow old like a garment, and the moth shall consume you. Who among you fears the Lord? Let him give ear unto the voice of His Servant: ye that walk in darkness, and have no light, trust in the name of the Lord, and stay yourselves upon God. Behold, all of you kindle a fire, and strengthen the flame: walk ye by the light of your fire, and by the flame which ye have kindled; for My sake have these things come upon you, in sorrow shall ye sleep.

*Then, the Little Litany. The Trisagion.*[^164]

*Prokeimenon of the Epistle, Tone 7: The princes of the peoples assembled together against the Lord, and against His Christ.*
*Verse: Why did the nations rage, and the peoples meditate on vain things?* [LXX Ps. 2:2, 1] *Vespers*

*The Epistle to the Corinthians, the First Epistle, section 149 [1 Cor. 11:23–32]:*[^165]
Brethren, I received of the Lord that which also I delivered unto you, that the Lord Jesus in the night in which He was betrayed took bread, and having given thanks He brake it, and said: "Take, eat; this is My Body, which is broken for you; this do in remembrance of Me." Likewise also the cup, after supper, saying: "This cup is the new testament in My Blood; this do, as often as ye drink it, in remembrance of Me." For as often as ye eat this Bread, and drink this Cup, ye do proclaim the Lord's death, till He come. Therefore whosoever shall eat this Bread, or drink the Cup of the Lord unworthily, shall be guilty of the Body and Blood of the Lord. But let a man examine himself, and so let him eat of the Bread, and drink of the Cup. For he that eats and drinks unworthily, eats and drinks judgment unto himself, not discerning the Lord's Body. For this cause many are weak and sickly among you, and rather many sleep. For if we would judge ourselves, we should not be judged. But when we are judged, we are chastened of the Lord, that we should not be condemned with the world.

*Alleluia, Tone 6.*
*Blessed is he that considers the poor and needy; in the evil day the Lord will deliver him.* [Ps. 40:2 LXX]
*Verse: Mine enemies have spoken evil against me: When will he die, and his name perish?* [Ps. 40:6 LXX]
*Verse: He that ate of my bread hath lifted up his heel against me.* [Ps. 40:10 LXX]

*The Gospel according to Matthew, section 107 [Matt. 26:1–20]:*
The Lord said unto His disciples: "Ye know that after two days is the Passover..."

*And according to order, the Divine Liturgy of Saint Basil the Great.*
*And instead of the Cherubic Hymn, we sing this troparion, in Tone 6, thrice:*[^166]
Of Thy Mystical Supper, O Son of God, receive me today as a partaker; for I will not speak of the mystery unto Thine enemies, neither will I give Thee a kiss as did Judas, but like the thief do I confess Thee: Remember me, O Lord, in Thy Kingdom.

*The same is also the Communion Hymn. And instead of "Let our mouths be filled": the same we sing repeatedly, until the brethren have partaken of the Divine Mysteries.*[^167]
*After the Prayer behind the Ambo, the Foot-Washing takes place, and its order, even as the Typikon shows. Then the antidoron is given, and the Dismissal.*
*At the refectory, we partake of wine and oil.*

[Engraving: Floral vignette tailpiece.]

[Engraving: Decorative headpiece depicting angels and floral foliage.]

## THE ORDER OF THE FOOT-WASHING ON HOLY AND GREAT THURSDAY[^168]

*After the conclusion of the Prayer behind the Ambo, if the Foot-Washing is to take place, all the priests and deacons go forth unto the prepared place, the Hierarch following them. And going forth they sing Psalm 50:*
Have mercy upon me, O God, according to Thy great mercy...

*Then the Heirmos, Tone 6, Ode 5:*[^169]
Bound together by the bond of love, the Apostles, having dedicated themselves to Christ Who rules over all, had their beautiful feet cleansed, proclaiming peace unto all.

*The Hierarch reads the following troparia unto 4:*
Holding the unrestrained and lofty water in the air, bridling the abysses and restraining the seas, the Wisdom of God pours water into the basin, and the Master washes the feet of His servants.[^170]

The Master shows His disciples an example of humility; He Who clothes the heaven with clouds girds Himself with a towel and bows His knees to wash the feet of His servants, in Whose hand is the breath of all that exist.

*Then the Stichera Idiomela of the Foot-Washing are sung.*
*Tone 1:*
Girded with a towel and having washed the feet of the disciples, O Christ God: wash away from us the soul's defilement, and gird us with the spiritual bond, that we may perform Thy commandments, and praise and hymn Thy goodness.

Desiring to receive a great benefaction, O ye faithful, let us run with piety unto the honorable Foot-Washing, not washing away bodily defilement, but mystically cleansing our souls; for Christ our Savior, Who looks upon the earth and causes it to tremble, bows Himself down and touches mortal feet, granting a firm ascent over every adverse power. Unto Him let us cry aloud in thanksgiving: O Thou Who hast shown us humility as an excellent way of exaltation, save us, O Good One, as the Lover of mankind!

Peter was ashamed that his feet should be washed by the most pure Hands whereby Adam was fashioned; but having heard: "If I wash thee not, thou hast no part with Me," held fast by great trembling, he cried aloud unto Thee, O Lord: "Not my feet only shalt Thou wash, but also my hands and my head!" O the great gifts of the Master! He makes His disciples priests of grace, and promises to have a portion with them unto ineffable glory, even as He said concerning the Mystical Cup, that they should drink it new with Him in the Heavenly Kingdom; of which do Thou also account us worthy, as Thou art merciful and the Lover of mankind.

*Tone 8:*[^171]
Today He Who is unapproachable in Essence takes upon Himself the work of a servant: He Who clothes the heaven with clouds is girded with a towel; He Who divided the Red Sea pours water into the basin. And having bowed His knees...

*On Holy and Great Thursday*

...He began to wash the feet of the disciples, and to dry them with the towel wherewith He was girded. When therefore He had washed the feet of the disciples, He said unto them: "Ye are clean, but not all," signifying the one that was betraying Him.

It had been better for thee, O Judas, if thou hadst not been conceived in the womb of thy mother; it had been better for thee, O betrayer, if thou hadst not been born, alien from the Son of God![^172] For thy sake the crown of Christ's disciples was unfastened, and the crucified thief embraces the True Vine; through thee the vineyard-wall was broken down, and the lawless destroy the temple not made with hands. Oh, how thou wast ashamed of the sinful woman's myrrh as exceeding costly, yet wast not afraid to betray the blood of the Righteous One into the hands of the lawless! It had been better for thee, O betrayer, if thou hadst not been born, alien from the Son of God!

*Glory, both now.*
Held fast by the devilish slumber, Judas fell asleep unto death; it is a time to watch, and a time to be sober: let the heart groan, let the eyes shed tears, let a psalm be sung, for great is the power of the Cross! Christ is at the doors, the immolated Pascha is come: glory be to Thee, O Christ God, glory be to Thee!

*Then the Litany by the Deacon, while the Hierarch and Priests are seated:*[^173]
**Deacon:** In peace let us pray to the Lord.
**Choir:** Lord, have mercy.
**Deacon:** For the peace from above, and the salvation of our souls, let us pray to the Lord.
**Deacon:** For the peace of the whole world, the good estate of the holy churches of God, and the union of all, let us pray to the Lord.
**Deacon:** For this holy temple, and for them that with faith, reverence, and the fear of God enter therein, let us pray to the Lord.
**Deacon:** For our Most Reverend Archbishop, Metropolitan *[Name]*, and for our God-loving Bishop *[Name]*, for the honorable Priesthood, the Diaconate in Christ, for all the clergy and the people, let us pray to the Lord.
**Deacon:** For our most faithful Emperor *[Name]*, for his entire court and armed forces, let us pray to the Lord.
**Deacon:** That He may conquer and subdue under his feet every enemy and adversary, let us pray to the Lord.
**Deacon:** For this city, and for every city, countryside, and land, and for them that with faith dwell therein, let us pray to the Lord.
**Deacon:** For favorable weather, abundance of the fruits of the earth, and peaceful times, let us pray to the Lord.
**Deacon:** For them that travel by sea, land, and air, for the sick, the suffering, the captives, and for their salvation, let us pray to the Lord.
**Deacon:** That this Foot-Washing may be blessed and sanctified, by the power, operation, and descent of the Holy Spirit, let us pray to the Lord.
**Deacon:** That it may serve for the washing away of the defilement of our sins, let us pray to the Lord.
**Deacon:** That we may be delivered from all affliction, wrath, and necessity, let us pray to the Lord.
**Deacon:** Help us, save us, have mercy on us, and keep us, O God, by Thy grace.

*The Order of the Foot-Washing*

*The Hierarch rises with all.*
**Deacon:** Commemorating our all-holy, most pure, most blessed, glorious Lady, the Mother of God and Ever-Virgin Mary, with all the saints, let us commit ourselves and one another, and all our life unto Christ our God.
**Choir:** To Thee, O Lord.

*The Hierarch gives the exclamation:*
**Hierarch:** For Thou art the cleansing of our souls, and unto Thee do we send up glory: to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages.
**Choir:** Amen.

*Then he prays with a loud voice:*[^174]
**Hierarch:** O Lord and God most good, unapproachable in Thy Divinity, Who didst take upon Thyself the form of a servant in the appearance of a bondservant, and to prefigure saving humility didst wash the feet of Thy disciples with Thine undefiled hands, and didst wipe them with a towel: look down even now upon us, Thine unworthy servants, who imitate the surpassing glory of so great a condescension of Thine; and grant that we may be washed clean from the defilements of the flesh and the impurities of the soul by the touch of this water. Grant us the invisible descent of Thy Holy Spirit; establish our souls and bodies against the deceitful serpent that lies in wait for our heel, that having been made pure, we may serve Thee acceptably, treading upon serpents and scorpions, and over all the power of the enemy. For unto Thee is due all glory, honor, and worship, with Thine unoriginate Father, and Thine all-holy, good, and life-creating Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages.
**Choir:** Amen.

**Hierarch:** Peace be unto all.
**Choir:** And to thy spirit.
**Deacon:** Let us bow our heads unto the Lord.
**Choir:** To Thee, O Lord.

*The Prayer said secretly:*[^175]
**Hierarch:** O Lord our God, Who didst show us the measure of humility in Thy surpassing descent, and by calling the last to be first through voluntary choice, hast taught us to serve one another: lift us up through divine humility; preserve us undefiled, being ever washed with tears and cleansed by the enlightenment of the purifications of Thy grace, that falling down before Thee in truth at all times, we may find mercy and compassions at Thy dread judgment-seat.

*Exclamation:*
**Hierarch:** For Thou art a merciful God and the Lover of mankind, and unto Thee do we send up glory, with Thine unoriginate Father, and Thine all-holy, good, and life-creating Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages.
**Choir:** Amen.

*Then the protodeacon on the ambo says:*
**Deacon:** And that we may be accounted worthy to hear the Holy Gospel, in peace let us entreat the Lord God.
*And the deacon begins to read the First Gospel of the Foot-Washing. The Hierarch standing says:*[^176]
**Hierarch:** Wisdom! Upright, let us hear the Holy Gospel.
**Deacon:** The reading from the Holy Gospel according to John.

*On Holy and Great Thursday*

**Hierarch:** Let us attend.
*The deacon reads, while all stand:*
*[The Gospel according to John, section 44: John 13:3–11]*
**Deacon:** At that time, before the feast of the Passover, Jesus knowing that His hour was come that He should depart out of this world unto the Father, having loved His own that were in the world, He loved them unto the end. And supper having taken place, the devil having now put into the heart of Judas Iscariot, Simon's son, to betray Him; Jesus knowing that the Father had given all things into His hands, and that He came forth from God, and was going unto God, rises from supper, and lays aside His garments.
*(The Hierarch removes his vestments, the twelve being seated. And when the deacon says):*
**Deacon:** And taking a towel, He girded Himself.
*(Then the Hierarch girds himself with a linen towel, and when the deacon says):*
**Deacon:** After that He pours water into a basin:
*(The Hierarch with his own hands pours warm water into the pre-consecrated basin. And when the deacon says):*
**Deacon:** And began to wash the disciples' feet, and to wipe them with the towel wherewith He was girded.
*(This is repeated multiple times. And the Hierarch begins on the left side to wash the feet of the priests, beginning from the first, even unto the one holding the place of Peter. And he washes, falling upon his knees before each one, wiping the feet of each of those being washed, kissing them, and rising up. And when he comes unto the one holding the place of Peter, the deacon says):*[^177]
**Deacon:** Then comes He to Simon Peter; and Peter saith unto Him:
*(And the one holding the place of Peter says):*
**Representative of Peter:** Lord, dost Thou wash my feet?
*(The Deacon):*
**Deacon:** Jesus answered and said unto him:
*(The Hierarch):*
**Hierarch:** What I do thou knowest not now, but thou shalt understand hereafter.
*(The Deacon):*
**Deacon:** Peter saith unto Him:
*(And the one holding the place of Peter says):*
**Representative of Peter:** Thou shalt never wash my feet.
*(The Deacon):*
**Deacon:** Jesus answered him:
*(The Hierarch):*
**Hierarch:** If I wash thee not, thou hast no part with Me.
*(The Deacon):*
**Deacon:** Simon Peter saith unto Him:
*(And the one holding the place of Peter, having risen a little as though affrighted, and lifting up his hands, points with his right hand toward his feet, saying in a gentle voice, in the hearing of all):*
**Representative of Peter:** Lord, not my feet only,
*(and showing his hands, he says):*
**Representative of Peter:** but also my hands,
*(and then pointing to his head, having bowed it slightly, he says):*
**Representative of Peter:** and my head.
*(The Deacon):*
**Deacon:** Jesus saith unto him:
*(The Hierarch):*
**Hierarch:** He that is washed needs not save to wash his feet, but is clean every whit; and ye are clean, but not all.
*(And the one holding the place of Peter sits down, and the Hierarch washes his feet also, even as the rest. And the deacon concludes the Gospel):*
**Deacon:** For He knew who was betraying Him; therefore said He: "Ye are not all clean."

*The Hierarch, having washed all, removes the linen towel and puts on his vestments.*
*The deacon exclaims:*
**Deacon:** And that we may be accounted worthy to hear the Holy Gospel:
*And the Hierarch himself reads:*[^178]
*The Second Gospel of the Foot-Washing, from John, chapter 13, section 45 [John 13:12–17]:*
**Hierarch:** At that time: when Jesus had washed the disciples' feet, He took His garments, and sat down again, and said unto them: "Know ye what I have done unto you? Ye call Me Master and Lord, and ye say well; for so I am. If I then, the Lord and Master, have washed your feet, ye also ought to wash one another's feet. For I have given you an example, that ye should do as I have done unto you. Truly, truly, I say unto you, the servant is not greater than his Lord; neither he that is sent greater than He that sent him. If ye know these things, happy are ye if ye do them."

**Deacon:** Let us pray to the Lord.
**Choir:** Lord, have mercy.

*The Order of the Foot-Washing on Holy and Great Thursday*

*The Hierarch reads this prayer:*[^179]
**Hierarch:** O Lord our God, Who according to Thy great mercy didst pour out Thyself and take the form of a servant, Who at the time of Thy saving, life-creating, and voluntary Passions didst deign to sup with Thy holy disciples and Apostles, and thereafter, girding Thyself with a towel, didst wash the feet of Thy holy disciples, giving them an example of humility and of mutual love, and saying: "As I have done unto you, so do ye also unto one another": do Thou Thyself even now, O Master, having come into the midst of Thine unworthy servants who follow Thine example, wash away every defilement and impurity from our souls, that having washed away the dust of sins that hath cleaved unto us, and wiping one another with the towel of love, we may be enabled to please Thee all the days of our life, and find grace before Thee.

*Exclamation:*
**Hierarch:** For Thou art He that blesses and sanctifies all things, O Christ our God, and unto Thee do we send up glory, with Thine unoriginate Father, and Thine all-holy, good, and life-creating Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages.
**Choir:** Amen.

*After this, the brethren are sprinkled, while chanting the troparion, Tone 6:*
Having washed their feet, and having been pre-purified by the communion of Thy divine Mystery, O Christ, the ministers went up with Thee from Sion unto the great Mount of Olives, singing unto Thee the Lover of mankind.

*Then:*
*Blessed be the name of the Lord, thrice.*
*We sing Psalm 33:*
*I will bless the Lord at all times, unto the end.*

*The Dismissal:*
May He Who through His surpassing goodness showed us the better way through humility in the washing of the disciples' feet, and at His Mystical Supper gave us His Flesh to eat and His true Blood to drink for our salvation, and descended even unto the Cross and the tomb, Christ our true God, through the intercessions of His most pure Mother, of the holy, glorious, and all-laudable Apostles, of our father among the saints Basil the Great, Archbishop of Caesarea in Cappadocia, and of all the saints, have mercy on us and save us, for He is good and the Lover of mankind.

[Engraving: Medallion with cross and Greek letters Alpha and Omega: A + Ω.]

*On Holy and Great Thursday at Compline*

[Engraving: Decorative headpiece vignette with floral ornament.]

*On the same Holy and Great Thursday at Compline, we say Small Compline, whereat we sing the Tri-ode.*[^180]
*The work of master Andrew of Crete.*

*Tone 8. Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Dispel the gloom of my soul, O Light-giver Christ God, having driven away the primordial darkness of the deep, and grant me the light of Thy commandments, O Word, that waking early I may glorify Thee.

*Troparia:*
The evening was adorned, and the Passover was prepared for Thee, even as Thou didst say, O Christ; but Judas was meditating Thy betrayal, and while being with Thee, was agreeing upon thirty pieces of silver through avarice, outside of Thy worth.

Christ rises voluntarily from supper, and is girded about the loins with a towel, and bows His neck, while Peter cries aloud: "Thou shalt never wash my feet, O my Creator! but nevertheless wash them."

The deceitful disciple Judas took the Bread in his hands for betrayal, wherewith also he accomplished the betrayal against Thee; he stretched out the feet which Thou Thyself hadst washed, and hadst wiped with the towel.

With a deceitful kiss Judas then opened his mouth unto Thee, wherewith he unworthily dipped Thy Body, O Word, crying unto Thee: "Hail, Master!", being a kisser and a betrayer, a servant and a deceiver.

Peter, as he then beheld what was taking place, being seized with terror, was convicted by the simple question of a servant-maid: that he had denied Thee the Lord, not only as Thou hadst spoken, but even as Thou didst know, O Thou Who knowest all things.

The Fashioner is struck with blows, and creation is struck with His reproach; He is smitten with a reed willingly, and the things on high bow down; the Judge is spat upon, and all the foundations of the earth are shaken.

*Glory:*
God, Who adorned the whole earth with flowers, is crowned with thorns, and receives wounds, and endures reproach with long-suffering, and wears the purple robe of mockery; and being God, He endures all things and suffers in His flesh.

*Both now:*
Let John cry aloud, declaring Thy incarnation with divine teachings: The Word was made flesh from the Virgin without change, and remained God by nature even as He was, not departing from His Father's bosom.

*On Holy and Great Thursday*

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
He Who covers His chambers with waters, Who sets the sand as a bound unto the sea, and Who upholds all things: Thee the sun sings, Thee the moon glorifies, unto Thee all creation brings a hymn, as unto the Creator of all unto ages of ages.

*Troparia:*
O Jesus, Who clothest the heaven with clouds, reigning on the throne of glory with the incorrupt Father: Thou, taking a towel, didst gird Thyself therewith to wash the earthly feet, being Thyself all Fire, O Word, though Thou wast incarnate!

When Jesus had washed all, having sat down, He says unto His disciples: "Know ye all what I have done now? For I have given an example of humility unto all of you: that whosoever desires to be first, let him be the last of all by his own choice."

"Ye are clean indeed, but not all of you," Christ said unto His friends reclining at supper; and they, not knowing the word one from another, said: "Is it I?" Therefore after these things He openly declares the name of the betrayer.

When the Judge of all had spoken these things, having come unto the Mount of Olives with the disciples, He then said: "Come and let us depart hence, for the betrayer is already at hand; let no one deny Me, for I suffer voluntarily."

O the deceitful kiss! "Hail, Master!", Judas says unto Christ, and along with the word He delivers Him unto the slaughter; for this sign had He given unto the lawless: "Whomsoever I shall kiss, that same is He Whom I promised to deliver unto you."

Having been seized by lawless men, O our God, and not gainsaying at all, nor crying aloud, O Lamb of God, Thou didst endure all things: to be examined, and to be judged, and to be beaten, to be bound and to be led with weapons and staves unto Caiaphas.

"Let Jesus Christ be crucified!", the Hebrew people cried aloud with the priests and scribes. O faithless people! What evil hath He done Who appeared, Who raised Lazarus from the tomb, and made a way for men unto salvation?

While Pilate sat in judgment, the lawless people, shouting, cried aloud: "Crucify this man, and release unto us Barabbas the murderer who is bound; but having first scourged Christ, take Him away, take Him away, crucify Him with the malefactors!"

*Let us bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord.*
O ineffable emptying! O unutterable counsel! Being Thyself Fire, Thou didst wash the feet of the betrayer, O Savior; and having washed them, Thou didst not consume them, and giving him Bread at the Supper, Thou didst instruct him in the mystical Service!

*Both now, Theotokion:*
O strange report! God is the Son of a woman, and a seedless Childbearing, an unwedded Mother, and He that is born is God! O fearful report! O marvelous Conception! O incorrupt Birthgiving of the Virgin! truly are all things beyond the mind, and higher than understanding!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Blessed be the Lord God of Israel, Who hath raised up a horn of salvation for us in the house of His servant David; wherein the Dayspring from on high hath visited us, and hath guided our feet into the way of peace.

*Troparion:*
"Sleep ye again?", Christ said unto the disciples: "Watch, the hour is at hand; arise now, let us be going, My friends; behold, the disciple who is betrayer, having the whole cohort, comes to deliver Me unto the murderers!"

*At Compline*

*Troparia:*
Thy kiss was deceitful, and bitter thine embrace: to whom dost thou cry, deceiver? For when saying: "Hail, Rabbi!", Christ said unto Judas: "Friend, for what purpose art thou come?"—saying: "For if thou art come to kiss, why dost thou offer a knife smeared with honey?"

While Pilate sat in judgment, O guiltless Judge, Thou didst come willingly to stand before him, O Christ, and to deliver us from our debts; therefore didst Thou endure to be wounded in the flesh, O Good One, that we all might receive freedom.

O abyss of compassions! How doth the Fire stand before Pilate, who is grass and reed and earth, whom the Fire of Christ's Divinity did not consume, but waited with long-suffering, being free by nature, as the Lover of mankind!

"Take Him away, take Him away, crucify Him who is called Christ!", the Jews once cried out unto Pilate; and he washed his hands, and wrote their accusation against Him with a reed, which grants immortality unto all.

Crying aloud, the lawless, pressing upon Pilate, said: "Take Him away, take Him away, crucify Christ!", asking to put Him to death as one condemned: Did not This Man raise the dead, cleanse lepers, heal the woman with an issue of blood, and strengthen paralytics?

"For what evil hath He done, that ye cry out so greatly: 'Take Him away, take Him away, crucify Him!'?", Pilate once cried unto the senseless people: "I find no cause in Him!" But they bitterly cried: "Take Him away, take Him away, crucify the Savior of all!"

O lawless Jews! O senseless people! Will ye not remember any of Christ's miracles, the multitude of healings? Nay, do ye not perceive all His divine power, even as your first fathers did not, and now do ye not understand?

*Glory:*
Having been beaten, O my Creator, Thou wast delivered up to be crucified for my sake, that having wrought my salvation in the midst of the earth, Thou mightest pour forth life unto the world, and make immortal by Thy precious Blood those who worship Thee.

*Both now, Theotokion:*
Thy Lamb, O Master, standing by the Cross, and weeping for Thee the Creator of all, beheld Thy long-suffering; for willingly wast Thou born in the flesh, and didst endure all the Sufferings therein, that Thou mightest save the world.

[Engraving: The Mocking, Scourging, and Crowning with Thorns of the Lord Jesus Christ. Christ is bound, clothed in the purple robe of mockery and holding a reed in His hands, surrounded by mocking soldiers and scribes.][^181]

*On Holy and Great Friday*

[Engraving: Decorative headpiece vignette with floral ornament.]

## THE OFFICE OF THE HOLY AND SAVING PASSIONS OF OUR LORD JESUS CHRIST[^182]

*On Holy and Great Thursday evening, at the 2nd hour of the night [approx. 8:00 PM], the semantron is sounded. And when we have assembled in the temple, the priest having given the blessing, we sing the beginning according to custom, even as was indicated on Thursday of the 5th week of Great Lent. Then the Six Psalms. After this, the Great Litany, and after the exclamation we sing:*
*Alleluia, in Tone 8. And the Troparion:*[^183]
When the glorious disciples were enlightened at the washing of the Supper, then Judas the ungodly was darkened, ailing with avarice, and unto lawless judges he delivers Thee, the righteous Judge. Behold, O lover of possessions, him that because of them made use of the halter! Flee from the insatiable soul that dared such things against the Master! O Thou Who art good toward all, Lord, glory be to Thee!
*Thrice, slowly and with sweet melody.*

*Then, the Little Litany. And candles are distributed unto the brethren by the ecclesiarch. And the priest says:*
**Priest:** And that we may be accounted worthy...
*And he reads:*
*The First Gospel:*[^184]
*From John, the Testament of the Holy Passions, section 46 [John 13:31–18:1]:*
**Priest:** The Lord said unto His disciples: "Now is the Son of man glorified..."
*The end is:* "...and His disciples."

*And when the Gospel is finished, we sing:*
**Choir:** Glory to Thy long-suffering, O Lord!

*And immediately we sing the Antiphons, repeating all the troparia.*
*Antiphon 1, Tone 8:*[^185]
The rulers of the peoples took counsel together against the Lord, and against His Christ.
*The same.*
A lawless word have they laid against Me: O Lord, Lord, forsake Me not!
Let us present our senses pure unto Christ, and as His friends let us sacrifice our souls for His sake, and let us not be weighed down with earthly cares...

...choked like Judas, but let us cry out in our inner chambers: Our Father Who art in the heavens, deliver us from the evil one.

*Glory, Theotokion:*
A Virgin didst thou bear, knowing not wedlock, and a Virgin didst thou remain, O unwedded Mother, Mary Theotokos: pray Christ our God, that we may be saved.

*Both now, the same.*

*Antiphon 2, Tone 6:*
Judas ran saying unto the lawless scribes: "What will ye give me, and I will deliver Him unto you?" And in the midst of them that took counsel, Thou Thyself didst stand unseen, being counselled against: O Knower of hearts, spare our souls!

*Troparion:*
In mercy let us serve God, even as Mary did at the supper, and let us not acquire avarice, as did Judas: that we may ever be with Christ our God.

*Troparion:*
To show mercy unto Adam, as the Immortal One Thou hast come even unto death, O My Son, yet the Jews have no pity upon my childlessness; but O Master, having arisen, O Word, have mercy upon us.

*Glory, Theotokion:*
Him Whom thou didst bear ineffably, O Virgin, cease not ever to entreat as the Lover of mankind, that He may save from perils all who flee unto thee.

*Both now, the same.*

*Antiphon 3, Tone 2:*
Because of the raising of Lazarus, O Lord, the Hebrew children cried Hosanna unto Thee, O Lover of mankind; but lawless Judas would not understand.

*Troparion:*
At Thy Supper, O Christ God, Thou didst foretell unto Thy disciples: "One of you shall betray Me"; but lawless Judas would not understand.

*Troparion:*
When John asked: "Lord, who is he that betrays Thee?", Thou didst point him out by the bread; but lawless Judas would not understand.

*Antiphon 3 (continued):*
*Troparion:*
For thirty pieces of silver, O Lord, and with a deceitful kiss, did the Jews seek to kill Thee; but lawless Judas would not understand.

*Troparion:*
At Thy Foot-Washing, O Christ God, Thou didst command Thy disciples: "Do ye thus, even as ye see"; but lawless Judas would not understand.

*Troparion:*
"Watch and pray, that ye enter not into temptation," didst Thou say unto Thy disciples, O Christ our God; but lawless Judas would not understand.

*Troparion:*
Weeping, O Christ, She that bare Thee foresaw the words which of old Simeon cried aloud unto the end; but the lawless people would not understand Thee.

*Glory, Theotokion:*
Save Thy servants from perils, O Theotokos, for after God we all flee unto thee, as unto an unbreakable wall and protection.

*Both now, the same.*

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 7:*[^186]
*(We do not sit at such sessional hymns, because the priest censes the holy altar, but standing we sing them.)*
Feeding the disciples at the Supper, and knowing the pretense of betrayal, thereon Thou didst convict Judas, knowing him indeed to be incorrigible, yet desiring to make known unto all that willingly Thou wast betrayed, that Thou mightest snatch the world from the alien: O Long-suffering One, glory be to Thee!

*Glory: the conclusion.*
*Both now: the whole hymn.*

*Then:*
**Priest:** And that we may be accounted worthy...
*And the priest reads:*
*The Second Gospel:*[^187]
*From John, section 58 [John 18:1–28]:*
**Priest:** At that time, Jesus went forth with His disciples...
*The end is:* "...but that they might eat the passover."

*After the Gospel we sing:*
*Antiphon 4, Tone 5:*
Today Judas forsakes the Master, and receives the devil; he is blinded by the passion of avarice, he falls away from the light, darkened: for how could he behold the Light, who sold the Luminary for thirty pieces of silver? But unto us hath shone forth He that suffered for the world, unto Whom let us cry aloud: O Thou Who didst suffer and have compassion with mankind, O Lord, glory be to Thee!

*Troparion:*
Today Judas feigns godliness, and is estranged from the gift of grace; being a disciple, he becomes a betrayer; in the customary kiss he conceals deceit, and prefers before the Master's love foolishly to be in bondage unto avarice, having become the leader of the lawless assembly: but we, having the salvation of Christ, Him let us glorify.

*Tone 1:*
*Troparion:*
Let us acquire brotherly love as brethren in Christ, and not unmercifulness toward our neighbors: that we be not condemned as the unmerciful servant on account of the pence, and having repented like Judas, profit nothing.

*Glory, Theotokion:*
Most glorious things were spoken of thee everywhere, in that thou didst bear in the flesh the Creator of all, O Mary Theotokos, all-praised and unwedded.

*Both now, the same.*

*Antiphon 5, Tone 6:*
The disciple agreed upon the price of the Master, and for thirty pieces of silver sold the Lord, with a deceitful kiss delivering Him unto the lawless for death.

*Troparion:*
Today the Creator of heaven and earth said unto His disciples: "The hour hath drawn near, and Judas that betrays Me is at hand; let no one deny Me, beholding Me on the Cross between two thieves: for I suffer as man, and I save as the Lover of mankind them that believe in Me."

*Antiphon 5 (continued):*
*Troparion:*
Today the Pure One, beholding the Creator of creation separated from His disciples and held alone by the Jews, cried out maternally unto herself, pondering the things of old: "What a strange wonder! O how hast Thou forsaken me, O Son, Who formerly didst preserve me pure!"

*Glory, Theotokion:*
O thou who ineffably in the last times didst conceive and bear thy Creator: Him entreat, that our souls may be saved.

*Both now, the same.*

*Antiphon 6, Tone 7:*
Today Judas watches to betray the Lord, the pre-eternal Savior of the world, Who with five loaves satisfied the multitude; today the lawless one renounces the Master; having been a disciple, he betrayed the Sovereign Lord: for money he sold Him Who satisfied man with manna.

*Troparion:*
Today the Jews nailed unto the Cross the Lord Who parted the sea with a rod and led them through the wilderness; today with a lance they pierced His side, Who for their sake smote Egypt with plagues; and with gall they gave Him to drink, Who rained down manna as food for them.

*Troparion:*
O Lord, coming unto Thy voluntary Passion, Thou didst cry aloud unto Thy disciples: "If ye were not able to watch with Me even one hour, how did ye promise to die for My sake? At least behold Judas, how he sleeps not, but hastens to betray Me unto the lawless; arise, pray, lest anyone deny Me, beholding Me on the Cross: O Long-suffering One, glory be to Thee!"

*Troparion:*
O Lord, She that bare Thee, pondering Thine ineffable Nativity, cried aloud unto Thy crucifiers: "Even if the Bodiless One became incarnate, yet He is not changed from what He was: why then have ye come to kill Life? Even if ye lift Him up upon the Tree, He will give life; and if ye send Him into the tomb, He will raise the dead. O Long-suffering One, arise, show Thy Resurrection, O Thou Who hast raised us, Lover of mankind, glory be to Thee!"

*Glory, Theotokion:*
Rejoice, O Theotokos, who didst contain in thy womb Him Whom the heavens cannot contain; rejoice, O Virgin, the proclamation of the prophets, through whom Emmanuel hath shone forth unto us; rejoice, Mother of Christ God.

*Both now, the same.*

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 7:*[^188]
What manner of reason, O Judas, made thee a betrayer of the Savior? Did He separate thee from the choir of the Apostles? Did He deprive thee of the gift of healings? When supping with them, did He cast thee away from the table? When washing the feet of the others, did He overlook thine? O of how many good things hast thou been unmindful! And thine ungrateful character is indeed rebuked, while His boundless long-suffering and great mercy are proclaimed.

*Glory, both now, the same.*

*The Third Gospel:*[^189]
*From Matthew, section 109 [Matt. 26:57–75]:*
**Priest:** At that time, the soldiers having taken Jesus led Him away unto Caiaphas the high priest...
*The end is:* "...and he wept bitterly."

*Antiphon 7, Tone 8:*
When the lawless took Thee, enduring it, Thou didst cry out thus, O Lord: "Though ye have smitten the Shepherd, and scattered the twelve sheep, My disciples, I could present more than twelve legions of angels; but I suffer long, that the obscure and secret things may be fulfilled, which I showed unto you by My prophets: O Lord, glory be to Thee!"

*Troparion:*
Having thrice denied Thee, Peter immediately understood that which was spoken unto him, but he brought unto Thee tears of repentance: O God, cleanse me and save me!

*Troparion:*
She who transcended the laws of nature understood not the manner of the Crucifixion; therefore she brought unto Thee tears of love, saying: "O God, arise now, and save me!"

*Glory, Theotokion:*
As the saving gate, and fair paradise, and cloud of the co-eternal Light, let us all hymn the holy Virgin, crying unto her: Rejoice!

*Both now, the same.*

*Antiphon 8, Tone 2:*
Say, ye lawless ones, what heard ye from our Savior? Did He not set forth the Law and the prophetic teachings? How then did ye take counsel to deliver unto Pilate God the Word from God, and the Redeemer of our souls?

*Troparion:*
"Let Him be crucified!" cried they that ever enjoyed Thy gifts, and they asked to receive a malefactor instead of their Benefactor, those murderers of the righteous; but Thou didst keep silence, O Christ, enduring their insolence, desiring to suffer and to save us, as the Lover of mankind.

*Troparion:*
Beholding Thee crucified, the Pure One, Who bare Thee from her all-pure womb, seized with maternal pangs, asked for the joy of Thy Resurrection, crying aloud unto Thee: "Destroy the handwriting of Adam, O Thou Who willest to suffer, and save us, as the Lover of mankind!"

*Glory, Theotokion:*
Seeing that we have no boldness because of our manifold sins, do thou entreat Him that was born of thee, O Virgin Theotokos; for a Mother's supplication avails much unto the good pleasure of the Master; overlook not the prayers of sinners, O All-pure One, for He is merciful, and able to save, Who also was pleased to suffer for our sake.

*Both now, the same.*

*Antiphon 9, Tone 3:*
They weighed thirty pieces of silver, the price of Him that was priced, on Whom they of the sons of Israel set a price: watch and pray, that ye enter not into temptation; the spirit indeed is willing, but the flesh is weak; therefore watch!

*Troparion:*
They gave Me gall for My meat, and in My thirst they gave Me vinegar to drink; but do Thou, O Lord, raise Me up, and I will requite them. [Ps. 68:22, 40:11 LXX]

*Troparion:*
The Pure One was dismayed, beholding Thy voluntary nailing unto the Cross, and cried aloud: "O Christ, merciful One, have compassion on Thy flock!"

*Glory, Theotokion:*
We from the Gentiles hymn thee, O pure Theotokos, for thou didst bear Christ our God, Who through thee hath delivered mankind from the curse.

*Both now, the same.*

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8:*[^190]
O how Judas, formerly Thy disciple, meditated betrayal against Thee, supping deceitfully, a plotter and unrighteous man! He went and said unto the priests: "What will ye give me, and I will deliver unto you Him that hath broken the Law and defiled the Sabbath?" O Long-suffering Lord, glory be to Thee!

*Glory, both now, the same.*

*The Fourth Gospel:*[^191]
*From John, section 54 [John 18:28–19:16]:*
**Priest:** At that time, they led Jesus from Caiaphas unto the praetorium...
*The end is:* "...and he delivered Him unto them to be crucified."

*Antiphon 10, Tone 6:*
He that covers Himself with light as with a garment, stood naked at the judgment, and received blows upon the cheek from the hands which He had fashioned; and the lawless people nailed the Lord of glory unto the Cross: then was the veil of the temple rent in twain, the sun was darkened, unable to endure the sight of God outraged, before Whom all creation trembles: unto Him let us bow down!

*Troparion:*
The disciple denied Thee, the thief cried aloud: "Remember me, O Lord, in Thy Kingdom!"

*Troparion:*
She that bare Thee without passion entreats Thee for us: remember, O Lover of mankind, them that hymn Thee in faith.

*Glory, both now, Theotokion:*
O Virgin, who didst receive joy from the angel and didst bear thine own Creator: save them that magnify thee.

*[From the Greek:]*[^192]
Pacify the world, O Lord, Who from the Virgin didst deign to bear flesh for Thy servants, that in concord we may hymn Thy glory, O Lover of mankind.

*Antiphon 11, Tone 6:*
For the good things which Thou didst do, O Christ, unto the Hebrew race, they condemned Thee to be crucified, having given Thee vinegar and gall to drink; but give them, O Lord, according to their works, for they understood not Thy condescension.

*Antiphon 11 (continued):*
*Troparion:*
The Hebrew tribes were not satisfied with the betrayal, O Christ, but wagged their heads, bringing blasphemy and mockery; but give them, O Lord, according to their works, for they understood not Thy dispensation.

*Troparion:*
Neither that the earth was shaken, nor that the rocks were rent did persuade the Hebrews, neither the veil of the temple, nor the resurrection of the dead; but give them, O Lord, according to their works, for vainly did they meditate against Thee.

*Troparion:*
Beholding the Giver of life and Ruler over death lifeless and dead upon the Tree, the Mother cried aloud: "Behold how creation grievs with me, O Son! But shine forth from the dead, O unwaning Light, Jesus, for Thou art the Father's Radiance!"

*Glory, Theotokion:*
We have known God Who was incarnate of thee, O Virgin Theotokos, thou only pure, only blessed one: therefore unceasingly hymning thee do we magnify thee.

*Both now, the same.*

*Antiphon 12, Tone 8:*
Thus saith the Lord unto the Jews: "O My people, what have I done unto you, or wherein have I wearied you? I gave light unto your blind, I cleansed your lepers, I raised up the man that was upon his bed: O My people, what have I done unto you, and what have ye repaid Me? For manna, gall; for water, vinegar; instead of loving Me, ye nailed Me unto the Cross: no longer will I endure it, I will call My Gentiles, and they shall glorify Me with the Father and the Spirit, and I will grant them life eternal."

*Troparion:*
Today the veil of the temple is rent in twain for the conviction of the lawless, and the sun hids its rays, beholding the Master crucified.

*Troparion:*
Ye lawgivers of Israel, Jews and Pharisees, the apostolic choir cries aloud unto you: "Behold the Temple which ye destroyed; behold the Lamb Whom ye crucified, and delivered unto the tomb; but by His own power He is risen! Deceive not yourselves, O Jews, for He it is that saved in the sea, and fed in the wilderness: He is the Life, and the Light, and the Peace of the world."

*Troparion:*
Unto the lawless, in like manner, whose destruction is all-ruinous, the Virgin and Mother cries aloud unto you: "Behold God Who took flesh, behold the earthly One Who deified the nature subject to the Law, accepts the Passion, by His power moving creation: the rocks are rent, and the earth is shaken, He rends the vesture of the temple, the sun is dismayed at the audacity: be ye ashamed, O rebellious ones, and understand Him Who fed you!"

*Glory, Theotokion:*
Rejoice, O Gate of the King of glory, which the Most High alone passed through, and left sealed again, unto the salvation of our souls.

*Both now, the same.*

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8:*[^193]
When Thou didst stand before Caiaphas, O God, and wast delivered unto Pilate, O Judge, the heavenly Powers were shaken with fear; and when Thou wast lifted up upon the Tree between two thieves, Thou wast numbered with the lawless, O Sinless One, in order to save mankind: O Guileless Lord, glory be to Thee!

*Glory, both now, the same.*

*The Fifth Gospel:*[^194]
*From Matthew, section 111 [Matt. 27:3–32]:*
**Priest:** At that time, Judas who had betrayed Him, seeing that Jesus was condemned...
*The end is:* "...to bear His Cross."

*Antiphon 13, Tone 6:*
The assembly of the Jews asked of Pilate that they might crucify Thee, O Lord; for having found no guilt in Thee, they released Barabbas who was guilty, and condemned Thee the Righteous One, inheriting the sin of foul murder; but give them, O Lord, their recompense, for vainly did they meditate against Thee.

*Troparion:*
Christ, the Power of God and the Wisdom of God, before Whom all things are dismayed and tremble, and Whom every tongue doth praise, did the priests strike upon the cheek, and they gave Him gall; yet He was pleased to suffer all things, desiring to save us from our iniquities by His own Blood, as the Lover of mankind.

*Troparion:*
"O Giver of the Law and the Prophets, how dost Thou stand willingly as one judged under them that are subject to the Law, O Son and beginingless God? And how shall the realms of Hades receive Thee, Who holdest all things in Thy hand?" said the Pure One, beholding the Immortal One hanging by His own will.

*Glory, Theotokion:*
O Theotokos, who by the word beyond reason didst bear thine own Creator: Him entreat, that our souls may be saved.

*Both now, the same.*

*Antiphon 14, Tone 8:*
O Lord, Who didst receive as companion the thief who had defiled his hands in blood: number us also with him, as Thou art good and the Lover of mankind.

*Antiphon 14 (continued):*
*Troparion:*
A small voice did the thief utter upon the cross, yet great faith did he find; in a single moment he was saved, and being the first to open the gates of paradise, he entered in: O Thou Who didst accept his repentance, O Lord, glory be to Thee!

*Troparion:*
No small pang had the Pure One, standing by the Cross, and seeing the audacity of murderous destruction, and with great weeping, terribly lamenting she said: "O Lord Who knowest the infirmity of the earthly, glory be to Thee!"

*Glory, Theotokion:*
Rejoice, thou who by the angel didst receive the joy of the world; rejoice, thou who didst bear thy Creator and Lord; rejoice, thou who wast accounted worthy to be the Mother of God.

*Both now, the same.*

*Antiphon 15, Tone 6:*[^195]
Today He is suspended upon the Tree Who suspended the earth upon the waters; with a crown of thorns is He invested Who is the King of angels; in a mockery of purple is He arrayed Who decks the heaven with clouds; blows upon the face did He receive Who in the Jordan set Adam free; with nails was He pierced Who is the Bridegroom of the Church; with a lance was He pierced Who is the Virgin's Son. We worship Thy Sufferings, O Christ! We worship Thy Sufferings, O Christ! We worship Thy Sufferings, O Christ! Show us also Thy glorious Resurrection!

*Troparion:*
Not as the Jews let us keep festival; for Christ our Passover hath been sacrificed for us: but let us cleanse ourselves from every defilement, and purely let us pray unto Him: Arise, O Lord, and save us, as the Lover of mankind!

*Troparion:*
Thy Cross, O Lord, is life and defense unto Thy people, and trusting therein, we praise Thee, our crucified God: have mercy on us!

*Troparion:*
Being the Luminary of understanding, Thou hast shone forth among Thy people, O Jesus Lover of mankind, willingly becoming poor, desiring to open Eden, which Adam closed by transgression of old.

*Glory, Theotokion:*
Beholding Thee suspended, O Christ, She that bare Thee cried aloud: "What is this strange mystery which I behold, O my Son? How upon the Tree dost Thou die in the flesh, being affixed thereto, O Giver of life?"

*Both now, the same:*
*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4:*[^196]
Thou hast redeemed us from the curse of the Law by Thy precious Blood, having been nailed unto the Cross and pierced with a lance: Thou hast poured forth immortality unto men, O our Savior, glory be to Thee!

*Glory, both now, the same.*

*The Sixth Gospel:*[^197]  
*From Mark, section 67 (Mark 15:16–32):*  
*At that time, the soldiers led Jesus away: End: That we may see and believe Him.*

*Then, The Beatitudes, we set 8 verses, Tone 4:*  
*Refrain:* In Thy Kingdom remember us, O Lord, when Thou comest into Thy Kingdom.  
*Verse:* Blessed are the poor in spirit, for theirs is the kingdom of heaven.  
*Verse:* Blessed are they that mourn, for they shall be comforted.  
*Verse:* Blessed are the meek, for they shall inherit the earth.  

*Verse 8:* *Blessed are they that hunger and thirst after righteousness, for they shall be filled.*  
*Troparion:*  
By a tree was Adam banished from paradise, but by the tree of the Cross the thief dwelt in paradise; for the one, having tasted, rejected the commandment of Him that made him; while the other, being crucified with Thee, confessed Thee as the hidden God: Remember us also, O Savior, in Thy Kingdom!

*Verse 7:* *Blessed are the merciful, for they shall obtain mercy.*  
*Troparion:*  
The lawless bought the Creator of the Law from a disciple, and as a transgressor of the Law they presented Him before Pilate's judgment seat, crying aloud: "Crucify Him Who gave them manna in the wilderness!" But imitating the righteous thief, by faith we cry: Remember us also, O Savior, in Thy Kingdom!

*Verse 6:* *Blessed are the pure in heart, for they shall see God.*  
*Troparion:*  
The assembly of Deicides, the lawless Jewish nation, cried frenziedly unto Pilate, saying: "Crucify Christ Who is innocent, and rather release unto us Barabbas!" But we utter unto Him the voice of the wise thief: Remember us also, O Savior, in Thy Kingdom!

*Verse 5:* *Blessed are the peacemakers, for they shall be called the children of God.*  
*Troparion:*  
Thy life-bearing side, pouring forth a fountain as out of Eden, waters Thy Church, O Christ, as a rational paradise, and parting thence...

...as into four rivers, into the four Gospels, watering the world, it gladdens creation, and faithfully teaches the nations to worship Thy Kingdom.

*Verse 4:* *Blessed are they that are persecuted for righteousness' sake, for theirs is the kingdom of heaven.*  
*Troparion:*
