A terror it was to behold the Creator of heaven and earth hanging upon the Cross, the sun darkened, the day turning back into night, and the earth sending forth from the tombs the bodies of the dead; with them we worship Thee: save us!  
*Twice.*

[Folio 131 verso]

*Verse: They parted My garments among them, and upon My vesture did they cast lots.*

*Tone 2:*  
When the lawless nailed the Lord of glory to the Cross, He cried unto them: "Wherein have I grieved you, or wherein have I provoked you to anger? Before Me, who delivered you from affliction? And now, what do ye repay Me? Evil for good! In return for the pillar of fire, ye nailed Me to the Cross; in return for the cloud, ye dug a tomb for Me; in return for the manna, ye brought Me gall; in return for the water, ye gave Me vinegar to drink! Henceforth will I call the nations, and they shall glorify Me with the Father and with the Holy Spirit!"

*Verse: They gave Me gall for My food, and for My thirst they gave Me vinegar to drink.*

*Again the same sticheron.*

*Glory. Tone 6:*  
Today He Who hung the earth upon the waters is hung upon the Tree; He Who is King of the angels is girt with a crown of thorns; He Who covers the heaven with clouds is arrayed in mock purple; He Who set Adam free in the Jordan receives buffeting; the Bridegroom of the Church is transfixed with nails; the Son of the Virgin is pierced with a lance. We venerate Thy Sufferings, O Christ! We venerate Thy Sufferings, O Christ! We venerate Thy Sufferings, O Christ: show us also Thy glorious Resurrection!

*Both now, the same.*

*Prokeimenon, Tone 6:*  
The fool hath said in his heart: "There is no God."  
*Verse: There is none that does good.*

*Reading of the Prophecy of Jeremiah:*[^255]  
O Lord, make it known unto me, and I shall know; then I beheld... The end thereof: ...and each one into his own land. (see fol. 74)[^256]

*Epistle to the Hebrews (Section 324, Heb. 10:19–31):*[^257]  
Brethren, having boldness to enter into the Holiest by the Blood of Jesus Christ, by a new and living way, which He hath consecrated for us through the veil, that is to say, His flesh; and having a Great Priest over the house of God: let us draw near with a true heart in full assurance of faith, having our hearts sprinkled from an evil conscience, and our bodies washed with pure water; let us hold fast the confession of our hope without wavering; for He is faithful that promised; and let us consider one another to provoke unto love and to good works, not forsaking the assembling of ourselves together, as the manner of some is, but exhorting one another; and so much the more, as ye see the [judgment] day approaching. For if we sin willfully after that we have received the knowledge of the truth, there remains no more sacrifice for sins, but a certain fearful looking for of judgment and fiery indignation, which shall devour the adversaries. He that despised Moses' law died without mercy under two or three witnesses: of how much sorer punishment, suppose ye, shall he be thought worthy, who hath trodden under foot the Son of God, and hath counted the Blood of the covenant, wherewith he was sanctified, an unholy thing, and hath done despite unto the Spirit of grace? For we know Him that hath said: "Vengeance belongs unto Me, I will recompense, saith the Lord." And again: "The Lord shall judge His people." It is a fearful thing to fall into the hands of the living God.

[Folio 132 recto]

*The Gospel according to John (Section 61, John 18:28–19:37):*[^258]  
At that time, they led Jesus from Caiaphas unto the hall of judgment; and it was early; and they themselves went not into the judgment hall, lest they should be defiled, but that they might eat the passover. Pilate then went out unto them, and said: "What accusation bring ye against this Man?" They answered and said unto him: "If He were not a malefactor, we would not have delivered Him up unto thee." Then said Pilate unto them: "Take ye Him, and judge Him according to your law." The Jews therefore said unto him: "It is not lawful for us to put any man to death." That the saying of Jesus might be fulfilled, which He spoke, signifying what death He should die. Then Pilate entered into the judgment hall again, and called Jesus, and said unto Him: "Art Thou the King of the Jews?" Jesus answered him: "Sayest thou this thing of thyself, or did others tell it thee of Me?" Pilate answered: "Am I a Jew? Thine own nation and the chief priests have delivered Thee unto me: what hast Thou done?" Jesus answered: "My kingdom is not of this world: if My kingdom were of this world, then would My servants fight, that I should not be delivered to the Jews: but now is My kingdom not from hence." Pilate therefore said unto Him: "Art Thou a King then?" Jesus answered: "Thou sayest that I am a King. To this end was I born, and for this cause came I into the world, that I should bear witness unto the truth. Every one that is of the truth hears My voice." Pilate saith unto Him: "What is truth?" And when he had said this, he went out again unto the Jews, and saith unto them: "I find in Him no fault at all. But ye have a custom, that I should release unto you one at the passover: will ye therefore that I release unto you the King of the Jews?" Then cried they all again, saying: "Not this Man, but Barabbas!" Now Barabbas was a robber. Then Pilate therefore took Jesus, and scourged Him. And the soldiers plaited a crown of thorns, and put it on His head, and they put on Him a purple robe, and said: "Hail, King of the Jews!" and they smote Him on the cheeks. Pilate therefore went forth again, and saith unto them: "Behold, I bring Him forth to you, that ye may know that I find no fault in Him." Then came Jesus forth, wearing the crown of thorns, and the purple robe. And Pilate saith unto them: "Behold the Man!" When the chief priests therefore and officers saw Him, they cried out, saying: "Crucify Him, crucify Him!" Pilate saith unto them: "Take ye Him, and crucify Him: for I find no fault in Him." The Jews answered him: "We have a law, and by our law He ought to die, because He made Himself the Son of God." When Pilate therefore heard that saying, he was the more afraid; and went again into the judgment hall, and saith unto Jesus: "Whence art Thou?" But Jesus gave him no answer. Then saith Pilate unto Him: "Speakest Thou not unto me? Knowest Thou not that I have power to crucify Thee, and have power to release Thee?" Jesus answered: "Thou couldest have no power at all against Me, except it were given thee from above: therefore he that delivered Me unto thee hath the greater sin." And from thenceforth Pilate sought to release Him; but the Jews cried out, saying: "If thou let this Man go, thou art not Caesar's friend: whosoever makes himself a king speaks against Caesar." When Pilate therefore heard that saying, he brought Jesus forth, and sat down in the judgment seat in a place that is called the Pavement, but in the Hebrew, Gabbatha. And it was the Preparation of the passover, and about the sixth hour; and he saith unto the Jews: "Behold your King!" But they cried out: "Away with Him, away with Him, crucify Him!" Pilate saith unto them: "Shall I crucify your King?" The chief priests answered: "We have no king but Caesar." Then delivered he Him therefore unto them to be crucified. And they took Jesus, and led Him away. And He bearing His Cross went forth into a place called the Place of a Skull, which is called in the Hebrew Golgotha; where they crucified Him, and two other with Him, on either side one...

[Folio 133 verso]

...and Jesus in the midst. And Pilate wrote a title, and put it on the Cross. And the writing was: JESUS OF NAZARETH THE KING OF THE JEWS. This title then read many of the Jews: for the place where Jesus was crucified was nigh to the city: and it was written in Hebrew, and Greek, and Latin. Then said the chief priests of the Jews to Pilate: "Write not: The King of the Jews; but that He said: I am King of the Jews." Pilate answered: "What I have written I have written." Then the soldiers, when they had crucified Jesus, took His garments, and made four parts, to every soldier a part; and also His coat: now the coat was without seam, woven from the top throughout. They said therefore among themselves: "Let us not rend it, but cast lots for it, whose it shall be": that the Scripture might be fulfilled, which saith: "They parted My raiment among them, and for My vesture they did cast lots." These things therefore the soldiers did. Now there stood by the Cross of Jesus His Mother, and His Mother's sister, Mary the wife of Cleophas, and Mary Magdalene. When Jesus therefore saw His Mother, and the disciple standing by, whom He loved, He saith unto His Mother: "Woman, behold thy son!" Then saith He to the disciple: "Behold thy Mother!" And from that hour that disciple took her unto his own home. After this, Jesus knowing that all things were now accomplished, that the Scripture might be fulfilled, saith: "I thirst." Now there was set a vessel full of vinegar: and they filled a sponge with vinegar, and put it upon hyssop, and put it to His mouth. When Jesus therefore had received the vinegar, He said: "It is finished": and He bowed His head, and gave up the ghost. The Jews therefore, because it was the Preparation, that the bodies should not remain upon the Cross on the Sabbath day (for that Sabbath day was a high day), besought Pilate that their legs might be broken, and that they might be taken away. Then came the soldiers, and brake the legs of the first, and of the other who was crucified with Him. But when they came to Jesus, and saw that He was dead already, they brake not His legs: but one of the soldiers with a spear pierced His side, and forthwith came there out blood and water. And he that saw it bare record, and his record is true: and he knows that he saith true, that ye might believe. For these things were done, that the Scripture should be fulfilled: "A bone of Him shall not be broken." And again another Scripture saith: "They shall look on Him Whom they pierced."

*Then a reading is set forth: The Homily on the Cross.*[^259]

*Then:*  
Deliver us not up utterly, for Thy name's sake, neither disannul Thou Thy covenant; and cause not Thy mercy to depart from us, for Abraham's sake Thy beloved, and for Isaac's sake Thy servant, and Israel's Thy holy one.

*Trisagion. After Our Father.*

*Kontakion: "For our sake crucified..." Lord, have mercy (40 times). Prayer: "Thou Who at every season..." Lord, have mercy (thrice).*

*Glory, both now: More honorable than the Cherubim. In the name of the Lord, bless, father.*

**Priest:** God be bountiful unto us, and bless us, and cause His face to shine upon us, and have mercy on us.

*And the prayer:*[^260]  
O Master, Lord Jesus Christ our God, Who hast long-suffered over our transgressions, and hast brought us even unto this present hour, wherein, hanging upon the life-creating Tree, Thou didst open the entrance into Paradise unto the prudent thief, and by death didst destroy death: Cleanse us sinners and Thine unworthy servants; for we have sinned and committed iniquity, and we are not worthy to lift up our eyes and to look upon the height...

[Folio 134 recto]

...of heaven, because we have forsaken the path of Thy righteousness, and have walked in the wills of our own hearts. But we implore Thine immeasurable goodness: spare us, O Lord, according to the multitude of Thy mercy, and save us for Thy holy name's sake, for our days have vanished in vanity. Rescue us from the hand of the adversary, and forgive us our sins, and mortify our carnal mind; that, putting off the old man, we may put on the new, and live unto Thee, our Master and Benefactor; and thus, following Thy commandments, we may attain unto everlasting rest, where is the dwelling-place of all them that rejoice. For Thou art in truth the true joy and gladness of them that love Thee, O Christ our God, and unto Thee do we send up glory, with Thine unoriginate Father, and Thine all-holy, good, and life-creating Spirit, now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.

*Then the Beatitudes quickly:*[^261]

*In Thy kingdom when Thou comest:*  
*Glory, both now: Remember us, O Lord:*  
*The heavenly choir:*  
*Glory: The choir of holy angels:*  
*Both now: I believe in one God:*  
*Remit, forgive. Then, Our Father:*  
*And the Kontakion: "For our sake crucified..." Lord, have mercy (40 times). And the prayer: "All-Holy Trinity..." Then: "Blessed be the name of the Lord" (thrice). Glory, both now:*  
*Psalm 33:*  
*I will bless the Lord at all times:*  
*And the dismissal.*

*It is fitting to know also this: that we have received in Palestine, on this holy day of Great Friday, not to celebrate the Presanctified nor again the complete Liturgy; neither do we set a table, nor do we eat on this day of the Crucifixion.*[^262]  
*If anyone be exceedingly infirm, or very aged, and unable to remain fasting, bread and water are given to him after the setting of the sun.*  
*For thus have we received from the Holy Precepts of the Holy Apostles: not to eat on Great Friday; for the word of the Lord which the Lord spoke unto the Pharisees: "When the Bridegroom shall be taken away from them, then shall they fast in those days." Here the most blessed Apostles received this, and this shalt thou find in the Apostolic Constitutions, if thou study them attentively. Moreover, the canonical epistle of the most holy Dionysius, Archbishop of Alexandria, clearly sets this forth.*

*[Plate: The Crucifixion of our Lord Jesus Christ]*[^263]

*Full-page engraving: The Crucifixion of the Lord upon Golgotha.*  
*At the top of the Cross is the titulus:* I.N (*Ц.I.*) *[Jesus of Nazareth, King of the Jews].*  
*Above the head of the Savior is the cruciform halo inscribed:* (*Ѻ Ѡ Н*) *[He Who Is].*  
*To the right of the Cross: The Most Holy Theotokos, standing with hands clasped upon her breast, inscribed above:* (*М҃Р Ѳ҃Ꙋ*) *[Mother of God].*  
*To the left of the Cross: The Holy Apostle John the Theologian, lifting his eyes toward the Crucified, inscribed above:* (*С. А҆ПЛЪ І҆ѠАННЪ БОГО:*) *[Saint Apostle John the Theologian].*  
*At the foot of the Cross: Mount Golgotha, rocks, the human skull (the skull of Adam), and wooden wedges. In the distant background are visible the walls and towers of Jerusalem, beneath a sky darkened by clouds.*  
*Below the image, within a decorative vignette, is engraved the Gospel verse:*  
"For God so loved the world..." [John 3:16]

## ON HOLY AND GREAT FRIDAY, AT VESPERS[^264]

*At the tenth hour of the day the klepalo is struck.*[^265]

*And the priest having given the blessing, the Lamp-lighting [Psalms] are begun; at "Lord, I have cried," we set 6 verses:*

*And we sing idiomel stichera.*

*Tone 1:*

All creation was altered by fear, beholding Thee hanging upon the Cross, O Christ: the sun was darkened, and the foundations of the earth were shaken; all things suffered with the Creator of all. O Thou Who didst endure willingly for our sake, Lord, glory to Thee! *(Twice.)*

*Tone 2:*

Ye wicked and lawless people, why do ye meditate on vain things? Why did ye condemn to death the Life of all? A great wonder! That the Creator of the world is delivered into the hands of the lawless, and the Lover of mankind is lifted up upon the Tree, that He may free the prisoners in hades, who cry: O long-suffering Lord, glory to Thee!

Today the blameless Virgin, seeing Thee lifted up upon the Cross, O Word, weeping with a mother's bowels, was bitterly wounded in her heart; and groaning sorrowfully from the depths of her soul, tearing her face and hair, and beating her breast, she cried out piteously: Woe is me, divine Child! Woe is me, Light of the world! Why hast Thou set from mine eyes, O Lamb of God? Therefore the hosts of the bodiless were held with trembling, saying: O incomprehensible Lord, glory to Thee!

Beholding Thee hanging upon the Tree, O Christ, the Creator and God of all, she who bore Thee without seed cried bitterly: My Son, whither has the beauty of Thy countenance departed? I cannot bear to behold Thee...

...unjustly crucified: make haste therefore to arise, that I also may see Thy three-day Resurrection from the dead!

*Tone 6:*

Today the Master of creation stands before Pilate, and to the Cross is delivered the Creator of all: as a sheep led of His own will, He is nailed with nails, and in His side is pierced, and with a sponge is given drink by He Who rained down manna; upon the cheek is buffeted the Redeemer of the world, and by His own servants is mocked the Creator of all. O Master's love for mankind! For them that crucified Him He prayed to His Father, saying: Father, forgive them this sin; for the lawless know not what injustice they do.

*Glory, in the same tone [Tone 6]:*

O how the lawless assembly condemned to death the King of creation! Not being ashamed of the benefits which He, putting them in remembrance, had confirmed before, saying unto them: My people, what have I done unto you? Have I not filled Judea with wonders? Have I not raised the dead with a single word? Have I not healed every disease and sickness? What then do ye render unto Me? Why do ye not remember Me? In return for healings, ye have laid wounds upon Me; instead of life, ye put Me to death; hanging upon the Tree as an evildoer the Benefactor, as a transgressor the Lawgiver, as a condemned man the King of all! O long-suffering Lord, glory to Thee!

*Both now, in the same tone [Tone 6]:*

A fearsome and most glorious mystery is seen accomplished today: He Who is untouchable is held; He Who loosed Adam from the curse is bound; He Who tris the hearts and reins is unjustly tried; He Who shut up the abyss is shut up in prison; before Pilate stands He before Whom heavenly powers stand with trembling; with the hand of the creature the Creator is buffeted; to the Tree is condemned He Who judges the living and the dead; in the tomb is enclosed the Destroyer of hades! O Thou Who didst endure all things mercifully, and hast saved all from the curse, O innocent Lord, glory to Thee!

*Entrance with the Gospel.*

*O gladsome Light:*

*Prokeimenon, Tone 6:*

They divided my garments among them, and upon my vesture they cast lots.[^266]

*Verse: O God, my God, attend to me: why hast Thou forsaken me?*

*The Reading from Exodus (Chapter 33):*[^267]

The Lord spoke unto Moses face to face, as if one should speak unto his friend; and he returned into the camp; but his minister Joshua, the son of Nun, a young man, departed not out of the tabernacle. And Moses said unto the Lord: Behold, Thou sayest unto me: Bring up this people; yet Thou hast not shown me whom Thou wilt send with me. But Thou hast said unto me: I know thee above all, and thou hast found grace with Me. If therefore I have found grace in Thy sight, manifest Thyself unto me, that I may clearly see Thee, that I may find grace in Thy sight, and that I may know that this great nation is Thy people. And the Lord said unto him: I Myself will go before thee, and I will give thee rest. And Moses said unto Him: If Thou Thyself go not...

...with us, lead me not up hence. And how shall it be known truly that I and Thy people have found grace with Thee, unless Thou go with us? And so shall I and Thy people be glorified beyond all the nations that are upon the earth. And the Lord said unto Moses: I will do this word also that thou hast spoken; for thou hast found grace before Me, and I know thee above all. And Moses said: Show me Thy glory. And the Lord said unto Moses: I will go before thee in My glory, and I will call upon the name of the Lord before thee; and I will have mercy on whom I will have mercy, and I will have compassion on whom I will have compassion. And He said: Thou canst not see My face; for man shall not see My face, and live. And the Lord said: Behold, there is a place by Me, and thou shalt stand upon the rock; and when My glory passes by, I will put thee in a cleft of the rock, and will cover thee with My hand over thee, until I have passed by. And I will take away My hand, and then shalt thou see My back; but My face shall not be revealed unto thee.

*Prokeimenon, Tone 4:*

Judge, O Lord, them that wrong me; fight against them that fight against me.[^268]

*Verse: Take hold of arms and shield, and arise to my help.*

*The Reading from Job (Chapter 42):*[^269]

The Lord blessed the latter days of Job more than the former; and he had fourteen thousand sheep, six thousand camels, a thousand yoke of oxen, and a thousand she-asses of the pasture. And there were born unto him seven sons and three daughters. And he called the name of the first, Day; and the second, Cassia; and the third, Amalthea's Horn. And there were not found women so fair as the daughters of Job in all that is under heaven; and their father gave them an inheritance among their brethren. And Job lived after his affliction a hundred and seventy years; and all the years he lived were two hundred and forty-eight. And Job saw his sons, and his sons' sons, unto the fourth generation. And Job died, an old man, and full of days. And it is written that he shall rise again with those whom the Lord raises up: thus is it interpreted from the Syriac book. He dwelt in the land of Ausitis, on the borders of Idumaea and Arabia; and his name before was Jobab. And taking an Arabian wife, he begat a son whose name was Enon. And he was the son of his father Zaretha, a son of the sons of Esau, and of his mother Bosorra; so that he was the fifth from Abraham.

*The Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah (Chapter 52):*[^270]

*Thus saith the Lord: Behold, My Servant shall understand... The End: ...Than she that hath a husband.*

*(see fol. 124).*[^271]

*And immediately:*

*Prokeimenon of the Apostle, Tone 6:*

They laid me in the lowest pit, in dark places and in the shadow of death.[^272]

*Verse: O Lord God of my salvation, I have cried day and night before Thee.*

*The Reading from the Epistle of the Holy Apostle Paul to the Corinthians (Selection 125):*[^273]

Brethren: The word of the cross is foolishness unto them that perish, but unto us who are being saved it is the power of God. For it is written: I will destroy the wisdom of the wise, and will reject the understanding of the prudent. Where is the wise? Where is the scribe? Where is the disputer of this age? Hath not God made foolish the wisdom of this world? For after that in the wisdom of God the world...

...by wisdom knew not God, it pleased God by the foolishness of preaching to save them that believe. For the Jews require signs, and the Greeks seek after wisdom; but we preach Christ crucified, unto the Jews a stumbling block, and unto the Greeks foolishness; but unto them that are called, both Jews and Greeks, Christ the power of God and the wisdom of God. Because the foolishness of God is wiser than men, and the weakness of God is stronger than men. For see your calling, brethren, that not many wise men after the flesh, not many mighty, not many noble, are called. But God hath chosen the foolish things of the world to put to shame the wise; and God hath chosen the weak things of the world to put to shame the mighty; and base things of the world, and things that are despised, hath God chosen, and things that are not, to bring to naught things that are; that no flesh should boast before God. But of Him are ye in Christ Jesus, Who of God was made unto us wisdom, and righteousness, and sanctification, and redemption: that, according as it is written: He that gloris, let him glory in the Lord. And I, brethren, when I came to you, came not with excellency of speech or of wisdom, declaring unto you the testimony of God. For I determined not to know anything among you, save Jesus Christ, and Him crucified.

*Alleluia, Tone 1:*

*Save me, O God, for the waters are come in even unto my soul.*[^274]

*Verse: My soul looked for reproach and affliction.*

*Verse: Let their eyes be darkened, that they see not.*

*The Holy Gospel according to Matthew (Selection 110):*[^275]

*At that time, all the chief priests took counsel... The End: ...opposite the tomb.*

*Then, the customary Litany: Let us all say with our whole soul... And afterwards: Vouchsafe, O Lord:*

*Let us complete our evening prayer... and the rest.*

*And after the exclamation we sing at the Aposticha stichera, 4 prosomoia.*

*Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Є҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*[^276]

When from the Tree the Arimathean took Thee down dead, the Life of all, he wrapped Thee, O Christ, in fine linen with myrrh, and was moved by love to kiss with heart and lips Thy incorrupt Body; yet held by fear, he cried unto Thee rejoicing: Glory to Thy condescension, O Lover of mankind!

*Verse: The Lord is King, He is clothed with majesty.*

When in a new tomb Thou wast laid for all, O Redeemer of all, hades, laughing mockingly, seeing Thee was terrified: the bars were crushed, the gates were broken, the tombs were opened, the dead arose! Then Adam thankfully and rejoicing cried unto Thee: Glory to Thy condescension, O Lover of mankind!

*Verse: For He hath established the world, which shall not be moved.*

When in the tomb according to the flesh Thou didst will to be enclosed, O Thou Who in the nature of Godhead remainest uncircumscribed and boundless, Thou didst shut up the treasuries of death, and didst empty all the kingdoms of hades, O Christ: then also didst Thou vouchsafe this Sabbath of divine blessing and glory, and of Thy radiance!

*Verse: Holiness becomes Thy house, O Lord, unto length of days.*

When the Powers beheld Thee, O Christ, slandered by the lawless as a deceiver, they were astounded at Thine ineffable long-suffering, and at the stone of the tomb sealed by the hands of those who had pierced Thy spotless side; yet rejoicing in our salvation, they cried unto Thee: Glory to Thy condescension, O Lover of mankind!

*Glory, both now, Tone 5:*

Thee Who clothest Thyself with light as with a garment, Joseph with Nicodemus took down from the Tree; and beholding Thee dead, naked, and unburied, he took up a compassionate lamentation, and weeping said: Woe is me, sweetest Jesus! Whom a little while ago the sun, seeing hanging upon the Cross, wrapped itself in darkness, and the earth quaked with fear, and the veil of the temple was rent in twain; but lo, now I behold Thee, for my sake willingly undergoing death! How shall I bury Thee, my God? Or with what winding-sheet shall I wrap Thee? With what hands shall I touch Thine incorrupt Body? Or what songs shall I sing for Thy departure, O Compassionate One? I magnify Thy Passions, I hymn also Thy Burial with the Resurrection, crying: Lord, glory to Thee!

*Then: Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart. Trisagion.*

*After Our Father:*

*Troparion, Tone 2:*

The noble Joseph, taking down Thy most pure Body from the Tree, wrapped it in clean linen and spices, and laid it in a new tomb.

*Another Troparion:*

Standing by the tomb, the Angel cried unto the myrrh-bearing women: Myrrh is fitting for the dead, but Christ hath shown Himself a stranger to corruption.

*Wisdom, and*

*The Dismissal:*

May He Who for the sake of us men and for our salvation, endured in the flesh the dread Passions, and the life-creating Cross, and the voluntary Burial, for the sake of our salvation, Christ our true God, through the prayers of His most pure Mother, and of all the Saints, have mercy on us and save us, as He is good and the Lover of mankind.

*Small Compline is sung in the cells.*

## ON HOLY AND GREAT FRIDAY, AT COMPLINE

*Which we sing in the cells, whereon we sing:*

*The Canon on the Crucifixion of the Lord, and on the Lament of the Most Holy Theotokos.*[^277]

*The Heirmoi twice, troparia to 6; and at the end the Heirmos by both choirs together.*

*The work of Symeon the Metaphrast.*[^278]

*Tone 6. Ode 1. Heirmos:*

Traversing the deep on foot as if on dry land, Israel, seeing their pursuer Pharaoh drowned, cried out: Let us sing a song of victory unto God!

*Refrain: Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*

*Troparion:*

When the pure Virgin saw her Son and Lord suspended upon the Cross, she was torn with grief and cried bitterly, groaning with the other women, and saying:

*Troparion:*

I see Thee now, my beloved Child and darling, hanging upon the Cross, and I am bitterly wounded in heart, said the pure Virgin; but give a word, O Good One, unto Thine handmaid.

*Glory:*

*Troparion:*

Willingly, my Son and Creator, dost Thou endure cruel death upon the Tree, the Virgin said, standing by the Cross with the beloved disciple.

*Both now:*

*Theotokion:*

Now am I deprived of my hope, joy, and gladness, of my Son and Lord: woe is me, I suffer sorrow of heart, said the pure Virgin, weeping.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

There is none holy like Thee, O Lord my God, Who hast exalted the horn of Thy faithful, O Good One, and established us upon the rock of Thy confession.

*Troparion:*

For fear of the Jews Peter hid himself, and all the faithful fled, forsaking Christ, the Virgin said, lamenting.

*Troparion:*

At Thy dread and strange Nativity, my Son, I was magnified above all mothers; but woe is me, now seeing Thee upon the Tree, I burn within my bowels.

*Glory:*

*Troparion:*

I see my bowels taken down from the Tree into the hands wherewith I held Thee as an Infant, said the pure Virgin; but no one, woe is me, hath granted this.

*Both now:*

*Theotokion:*

Lo, my sweet Light, my Hope and good Life, my God is extinguished upon the Cross, and I burn within my bowels, the Virgin said, groaning.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*

Christ is my power, God and Lord, the venerable Church sings in godly manner, crying from a pure mind, keeping festival in the Lord.

*Troparion:*

O unwaning Sun, eternal God, and Creator of all creation, O Lord, how dost Thou endure Passion upon the Cross? the pure Virgin said, weeping.

*Troparion:*

Weeping, the unwedded Virgin said unto the noble counselor: Make haste, Joseph, go to Pilate, and beg to take down from the Tree thy Teacher.

*Glory:*

*Troparion:*

Seeing the All-Pure shedding bitter tears, Joseph was troubled, and weeping went to Pilate, crying with tears: Give me the Body of my God!

*Both now:*

*Theotokion:*

Seeing Thee wounded, and without glory, naked upon the Tree, my Child, I burn within my bowels, lamenting as a Mother, the Virgin uttered.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

With Thy divine light, O Good One, illumine with love the souls of them that rise early unto Thee, I pray, that they may know Thee, O Word of God, the true God, Who callest them from the darkness of sin.

*Troparion:*

Torn with grief, weeping, and wondering together with Nicodemus, Joseph took Thee down, and kissing the most pure Body, he wept and groaned, singing of Him as God.

*Troparion:*

Receiving Him with tears, the unwedded Mother laid Him upon her knees, praying unto Him with tears, and kissing Him, weeping bitterly and crying out:

*Glory:*

*Troparion:*

My only Hope and Life, Master, my Son and God, the Light of mine eyes had I, Thine handmaid; but now am I deprived of Thee, my sweet Child and darling!

*Both now:*

*Theotokion:*

Pains and sorrows and sighings have found me, woe is me! the pure Virgin said, weeping bitterly, seeing Thee, my beloved Child, naked and forsaken, and anointed with spices as a corpse.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

Beholding the sea of life surging with the tempest of temptations, fleeing to Thy calm haven, I cry unto Thee: Lead up my life from corruption, O Most Merciful One!

*Troparion:*

I see Thee dead, O Lover of mankind, Who didst quicken the dead and holdest all things, and I am sorely wounded in my bowels; I would fain die with Thee, the All-Pure said: for I cannot bear to behold Thee dead without breath.

*Troparion:*

I marvel beholding Thee, O most good God and all-wise Lord, without glory, without breath, and formless; and weeping I hold Thee, for I hoped not, woe is me, to see Thee, my Son and God!

*Glory:*

*Troparion:*

Wilt Thou not speak a word unto Thine handmaid, O Word of God? Wilt Thou not have compassion, O Master, on her who bore Thee? said the pure Virgin, lamenting and weeping, kissing the Body of her Lord.

*Both now:*

*Theotokion:*

I consider, O Master, that no more shall I hear Thy sweet voice, nor see the beauty of Thy face, as Thine handmaid did before; for Thou hast set from mine eyes, my Son!

*Kontakion, Tone 2:*[^279]

Come, let us all hymn Him Who was crucified for our sake; for Mary beheld Him upon the Tree, and said: Though Thou endurest crucifixion, Thou art my Son and my God.

*Ikos:*[^280]

Seeing her own Lamb drawn unto the slaughter, Mary followed with hair disheveled, together with the other women, crying thus: Whither goest Thou, Child? For what cause dost Thou accomplish this swift course? Is there again another marriage in Cana, and dost Thou now hasten thither, that Thou mayest make wine for them from water? Shall I go with Thee, Child, or shall I rather wait for Thee? Give me a word, O Word; pass me not by in silence, Thou Who didst keep me pure: for Thou art my Son and my God.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

An Angel made the furnace sprinkle dew for the righteous youths, but the command of God consuming the Chaldeans persuaded the tyrant to cry: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*

Where, my Son and God, is the good tidings of old which Gabriel spoke unto me, calling Thee King, Son, and God of the Most High? But now I see Thee, my sweet Light, a naked and wounded corpse!

*Troparion:*

O Thou Who deliverest from afflictions, now receive me with Thee, my Son and God, that I also, O Master, may descend into hades with Thee; leave me not alone, for I can no longer bear to live without seeing Thee, my sweet Light!

*Glory:*

*Troparion:*

Weeping bitterly with the other myrrh-bearing women, and seeing Christ borne away, the Blameless Virgin said: Woe is me, what do I see! Whither goest Thou now, my Son, and leavest me alone?

*Both now:*

*Theotokion:*

Fainting and weeping, the Blameless Virgin said unto the myrrh-bearing women: Mourn with me, and weep bitterly with me; for behold, my sweet Light and your Teacher is delivered unto the tomb!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

Out of the flame didst Thou drop dew upon the righteous, and the sacrifice of the just didst Thou consume with water; for Thou doest all things, O Christ, merely by willing: Thee do we exalt unto all the ages!

*Troparion:*

Seeing the Virgin weeping, Joseph was completely rent with grief, and cried bitterly: How shall I, O my God, now bury Thee, Thy servant? With what winding-sheets shall I wrap Thy Body?

*Troparion:*

Beyond all understanding surpasses Thy strange vision, O Lord Who upholdest all creation: therefore Joseph, carrying Thee as a dead man in his hands, with Nicodemus bears Thee and buris Thee.

*Glory:*

*Troparion:*

A strange and most glorious mystery do I see, the Virgin cried unto her Son and Lord: how art Thou laid in a poor tomb, O Thou Who raisest up the dead in the tombs by Thy command?

*Both now:*

*Theotokion:*

Neither will I arise from Thy tomb, my Child, nor will I cease shedding tears, Thine handmaid, until I also descend into hades; for I cannot bear separation from Thee, my Son!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*

God cannot be seen of men, upon Whom the ranks of Angels dare not gaze; but through thee, O All-Pure, was the Word incarnate made visible unto men: Whom magnifying, with the heavenly hosts we call thee blessed.

*Troparion:*

Joy shall never touch me henceforth, the Blameless Virgin said, weeping: my Light and my Joy is departed into the tomb; but I will not leave Him alone, here will I die, and be buried with Him!

*Troparion:*

Heal now the wound of my soul, my Child, the All-Pure cried with tears: arise, and assuage my pain and sorrow; for Thou canst do whatsoever Thou willest, O Master, even though Thou hast been buried willingly.

*Glory:*

*Troparion:*

O how the abyss of compassion is hidden from thee! the Lord said secretly unto His Mother: for willing to save My creation, I have chosen to die; but I will also arise, and will magnify thee, as God of heaven and earth.

*Both now:*

*Theotokion:*

I will hymn Thy mercy, O Lover of mankind, and I worship the riches of Thy compassion, O Master: for willing to save Thy creation, Thou hast undergone death, said the All-Pure; but by Thy Resurrection, O Savior, have mercy on us all.

*Then, instead of "It is truly meet," the Heirmos:*

*God cannot be seen of men...*

*Trisagion. After Our Father:*

*Kontakion:*

Come, let us all hymn Him Who was crucified for our sake; for Mary beheld Him upon the Tree, and said: Though Thou endurest crucifixion, Thou art my Son and my God.

*Lord, have mercy (40 times).*

*Thou Who at all times:*

*Lord, have mercy (thrice). Glory, both now: More honorable than the Cherubim: In the name of the Lord, bless, father.*

**Priest:** *Through the prayers of our holy fathers:*

*And the prayer:*[^281]

*O Spotless, undefiled:*

*And the dismissal.*

*The Midnight Office we sing in the cells.*[^282]

*[Tailpiece: Vignette depicting the Alpha and the Omega]*[^283] *[Headpiece Plate: The Entombment of our Lord Jesus Christ]*[^284]

*A detailed woodcut engraving depicting the Lamentation at the Tomb: The Body of the Savior, wrapped in a pure winding-sheet, is laid into the tomb by the righteous Joseph of Arimathea and Nicodemus, surrounded by the weeping Most Holy Mother of God, the Holy Apostle John the Theologian, and the Myrrh-bearing women, beneath a cavernous rock. The central illustration is flanked on both sides by elaborate vertical ornamental borders of intertwining floral foliage.*

## ON HOLY AND GREAT SATURDAY[^285]

*The klepalo is struck for Matins at the seventh hour of the night.[^286] And having gathered into the church, we sing:*

*Matins*

*The priest having begun:* Blessed is our God: *then, the Trisagion; after "Our Father": Priest:* For Thine is the kingdom: *and after:* Amen, *Lord, have mercy (12 times), and immediately:*

**Priest:** Glory to the holy, consubstantial, life-creating, and undivided Trinity, (to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit,) always, now and ever, and unto ages of ages.

**Choir:** Amen.

Glory to God in the highest: *And the Six Psalms are chanted.*

*And after the Six Psalms, the Great Litany.*

*At "God is the Lord":*

*Troparion, Tone 2:*[^287]

The noble Joseph, having taken down Thy most pure Body from the Tree, wrapped it in a clean winding-sheet, and covering it with fragrant spices, laid it in a new tomb.

*Glory:*

*Troparion, in the same tone [Tone 2]:*

When Thou didst descend unto death, O Life immortal, then didst Thou put hades to death with the radiance of Thy Divinity; and when Thou didst also raise up the dead from the subterranean depths, all the heavenly Powers cried out: O Life-giver, Christ our God, glory to Thee!

*Both now:*

*Both now:*

*Theotokion, in the same tone [Tone 2]:*

Standing before the tomb, the Angel cried unto the myrrh-bearing women: Myrrh is fitting for the dead, but Christ has shown Himself a stranger to corruption.

*After this, the Parrecclesiarch distributes candles to the brethren, and we sing:*[^288]

*Blessed are the undefiled:*[^289]

*Kathisma 17. In Tone 5, with refrains in three Staseis.*[^290]

*At the first Stasis the Superior censes; at the second and third, the priests according to rank.*

*Then first the Superior sings this refrain in the midst of the church, having censed cruciformly:*[^291]

*Refrain:*

We magnify Thee, O Christ the Life-giver, Who yesterday wast buried for our sake, Who didst endure and dost most gloriously quicken the dead.

*Verse 1 (Psalm 118:1): Blessed are the undefiled in the way, who walk in the law of the Lord.*

*Troparion:*

Thou wast laid in a tomb, O Christ the Life, and the hosts of Angels were astonished, glorifying Thy condescension.

*Verse 2 (Psalm 118:2): Blessed are they that search out His testimonies, and seek Him with their whole heart.*

*Troparion:*

O Life, how dost Thou die? How dost Thou dwell even in a tomb, yet destroyest the kingdom of death, and raisest up the dead from hades?

*Verse 3 (Psalm 118:3): For they that work iniquity have not walked in His ways.*

*Troparion:*

We magnify Thee, O Jesus the King, and we honor Thy burial and Thy sufferings, whereby Thou hast saved us from corruption.

*Verse 4 (Psalm 118:4): Thou hast commanded that Thy commandments be kept diligently.*

*Troparion:*

O Jesus, King of all, Who didst establish the measures of the earth, today Thou dwellest in a narrow tomb, raising up the dead from the graves.

*Verse 5 (Psalm 118:5): O that my ways were directed to keep Thine ordinances!*

*Troparion:*

O my Jesus Christ, King of all, seeking what hast Thou come unto them that are in hades? Is it to release the human race?

*Verse 6 (Psalm 118:6): Then shall I not be ashamed, when I have respect unto all Thy commandments.*

*Troparion:*

The Master of all is seen dead, and is laid in a new tomb, He Who emptied the tombs of the dead.

*Verse 7 (Psalm 118:7): I will give thanks unto Thee with uprightness of heart, when I have learned the judgments of Thy righteousness.*

*Troparion:*

O Life, Thou hast been laid in a tomb, O Christ, and by Thy death Thou hast destroyed death, and poured forth life unto the world.

*Verse 8 (Psalm 118:8): I will keep Thine ordinances; O forsake me not utterly.*

*Troparion:*

Among evildoers as an evildoer hast Thou been numbered, O Christ, justifying us all from the wickedness of the ancient deceiver.

*Verse 9 (Psalm 118:9): Wherewithal shall a young man direct his way? By keeping Thy words.*

*Troparion:*

Fair in beauty beyond all the sons of men, He Who adorned the nature of all things appears dead, without form or beauty.[^292]

*Verse 10 (Psalm 118:10): With my whole heart have I sought Thee; cast me not away from Thy commandments.*

*Troparion:*

How shall hades endure Thy coming, O Savior, and not rather be tormented, being darkened, and blinded by the lightning radiance of Thy light?

*Verse 11 (Psalm 118:11): In my heart have I hidden Thy words, that I might not sin against Thee.*

*Troparion:*

O my sweet Jesus, and saving Light, how hast Thou been hidden in a dark tomb? O Thine ineffable and unutterable endurance!

*Verse 12 (Psalm 118:12): Blessed art Thou, O Lord; teach me Thine ordinances.*

*Troparion:*

Even the noetic hosts and the bodiless multitude are astonished, O Christ, at the mystery of Thine ineffable and unutterable burial.

*Verse 13 (Psalm 118:13): With my lips have I declared all the judgments of Thy mouth.*

*Troparion:*

O strange wonders! O new things! The Giver of my breath is borne breathless, buried by the hands of Joseph.

*Verse 14 (Psalm 118:14): In the way of Thy testimonies have I delighted, as in all manner of riches.*

*Troparion:*

Even when Thou didst go down into the tomb, Thou wast in nowise separated from the Father's bosom, O Christ: this is strange and most glorious together.

*Verse 15 (Psalm 118:15): I will meditate on Thy commandments, and have understanding in Thy ways.*

*Troparion:*

True King of heaven and earth, though Thou art shut up in a very small tomb, Thou hast made Thyself known unto all creation, O Jesus.

*Verse 16 (Psalm 118:16): I will meditate on Thine ordinances; I will not forget Thy words.*

*Troparion:*

When Thou wast laid in the tomb, O Christ the Creator, the foundations of hades were shaken, and the graves of men were opened.

*Verse 17 (Psalm 118:17): Reward Thy servant; quicken me, and I will keep Thy words.*

*Troparion:*

He Who holds the earth in His hand is now held dead in the flesh beneath the earth, delivering the dead from the grasp of hades.

*Verse 18 (Psalm 118:18): Open Thou mine eyes, and I will perceive the wonders of Thy law.*

*Troparion:*

Thou hast ascended from corruption, O my Savior Life, when Thou didst die and come unto the dead, breaking in pieces the bars of hades.

*Verse 19 (Psalm 118:19): I am a stranger upon the earth; hide not Thy commandments from me.*

*Troparion:*

Even as a lamp of light, the Flesh of God is now hidden under the earth as beneath a bushel, and drives away the darkness in hades.

*Verse 20 (Psalm 118:20): My soul hath longed to desire Thy judgments at all times.*

*Troparion:*

The multitude of the noetic hosts hastens with Joseph and Nicodemus to bury Thee, Whom nothing can contain, in a narrow tomb.

*Verse 21 (Psalm 118:21): Thou hast rebuked the proud; accursed are they that decline from Thy commandments.*

*Troparion:*

Put to death willingly, and laid beneath the earth, O my Jesus, Fountain of life, Thou hast quickened me who was put to death by bitter transgression.

*Verse 22 (Psalm 118:22): Remove from me reproach and contempt, for I have sought out Thy testimonies.*

*Troparion:*

All creation was altered by Thy Passion; for all things suffered with Thee, O Word, knowing Thee to be the Upholder of all.

*Verse 23 (Psalm 118:23): For princes also sat and spoke against me, but Thy servant meditated on Thine ordinances.*

*Troparion:*

Having received into its belly the Rock of Life, all-devouring hades vomited forth the dead whom it had swallowed up from the ages.[^293]

*Verse 24 (Psalm 118:24): For Thy testimonies are my meditation, and Thine ordinances are my counsels.*

*Troparion:*

Thou wast laid in a new tomb, O Christ, and hast renewed human nature, having risen from the dead in divine majesty.

*Verse 25 (Psalm 118:25): My soul hath cleaved unto the earth; quicken me according to Thy word.*

*Troparion:*

Thou didst come down to earth that Thou mightest save Adam; and not finding him on earth, O Master, Thou didst descend seeking him even unto hades.

*Verse 26 (Psalm 118:26): I declared my ways, and Thou heardest me; teach me Thine ordinances.*

*Troparion:*

All the earth quaked with fear, O Word, and the day-star hid its rays, when Thy greatest Light was hidden in the earth.

*Verse 27 (Psalm 118:27): Make me to understand the way of Thine ordinances, and I will meditate on Thy wonders.*

*Troparion:*

As a man indeed Thou diest willingly, O Savior; but as God Thou hast raised up the dead from the tombs and from the depths of sin.

*Verse 28 (Psalm 118:28): My soul hath slumbered for heaviness; strengthen Thou me in Thy words.*

*Troparion:*

Shedding tearful lamentations over Thee as a mother, O Jesus, the Pure Virgin cried: How shall I bury Thee, O Savior?

*Verse 29 (Psalm 118:29): Remove from me the way of injustice, and have mercy on me by Thy law.*

*Troparion:*

Like a grain of wheat, having fallen into the furrows of the earth, Thou hast yielded an ear of many grains, raising up the mortals born of Adam.[^294]

*Verse 30 (Psalm 118:30): I have chosen the way of truth, and Thy judgments have I not forgotten.*

*Troparion:*

Under the earth hast Thou been hidden like the sun, and covered by the night of death; but arise, O Savior, shining more brightly!

*Verse 31 (Psalm 118:31): I have cleaved unto Thy testimonies; O Lord, put me not to shame.*

*Troparion:*

Even as the moon hids the circle of the sun, O Savior, so now the tomb hath hidden Thee, Who didst finish Thy course by death in the flesh.

*Verse 32 (Psalm 118:32): I ran the way of Thy commandments, when Thou didst enlarge my heart.*

*Troparion:*

Christ, having tasted death, delivered mortals from death, and now grants life unto all.

*Verse 33 (Psalm 118:33): Teach me, O Lord, the way of Thine ordinances, and I will seek it out continually.*

*Troparion:*

Adam, of old put to death through envy, dost Thou bring back to life by Thy putting to death, O Savior, being revealed in the flesh as the New Adam.[^295]

*Verse 34 (Psalm 118:34): Give me understanding, and I will search out Thy law, and will keep it with my whole heart.*

*Troparion:*

Beholding Thee stretched out dead for our sake, the noetic orders were terrified, O Savior, covering themselves with their wings.

*Verse 35 (Psalm 118:35): Guide me in the path of Thy commandments, for therein is my desire.*

*Troparion:*

Having taken Thee down dead from the Tree, O Word, Joseph hath now laid Thee in a tomb; but arise, saving all, as God!

*Verse 36 (Psalm 118:36): Incline my heart unto Thy testimonies, and not to covetousness.*

*Troparion:*

Having been the joy of the Angels, O Savior, Thou hast now become the cause of grief to them, being seen in the flesh as a breathless corpse.

*Verse 37 (Psalm 118:37): Turn away mine eyes that they may not behold vanity; quicken me in Thy way.*

*Troparion:*

Thou wast lifted up upon the Tree, and Thou raisest up living men with Thyself; and having gone beneath the earth, Thou dost resurrect them that lie under it.

*Verse 38 (Psalm 118:38): Establish Thy word unto Thy servant in Thy fear.*

*Troparion:*

Like a lion having fallen asleep in the flesh, O Savior, as a young lion Thou arisest from the dead, having put off the old age of the flesh.[^296]

*Verse 39 (Psalm 118:39): Take away my reproach which I have feared, for Thy judgments are good.*

*Troparion:*

Thou wast pierced in Thy ribs, O Thou Who tookest the rib of Adam, from whence Thou didst fashion Eve, and didst pour forth cleansing streams.[^297]

*Verse 40 (Psalm 118:40): Behold, I have longed after Thy commandments; quicken me in Thy righteousness.*

*Troparion:*

In mystery indeed of old was the lamb sacrificed; but Thou, having been openly sacrificed, O Guileless One, hast cleansed all creation, O Savior.

*Verse 41 (Psalm 118:41): Let Thy mercy come also unto me, O Lord, even Thy salvation, according to Thy word.*

*Troparion:*

Who shall declare this dread and truly new manner? For He Who rules over creation today accepts the Passion, and dies for our sake.

*Verse 42 (Psalm 118:42): So shall I have an answer for them that reproach me, for I trust in Thy words.*

*Troparion:*

The Angels, astonished, cried out: How is the Treasure of life seen dead? How is God enclosed in a tomb?

*Verse 43 (Psalm 118:43): And take not the word of truth utterly out of my mouth, for I have hoped in Thy judgments.*

*Troparion:*

From Thy pierced side, O Savior, Thou dost drop forth life for the life of him who was saved by life, and dost quicken me with Him.

*Verse 44 (Psalm 118:44): So shall I keep Thy law continually, for ever and unto ages of ages.*

*Troparion:*

Stretched out upon the Tree, Thou hast gathered men together; and pierced in Thy side, Thou pourest forth life-giving remission unto all, O Jesus.

*Verse 45 (Psalm 118:45): And I walked at liberty, for I sought Thy commandments.*

*Troparion:*

The noble Joseph, O Savior, figures forth a dread mystery, and buris Thee nobly as a corpse, and is struck with dread at Thy fearsome image.

*Verse 46 (Psalm 118:46): I spoke also of Thy testimonies before kings, and was not ashamed.*

*Troparion:*

Having gone down of Thine own will beneath the earth as dead, Thou leadest up from earth unto heavenly realms those who had fallen thence, O Jesus.

*Verse 47 (Psalm 118:47): And I meditated on Thy commandments, which I have loved exceedingly.*

*Troparion:*

Though Thou wast seen dead, yet as living God Thou leadest up from earth unto heavenly realms those who had fallen thence, O Jesus.

*Verse 48 (Psalm 118:48): My hands also have I lifted up unto Thy commandments, which I have loved.*

*Troparion:*

Though Thou wast seen dead, yet living as God, Thou hast quickened dead men, having slain him who put me to death.

*Verse 49 (Psalm 118:49): And I meditated on Thine ordinances.*

*Troparion:*

O what joy! O what manifold sweetness, with which Thou didst fill them that are in hades, having shone forth light in its dark depths!

*Verse 50 (Psalm 118:50): Remember Thy words unto Thy servant, upon which Thou hast caused me to hope.*

*Troparion:*

I bow down before Thy Passion, I hymn Thy Burial, I magnify Thy power, O Lover of mankind, whereby I have been delivered from corrupting passions.

*Verse 51 (Psalm 118:51): This has comforted me in my humiliation, for Thy word hath quickened me.*

*Troparion:*

Against Thee was a sword drawn, O Christ; and the sword of the strong one is truly blunted, and the flaming sword of Eden is turned back.[^298]

*Verse 52 (Psalm 118:52): The proud have transgressed exceedingly, yet have I not declined from Thy law.*

*Troparion:*

The Ewe beholding her Lamb drawn to slaughter, pierced with sorrow, cried aloud, moving the flock also to cry with her.[^299]

*Verse 53 (Psalm 118:53): I remembered Thy judgments of old, O Lord, and was comforted.*

*Troparion:*

Though Thou art buried in a tomb, though Thou goest down into hades, yet Thou hast emptied the tombs and stripped hades bare, O Christ!

*Verse 54 (Psalm 118:54): Sorrow took hold on me from sinners that forsake Thy law.*

*Troparion:*

Willingly didst Thou descend beneath the earth, O Savior; Thou hast quickened dead men, and brought them up into the Father's glory.

*Verse 55 (Psalm 118:55): Thy statutes were my songs in the place of my pilgrimage.*

*Troparion:*

One of the Trinity in the flesh endured a shameful death for our sake; the sun is struck with horror, and the earth doth tremble.

*Verse 56 (Psalm 118:56): I remembered Thy name in the night, O Lord, and kept Thy law.*

*Troparion:*

As from a bitter spring, the offspring of the tribe of Judah laid Jesus in a pit, the Fosterer and Giver of the manna.

*Verse 57 (Psalm 118:57): This I had, because I sought out Thine ordinances.*

*Troparion:*

The Judge stood as one judged before the judge Pilate, and was unjustly condemned to death upon the wood of the Cross.

*Verse 58 (Psalm 118:58): Thou art my portion, O Lord; I said that I would keep Thy law.*

*Troparion:*

O proud Israel, murderous people, what suffered ye, that ye released Barabbas and delivered the Savior to the Cross?

*Verse 59 (Psalm 118:59): I entreated Thy face with my whole heart; be merciful unto me according to Thy word.*

*Troparion:*

Having fashioned Adam from the earth with Thy hand, for his sake Thou becamest man in nature, and wast crucified of Thine own will.

*Verse 60 (Psalm 118:60): I thought on Thy ways, and turned my feet unto Thy testimonies.*

*Troparion:*

Obedient unto Thy Father, O Word, Thou didst descend even unto cruel hades, and didst resurrect the human race.

*Verse 61 (Psalm 118:61): I made haste, and delayed not, to keep Thy commandments.*

*Troparion:*

Woe is me, Light of the world! Woe is me, my Light, my beloved Jesus! cried the Virgin, weeping bitterly.

*Verse 62 (Psalm 118:62): The cords of sinners have entangled me, but I have not forgotten Thy law.*

*Troparion:*

Envious, murderous, and proud people, let them at least be ashamed before the winding-sheet and the napkin itself, when Christ is risen![^300]

*Verse 63 (Psalm 118:63): At midnight I arose to give thanks unto Thee for the judgments of Thy righteousness.*

*Troparion:*

Come now, foul murderous disciple, and show me the disposition of thy malice, whereby thou becamest the betrayer of Christ.[^301]

*Verse 64 (Psalm 118:64): I am a companion of all them that fear Thee, and keep Thy commandments.*

*Troparion:*

Falsely pretendest thou to love mankind, wretched, blind, all-destructive, faithless traitor, who didst sell the myrrh for a price!

*Verse 65 (Psalm 118:65): The earth, O Lord, is full of Thy mercy; teach me Thine ordinances.*

*Troparion:*

What price hadst thou for the priceless heavenly Myrrh? What didst thou receive worthy of Him? Madness didst thou gain, most accursed Satan!

*Verse 66 (Psalm 118:66): Thou hast dealt kindly with Thy servant, O Lord, according to Thy word.*

*Troparion:*

If thou art a lover of the poor, and grievest over the myrrh poured out for the purification of the soul, how sellest thou the Radiant One for gold?

*Verse 67 (Psalm 118:67): Teach me goodness, and discipline, and knowledge, for I have believed Thy commandments.*

*Troparion:*

O Word of God! O my Joy! How shall I endure Thy three-day Burial? Now am I rent in my maternal bowels.

*Verse 68 (Psalm 118:68): Before I was humbled I went astray, therefore have I kept Thy word.*

*Troparion:*

Who will give me water and fountains of tears, cried the Maiden Bride of God, that I may weep for my sweet Jesus?

*Verse 69 (Psalm 118:69): Good art Thou, O Lord, and in Thy goodness teach me Thine ordinances.*

*Troparion:*

O ye mountains and hills, and multitudes of men, weep, and wail all together with me, the Mother of your God!

*Verse 70 (Psalm 118:70): The iniquity of the proud is multiplied against me, but I will search out Thy commandments with my whole heart.*

*Troparion:*

When shall I behold Thee, O Savior, Light timeless, the joy and sweetness of my heart? the Virgin cried bitterly.

*Verse 71 (Psalm 118:70b): Their heart is curdled like milk, but I have meditated on Thy law.*

*Troparion:*

Though as a quarry-cut rock, O Savior, Thou didst receive a cutting, yet Thou hast poured forth a living stream, being Thyself the Fount of life.[^302]

*Verse 72 (Psalm 118:71): It is good for me that Thou hast humbled me, that I might learn Thine ordinances.*

*Troparion:*

As from a single spring, drinking of the two-fold river pouring forth from Thy side, we acquire immortal life.[^303]

*Verse 73 (Psalm 118:72): The law of Thy mouth is good unto me, above thousands of gold and silver.*

*Troparion:*

Of Thine own will wast Thou seen dead in the tomb, O Word, yet Thou livest, and even as Thou didst foretell, O my Savior, Thou dost raise up mankind by Thy Resurrection!

*Glory:*

*Troparion:*

We hymn Thee, O Word, God of all, together with the Father and Thy Holy Spirit, and we glorify Thy divine Burial.

*Both now:*

*Theotokion:*

We call thee blessed, O pure Theotokos, and faithfully we honor the three-day Burial of thy Son and our God.

*And again the first refrain is sung by both choirs together:*

*Refrain:*

We magnify Thee, O Christ the Life-giver, Who yesterday wast buried for our sake, Who didst endure and dost most gloriously quicken the dead.

*Then, the Little Litany.*

*And the exclamation after the first Stasis:*

**Priest:** For blessed is Thy name, and glorified is Thy kingdom, of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, now and ever, and unto ages of ages.

**Choir:** Amen.

*And having censed, he begins the second stasis.*

*Second Stasis*[^304]

*The left choir begins in good order the troparion:*

*Troparion [without verse]:*

It is truly meet to magnify Thee, O Christ the Life-giver, Who didst smash the gates of hades, and destroy the tyranny of the devil.

*And again the choir of the first side sings the second troparion without a verse:*

*Troparion [without verse]:*

It is truly meet to magnify Thee, the Giver of life, Who didst stretch out Thy hands upon the Cross, and crush the power of the enemy.

*Verse 74 (Psalm 118:73): Thy hands have made me and fashioned me; give me understanding, and I will learn Thy commandments.*

*Troparion:*

It is truly meet to magnify Thee, Creator of all; for by Thy Sufferings we have attained dispassion, having been delivered from corruption.

*Verse 75 (Psalm 118:74): They that fear Thee will see me and rejoice, because I have hoped in Thy words.*

*Troparion:*

The earth was filled with dread, O Savior, and the sun hid itself, when Thou, the never-setting Light, O Christ, didst set in the tomb in the flesh.

*Verse 76 (Psalm 118:75): I know, O Lord, that Thy judgments are righteousness, and that in truth Thou hast humbled me.*

*Troparion:*

Thou hast fallen asleep, O Christ, with a natural life-giving sleep in the tomb, and from the heavy sleep of sin hast Thou awakened mankind.[^305]

*Verse 77 (Psalm 118:76): Let Thy mercy, I pray Thee, comfort me, according to Thy word unto Thy servant.*

*Troparion:*

Alone among women without pains did I bear Thee, my Child; but now I suffer unbearable pangs at Thy Passion, said the All-Pure.[^306]

*Verse 78 (Psalm 118:77): Let Thy compassions come unto me, and I shall live, for Thy law is my meditation.*

*Troparion:*

Beholding Thee on high, O Savior, being inseparably with the Father, yet below stretched out dead upon the earth, the Seraphim are struck with horror.

*Verse 79 (Psalm 118:78): Let the proud be ashamed, for they have transgressed against me unjustly, but I will meditate on Thy commandments.*

*Troparion:*

The veil of the temple is rent by Thy Crucifixion; the luminaries, O Word, conceal their light, when Thou, the Sun, art hidden under the earth.[^307]

*Verse 80 (Psalm 118:79): Let those that fear Thee turn unto me, and those that know Thy testimonies.*

*Troparion:*

He Who established the circle of the earth in the beginning by His nod alone, hath gone beneath the earth breathless as a man: be astonished, O heaven, at the sight!

*Verse 81 (Psalm 118:80): Let my heart be blameless in Thine ordinances, that I may not be put to shame.*

*Troparion:*

Thou hast gone down beneath the earth, O Thou Who didst fashion man with Thine own hand, that Thou mightest raise up from their fall the gatherings of men by Thine all-powerful might.

*Verse 82 (Psalm 118:81): My soul faints for Thy salvation, and I have hoped in Thy words.*

*Troparion:*

Come, let us chant a sacred lamentation unto Christ Who is dead, even as of old the myrrh-bearing women, that with them we also may hear: "Rejoice!"[^308]

*Verse 83 (Psalm 118:82): Mine eyes fail for Thy word, saying: When wilt Thou comfort me?*

*Troparion:*

Truly Thou art inexhaustible Myrrh, O Word; therefore unto Thee, the Living One, as unto one dead, the myrrh-bearing women brought myrrh.

*Verse 84 (Psalm 118:83): For I am become like a wineskin in the frost; yet have I not forgotten Thine ordinances.*

*Troparion:*

Buried, O Christ, Thou dost shatter the realms of hades, and by death Thou dost put death to death, and deliver from corruption the earthborn.

*Verse 85 (Psalm 118:84): How many are the days of Thy servant? When wilt Thou execute judgment on them that persecute me?*

*Troparion:*

The Wisdom of God, pouring forth streams of life, descending into the tomb, quickens them that dwell in the sunsetless realms of hades.

*Verse 86 (Psalm 118:85): Transgressors have told me fables, but not as Thy law, O Lord.*

*Troparion:*

"That I may renew shattered human nature, I am wounded by death willingly in the flesh; O My Mother, tear not thyself with lamentations."

*Verse 87 (Psalm 118:86): All Thy commandments are truth; they persecuted me wrongfully, help Thou me.*

*Troparion:*

Thou hast gone beneath the earth, O Light-bearer of righteousness, and hast raised up the dead as from sleep, driving away all the darkness that was in hades.

*Verse 88 (Psalm 118:87): They had almost made an end of me upon earth, but I forsook not Thy commandments.*

*Troparion:*

A twofold-sprouting grain, naturally living, is sown in the depths of the earth with tears today; but having sprouted, it gladdens the world.[^309]

*Verse 89 (Psalm 118:88): Quicken me according to Thy mercy, and I will keep the testimonies of Thy mouth.*

*Troparion:*

Adam was afraid when God walked in Paradise, but rejoices now when He hath descended into hades, he who fell of old and is now raised up.

*Verse 90 (Psalm 118:89): For ever, O Lord, Thy word abides in heaven.*

*Troparion:*

She who bore Thee, O Christ, offers tears as sacrifices unto Thee Who art laid in the tomb in the flesh, crying aloud: "Arise, O Child, even as Thou hast foretold!"

*Verse 91 (Psalm 118:90): Thy truth endures from generation to generation; Thou hast established the earth, and it abides.*

*Troparion:*

Joseph reverently hiding Thee in a new tomb, sings unto Thee, O Savior, funeral hymns meet for God, blended with lamentations.[^310]

*Verse 92 (Psalm 118:91): By Thine ordinance the day abides, for all things are Thy servants.*

*Troparion:*

Beholding Thee nailed to the Cross with nails, O Word, Thy Mother piercs her soul with nails of bitter grief and with arrows.

*Verse 93 (Psalm 118:92): Unless Thy law had been my meditation, I should then have perished in my humiliation.*

*Troparion:*

Beholding Thee, the Delight of all, made to drink of a bitter draught, Thy Mother bitterly moistens her face with tears.

*Verse 94 (Psalm 118:93): I will never forget Thine ordinances, for with them Thou hast quickened me.*

*Troparion:*

"Cruelly am I wounded, and torn in my maternal womb, O Word, beholding Thy wrongful slaughter," said the All-Pure with weeping.

*Verse 95 (Psalm 118:94): I am Thine, save me, for I have sought Thine ordinances.*

*Troparion:*

"How shall I close Thy sweet eyes and Thy lips, O Word? And how shall I bury Thee as a corpse? I am seized with dread!" cried Joseph.

*Verse 96 (Psalm 118:95): Sinners waited for me to destroy me, but I understood Thy testimonies.*

*Troparion:*

Joseph and Nicodemus now sing hymns at the tomb unto Christ Who is dead, and with them also sing the Seraphim.

*Verse 97 (Psalm 118:96): I have seen an end of all perfection, but Thy commandment is exceedingly broad.*

*Troparion:*

Thou dost set beneath the earth, O Savior, Sun of righteousness; therefore the Moon who bore Thee faints with sorrows, deprived of Thy countenance.

*Verse 98 (Psalm 118:97): How have I loved Thy law, O Lord! It is my meditation all the day.*

*Troparion:*

Hades was struck with terror, O Savior, beholding Thee, the Giver of life, despoiling its wealth and raising up the dead from the ages.

*Verse 99 (Psalm 118:98): Thou hast made me wiser than mine enemies by Thy commandment, for it is mine for ever.*

*Troparion:*

The sun shines forth its light after the night, O Word; and Thou having risen, shinest forth brightly after death, as from a bridal chamber.

*Verse 100 (Psalm 118:99): I have understood more than all my teachers, for Thy testimonies are my meditation.*

*Troparion:*

The earth, receiving Thee the Creator into its bosom, seized with trembling, O Savior, doth shake, having put the dead to sleep by its shaking.

*Verse 101 (Psalm 118:100): I have understood more than the ancients, because I sought Thy commandments.*

*Troparion:*

Nicodemus and the noble Joseph, now anointing Thee with myrrh in a manner newly meet, O Christ, cried aloud: "Shudder, all the earth!"[^311]

*Verse 102 (Psalm 118:101): I have restrained my feet from every evil way, that I might keep Thy words.*

*Troparion:*

Thou hast set, O Creator of light, and with Thee the light of the sun hath set; and creation is held in trembling, proclaiming Thee the Maker of all.

*Verse 103 (Psalm 118:102): I have not declined from Thy judgments, for Thou hast laid down a law for me.*

*Troparion:*

A smoothed stone covers the Cornerstone, and a mortal man covers God as dead now in the tomb: shudder, O earth![^312]

*Verse 104 (Psalm 118:103): How sweet are Thy words unto my throat! Yea, sweeter than honey unto my mouth!*

*Troparion:*

"Behold the disciple whom Thou lovedst, and Thy Mother, O Child, and grant a most sweet word!" cried the Pure One, weeping.

*Verse 105 (Psalm 118:104): Through Thy commandments I have gained understanding; therefore I have hated every way of unrighteousness.*

*Troparion:*

Thou, O Word, being the Giver of life, when stretched out upon the Cross, didst not put the Jews to death, but didst raise up even their dead.

*Verse 106 (Psalm 118:105): Thy law is a lamp unto my feet, and a light unto my paths.*

*Troparion:*

Thou hadst no beauty beforehand, O Word, nor comeliness, when Thou didst suffer; but having risen, Thou hast shone forth, adorning men with divine rays.

*Verse 107 (Psalm 118:106): I have sworn, and have determined, to keep the judgments of Thy righteousness.*

*Troparion:*

Thou hast set in the flesh into the earth, O Never-setting Light-bearer; and the sun, unable to endure the sight, was darkened, while it was yet midday.

*Verse 108 (Psalm 118:107): I have been humbled exceedingly, O Lord; quicken me according to Thy word.*

*Troparion:*

The sun and moon darkened together, O Savior, were figured forth as wise servants who clothe themselves in black garments.

*Verse 109 (Psalm 118:108): Accept, I beseech Thee, the freewill offerings of my mouth, O Lord, and teach me Thy judgments.*

*Troparion:*

The centurion saw Thee to be God, even though Thou wast put to death; "How then, O my God, shall I touch Thee with my hands? I am seized with dread!" cried Joseph.

*Verse 110 (Psalm 118:109): My soul is continually in Thy hands, and I have not forgotten Thy law.*

*Troparion:*

Adam fell asleep, but brought forth death from his rib; but Thou now hast fallen asleep, O Word of God, and pourest forth life unto the world from Thy side.

*Verse 111 (Psalm 118:110): Sinners have laid a snare for me, yet have I not strayed from Thy commandments.*

*Troparion:*

Thou didst fall asleep for a little while, and hast quickened them that were dead; and having risen, Thou hast raised up them that slept from the ages, O Good One.

*Verse 112 (Psalm 118:111): I have inherited Thy testimonies for ever, for they are the rejoicing of my heart.*

*Troparion:*

Thou wast taken from the earth, but Thou hast poured forth the wine of salvation, O Life-flowing Vine: I glorify Thy Passion and Thy Cross.[^313]

*Verse 113 (Psalm 118:112): I have inclined my heart to perform Thine ordinances for ever, for a reward.*

*Troparion:*

How do the spiritual choirs of angels hymn Thee, O Savior, stripped naked, blood-stained, and condemned, enduring the audacity of Thy crucifiers?

*Verse 114 (Psalm 118:113): I have hated transgressors, but Thy law have I loved.*

*Troparion:*

O doomed, most crooked race of the Hebrews, thou knewest of the raising up of the Temple; therefore why didst thou condemn Christ?[^314]

*Verse 115 (Psalm 118:114): Thou art my helper and my protector; I have hoped in Thy words.*

*Troparion:*

In a garment of mockery dost thou clothe the Adorner of all, Who established the heaven and adorned the earth marvelously.

*Verse 116 (Psalm 118:115): Depart from me, ye evildoers, and I will search out the commandments of my God.*

*Troparion:*

Even as the pelican wounded in its side, O Word, Thou hast quickened Thy dead children, dropping down living streams upon them.[^315]

*Verse 117 (Psalm 118:116): Uphold me according to Thy word, and I shall live, and put me not to shame because of my expectation.*

*Troparion:*

Of old Joshua stayed the sun while smiting the foreigners; but Thou hast concealed Thyself, casting down the ruler of darkness.[^316]

*Verse 118 (Psalm 118:117): Help Thou me, and I shall be saved, and I will meditate on Thine ordinances continually.*

*Troparion:*

Without departing from the Father's bosom, O Compassionate One, Thou wast well pleased to become man, and didst descend into hades, O Christ.

*Verse 119 (Psalm 118:118): Thou hast set at naught all them that depart from Thine ordinances, for their inward thought is unrighteous.*

*Troparion:*

He Who was crucified was lifted up, He Who suspended the earth upon the waters; and as one breathless He now lies in it, which unable to bear, shook violently.

*Verse 120 (Psalm 118:119): I have accounted all the sinners of the earth as transgressors; therefore I have loved Thy testimonies.*

*Troparion:*

"Woe is me, O my Son!" lamented the Virgin who knew not wedlock, saying: "For He in Whom I hoped as King, I now behold condemned upon the Cross."

*Verse 121 (Psalm 118:120): Nail my flesh with the fear of Thee, for I am afraid of Thy judgments.*

*Troparion:*

"These things Gabriel announced unto me when he flew down, who spoke of the eternal Kingdom of Jesus my Son."

*Verse 122 (Psalm 118:121): I have executed judgment and righteousness; leave me not unto them that wrong me.*

*Troparion:*

"O woe! Simeon's prophecy is accomplished: for Thy sword hath passed through my heart, O Emmanuel!"[^317]

*Verse 123 (Psalm 118:122): Receive Thy servant unto good, let not the proud accuse me falsely.*

*Troparion:*

Be ashamed at least before the dead, O Jews, whom the Giver of life raised up, Whom ye slew out of envy!

*Verse 124 (Psalm 118:123): Mine eyes have failed for Thy salvation, and for the word of Thy righteousness.*

*Troparion:*

The sun was struck with dread, O Invisible Light, beholding Thee, my Christ, hidden in the tomb, breathless; and it darkened its light.

*Verse 125 (Psalm 118:124): Deal with Thy servant according to Thy mercy, and teach me Thine ordinances.*

*Troparion:*

Thy most pure Mother wept bitterly, O Word, when in the tomb she beheld Thee, the ineffable and unoriginate God.

*Verse 126 (Psalm 118:125): I am Thy servant; give me understanding, and I shall know Thy testimonies.*

*Troparion:*

Beholding Thy deadness, O incorruptible Christ, Thy Mother bitterly spoke unto Thee: "Tarry not, O Life, among the dead!"

*Verse 127 (Psalm 118:126): It is time for the Lord to act, for they have made void Thy law.*

*Troparion:*

Cruel hades trembled when it saw Thee, O Sun of glory, Immortal One, and gave up its captives in haste.

*Verse 128 (Psalm 118:127): Therefore have I loved Thy commandments above gold and topaz.*

*Troparion:*

A great and dread sight is now beheld! The Author of life hath undergone death, willing to quicken all.

*Verse 129 (Psalm 118:128): Therefore I directed myself according to all Thy commandments; I have hated every way of unrighteousness.*

*Troparion:*

Thou art pierced in Thy side, and art nailed by Thy hands, O Master, healing the wound of the side and the intemperance of the hands of our forebears.[^318]

*Verse 130 (Psalm 118:129): Marvelous are Thy testimonies; therefore hath my soul searched them out.*

*Troparion:*

Of old every one in the house wept for the son of Rachel; and for the Son of the Virgin the company of disciples lamented with His Mother.[^319]

*Verse 131 (Psalm 118:130): The declaration of Thy words gives light, and gives understanding unto infants.*

*Troparion:*

Blows of the hands they gave unto the cheek of Christ, Who fashioned man with His hand, and shattered the jaws of the wild beast.

*Verse 132 (Psalm 118:131): I opened my mouth, and drew in breath, for I longed for Thy commandments.*

*Troparion:*

With hymns we all the faithful celebrate Thy Crucifixion and Burial now, O Christ, having been delivered from death by Thy Burial.

*Glory, Triadikon:*

*Troparion:*

O unoriginate God, co-eternal Word, and Holy Spirit: strengthen the sceptre of the Orthodox Emperor against adversaries, for Thou art good.[^320]

*Both now, Theotokion:*

*Theotokion:*

O all-pure Virgin who didst bear the Life, appease ecclesiastical scandals, and grant peace, for thou art good.

*And again the first troparion is sung by both choirs:*

*Troparion:*

It is truly meet to magnify Thee, O Christ the Life-giver, Who didst smash the gates of hades, and destroy the tyranny of the devil.

*Then, the Little Litany.*

*Exclamation after the Second Stasis:*

**Priest:** For holy art Thou, our God, Who restest upon the Cherubimic throne of glory, and unto Thee do we send up glory, together with Thine unoriginate Father, and Thy most-holy, good, and life-creating Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages.

**Choir:** Amen.

*And we begin the Third Stasis, as the priest censes.*

*Third Stasis*[^321]

*Again the right choir, that is, the first choir, begins with sweet chanting, in Tone 3:*

*Troparion [without verse]:*

Every generation, O my Christ, offers a hymn unto Thy Burial.

*Verse 133 (Psalm 118:132): Look upon me, and be merciful unto me, according to the judgment of them that love Thy name.*

*Troparion:*

Taking Thee down from the Tree, he of Arimathea wrapps Thee in a winding-sheet and buris Thee in a tomb.

*Verse 134 (Psalm 118:133): Order my steps according to Thy word, and let no iniquity have dominion over me.*

*Troparion:*

The myrrh-bearing women came, bringing myrrh unto Thee, O my Christ, most wisely.

*Verse 135 (Psalm 118:134): Deliver me from the false accusation of men, and I will keep Thy commandments.*

*Troparion:*

Come, all creation, let us offer funeral hymns unto the Creator!

*Verse 136 (Psalm 118:135): Make Thy face to shine upon Thy servant, and teach me Thine ordinances.*

*Troparion:*

With the myrrh-bearing women let us all wisely anoint the Living One as dead.

*Verse 137 (Psalm 118:136): Mine eyes have sent forth streams of water, because I kept not Thy law.*

*Troparion:*

O thrice-blessed Joseph, bury the Body of Christ the Giver of life!

*Verse 138 (Psalm 118:137): Righteous art Thou, O Lord, and upright are Thy judgments.*

*Troparion:*

Those whom He nourished with manna lifted up their heel against their Benefactor.

*Verse 139 (Psalm 118:138): Thou hast commanded righteousness in Thy testimonies, and truth exceedingly.*

*Troparion:*

Those whom He nourished with manna bring gall together with vinegar unto the Savior.[^322]

*Verse 140 (Psalm 118:139): Thy zeal hath consumed me, because mine enemies have forgotten Thy words.*

*Troparion:*

O what madness and Christ-murder of the prophet-slayers!

*Verse 141 (Psalm 118:140): Thy word is tried exceedingly, and Thy servant hath loved it.*

*Troparion:*

As a senseless servant, the disciple betrayed the Abyss of Wisdom.

*Verse 142 (Psalm 118:141): I am young and despised, yet have I not forgotten Thine ordinances.*

*Troparion:*

Having forsaken the Redeemer, treacherous Judas was left a captive.

*Verse 143 (Psalm 118:142): Thy righteousness is an everlasting righteousness, and Thy law is truth.*

*Troparion:*

According to Solomon, the mouth of unlawful Hebrews is a deep pit.[^323]

*Verse 144 (Psalm 118:143): Afflictions and necessities have found me; Thy commandments are my meditation.*

*Troparion:*

In the crooked paths of the unlawful Hebrews are thistles and snares.

*Verse 145 (Psalm 118:144): Thy testimonies are righteousness for ever; give me understanding, and I shall live.*

*Troparion:*

Joseph with Nicodemus buris the Creator as a corpse.

*Verse 146 (Psalm 118:145): I cried with my whole heart, hear me, O Lord; I will search out Thine ordinances.*

*Troparion:*

O Savior, Giver of life, glory to Thy dominion which hath destroyed hades!

*Verse 147 (Psalm 118:146): I cried unto Thee; save me, and I will keep Thy testimonies.*

*Troparion:*

Beholding Thee lying dead, O Word, the All-Pure wept with maternal love.

*Verse 148 (Psalm 118:147): I prevented the unseasonable hour, and cried; I hoped in Thy words.*

*Troparion:*

"O my sweetest Springtime, O my sweetest Child! Whither hath Thy beauty set?"[^324]

*Verse 149 (Psalm 118:148): Mine eyes prevented the morning dawn, that I might meditate on Thy words.*

*Troparion:*

Thy most pure Mother stirred up lamentation when Thou, O Word, wast dead.

*Verse 150 (Psalm 118:149): Hear my voice, O Lord, according to Thy mercy; quicken me according to Thy judgment.*

*Troparion:*

The women came with myrrh to anoint Christ, the divine Myrrh.

*Verse 151 (Psalm 118:150): They that persecute me wrongfully have drawn nigh, but are far removed from Thy law.*

*Troparion:*

By death Thou dost put death to death, O my God, by Thy divine power.

*Verse 152 (Psalm 118:151): Near art Thou, O Lord, and all Thy paths are truth.*

*Troparion:*

The deceiver is deceived, and he who was deceived is delivered by Thy wisdom, O my God.

*Verse 153 (Psalm 118:152): From the beginning I have known from Thy testimonies, that Thou hast founded them for ever.*

*Troparion:*

Unto the bottom of hades was the betrayer brought down, into the pit of corruption.

*Verse 154 (Psalm 118:153): Behold my humiliation, and deliver me, for I have not forgotten Thy law.*

*Troparion:*

Thistles and snares are the paths of thrice-wretched, senseless Judas.

*Verse 155 (Psalm 118:154): Judge my cause, O Lord, and deliver me; quicken me for Thy word's sake.*

*Troparion:*

All Thy crucifiers stumble, O Word, Son of God, King of all.
