*Verse 156 (Psalm 118:155): Salvation is far from sinners, for they have not searched out Thine ordinances.*

*Troparion:*

Into the pit of corruption all the men of blood stumble.

*Verse 157 (Psalm 118:156): Many are Thy compassions, O Lord; quicken me according to Thy judgment.*

*Troparion:*

"O Son of God, King of all, my God, my Creator, how hast Thou undergone the Passion?"

*Verse 158 (Psalm 118:157): Many are they that persecute me and afflict me, yet have I not declined from Thy testimonies.*

*Troparion:*

The Heifer cried aloud, beholding the Calf suspended upon the Tree.[^325]

*Verse 159 (Psalm 118:158): I beheld them that deal foolishly, and was grieved, because they kept not Thy words.*

*Troparion:*

Joseph buris the Life-bearing Body with Nicodemus.

*Verse 160 (Psalm 118:159): Behold how I have loved Thy commandments, O Lord; quicken me according to Thy mercy.*

*Troparion:*

The Maiden cried aloud, shedding fervent tears, pierced through in her maternal heart.

*Verse 161 (Psalm 118:160): The beginning of Thy words is truth, and all the judgments of Thy righteousness endure for ever.*

*Troparion:*

"O Light of mine eyes, my sweetest Child, how art Thou now hidden in a tomb?"

*Verse 162 (Psalm 118:161): Princes persecuted me without a cause, and my heart was afraid because of Thy words.*

*Troparion:*

"To free Adam and Eve, O Mother, weep not: these things I suffer."

*Verse 163 (Psalm 118:162): I will rejoice in Thy words, as one that finds great spoil.*

*Troparion:*

"I glorify Thine extreme compassion, O my Son, for the sake of which Thou sufferest these things."

*Verse 164 (Psalm 118:163): I have hated and abhorred iniquity, but Thy law have I loved.*

*Troparion:*

Thou wast made to drink vinegar and gall, O Compassionate One, undoing the ancient tasting.

*Verse 165 (Psalm 118:164): Seven times a day have I praised Thee, because of the judgments of Thy righteousness.*

*Troparion:*

Thou wast nailed upon the Cross, Who of old didst cover Thy people with a pillar of cloud.[^326]

*Verse 166 (Psalm 118:165): Great peace have they that love Thy law, and there is no stumbling block for them.*

*Troparion:*

The myrrh-bearing women, having come unto the tomb, O Savior, brought myrrh unto Thee.

*Verse 167 (Psalm 118:166): I have looked for Thy salvation, O Lord, and have loved Thy commandments.*

*Troparion:*

Arise, O Compassionate One, raising us up from the abysses of hades!

*Verse 168 (Psalm 118:167): My soul hath kept Thy testimonies, and loved them exceedingly.*

*Troparion:*

"Arise, O Giver of life!" saith the Mother who bore Thee, shedding tears.

*Verse 169 (Psalm 118:168): I have kept Thy commandments and Thy testimonies, for all my ways are before Thee, O Lord.*

*Troparion:*

Hasten to arise, dispelling the sorrow, O Word, of her who purely gave Thee birth.

*Verse 170 (Psalm 118:169): Let my supplication draw near before Thee, O Lord; give me understanding according to Thy word.*

*Troparion:*

The Heavenly Powers were seized with fear, beholding Thee dead.

*Verse 171 (Psalm 118:170): Let my request come before Thee, O Lord; deliver me according to Thy word.*

*Troparion:*

Unto them that honor Thy Sufferings with love and fear, grant remission of transgressions.

*Verse 172 (Psalm 118:171): My lips shall pour forth praise, when Thou hast taught me Thine ordinances.*

*Troparion:*

O dread and fearful sight, O Word of God! How doth the earth cover Thee?

*Verse 173 (Psalm 118:172): My tongue shall declare Thy words, for all Thy commandments are righteousness.*

*Troparion:*

He who carried Thee of old, O Savior, Joseph, flees; and now another buris Thee.[^327]

*Verse 174 (Psalm 118:173): Let Thy hand be forth to save me, for I have chosen Thy commandments.*

*Troparion:*

Thy most pure Mother weeps and laments over Thee, my Savior, Who art dead.

*Verse 175 (Psalm 118:174): I have longed for Thy salvation, O Lord, and Thy law is my meditation.*

*Troparion:*

Minds are astonished at the strange and dreadful Burial of Thee, the Creator of all.

*Verse 176 (Psalm 118:175): My soul shall live, and shall praise Thee, and Thy judgments shall help me.*

*Troparion:*

The myrrh-bearing women poured out myrrh upon the Tomb, having come exceedingly early.

*Verse 177 (Psalm 118:176): I have gone astray like a lost sheep; seek Thy servant, for I have not forgotten Thy commandments.*

*Troparion:*

Grant peace unto the Church, and salvation unto Thy people, by Thy Rising![^328]

*Glory:*

*Troparion:*

O Trinity, my God: Father, Son, and Spirit, have mercy upon the world!

*Both now:*

*Theotokion:*

To behold the Resurrection of thy Son, O Virgin, vouchsafe thy servants.

*And immediately we sing these Troparia, in Tone 5:*[^329]

*Verse:* Blessed art Thou, O Lord, teach me Thine ordinances.

*Troparion:*

The assembly of the Angels was amazed, beholding Thee accounted among the dead, yet shattering the power of death, O Savior, and with Thyself raising up Adam, and delivering all from hades.

*Verse:* Blessed art Thou, O Lord, teach me Thine ordinances.

*Troparion:*

"Why mingle ye myrrh with compassionate tears, O disciples?" the radiant Angel within the tomb spoke unto the Myrrh-bearing women; "Behold the tomb and understand, for the Savior is risen from the tomb!"[^330]

*Verse:* Blessed art Thou, O Lord, teach me Thine ordinances.

*Troparion:*

Exceedingly early the Myrrh-bearing women ran unto Thy tomb lamenting, but an Angel stood before them and said: "The time for lamentation hath ceased; weep not, but announce the Resurrection unto the Apostles!"

*Verse:* Blessed art Thou, O Lord, teach me Thine ordinances.

*Troparion:*

The Myrrh-bearing women, having come unto Thy tomb with myrrh, O Savior, lamented; but an Angel spoke unto them, saying: "Why think ye of the Living among the dead? For as God He is risen from the tomb!"

*Glory, Triadikon:*

*Troparion:*

Let us worship the Father, and His Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Holy Trinity in one essence, crying with the Seraphim: "Holy, holy, holy art Thou, O Lord!"

*Both now, Theotokion:*

*Theotokion:*

Having borne the Giver of life, O Virgin, thou hast delivered Adam from sin, and given joy unto Eve in place of sorrow; and He Who was incarnate from thee, God and man, hath directed unto this life them that had fallen from it.

*Alleluia, alleluia, alleluia, glory to Thee, O God. (Three times)*

*Then, the Little Litany.*

*Exclamation:*

**Priest:** For Thou art the King of peace, O Christ our God, and unto Thee do we send up glory, together with Thine unoriginate Father, and Thy most-holy, good, and life-creating Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages.

**Choir:** Amen.

*And we sing:*

*And we sing the Sessional Hymn of the day, Tone 1:*

*Tone 1, Podoben: "Thy Tomb, O Savior" (Гро́бъ тво́й, Сп҃се):*

Having begged Thy precious Body of Pilate, Joseph anoints It with divine spices and with a clean shroud, and lays It in a new tomb; therefore, rising early, the myrrh-bearing women cried aloud: "Show unto us, as Thou hast foretold, O Christ, the Resurrection!"

*Glory, the ending:* "therefore, rising early, the myrrh-bearing women cried aloud: 'Show unto us, as Thou hast foretold, O Christ, the Resurrection!'"

*Both now, another sessional hymn:*

*Tone 1, Podoben: "Thy Tomb, O Savior" (Гро́бъ тво́й, Сп҃се):*

The angelic choirs were struck with awe, beholding Him Who sits in the bosom of the Father, how He is laid in the tomb as dead, the Immortal One; Whom the angelic hosts surround, and they glorify Him with the dead in hades as Creator and Lord.

*Then takes place the reading in the Commentary on the Gospel of Matthew, pericope 114:*

*On the next day, which is after Friday: The Resurrection of Christ: we do not say. But immediately Psalm 50.*

*And we sing the Canon:*

*The Heirmos twice, and the troparia for 12. And again after them the Heirmos by both choirs together. And this Canon, from the First Ode unto the Sixth, is the creation of Mark the Monk, Bishop of Idrus. The Heirmoi are the creation of a certain woman named Kassia. And from the Sixth Ode unto the end, the creation of the Lord Cosmas.[^331]*

*The acrostic of the Canon: Καὶ σήμερον δὲ Σάββατον μέλπω μέγα, which is: And today also, I sing the Great Sabbath.[^332]*

*Tone 6. Ode 1. Heirmos:*

The children of the saved concealed under the earth Him Who of old hid the pursuing tyrant beneath the waves of the sea; but let us, as the maidens, sing unto the Lord, for He is gloriously glorified.

*Troparion:*

O Lord my God, an exodus hymn and a funeral song will I sing unto Thee, Who by Thy Burial hast opened the portals of my life, and by death hast put death and hades to death.

*Troparion:*

Beholding Thee upon the throne on high, and below in the tomb, things celestial and subterranean, O my Savior, were shaken by Thy putting to death; for beyond understanding wast Thou seen dead, O Author of life.

*Troparion:*

That Thou mightest fill all things with Thy glory, Thou didst descend into the lowest parts of the earth; for from Thee my nature that was in Adam was not hidden, and being buried, Thou dost renew me corrupted, O Lover of mankind.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

Creation, beholding Thee Who didst suspend all the earth unrestrained upon the waters, hanging upon Golgotha, was seized with great awe and shook, crying aloud: "There is none holy save Thee, O Lord!"

*Troparion:*

Figures of Thy burial Thou didst show forth, having multiplied visions; but now Thou hast made plain Thy hidden mysteries as God-man, even unto them that are in hades, O Master, who cry aloud: "There is none holy save Thee, O Lord!"

*Troparion:*

Thou didst stretch out Thy hands, and didst unite things that before were sundered; and by the vesture of the shroud and the tomb, O Savior, Thou hast released them that were bound, who cry aloud: "There is none holy save Thee, O Lord!"

*Troparion:*

By tomb and by seals, O Incomprehensible One, Thou wast held willingly; for Thou hast shown forth Thy power by deeds unto them that chant with divine operation: "There is none holy save Thee, O Lord, Lover of mankind!"

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 1:*

The soldiers guarding Thy tomb, O Savior, became as dead from the radiance of the Angel that appeared, who announced the Resurrection unto the women; Thee do we glorify, the Destroyer of corruption; before Thee do we fall down, Who hast risen from the tomb, and our only God.

*Glory, both now: The same.*

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*

Foreseeing Thy divine emptying upon the Cross, Habakkuk cried aloud in amazement: "Thou hast cut through the power of the mighty, O Good One, communing with them that are in hades, as Almighty!"

*Troparion:*

Today Thou hast sanctified the seventh day, which of old Thou didst bless by the resting from works; for Thou dost transform and renew all things, keeping the Sabbath, O my Savior, and rebuilding.

*Troparion:*

By the power of the better part, Thy soul was separated from the flesh that held Thee captive; for tearing asunder both bonds, of death and hades, O Word, by Thy mighty power.[^333]

*Troparion:*

Hades was embittered, O Word, meeting Thee; beholding a deified man, wounded with stripes, and the All-powerful Worker; and at the dreadful sight it perished.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

Beholding the sunsetless Light of Thy divine manifestation, O Christ, which in mercy was shown unto us, Isaiah, having risen early from the night, cried aloud: "The dead shall rise, and they that are in the tombs shall be raised up, and all the earthborn shall rejoice!"

*Troparion:*

Thou dost renew the earthborn, O Creator, having become dust; and the shroud and the tomb show forth the mystery that is in Thee, O Word; for the noble counsellor figurs the counsel of Him that begat Thee, Who in Thee magnificently renews me.

*Troparion:*

By death Thou dost transform the mortal, and by Burial the corruptible; for Thou makest incorruptible, in a manner meet for God, making immortal that which Thou didst assume; for Thy flesh saw not corruption, O Master, nor was Thy soul left in hades in marvelous-meet manner.[^334]

*Troparion:*

Coming forth from the unwedded Virgin, and pierced in the rib, O my Creator, from it Thou hast wrought the renewal of Eve, having become Adam, having slept beyond nature a natural sleep, and having raised up life from sleep and corruption, as Almighty.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

Jonah was seized, but not held fast in the belly of the whale; for bearing the figure of Thee, Who didst suffer and gavest Thyself unto burial, he came forth from the monster as from a bridal chamber; and he cried aloud unto the guard: "Ye that observe vain and false things, have forsaken your own mercy!"

*Troparion:*

Thou wast struck, but wast not divided, O Word, from the flesh whereof Thou didst partake; for even if Thy temple was dissolved at the time of the Passion, yet even so one was the hypostasis of Thy Divinity and Thy Flesh; for in both Thou art one Son, the Word of God, God and man.[^335]

*Troparion:*

Man-slaying, but not God-slaying, was the transgression of Adam; for even if the earthly substance of Thy flesh suffered, yet the Divinity remained impassible; and Thy corruptible nature Thou didst translate unto incorruption, and by the Resurrection Thou didst reveal the fountain of incorruptible life.

*Troparion:*

Hades reigns, but it doth not endure for ever over the human race; for Thou being laid in the tomb, O Mighty One, with life-bearing palm hast shattered the keys of death, and didst proclaim unto them that slept there from the ages true deliverance, having become, O Savior, the Firstborn of the dead.

*Kontakion, Tone 6:*

He Who shut up the abyss is seen dead, and bound with myrrh and a shroud, the Immortal One is laid in a tomb as mortal; and women came to anoint Him with myrrh, weeping bitterly and crying aloud: "This is the most blessed Sabbath, wherein Christ, having fallen asleep, shall rise on the third day!"

*Ikos:*

He Who upholds all things was lifted up upon the Cross, and all creation weeps, beholding Him hanging naked upon the Tree; the sun hid its rays, and the stars withdrew their light; and the earth was shaken with great fear, and the sea fled, and the rocks were split; and many tombs were opened, and the bodies of the holy men arose; hades groans below, and the Jews take counsel to slander Christ's Resurrection; but the women cry aloud: "This is the most blessed Sabbath, wherein Christ, having fallen asleep, shall rise on the third day!"

*Synaxarion:*[^336]

*Verse:* In vain dost thou guard the tomb, O watch:  
For the sepulchre cannot contain the Fountain of Life.

On Holy and Great Saturday, we celebrate the godly-bodily Burial of our Lord, God, and Savior Jesus Christ, and His Descent into hades, whereby our race, having been recalled from corruption, passed over unto eternal life.

For holy Forty Days surpasses all days, and this Holy and Great Week is greater than it, and this Great and Holy Saturday is greater than Great Week. It is called Great Week, not because these days or hours are larger, but because great and supernatural wonders and preeminent deeds of our Savior were wrought therein, and especially on this day.

For even as at the first creation of the world, God, having wrought every work, and lastly on the sixth day having created man, the most efficacious work, on the seventh day rested from all His works, and sanctified it, naming it Sabbath, which is interpreted as rest: so also in the cultivation of the spiritual world, having preeminently wrought all things, and on the sixth day having recreated corrupted man and renewed him by the life-bearing Cross and death, on this present seventh day again He rested with a perfect rest from works, having fallen asleep with a life-natural and saving sleep.

The Word of God, therefore, descends with the Flesh into the tomb, and descends also into hades with His incorruptible and divine Soul, which was separated by death from the Body, which also He delivered into the hands of the Father, unto Whom also He offered His Blood, which became our redemption, without being asked. For the Soul of the Lord was not held fast in hades as the souls of other saints; for how could it be? Having brought nothing of the ancestral curse, as they did. Nor did our enemy the devil take His Blood, by which we were bought, even though he held us; for how could he, when the robber devil was not only unable to take it from God, but could not even take God Himself?[^337]

Nevertheless, bodily and with the Divinity our Lord Jesus Christ dwelt in the tomb, being supremely united to the flesh; and He was with the thief in Paradise, and was in hades, as was said, with His deified Soul; and supernaturally He was with the Father, seated together with the Spirit, as the uncircumscribed God, and He was everywhere, the Divinity suffering nothing in the tomb, even as neither upon the Cross.

And thus the Lord's Body suffered that which is the dissolution of the soul from the body; but corruption—that is, the destruction of the flesh and limbs, and complete annihilation—He suffered in no wise.

And Joseph, therefore, having taken down the holy Body of the Lord, buris It in a new tomb, and hard by the Jews in a garden he laid a very great stone over the entrance of the tomb.

And the Jews after Friday, having approached, say unto Pilate: "Sir, we remember that that deceiver said while He was yet alive: After three days I will rise; it seems good to us, therefore, that thy authority command the soldiers to secure the tomb." Pilate, perplexed, answered: "If he is a deceiver, why are ye anxious concerning what he said? When he was alive, as confessedly he died. And when did he say: I will rise? Perhaps they gathered this from the sign of Jonah? In any case, if the tomb be secured, he will not be stolen." Oh, how senseless they are! Whatsoever they wrought against themselves, not understanding what they were doing! But Pilate having commanded, they themselves with the military rank and careful sealing secured the tomb, so that since the guard and seals were not foreign, the Resurrection of the Lord might not be slandered. But hades from henceforth is curtailed and at a loss, having felt the firmer power, and after a little while it spews forth, by an unjust swallowing, Christ the firmer and cornerstone; and those whom from the ages it held in its belly, it made food for Him.

By Thine ineffable condescension, O Christ our God, have mercy on us. Amen.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

An ineffable wonder! He Who delivered the holy children from the flame in the furnace, is laid dead and breathless in the tomb, for the salvation of us who sing: Blessed art Thou, O God our Redeemer!

*Troparion:*

Hades was wounded, having received into its heart Him Who was wounded in the rib with a spear, and it groans, consumed by divine fire, unto the salvation of us who sing: Blessed art Thou, O God our Redeemer!

*Troparion:*

Rich is the tomb; for having received within itself the Creator as asleep, it was shown forth as a divine treasure of life, unto the salvation of us who sing: Blessed art Thou, O God our Redeemer!

*Troparion:*

According to the law of the departed, He Who is the Life of all accepts deposition in the tomb, and shows it to be the fountain of resurrection, unto the salvation of us who sing: Blessed art Thou, O God our Redeemer!

*Troparion:*

One and undivided was the Divinity of Christ, in hades, and in the tomb, and in Eden, together with the Father and the Spirit, unto the salvation of us who sing: Blessed art Thou, O God our Redeemer![^338]

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

Be astonished with fear, O heaven, and let the foundations of the earth be shaken! For behold, He that dwells in the highest is accounted among the dead, and is sheltered as a stranger in a narrow tomb; Whom, ye children, bless; ye priests, sing; ye people, exalt supremely unto all ages!

*Troparion:*

The most pure Temple is destroyed, but raises up the fallen tabernacle; for the Second Adam, He that dwells in the highest, hath descended even unto the treasure-houses of hades; Whom, ye children, bless; ye priests, sing; ye people, exalt supremely unto all ages!

*Troparion:*

The boldness of the disciples ceased, but Joseph of Arimathea is preeminent; for beholding God over all dead and naked, he asks and buris Him, crying: Ye children, bless; ye priests, sing; ye people, exalt supremely unto all ages!

*Troparion:*

O new wonders! O goodness! O ineffable forbearance! For of His own will He that dwells in the highest is sealed under the earth, and as a deceiver God is slandered; Whom, ye children, bless; ye priests, sing; ye people, exalt supremely unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^339]

Lament Me not, O Mother, beholding in the tomb the Son Whom thou didst conceive in the womb without seed; for I will arise and be glorified, and will ceaselessly exalt with glory, as God, them that magnify thee with faith and love.

*Troparion:*

At Thy strange birth, having escaped the birth-pangs beyond nature, I was blessed, O unoriginate Son; but now beholding Thee, my God, a breathless corpse, with the sword of sorrow am I cruelly pierced; but arise, that I may be magnified!

*Troparion:*

The earth covers Me willingly, but the gatekeepers of hades tremble, beholding Me clothed in a blood-stained garment of vengeance, O Mother; for having struck down the enemies by the Cross as God, I will arise again, and will magnify thee.

*Troparion:*

Let creation rejoice, let all the earthborn be glad, for hades the enemy hath been despoiled; let the women meet Me with myrrh; I deliver Adam with Eve and all their race, and on the third day I will arise.

*Exapostilarion, Tone 2:*

Holy is the Lord our God. *(Three times)*

*At the Praises, we set 4 verses, and we sing these Idiomel Stichera, Tone 2:*

Today a tomb holds Him Who holds creation with His palm; a stone covers Him Who covered the heavens with virtue; Life sleeps, and hades trembles, and Adam is loosed from bonds; glory to Thy dispensation, whereby having accomplished all things, Thou hast granted unto us, O God, an everlasting rest: Thy most holy Resurrection from the dead.

What is this sight that is seen? What is this present rest? The King of the ages, having accomplished the dispensation through the Passion, keeps the Sabbath in the tomb, granting unto us a new Sabbath-rest; unto Him let us cry aloud: Arise, O God, judge the earth, for Thou dost reign for ever, having boundless, great mercy!

Come, let us see our Life lying in the tomb, that He may give life unto them that lie in the tombs; come today, beholding Him asleep of Judah, let us cry aloud unto Him prophetically: Having couched, Thou didst sleep as a lion; who shall rouse Thee O King? But arise by Thine own authority, Who didst give Thyself willingly for our sake; O Lord, glory to Thee!

*Tone 6:*

Joseph begged the Body of Jesus, and laid It in his own new tomb; for it was meet for Him to go forth from the tomb as from a bridal chamber. O Thou Who didst crush the power of death and open the gates of Paradise unto men: O Lord, glory to Thee!

*Glory, Tone 6:*[^340]

The great Moses mystically foreshadowed this present day, saying: And God blessed the seventh day; for this is the blessed Sabbath, this is the day of rest, whereon the Only-begotten Son of God rested from all His works, keeping the Sabbath in the flesh through the dispensation unto death; and returning again unto that which He was by the Resurrection, He granted unto us eternal life, as the only good One and Lover of mankind.

*Both now, Theotokion, Tone 2:*

Most blessed art thou, O Virgin Theotokos; for through Him Who was incarnate of thee, hades was despoiled, Adam was recalled, the curse was abolished, Eve was set free, death was put to death, and we were quickened. Therefore singing praises we cry aloud: Blessed art Thou, O Christ our God, Who wast thus well pleased, glory to Thee!

*The Great Doxology.*

*The Priest vests in all the priestly vestments, and comes down with the Holy Gospel while the Trisagion is being sung. And when this is finished, we sing the Funeral Sticheron:[^341]*

*Tone 5:*

Come, let us call blessed the ever-memorable Joseph, who came unto Pilate by night and begged the Life of all: Give me this Stranger, Who hath not where to lay His head; give me this Stranger, Whom the deceitful disciple delivered unto death; give me this Stranger, Whom His Mother, beholding hanging upon the Cross, lamented and with maternal voice cried aloud: "Woe is me, my Child! Woe is me, my Light, and my beloved womb!

For that which was foretold by Simeon in the temple hath come to pass: A sword shall pierce thine own soul.[^342] But turn Thy weeping into the joy of Thy Resurrection: We worship Thy Sufferings, O Christ! We worship Thy Sufferings, O Christ! We worship Thy Sufferings, O Christ, and Thy holy Resurrection!"

*And thereafter the Priest saith:*

**Priest:** Let us attend. Peace be unto all.

**Choir:** And to thy spirit.

**Priest:** Wisdom, let us attend.

*And we sing the Troparion once:*

*Troparion:*

The noble Joseph, taking down Thy most pure Body from the Tree, wrapped It in a clean shroud, and having covered It with sweet spices in a new tomb, laid It.

*And we say the Troparion of the Prophecy, Tone 2:*

*Troparion:*

Thou Who holdest the ends of the earth, didst deign to be held by a tomb, O Christ, that Thou mightest deliver mankind from the swallowing of hades, and having risen, mightest give life unto us, as immortal God.

*Glory, the ending:* "and having risen, mightest give life unto us, as immortal God."

*Both now, the whole:* "Thou Who holdest the ends of the earth, didst deign to be held by a tomb, O Christ, that Thou mightest deliver mankind from the swallowing of hades, and having risen, mightest give life unto us, as immortal God."

*Then, the Prokeimenon, Tone 4:*

*Prokeimenon, Tone 4 (Psalm 43:26, 1):* Arise, O Lord, help us, and redeem us for Thy name's sake.

*Verse:* O God, we have heard with our ears, and our fathers have told us.

*The Reading from the Prophecy of Ezekiel (Chapter 37):*[^343]

The hand of the Lord came upon me, and carried me forth in the Spirit of the Lord, and set me in the midst of the plain, and it was full of human bones. And He led me round about them on every side; and behold, there were very many upon the face of the plain, and lo, they were very dry. And He said unto me: "Son of man, shall these bones live?" And I said: "O Lord God, Thou knowest these things." And He said unto me: "Son of man, prophesy upon these bones, and say unto them: Ye dry bones, hear the word of the Lord! Thus saith the Lord God unto these bones: Behold, I will bring the breath of life into you; and I will lay sinews upon you, and bring up flesh upon you, and stretch skin over you, and give My Spirit into you, and ye shall live; and ye shall know that I am the Lord." And I prophesied as the Lord commanded me; and there was a voice as I was prophesying, and behold, there was an earthquake, and the bones came together, bone to bone, each to its joint. And I looked, and behold, sinews were upon them, and flesh grew, and went up, and there was stretched skin upon them above; but there was no breath in them. And He said unto me: "Prophesy unto the wind, prophesy, son of man, and say unto the wind: Thus saith the Lord God: Come from the four winds, O breath, and breathe upon these dead, that they may live!" And I prophesied as He commanded me, and the breath of life entered into them, and they lived, and stood upon their feet, an exceedingly great congregation. And the Lord said unto me, saying: "Son of man, these bones are the whole house of Israel; for they say: Our bones are dried, our hope is lost, we are slain. Therefore prophesy, son of man, and say unto them: Thus saith the Lord God: Behold, I will open your graves, and bring you up out of your graves, O My people, and bring you into the land of Israel. And ye shall know that I am the Lord, when I have opened your graves, that I may bring you up out of your graves, O My people; and I will put My Spirit in you, and ye shall live; and I will place you in your own land; and ye shall know that I the Lord have spoken, and will do it, saith the Lord God."

*Prokeimenon, Tone 7 (Psalm 9:32, 1):* Arise, O Lord my God, let Thy hand be lifted up, forget not Thy poor for ever.

*Verse:* I will confess Thee, O Lord, with my whole heart, I will tell of all Thy marvelous works.

*The Reading from the Epistle of the Holy Apostle Paul to the Corinthians (Pericope 123 [1 Cor 5:6-8; Gal 3:13-14]):*[^344]

Brethren, a little leaven leavens the whole lump. Cleanse out therefore the old leaven, that ye may be a new lump, as ye are unleavened; for our Pascha, Christ, hath been sacrificed for us. Therefore let us keep the feast, not with the old leaven, nor with the leaven of malice and wickedness, but with the unleavened bread of sincerity and truth. Christ hath redeemed us from the curse of the Law, being made a curse for us; for it is written: Cursed is every one that hangs on a tree: that the blessing of Abraham might come on the Gentiles through Christ Jesus; that we might receive the promise of the Spirit through faith.

*Alleluia, Tone 5.*

*Alleluia, Tone 5 (Psalm 67:1-3):*

Let God arise, and let His enemies be scattered, and let them that hate Him flee from before His face.

*Verse:* As smoke vanishes, so let them vanish; as wax melts before the fire.

*Verse:* So let sinners perish from the presence of God, but let the righteous be glad.

*The Reading from the Holy Gospel according to Matthew (Pericope 114 [Matt 27:62-66]):*

On the next day, which is after Friday, the chief priests and the Pharisees gathered together unto Pilate, saying: "Sir, we remember that that deceiver said, while He was yet alive: After three days I will rise. Command therefore that the tomb be secured until the third day, lest His disciples come by night and steal Him away, and say unto the people: He is risen from the dead; and the last error shall be worse than the first." Pilate said unto them: "Ye have a guard; go, secure it as ye know how." So they went, and secured the tomb, sealing the stone with the guard.

*Then, the Litany:* Let us all say: *And the rest as usual.*

*Then:* Let us complete our morning prayer: *Likewise and the rest as usual.*

*And the Dismissal is given:*

**Priest:** He Who for the sake of us men and for our salvation willingly was laid in the tomb in the Flesh, and descended into hades, Christ our true God, through the intercessions of His most pure Mother, and of all the saints, have mercy on us and save us, for He is good and the Lover of mankind.

**Choir:** Amen.

*The First Hour is sung in the narthex; and the Third, Sixth, and Ninth Hours are sung together simply in the narthex with the Beatitudes at their proper time.[^345]*

*[Typographical tailpiece: A detailed woodcut engraving depicting the Instruments of the Passion (Arma Christi): the Cross with the Title board (INRI), the Crown of Thorns, the Spear and the Reed with the sponge, the Ladder and Pincers, the Column of Flagellation with the binding cord and Rooster of Peter's denial, the seamless Tunic, the Thirty Pieces of Silver, the Lantern, the Pitcher and Basin of Pilate, the Nails, the Hammer, and the Dice.]*

## ON HOLY AND GREAT SATURDAY EVENING

*At the tenth hour of the day, the klepalo is struck for Vespers, and the Priest vests in his vestments; and when he hath given the blessing, we begin the Initial Psalm; thereafter, the Great Litany.[^346]*

*Then, "Lord, I have cried," in Tone 1. [In monasteries,] all the priests and deacons, when the chanter reaches the verse: "They shall fall into their own net," make a prostration, go forth, vest themselves also in their vestments, and perform the Proskomedia.[^347]*

*And we set 8 stichoi. And we sing 3 Resurrection Stichera of the Oktoechos, and 1 from the Aposticha.*

*Resurrection Stichera, Tone 1:*

*Accept our evening prayers, O holy Lord, and grant us remission of sins, for Thou alone art He Who hath manifested resurrection unto the world.*

*Encompass Zion, ye people, and surround her, and give glory within her unto Him Who is risen from the dead; for He is our God, Who hath delivered us from our iniquities.*

*Come, ye people, let us sing and worship Christ, glorifying His resurrection from the dead; for He is our God, Who hath delivered the world from the deceit of the enemy.*

*By Thy Passion, O Christ, we have been set free from passions, and by Thy Resurrection we have been delivered from corruption: O Lord, glory to Thee!*

*Other Idiomelon Stichera of Great Saturday, three, repeating one:*

*Tone 8:*

*Today hades groaning cries aloud: "It had been better for me if I had not received Him Who was born of Mary; for coming upon me, He hath dissolved my dominion, and hath broken the bronze gates; the souls which I held before, being God, He hath raised up. Glory, O Lord, to Thy Cross, and to Thy Resurrection!"*

*Today hades groaning cries aloud: "My power hath been dissolved; I received a Dead Man as one of the dead, yet Him I cannot hold at all, but with Him I lose those over whom I reigned; I had dead ones from the ages, but behold, He raises up all! Glory, O Lord, to Thy Cross, and to Thy Resurrection!"*

*Today hades groaning cries aloud: "My dominion hath been swallowed up; the Shepherd was crucified, and hath raised Adam; those over whom I reigned, I have lost; and whom I swallowed in my might, I have disgorged all. The Crucified One hath emptied the tombs; the power of death hath grown weak. Glory, O Lord, to Thy Cross, and to Thy Resurrection!"*

*Glory, Tone 6:*

*This present day Moses mystically prefigured, saying: "And God blessed the seventh day." For this is the blessed Sabbath; this is the day of rest, whereon the Only-begotten Son of God rested from all His works, keeping the Sabbath in the Flesh through the dispensation unto death; and returning again by the Resurrection unto that which He was, He hath granted us eternal life, as He alone is good and the Lover of mankind.[^348]*

*Both now, Theotokion (Dogmatikon), Tone 1:*

*Let us hymn Mary the Virgin, the universal Glory who sprouted forth from mankind and gave birth unto the Master, the Heavenly Gate, the song of the bodiless hosts, and the adornment of the faithful; for she was shown forth as Heaven and the Temple of the Godhead; she, having broken down the middle wall of enmity, brought in peace and opened the Kingdom. Having her, therefore, as the confirmation of our faith, we have as our champion the Lord Who was born of her. Take courage, therefore, take courage, O people of God, for He will defeat the enemies, as He is almighty!*

*Entrance with the Gospel.[^349]*

*O Gladsome Light:*

*We do not say a Prokeimenon, but straightway:*

**Priest:** Wisdom!

*And the Reader begins the readings in order, which are fifteen in number.[^350]*

*The First Reading: from Genesis (Chapter 1 [Gen 1:1-13]):*

In the beginning God created the heaven and the earth. And the earth was invisible and unformed, and darkness was over the deep, and the Spirit of God was borne over the water. And God said: "Let there be light," and there was light. And God saw the light, that it was good; and God divided between the light and the darkness. And God called the light Day, and the darkness He called Night. And there was evening, and there was morning, one day. And God said: "Let there be a firmament in the midst of the water, and let it divide between water and water." And it was so. And God made the firmament; and God divided between the water which was under the firmament, and the water which was above the firmament. And God called the firmament Heaven; and God saw that it was good. And there was evening, and there was morning, the second day. And God said: "Let the water which is under heaven be gathered into one gathering, and let the dry land appear." And it was so. And the water which was under heaven was gathered into its gatherings, and the dry land appeared. And God called the dry land Earth, and the gatherings of the waters He called Seas. And God saw that it was good. And God said: "Let the earth bring forth the herb of grass, yielding seed according to its kind and after its likeness, and the fruit tree bearing fruit, whose seed is in itself, according to its kind on the earth." And it was so. And the earth brought forth the herb of grass, yielding seed according to its kind and after its likeness, and the fruit tree bearing fruit, whose seed is in itself according to its kind on the earth. And God saw that it was good. And there was evening, and there was morning, the third day.

*The Second Reading: from the Prophecy of Isaiah (Chapter 60 [Isa 60:1-16]):*

Shine, shine, O Jerusalem, for thy light is come, and the glory of the Lord is risen upon thee! Behold, darkness shall cover the earth, and gross darkness the nations; but upon thee the Lord shall appear, and His glory shall be seen upon thee. And kings shall walk by thy light, and nations by thy brightness. Lift up thine eyes round about, and see thy children gathered together: behold, all thy sons have come from afar, and thy daughters shall be borne upon the shoulders. Then thou shalt see, and shalt rejoice, and shalt fear, and be astonished in thine heart; for the wealth of the sea shall be turned unto thee, and of the nations and peoples. And herds of camels shall come unto thee, and the camels of Midian and Ephah shall cover thee; all they from Sheba shall come, bringing gold, and they shall bring frankincense, and precious stones, and shall proclaim the salvation of the Lord. And all the flocks of Kedar shall be gathered unto thee, and the rams of Nebaioth shall come unto thee, and acceptable sacrifices shall be offered upon Mine altar, and the house of My prayer shall be glorified. Who are these that fly as clouds, and as doves with their young ones unto Me? The isles have waited for Me, and the ships of Tarshish among the first, to bring thy children from afar, and their silver and their gold with them, for the sake of the holy name of the Lord, and because the Holy One of Israel is glorified. And the sons of strangers shall build thy walls, and their kings shall minister unto thee; for in My wrath I smote thee, and in My mercy I loved thee. And thy gates shall be open continually; day and night they shall not be shut, to bring unto thee the host of the nations, and their kings led captive. For the nations and the kings that will not serve thee shall perish, and the nations shall be utterly laid waste. And the glory of Lebanon shall come unto thee, with cypress, and pine, and cedar together, to glorify My holy place; and I will glorify the place of My feet. And the sons of them that afflicted thee and provoked thee shall come bending unto thee, and all they that provoked thee shall worship at the soles of thy feet; and thou shalt be called the City of the Lord, Zion of the Holy One of Israel. Whereas thou hast been forsaken and hated, so that no man went through thee or helped thee, I will make thee an eternal excellency, a joy of generations unto generations. And thou shalt suck the milk of the nations, and shalt eat the wealth of kings; and thou shalt know that I the Lord am Thy Savior, and Thy Redeemer is the God of Israel.

*The Third Reading: from Exodus (Chapter 12 [Exod 12:1-11]):*

The Lord said unto Moses and Aaron in the land of Egypt, saying: "This month shall be unto you the beginning of months: it shall be the first month of the year unto you. Speak ye unto all the congregation of the children of Israel, saying: In the tenth day of this month let every man take a lamb according to the houses of their fathers, a lamb for every house. And if there be few in the house, so that they be not sufficient for a lamb, let him take with him his neighbor that is near unto him according to the number of souls; every man according to his sufficient need shall make his count for the lamb. Your lamb shall be without blemish, a male of one year; ye shall take it from the sheep or from the goats. And ye shall keep it until the fourteenth day of this month; and the whole multitude of the assembly of the children of Israel shall kill it toward evening. And they shall take of the blood, and strike it on the two side posts and on the upper lintel in the houses wherein they shall eat it. And they shall eat the flesh in that night, roast with fire; and unleavened bread with bitter herbs they shall eat. Eat not of it raw, nor boiled in water, but roast with fire: its head with its feet and with the purtenance thereof. Ye shall let nothing of it remain until the morning;

and a bone of it ye shall not break; and that which remains of it until the morning ye shall burn with fire. And thus shall ye eat it: with your loins girded, your sandals on your feet, and your staves in your hands; and ye shall eat it in haste: it is the Lord's Pascha.[^351]

*The Fourth Reading: from the Prophecy of Jonah (Chapter 1 [Jonah 1:1 - 2:10]):[^352]*

The word of the Lord came unto Jonah the son of Amittai, saying: "Arise, and go to Nineveh, that great city, and preach in it, for the cry of its wickedness is come up before Me." And Jonah arose to flee unto Tarshish from the presence of the Lord; and the prophet went down to Joppa, and found a ship going to Tarshish; and he paid his fare, and went into it, to sail with them unto Tarshish from the presence of the Lord. But the Lord raised up a great wind upon the sea, and there was a mighty tempest in the sea, so that the ship was in danger of being broken. And the mariners were afraid, and cried every man unto his god, and cast forth the vessels that were in the ship into the sea, to lighten it of them. But Jonah was gone down into the hold of the ship, and lay there fast asleep, and was snoring. And the shipmaster came to him, and said unto him: "Why snorest thou? Arise, and call upon thy God, if so be that God will save us, that we perish not!" And they said every one to his fellow: "Come, let us cast lots, that we may know for whose sake this evil is upon us!" And they cast lots, and the lot fell upon Jonah. And they said unto him: "Tell us, for whose sake is this evil upon us? What is thine occupation? and whence comest thou? Whither goest thou, and of what country, and of what people art thou?" And he said unto them: "I am a servant of the Lord, and I worship the Lord God of heaven, Who hath made the sea and the dry land." And the men were exceedingly afraid, and said unto him: "Why hast thou done this?" For the men knew that he fled from the presence of the Lord, because he had told them. And they said unto him: "What shall we do unto thee, that the sea may be calm unto us?" For the sea surged and lifted up its waves yet more. And Jonah said unto them: "Take me up, and cast me into the sea; so shall the sea be calm unto you: for I know that for my sake this great tempest is upon you." Nevertheless the men strove hard to bring the ship back to the land; but they could not, for the sea surged and was tempestuous against them. And they cried unto the Lord, and said: "By no means, O Lord, let us not perish for the sake of this man's life, and lay not upon us innocent blood; for Thou, O Lord, hast done as it pleased Thee!" And they took up Jonah, and cast him into the sea; and the sea ceased from its raging. And the men feared the Lord with a great fear, and offered a sacrifice unto the Lord, and made vows. And the Lord commanded a great whale to swallow up Jonah; and Jonah was in the belly of the whale three days and three nights. And Jonah the prophet prayed unto the Lord his God out of the whale's belly, and said:

*(A PRAYER.)*

"I cried by reason of mine affliction unto the Lord my God, and He heard me; out of the belly of hades my cry: Thou heardest my voice! For Thou didst cast me into the depths of the heart of the sea, and the floods compassed me about; all Thy billows and Thy waves passed over me. And I said: I am cast out of Thy sight; yet shall I look again toward Thy holy temple? The waters poured around me, even to my soul; the lowest deep compassed me about, my head was plunged into the clefts of the mountains. I went down into the earth whose bars are eternal barriers; yet let my life come up from corruption unto Thee, O Lord my God! When my soul was failing from me, I remembered the Lord; and let my prayer come unto Thee, into Thy holy temple. They that observe vanity and lies have forsaken their own mercy. But I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of praise and thanksgiving; as much as I have vowed, I will pay unto Thee for my salvation unto the Lord!"

And the Lord commanded the whale, and it vomited out Jonah upon the dry land. And the word of the Lord came unto Jonah the second time, saying:

*(Chapter 3)*

"Arise, and go unto Nineveh, that great city, and preach in it according to the former preaching which I spoke unto thee." And Jonah arose, and went unto Nineveh, according to the word of the Lord. Now Nineveh was an exceeding great city unto God, of about three days' journey. And Jonah began to enter into the city, about a day's journey, and he preached and said: "Yet three days, and Nineveh shall be overthrown!" And the men of Nineveh believed God, and proclaimed a fast, and put on sackcloth, from the greatest of them even to the least of them. And the word reached the king of Nineveh, and he arose from his throne, and laid aside his robe from him, and clothed himself with sackcloth, and sat in ashes. And it was proclaimed and published in Nineveh by the decree of the king and his nobles, saying: "Let neither man nor beast, herd nor flock, taste any thing: let them not feed, nor drink water! But let man and beast be clothed with sackcloth, and cry mightily unto God; and let them turn every one from his evil way, and from the injustice that is in their hands, saying: Who knows if God will turn and repent, and turn away from the fierceness of His anger, that we perish not?" And God saw their works, that they turned from their evil ways; and God repented of the evil which He had said that He would do unto them; and He did it not.

*(Chapter 4)*

And Jonah was grieved with great grief, and was confounded. And he prayed unto the Lord, and said: "O Lord, were not these my words when I was yet in my country? Therefore I fled before unto Tarshish, for I knew that Thou art a gracious God, and merciful, longsuffering, and of great kindness, and repentest Thee of the evils of men. And now, O Master, Lord, take, I pray Thee, my life from me; for it is better for me to die than to live." And the Lord said unto Jonah: "Art thou greatly grieved?" And Jonah went out of the city, and sat over against the city, and made him a booth there, and sat under it in the shadow, till he might see what would become of the city. And the Lord God commanded a gourd, and it grew up over the head of Jonah, to be a shadow over his head, to shade him from his griefs. And Jonah was exceeding glad of the gourd. But the Lord God commanded a worm early on the morrow, and it smote the gourd, that it withered. And it came to pass, when the sun did rise, that God commanded a vehement scorching wind; and the sun beat upon the head of Jonah, and he fainted, and renounced his soul, and said: "It is better for me to die than to live." And the Lord God said unto Jonah: "Art thou greatly grieved for the gourd?" And Jonah said: "I am greatly grieved, even unto death." And the Lord said: "Thou hast had pity on the gourd, for which thou hast not labored, neither madest it grow; which sprang up in a night, and perished in a night. And should not I spare Nineveh, that great city, wherein dwell more than sixscore thousand persons that cannot discern between their right hand and their left hand; and also much cattle?"

*The Fifth Reading: from the Book of Joshua the Son of Nun (Chapter 5 [Josh 5:10-15]):[^353]*

The children of Israel encamped in Gilgal, and kept the Passover on the fourteenth day of the month toward evening at the west of Jericho in the plain. And they ate of the grain of that land unleavened cakes and new corn. On that day the manna ceased, after they had eaten of the grain of the land, and the children of Israel had no more manna, but they ate of the fruits of the land of the Phoenicians in that year. And it came to pass, when Joshua was by Jericho, that he lifted up his eyes and looked, and behold, there stood a man over against him with his sword drawn in his hand; and Joshua went unto him, and said unto him: "Art thou for us, or for our adversaries?" And he said unto him: "I am the Commander of the host of the Lord; now am I come hither." And Joshua fell on his face to the earth, and worshiped him, and said: "Lord, what commandest Thou unto Thy servant?" And the Commander of the Lord's host said unto Joshua: "Loose thy sandal from off thy foot, for the place whereon thou standest is holy." And Joshua did so.

*The Sixth Reading: from Exodus (Chapters 13–14 [Exod 13:20 - 14:26]):*

The children of Israel took their journey from Succoth, and encamped in Etham, on the edge of the wilderness. And God led them; by day in a pillar of a cloud, to show them the way, and by night in a pillar of fire, to give them light. He took not away the pillar of the cloud by day, nor the pillar of fire by night, from before all the people.

*(Chapter 14)*

And the Lord spoke unto Moses, saying: "Speak unto the children of Israel, that they turn and encamp over against the village, between Migdol and the sea, over against Baal-zephon: before it shalt thou encamp by the sea. For Pharaoh will say of the children of Israel: They are entangled in the land, the wilderness hath shut them in. And I will harden Pharaoh's heart, that he shall follow after them; and I will be glorified upon Pharaoh, and upon all his host; that all the Egyptians may know that I am the Lord." And they did so. And it was told the king of Egypt that the people fled: and the heart of Pharaoh and of his servants was turned against the people, and they said: "Why have we done this, that we have let the children of Israel go from serving us?" And Pharaoh made ready his chariots, and gathered all his people with him: and he took six hundred chosen chariots, and all the horses of Egypt, and captains over every one of them. And the Lord hardened the heart of Pharaoh king of Egypt, and of his servants, and he pursued after the children of Israel; and the children of Israel went out with a high hand. But the Egyptians pursued after them, and found them encamping by the sea: all the horses and chariots of Pharaoh, and his horsemen, and his army, over against the village, opposite Baal-zephon. And Pharaoh drew near; and when the children of Israel lifted up their eyes, they saw: and behold, the Egyptians encamped after them; and they were sore afraid: and the children of Israel cried out unto the Lord. And they said unto Moses: "Because there were no graves in Egypt, hast thou taken us away to die in the wilderness? Why hast thou dealt thus with us, to carry us forth out of Egypt? Was not this the word that we spoke unto thee in Egypt, saying: Let us alone, that we may serve the Egyptians? For it had been better for us to serve the Egyptians, than that we should die in this wilderness." And Moses said unto the people: "Fear ye not, stand still, and see the salvation of the Lord, which He will show to you today: for the Egyptians whom ye have seen today, ye shall see them again no more for ever. The Lord will fight for you, and ye shall hold your peace." And the Lord said unto Moses: "Why criest thou unto Me? Speak unto the children of Israel, that they go forward! But lift thou up thy rod, and stretch out thy hand over the sea, and divide it: and the children of Israel shall go into the midst of the sea on dry ground. And behold, I will harden the heart of Pharaoh, and of all the Egyptians, and they shall follow them: and I will be glorified upon Pharaoh, and upon all his host, and upon his chariots, and upon his horsemen. And all the Egyptians shall know that I am the Lord, when I am glorified upon Pharaoh, upon his chariots, and upon his horsemen." And the Angel of God, which went before the camp of Israel, removed and went behind them; and the pillar of the cloud went from before their face, and stood behind them. And it came between the camp of the Egyptians and the camp of the children of Israel; and it was a cloud and darkness, and night came on, so that the one came not near the other all the night. And Moses stretched out his hand over the sea; and the Lord drove back the sea with a strong south wind all that night, and made the sea dry land, and the water was divided. And the children of Israel went into the midst of the sea upon the dry ground: and the waters were a wall unto them on their right hand, and on their left. And the Egyptians pursued, and went in after them to the midst of the sea, even all Pharaoh's horses, his chariots, and his horsemen. And it came to pass, that in the morning watch the Lord looked unto the host of the Egyptians through the pillar of fire and of the cloud, and troubled the host of the Egyptians. And He bound the axles of their chariots, that they drove them heavily. And the Egyptians said: "Let us flee from the face of Israel; for the Lord fights for them against the Egyptians!" And the Lord said unto Moses:

"Stretch out thy hand over the sea, that the waters may come again upon the Egyptians, upon their chariots, and upon their horsemen." And Moses stretched forth his hand over the sea, and the water returned to its place at daybreak; and the Egyptians fled against it; and the Lord shook off the Egyptians in the midst of the sea. And the water returned, and covered the chariots, and the horsemen, and all the host of Pharaoh that came into the sea after them; there remained not so much as one of them. But the children of Israel walked upon dry land in the midst of the sea; and the waters were a wall unto them on their right hand, and on their left. Thus the Lord saved Israel that day out of the hand of the Egyptians; and Israel saw the Egyptians dead upon the seashore. And Israel saw that great work which the Lord did upon the Egyptians; and the people feared the Lord, and believed the Lord, and His servant Moses. Then sang Moses and the children of Israel this song unto the Lord, and spoke, saying:[^354]

*(Chapter 15)*

*The Reader saith:* Let us sing unto the Lord: *and they sing in Tone 5:* For He hath been gloriously glorified!

*And the Reader proclaims the verses to each choir:*

*Verse:* The horse and his rider hath He thrown into the sea.

*Choir:* Let us sing unto the Lord:

*And the people on both choirs sing:* For He hath been gloriously glorified!

*Verse:* The Lord is my helper and protector unto salvation.

*Choir:* Let us sing unto the Lord:

*People:* For He hath been gloriously glorified!

*Verse:* This is my God, and I will glorify Him; my father's God, and I will exalt Him.

*Choir:* Let us sing unto the Lord:

*People:* For He hath been gloriously glorified!

*Verse:* The Lord shatters wars, the Lord is His name; Pharaoh's chariots and his host hath He cast into the sea.

*Choir:* Let us sing unto the Lord:

*People:* For He hath been gloriously glorified!

*Verse:* His chosen captains hath He drowned in the Red Sea.

*Choir:* Let us sing unto the Lord:

*People:* For He hath been gloriously glorified!

*Verse:* The depths have covered them: they sank into the bottom as a stone.

*Choir:* Let us sing unto the Lord:

*People:* For He hath been gloriously glorified!

*Verse:* Thy right hand, O Lord, is glorified in strength.

*Choir:* Let us sing unto the Lord:

*People:* For He hath been gloriously glorified!

*Verse:* Thy right hand, O Lord, hath dashed in pieces the enemy; and in the greatness of Thy glory Thou hast crushed the adversaries.

*Choir:* Let us sing unto the Lord:

*People:* For He hath been gloriously glorified!

*Verse:* Thou sentest forth Thy wrath, which consumed them as stubble; and with the blast of Thy nostrils the water was gathered together.

*Choir:* Let us sing unto the Lord:

*People:* For He hath been gloriously glorified!

*Verse:* The waters were congealed like a wall, the waves were congealed in the midst of the sea.

*Choir:* Let us sing unto the Lord:

*People:* For He hath been gloriously glorified!

*Verse:* The enemy said: "I will pursue, I will overtake, I will divide the spoil; my soul shall be filled with them; I will draw my sword, my hand shall dominate them!"

*Choir:* Let us sing unto the Lord:

*People:* For He hath been gloriously glorified!

*Verse:* Thou didst blow with Thy wind, the sea covered them; they sank as lead in the mighty water.

*Choir:* Let us sing unto the Lord:

*People:* For He hath been gloriously glorified!

*Verse:* Who is like unto Thee, O Lord, among the gods? Who is like Thee, glorified in holiness, marvelous in praises, doing wonders?

*Choir:* Let us sing unto the Lord:

*People:* For He hath been gloriously glorified!

*Verse:* Thou stretchedst out Thy right hand, the earth swallowed them. Thou in Thy mercy hast led forth this people whom Thou hast redeemed.

*Choir:* Let us sing unto the Lord:

*People:* For He hath been gloriously glorified!

*Verse:* Thou hast guided them in Thy strength unto Thy holy habitation. The nations heard and were wroth; pangs took hold on the inhabitants of Philistia.

*Choir:* Let us sing unto the Lord:

*People:* For He hath been gloriously glorified!

*Verse:* Then the rulers of Edom made haste, and the princes of the Moabites, trembling took hold upon them; all the inhabitants of Canaan melted away.

*Choir:* Let us sing unto the Lord:

*People:* For He hath been gloriously glorified!

*Verse:* Let fear and dread fall upon them; by the greatness of Thine arm let them be as still as a stone.

*Choir:* Let us sing unto the Lord:

*People:* For He hath been gloriously glorified!

*Verse:* Till Thy people pass over, O Lord, till this Thy people pass over, which Thou hast purchased.

*Choir:* Let us sing unto the Lord:

*People:* For He hath been gloriously glorified!

*Verse:* Bring them in, and plant them in the mountain of Thine inheritance, in the place, O Lord, which Thou hast made for Thee to dwell in, in the Sanctuary, O Lord, which Thy hands have prepared.

*Choir:* Let us sing unto the Lord:

*People:* For He hath been gloriously glorified!

*Verse:* The Lord shall reign for ages, and unto an age, and still! When the horse of Pharaoh went in with his chariots and his horsemen into the sea, the Lord brought again the waters of the sea upon them.

*Choir:* Let us sing unto the Lord:

*People:* For He hath been gloriously glorified!

*Verse:* But the children of Israel went on dry land in the midst of the sea.

*Choir:* Let us sing unto the Lord:

*People:* For He hath been gloriously glorified!

*Glory:*

*Choir:* Let us sing unto the Lord:

*Both now:*

*Choir:* Let us sing unto the Lord:

*And at the end, the Reader sings the same:*

*Reader:* For He hath been gloriously glorified!

*The Seventh Reading: from the Prophecy of Zephaniah (Chapter 3 [Zeph 3:8-15]):[^355]*

Thus saith the Lord: "Wait for Me, unto the day of My resurrection as a witness; for My judgment is to gather the nations, to assemble the kingdoms, to pour upon them Mine indignation, even all My fierce anger; for all the earth shall be devoured with the fire of My jealousy. For then will I turn to the people a pure language for their generation, that they may all call upon the name of the Lord, to serve Him under one yoke. From beyond the rivers of Ethiopia My suppliants, even the daughter of My dispersed, shall bring Mine offering. In that day shalt thou not be ashamed for all thy doings, wherein thou hast transgressed against Me; for then I will take away out of the midst of thee them that rejoice in thy pride, and thou shalt no more be haughty in My holy mountain. I will also leave in the midst of thee an afflicted and poor people, and they shall trust in the name of the Lord. The remnant of Israel shall not do iniquity, nor speak falsehood; neither shall a deceitful tongue be found in their mouth; for they shall feed and lie down, and none shall make them afraid. Sing, O daughter of Zion; shout, O Israel; be glad and rejoice with all the heart, O daughter of Jerusalem! The Lord hath taken away thy judgments, He hath delivered thee from the hand of thine enemies: the King of Israel, even the Lord, is in the midst of thee; thou shalt not see evil any more!"

*The Eighth Reading: from the Third Book of Kings (Chapter 17 [1 Kings 17:8-24 / 3 Kings 17:8-24]):[^356]*

The word of the Lord came unto Elijah, saying: "Arise, and get thee to Zarephath, which belongs to Zidon, and dwell there: behold, I have commanded a widow woman there to sustain thee." So he arose and went to Zarephath; and when he came to the gate of the city, behold, the widow woman was there gathering of sticks; and Elijah called after her, and said unto her: "Fetch me, I pray thee, a little water in a vessel, that I may drink." And as she was going to fetch it, Elijah called after her, and said unto her: "Bring me, I pray thee, a morsel of bread in thine hand, that I may eat." And the woman said unto the prophet: "As the Lord thy God lives, I have not a cake, but a handful of meal in a jar, and a little oil in a cruse; and behold, I am gathering two sticks, that I may go in and dress it for me and my children, that we may eat it, and die." And Elijah said unto her: "Fear not; go and do as thou hast said; but make me thereof a little cake first, and bring it unto me, and after make for thee and for thy children. For thus saith the Lord God of Israel: The jar of meal shall not fail, neither shall the cruse of oil diminish, until the day that the Lord sends rain upon the earth!" And the woman went and did according to the saying of Elijah; and she, and he, and her children, did eat. And from that day the jar of meal failed not, neither did the cruse of oil diminish, according to the word of the Lord, which He spoke by the hand of Elijah. And it came to pass after these things, that the son of the woman, the mistress of the house, fell sick; and his sickness was so sore, that there was no breath left in him. And she said unto Elijah: "What have I to do with thee, O thou man of God? Art thou come unto me to call my sin to remembrance, and to slay my son?" And Elijah said unto the woman: "Give me thy son." And he took him out of her bosom, and carried him up into an upper room, where he abode, and laid him upon his own bed. And Elijah cried unto the Lord, and said: "Woe is me, O Lord, the witness of the widow with whom I sojourn, hast Thou brought evil upon her by slaying her son?" And he breathed upon the child three times, and called upon the Lord, and said: "O Lord my God, I pray Thee, let this child's soul come into him again!" And it was so. And the child cried out, and he brought him down out of the upper room into the house, and delivered him unto his mother; and Elijah said: "See, thy son lives!" And the woman said unto Elijah: "Now by this I know that thou art a man of God, and that the word of the Lord in thy mouth is truth!"

*The Ninth Reading: from the Prophecy of Isaiah (Chapter 61 [Isa 61:10 - 62:5]):[^357]*

Let my soul rejoice in the Lord: for He hath clothed me with the robe of salvation, and with the garment of gladness hath He covered me; as a bridegroom hath He put a crown upon me, and as a bride hath He adorned me with beauty. For as the earth that causes her bud to spring forth, and as a garden causes the things that are sown in it to spring forth: so the Lord God will cause righteousness and praise to spring forth before all the nations. For Zion's sake will I not hold my peace, and for Jerusalem's sake I will not rest, until my righteousness go forth as light, and my salvation as a burning lamp. And the nations shall see thy righteousness, and kings thy glory; and they shall call thee by a new name, which the Lord shall name. Thou shalt also be a crown of beauty in the hand of the Lord, and a royal diadem in the hand of thy God. Thou shalt no more be termed Forsaken, neither shall thy land any more be termed Desolate: but thou shalt be called My Delight, and thy land Inhabited; for the Lord hath delighted in thee, and thy land shall be married. For as a young man dwells with a virgin, so shall thy sons dwell with thee; and as the bridegroom rejoices over the bride, so shall the Lord rejoice over thee.

*The Tenth Reading: from Genesis (Chapter 22 [Gen 22:1-15]):[^358]*

And it came to pass after these things, that God did tempt Abraham, and said unto him: "Abraham, Abraham!" And he said: "Behold, here I am." And He said: "Take now thy son, thine only son Isaac, whom thou lovest, and get thee into the high land, and offer him there for a whole-burnt-offering upon one of the mountains which I will tell thee of." And Abraham rose up early in the morning, and saddled his donkey, and took two of his young men with him, and Isaac his son, and clave the wood for the whole-burnt-offering, and rose up, and went unto the place of which God had told him, on the third day. Then Abraham lifted up his eyes, and saw the place afar off. And Abraham said unto his young men: "Abide ye here with the donkey; and I and the lad will go yonder and worship, and come again to you." And Abraham took the wood of the whole-burnt-offering, and laid it upon Isaac his son; and he took the fire in his hand, and a knife; and they went both of them together. And Isaac spoke unto Abraham his father, and said: "My father!" And he said: "Here am I, my son." And he said: "Behold the fire and the wood: but where is the lamb for a whole-burnt-offering?" And Abraham said: "God will provide Himself a lamb for a whole-burnt-offering, my son." So they went both of them together. And they came to the place which God had told him of; and Abraham built an altar there, and laid the wood in order, and bound Isaac his son, and laid him on the altar upon the wood. And Abraham stretched forth his hand, and took the knife to slay his son. And the Angel of the Lord called unto him out of heaven, and said: "Abraham, Abraham!" And he said: "Here am I." And He said: "Lay not thine hand upon the lad, neither do thou any thing unto him: for now I know that thou fearest God, seeing thou hast not spared thy beloved son for My sake." And Abraham lifted up his eyes, and looked, and behold, behind him a ram caught in a thicket by his horns; and Abraham went and took the ram, and offered him up for a whole-burnt-offering in the stead of his son Isaac. And Abraham called the name of that place "The Lord Hath Seen," as it is said to this day: "In the mount the Lord was seen." And the Angel the Angel of the Lord called unto Abraham the second time out of heaven, saying: "By Myself have I sworn, saith the Lord, because thou hast done this thing, and hast not spared thy beloved son for My sake: that in blessing I will bless thee, and in multiplying I will multiply thy seed as the stars of heaven, and as the sand which is upon the seashore; and thy seed shall possess the cities of their adversaries; and in thy seed shall all the nations of the earth be blessed; because thou hast obeyed My voice."

*The Eleventh Reading: from the Prophecy of Isaiah (Chapter 61 [Isa 61:1-9]):*[^359]

The Spirit of the Lord is upon Me, because He hath anointed Me; He hath sent Me to preach good tidings unto the poor, to heal the brokenhearted, to proclaim liberty to the captives, and recovery of sight to the blind: to proclaim the acceptable year of the Lord, and the day of recompense; to comfort all that mourn; to give unto the mourners of Zion glory instead of ashes, the anointing of joy to the mourners, the adornment of glory instead of the spirit of despair; and they shall be called generations of righteousness, the planting of the Lord for glory. And they shall build the eternal desolations, they shall raise up the former wastes, and they shall renew the desolate cities, the wastes of many generations. And strangers shall come and feed your flocks, and the sons of the alien shall be your plowmen and your vinedressers. But ye shall be named the Priests of the Lord, ministers of our God shall it be said unto you: ye shall eat the strength of the nations, and in their riches shall ye be marvelous. Thus shall they inherit their land the second time, and everlasting joy shall be upon their head. For I am the Lord Who loves righteousness, and hates robberies of unrighteousness; and I will give their labor unto the righteous, and I will make an everlasting covenant with them. And their seed shall be known among the nations, and their offspring among the people: all that see them shall acknowledge them, that they are the seed which God hath blessed, and with joy they shall rejoice in the Lord.

*The Twelfth Reading: from the Fourth Book of Kings (Chapter 4 [4 Kings 4:8-37 / 2 Kings 4:8-37]):*[^360]

And it fell on a day, that Elisha passed to Shunem, where was a great woman; and she constrained him to eat bread. And it was so, that as oft as he passed by, he turned in thither to eat bread. And the woman said unto her husband: "Behold now, I perceive that this holy man of God passes by us continually. Let us make for him a little chamber, and let us set for him there a bed, and a table, and a seat, and a lampstand: and it shall be, when he comes to us, that he shall turn in thither." And it fell on a day, that he came thither, and he turned into the chamber, and slept there. And he said to Gehazi his servant: "Call this Shunammite." And when he had called her, she stood before him. And he said unto him: "Say now unto her: Behold, thou hast cared for us with all this care; what is to be done for thee? Hast thou any word to the king, or to the captain of the host?" And she answered: "(There is none), I dwell among mine own people." And he said to Gehazi: "What then is to be done for her?" And Gehazi his servant answered: "Truly she hath no son, and her husband is old." And he said: "Call her." And when he had called her, she stood at the door. And Elisha said unto her: "At this season, according to the time of life, thou shalt conceive a son." And she said: "Nay, my lord, do not lie unto thine handmaid." And the woman conceived, and bare a son at that season according to the time of life, as Elisha had said unto her. And the child grew; and it fell on a day, that he went out to his father to the reapers. And he said unto his father: "My head, my head (aches)!" And he said to a servant: "Carry him to his mother." And he carried him to his mother, and he sat on her knees till noon, and then died. And she took him up, and laid him on the bed of the man of God, and shut the door upon him, and went out. And she called unto her husband, and said: "Send me, I pray thee, one of the young men, and one of the donkeys, and I will run to the man of God, and come again." And he said: "Why wilt thou go to him today? It is neither new moon, nor sabbath." And she said: "It is well." Then she saddled a donkey, and said to her servant: "Drive, and go forward; slack not thy riding for me, except I bid thee. Go, and proceed, and come unto the man of God to Mount Carmel." And she went, and came unto the man of God to Mount Carmel. And it came to pass, when Elisha saw her coming afar off, that he said to Gehazi his servant: "Behold, yonder is that Shunammite! Run now to meet her, and say unto her: Is it well with thee? Is it well with thy husband? Is it well with the child?" And she answered: "It is well." And when she came to the man of God to the hill, she caught him by the feet: but Gehazi came near to thrust her away. And Elisha said: "Let her alone; for her soul is vexed within her: and the Lord hath hidden it from me, and hath not told me." Then she said: "Did I desire a son of my lord? Did I not say, Do not deceive me?" Then Elisha said to Gehazi: "Gird up thy loins, and take my staff in thine hand, and go thy way: if thou meet any man, salute him not; and if any salute thee, answer him not again: and lay my staff upon the face of the child." And the mother of the child said: "As the Lord lives, and as thy soul lives, I will not leave thee!" And Elisha arose, and followed her. And Gehazi passed on before them, and laid the staff upon the face of the child; but there was neither voice, nor hearing. Therefore he went again to meet him, and told him, saying: "The child is not awaked." And when Elisha was come into the house, behold, the child was dead, and laid upon his bed. And Elisha went in, and shut the door upon them twain, and prayed unto the Lord. And he went up, and lay upon the child, and put his mouth upon his mouth, and his eyes upon his eyes, and his hands upon his hands: and he stretched himself upon the child, and breathed upon him, and the flesh of the child waxed warm. Then he returned, and walked in the house to and fro; and went up, and stretched himself upon the child seven times: and the child opened his eyes. And Elisha cried unto Gehazi, and said: "Call this Shunammite." So he called her. And when she was come in unto him, Elisha said: "Take up thy son." Then the woman went in, and fell at his feet, and bowed herself to the ground, and took up her son, and went out.

*The Thirteenth Reading: from the Prophecy of Isaiah (Chapters 63–64 [Isa 63:11 - 64:5]):*[^361]

Thus saith the Lord: "Where is He that brought them up out of the earth with the shepherd of His sheep? Where is He that put His Holy Spirit within them? That led them by the right hand of Moses with His glorious arm, dividing the water before them, to make Himself an everlasting name? That led them through the deep, as an horse through the wilderness, and they stumbled not? As cattle go down into the valley, the Spirit from the Lord came down and guided them: so didst Thou lead Thy people, to make Thyself a glorious name. Turn Thee, O Lord, from heaven, and behold from the habitation of Thy holiness and of Thy glory: where is Thy zeal and Thy strength? Where is the multitude of Thy mercies and of Thy compassions, seeing Thou hast endured us? For Thou art our Father, though Abraham knows us not, and Israel acknowledgs us not; but Thou, O Lord, art our Father: deliver us, from the beginning Thy name is upon us. O Lord, why hast Thou made us to err from Thy way, and hardened our heart from fearing Thee? Return for Thy servants' sake, the tribes of Thine inheritance: that we may inherit Thy holy mountain for a little while; our adversaries have trodden down Thy sanctuary. We are become as in the beginning, when Thou barest not rule over us, neither was Thy name called upon us. Oh that Thou wouldest rend the heavens, that the mountains might tremble and melt away at Thy presence! [Chapter 64] As wax melts before the fire, and fire consums the adversaries, so let Thy name be made known to Thine adversaries, that the nations may tremble at Thy presence! When Thou doest marvelous things:

the mountains shall tremble at Thy presence. From the beginning of the world men have not heard, neither have our eyes seen any God beside Thee, and Thy works, which Thou wilt do for them that wait for Thy mercy. For mercy shall meet them that work righteousness, and Thy ways shall be remembered."

*The Fourteenth Reading: from the Prophecy of Jeremiah (Chapter 31 [Jer 31:31-34]):*[^362]

Thus saith the Lord: "Behold, the days come, that I will make a new covenant with the house of Israel, and with the house of Judah: not according to the covenant that I made with their fathers in the day that I took them by the hand to bring them out of the land of Egypt; which My covenant they continued not in, and I regarded them not, saith the Lord. For this is the covenant that I will make with the house of Israel after those days, saith the Lord: I will put My laws into their mind, and write them in their hearts; and I will be to them a God, and they shall be to Me a people. And they shall not teach every man his neighbor, and every man his brother, saying: Know the Lord: for all shall know Me, from the least even unto the greatest of them: for I will be merciful to their unrighteousness, and their sins will I remember no more."

*The Fifteenth Reading: from the Prophecy of Daniel (Chapter 3 [Dan 3:1-56/88]):*[^363]

In the eighteenth year Nebuchadnezzar the king made an image of gold, whose height was threescore cubits, and the breadth thereof six cubits: he set it up in the plain of Dura, in the province of Babylon. Then Nebuchadnezzar the king sent to gather together the satraps, the captains, and the governors, the leaders and the chief magistrates, and them that were in authority, and all the rulers of the provinces, to come to the dedication of the image which Nebuchadnezzar the king had set up. Then the governors, the satraps, the captains, the leaders, the chief magistrates, and they that were over authorities, and all the rulers of the provinces, were gathered together unto the dedication of the image that Nebuchadnezzar the king had set up; and they stood before the image that Nebuchadnezzar the king had set up. Then an herald cried aloud: "To you it is commanded, O peoples, nations, and languages: that at what hour ye hear the sound of the trumpet, pipe, and harp, sackbut, psaltery, symphony, and all kinds of music, ye fall down and worship the golden image that Nebuchadnezzar the king hath set up. And whoso falls not down and worships, shall the same hour be cast into the midst of a burning fiery furnace." Therefore at that time, when all the people heard the sound of the trumpet, pipe, and harp, sackbut, psaltery, symphony, and all kinds of music, all the peoples, nations, and languages fell down and worshipped the golden image that Nebuchadnezzar the king had set up. Therefore at that time certain Chaldean men came near, and accused the Jews. They spake and said to the king Nebuchadnezzar: "O king, live for ever! Thou, O king, hast made a decree, that every man that shall hear the sound of the trumpet, pipe, and harp, sackbut, psaltery, symphony, and all kinds of music, and falls not down and worships the golden image, shall be cast into the midst of a burning fiery furnace. There are certain Jews whom thou hast set over the affairs of the province of Babylon: Shadrach, Meshach, and Abednego; these men, O king, have not regarded thy decree: they serve not thy gods, nor worship the golden image which thou hast set up." Then Nebuchadnezzar in his rage and fury commanded to bring Shadrach, Meshach, and Abednego. Then they brought these men before the king. Nebuchadnezzar spake and said unto them: "Is it true, O Shadrach, Meshach, and Abednego, do not ye serve my gods, nor worship the golden image which I have set up? Now if ye be ready that at what time ye hear the sound of the trumpet, pipe, and harp, sackbut, psaltery, symphony, and all kinds of music, ye fall down and worship the golden image which I have made, well: but if ye worship not, ye shall be cast the same hour into the midst of a burning fiery furnace; and who is that God that shall deliver you out of my hands?" Shadrach, Meshach, and Abednego answered and said to the king Nebuchadnezzar: "We have no need to answer thee in this matter. For our God Whom we serve is able to deliver us from the burning fiery furnace, and He will deliver us out of thine hand, O king. But if not, be it known unto thee, O king, that we will not serve thy gods, nor worship the golden image which thou hast set up."
