Then was Nebuchadnezzar full of fury, and the form of his visage was changed against Shadrach, Meshach, and Abednego: therefore he spake, and commanded that they should heat the furnace seven times more than it was wont to be heated. And he commanded mighty men of strength to bind Shadrach, Meshach, and Abednego, and to cast them into the burning fiery furnace. Then these men were bound in their coats, their hosen, and their hats, and their other garments, and were cast into the midst of the burning fiery furnace. Therefore because the king's commandment was urgent, and the furnace exceeding hot, the flame of the fire slew those men that took up Shadrach, Meshach, and Abednego. And these three men, Shadrach, Meshach, and Abednego, fell down bound into the midst of the burning fiery furnace. And they walked in the midst of the flame, singing unto God and blessing the Lord. Then Azarias stood up with them, and prayed on this manner; and opening his mouth in the midst of the fire, he said: "Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers: Thy name is worthy to be praised and glorified for evermore. For Thou art righteous in all the things that Thou hast done to us: yea, true are all Thy works, Thy ways are right, and all Thy judgments truth. In all the things that Thou hast brought upon us, and upon the holy city of our fathers, even Jerusalem, Thou hast executed true judgment: for according to truth and judgment didst Thou bring all these things upon us because of our sins. For we have sinned and committed iniquity, departing from Thee; and we have transgressed in all things, and have not obeyed Thy commandments, nor kept them, neither done as Thou hast commanded us, that it might go well with us. Therefore all that Thou hast brought upon us, and every thing that Thou hast done to us, Thou hast done in true judgment. And Thou didst deliver us into the hands of lawless enemies, most hateful forsakers of God, and to an unjust king, and the most wicked in all the world. And now we cannot open our mouths, we are become a shame and reproach to Thy servants, and to them that worship Thee. Yet deliver us not up wholly, for Thy name's sake, neither disannul Thou Thy covenant: and cause not Thy mercy to depart from us, for Thy beloved Abraham's sake, for Thy servant Isaac's sake, and for Thy holy Israel's sake; to whom Thou hast spoken and promised, that Thou wouldest multiply their seed as the stars of heaven, and as the sand that lies upon the seashore. For we, O Master, are become less than any nation, and be kept under this day in all the world because of our sins. Neither is there at this time prince, or prophet, or leader, or burnt offering, or sacrifice, or oblation, or incense, or place to sacrifice before Thee, and to find mercy. Nevertheless in a contrite heart and an humble spirit let us be accepted. Like as in the burnt offerings of rams and bullocks, and like as in ten thousands of fat lambs: so let our sacrifice be in Thy sight this day, and grant that we may wholly go after Thee: for they shall not be confounded that put their trust in Thee. And now we follow Thee with all our heart, we fear Thee, and seek Thy face. Put us not to shame: but deal with us after Thy lovingkindness, and according to the multitude of Thy mercies. Deliver us also according to Thy marvelous works, and give glory to Thy name, O Lord: and let all them that do Thy servants hurt be ashamed; and let them be confounded in all their power and might, and let their strength be broken; and let them know that Thou art the Lord God alone, and glorious over the whole world." And the king's servants, that put them in, ceased not to make the oven hot with rosin, pitch, tow, and small wood; so that the flame streamed forth above the furnace forty and nine cubits. And it passed through, and burned those Chaldeans it found about the furnace. But the Angel of the Lord came down into the oven together with Azarias and his fellows, and smote the flame of the fire out of the oven; and made the midst of the furnace as it had been a moist whistling wind, so that the fire touched them not at all, neither hurt nor troubled them. Then the three, as out of one mouth, praised, blessed, and glorified God in the furnace, saying: "Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers: and to be praised and exalted above all for ever; and blessed is Thy holy and glorious name: and to be praised and exalted above all for ever. Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thine holy glory: and to be praised and exalted above all for ever. Blessed art Thou that beholdest the depths, and sittest upon the Cherubim: and to be praised and exalted above all for ever. Blessed art Thou on the glorious throne of Thy kingdom: and to be praised and exalted above all for ever. Blessed art Thou in the firmament of heaven: and to be praised and exalted above all for ever."

*We stand here, and sing:*[^364]

Praise the Lord, and exalt Him above all for ever!

*The Reader says the verses:*

*Reader:* Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord:  
*Choir:* Praise the Lord, and exalt Him above all for ever!

*Reader:* Bless the Lord, ye Angels of the Lord, ye heavens of the Lord:  
*Choir:* Praise the Lord, and exalt Him above all for ever!

*Reader:* Bless the Lord, all ye waters that be above the heavens, all ye powers of the Lord:  
*Choir:* Praise the Lord, and exalt Him above all for ever!

*Reader:* Bless the Lord, O sun and moon, ye stars of heaven:  
*Choir:* Praise the Lord, and exalt Him above all for ever!

*Reader:* Bless the Lord, every shower and dew, and all ye winds:  
*Choir:* Praise the Lord, and exalt Him above all for ever!

*Reader:* Bless the Lord, O fire and heat, winter and summer:  
*Choir:* Praise the Lord, and exalt Him above all for ever!

*Reader:* Bless the Lord, ye dews and storms of snow, ice and cold:  
*Choir:* Praise the Lord, and exalt Him above all for ever!

*Reader:* Bless the Lord, ye frosts and snows, nights and days:  
*Choir:* Praise the Lord, and exalt Him above all for ever!

*Reader:* Bless the Lord, O light and darkness, lightnings and clouds:  
*Choir:* Praise the Lord, and exalt Him above all for ever!

*Reader:* Let the earth bless the Lord, the mountains and hills, and all things that spring up upon them:  
*Choir:* Praise the Lord, and exalt Him above all for ever!

*Reader:* Bless the Lord, ye fountains, seas and rivers, whales and all that move in the waters:  
*Choir:* Praise the Lord, and exalt Him above all for ever!

*Reader:* Bless the Lord, all ye fowls of the air, beasts and all cattle:  
*Choir:* Praise the Lord, and exalt Him above all for ever!

*Reader:* Bless the Lord, ye children of men, let Israel bless the Lord:  
*Choir:* Praise the Lord, and exalt Him above all for ever!

*Reader:* Bless the Lord, ye Priests of the Lord, ye servants of the Lord:  
*Choir:* Praise the Lord, and exalt Him above all for ever!

*Reader:* Bless the Lord, ye spirits and souls of the righteous, holy and humble men of heart:  
*Choir:* Praise the Lord, and exalt Him above all for ever!

*Reader:* Bless the Lord, Ananias, Azarias, and Misael:  
*Choir:* Praise the Lord, and exalt Him above all for ever!

*Reader:* Bless the Lord, ye Apostles, Prophets, and Martyrs of the Lord:  
*Choir:* Praise the Lord, and exalt Him above all for ever!

*Reader:* Let us bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit:  
*Choir:* Praise the Lord, we sing and exalt Him above all for ever!

*Reader:* Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.  
*Choir:* Praise the Lord, and exalt Him above all for ever!

*Reader and Choir:* We praise, we bless, we worship the Lord, singing and exalting Him above all for ever!

*Then the Little Litany.*

*And instead of the Trisagion:*[^365]

*As many of you as have been baptized into Christ have put on Christ: Alleluia.*

*Prokeimenon, Tone 5:*

*We say not the Psalm of David, but immediately the Prokeimenon:*

Let all the earth worship Thee, and sing unto Thee; yea, let them sing unto Thy name, O Most High!

*Verse:* Shout with jubilation unto the Lord, all the earth; chant now unto His name!

*The Epistle to the Romans, Pericope 91:*

Brethren: as many of us as were baptized into Jesus Christ were baptized into His death.[^366] Therefore we were buried with Him through baptism into death: that just as Christ was raised up from the dead by the glory of the Father, even so we also should walk in newness of life. For if we have been united together in the likeness of His death, certainly we shall also be [partakers] of His resurrection, knowing this, that our old man was crucified with Him, that the body of sin might be done away with, that we should no longer be slaves of sin. For he who hath died is freed from sin. Now if we died with Christ, we believe that we shall also live with Him, knowing that Christ, having been raised from the dead, dies no more; death hath no more dominion over Him. For the death that He died, He died unto sin once for all; but the life that He lives, He lives unto God. Likewise you also, reckon yourselves to be dead indeed unto sin, but alive unto God in Christ Jesus our Lord.

*Alleluia is not sung, but after the Priest says: "Peace be unto thee," immediately the Reader says instead of Alleluia:*[^367]

*Tone 7:*

Arise, O God, judge the earth: for Thou shalt inherit in all nations!

*And the Chanters sing the same, and the Reader says the verses:*

*Verse 1:* God stood in the congregation of gods, and in the midst He judges gods.

*Verse 2:* How long will ye judge unrighteously, and accept the persons of sinners?

*Verse 3:* Judge the orphan and the poor; justify the humble and the needy.

*Verse 4:* Rescue the poor and the needy; deliver him out of the hand of the sinner.

*Verse 5:* They have not known, neither have they understood; they walk in darkness.

*Verse 6:* Let all the foundations of the earth be shaken. I have said: Ye are gods, and all of you sons of the Most High; but ye die like men, and fall like one of the princes.

*And again:*

Arise, O God, judge the earth: for Thou shalt inherit in all nations!

*And while they sing: "Arise, O God, judge the earth," the Priest and Deacons divest themselves of their dark vestments, and vest in white.*

*The Gospel from Matthew, Pericope 115:*[^368]

*In the end of the Sabbath: Ending: unto the end of the world. Amen.*

*And according to order, the Divine Liturgy of the Great Basil.*

*And instead of the Cherubic Hymn, the following troparion is sung:*[^369]

*Tone 8:*

Let all mortal flesh keep silence, and stand with fear and trembling, and think of nothing earthly within itself: for the King of kings and Lord of lords comes forth to be slain, and to be given as food to the faithful. And the choirs of Angels go before Him with every principality and power: the many-eyed Cherubim, and the six-winged Seraphim, veiling their faces, and crying out the hymn: Alleluia, alleluia, alleluia!

*Communion Verse:*

The Lord awoke as one out of sleep, and is risen, saving us. Alleluia.

*And Antidoron is given by the Priest, and the Dismissal.*

*Afterwards, the customary blessing is given to the bread and wine prepared upon an analoy: the Priest approaches, and having censed them, he says the customary prayer of blessing.*[^370]

*Typikon Rubric: But if due to any great necessity there be no Liturgy, or if anyone wishes to read privately, let him read:*[^371]

*On the Aposticha, Resurrection Stichera:*

*Tone 1:*

By Thy Passion, O Christ, we have been set free from passions; and by Thy Resurrection we have been delivered from corruption: O Lord, glory to Thee!

*Verse 1: The Lord is King, He is clothed with majesty.*

Let creation rejoice, let the heavens be glad, let the nations clap their hands with gladness: for Christ our Savior hath nailed our sins unto the Cross; and having slain death, He hath bestowed life upon us, raising up fallen Adam, the father of all mankind, as the Lover of mankind.

*Verse 2: For He hath established the world, which shall not be moved.*

Being King of heaven and earth, O Incomprehensible One, of Thine own will Thou wast crucified for love of mankind: Whom hades meeting below was embittered, and the souls of the righteous receiving Thee rejoiced; and Adam, seeing Thee, the Creator, in the nethermost regions, arose. O marvel! How did the Life of all taste death? But as Thou didst desire, Thou hast enlightened the world, which cries aloud and saith: Risen from the dead, O Lord, glory to Thee!

*Verse 3: Holiness becomes Thine house, O Lord, unto length of days.*

The Myrrh-bearing women, bearing myrrh, with haste and lamentation reached Thy tomb; and not finding Thy most pure Body, but learning from the Angel of the new and most glorious wonder, said unto the Apostles: The Lord is risen, bestowing great mercy upon the world!

*Glory, both now: Theotokion:*

Behold, the prophecy of Isaiah is fulfilled: for a Virgin hast thou given birth, and after childbirth, as before childbirth, hast thou remained. For God was born of thee, and therefore He also made nature anew. But, O Mother of God, despise not the supplications of thy servants offered unto thee in thy temple, but as thou bearest the Tender-hearted One in thine arms, have compassion upon thy servants: and pray that our souls may be saved!

*Then, "Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart"; Trisagion; after "Our Father": Troparia: "The noble Joseph"; Glory: "When Thou didst descend unto death"; Both now: "Unto the Myrrh-bearing women at the tomb"; and the Dismissal.*

*The ecclesiarch must take careful heed that when the Liturgy is concluded, it be the second hour of the night.*[^372]

*And after the Dismissal we go forth from the Church unto the refectory, and having sat down as customary, each in his own place, we eat what is set before us in silence and reverence.*

*The Greek Typikon commands the brethren who remain continuously in the church to partake of the food set before them, the Reader having begun immediately after the blessing of the loaves to read the Acts of the Holy Apostles.*

*And having risen from the table, we begin:*

## ON HOLY AND GREAT SATURDAY, AT COMPLINE

*Whereon we sing the Canon:*

*Note: This Canon is not found in the Greek Triodion, neither do the Greek books prescribe Compline.*[^373]

*On the Bodily Burial of the Lord God and our Savior Jesus Christ, and on the Lamentation of the Most Holy Theotokos.*

*The work of Symeon the Logothete, with the Heirmos to 6.*

*Tone 6:*

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*

He Who in ancient times concealed the pursuer and tyrant with the billows of the sea, the children of the saved have hidden beneath the earth: but let us, as the maidens, sing unto the Lord: for He hath been gloriously glorified!

*Refrain:* Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.

*Troparion:*

Desiring to quicken Thine own creation, O my Savior, hanging upon the Cross and wounded with nails Thou wast; and Thy spotless Mother standing by was consumed in heart, O Master, beholding the streams of Thy blood.[^374]

*Troparion:*

Bowing Thy divine neck, O my Savior, Co-inherently-living upon the Tree Thou didst fall asleep: whereat all creation shakes, and the choirs of Angels tremble to sing, beholding Thee suffering in the Flesh.

*Glory:*

The Mother, desiring secretly to approach the secret disciple Joseph, and weeping in secret, made supplication unto him to beg Thy most pure Body of Pilate the lawless judge.

*Both now:*

Approach Pilate, doubt not in hope, ask for the most pure Body of Christ God; and having taken It down from the Tree, hide It in thy tomb, lest the unmerciful pierce His Side again.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

Thee Who didst hang the whole earth unsupported upon the waters, creation beholding hanging upon the Place of the Skull was seized with great trembling, crying aloud: None is holy save Thee, O Lord!

*Troparion:*

None dars to ask for the most pure Body of the Teacher, hanging upon the Tree, lifeless, dead, and wounded, shot through with reproaches, and above all pierced in the Side.

*Troparion:*

Peter, the first disciple, hath denied; all have forsaken the Teacher. Give ear, O noble one, and having begged It, take down the Body of my Master, and alone will I lament Him as the most beloved Child, dear unto His Mother!

*Glory:*

Joseph, when he beheld Thy weeping Mother, O my Christ, hearing her words of grief, was moved with pity and joined in her weeping, and quickly made haste to beg Thy most pure Body.

*Both now:*

Looking with favor upon Thy greatness, Pilate grants Thy divine Body: and Joseph, having taken It down from the Tree with Nicodemus, laid It in the earth, and the earth trembled, and hades surrendered the dead with haste.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*

Foreseeing Thy divine emptying upon the Cross, Habakkuk cried out in terror: Thou hast cut short the power of the mighty, O Good One, joining Thyself unto them that were in hades, as the Almighty!

*Troparion:*

Embracing Thee, the All-Blameless One kissed the slaughter of Thy most pure members, saying: I will die with Thee, for I cannot bear, O Child, to behold the shedding of Thy most pure Blood.

*Troparion:*

Receiving Thee, She who bare Thee, and laying Thee upon her knees, O Christ, weeping kissed Thy hands, wiping away from Thy ribs Thy dripping divine Blood, O Master.

*Glory:*

Kissing Thy voiceless mouth, She who bare Thee, O Christ, pressing Thee to herself, weeping embraced Thee, who art both Man and God.

*Both now:*

Persian kings brought gifts unto Thee, O Master, worshipping Thee as God; but now, O Child, Nicodemus brings ointments for burial as unto a mortal. How shall I bury Thee, O my God?

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

Early in the morning I wake unto Thee, Who for mercy's sake didst empty Thyself without change, and even unto suffering didst impassibly condescend, O Word of God: grant peace unto me who am fallen, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparion:*

I cannot bear, O Child, to hold Thee breathless, Whom I held for long as an infant playing in my arms. If Thou descendest unto Adam into hades, I also will descend and announce Thy mysteries unto Eve.

*Troparion:*

Weep not, O Mother, said Christ unto Her who divinely bare Him; for thou shalt see Me risen again, and radiantly proclaiming "Rejoice!" unto thee, and raising Adam with Eve.

*Glory:*

How can I bear the pangs of my inward parts, O Child, kissing Thine eyes extinguished upon the Tree?—which aforetime I beheld shining beautiful in splendor, and enlightening the blind by Thy divine behest.

*Both now:*

Shall I rather descend into hades to behold Thy glory, O my Son, and ascend again with Thee? For I cannot bear to hold Thy breathless Body motionless, and to kiss Thy wounded members.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

Jonah was caught, but not held fast in the belly of the sea monster; for bearing an image of Thee, Who didst suffer and wast given over to burial, he came forth from the monster as from a bridal chamber, and he cried aloud unto the guard: Ye that observe vanity and falsehood, ye have forsaken this mercy![^375]

*Troparion:*

How hast thou denied the Savior, O Peter, Whom thou didst confess as Christ, the Son of the living God, and hast left Him alone crucified upon the Tree, without breath and without form, nor didst thou make haste to hide His Body in a tomb? Thus spake the Honored One, lamenting with motherly grief.

*Troparion:*

Grant me a word from Thy sweetest lips, O Word! the Pure One cried unto Thee with weeping: I cannot bear to lay Thee breathless in the tomb, O my Son, Who art free among the dead! Behold Thy Mother weeping unceasingly, O Master, for a burning sword hath pierced my heart.

*Glory:*

Come, all ye Heavenly Powers, behold the Body of Christ abandoned by all His friends and acquaintances, and...

...all His chosen disciples, and be dismayed! I alone weep, lamenting my slaughtered Child, together with the one beloved disciple, His messenger.

*Both now:*

The undefiled Pure One called together the company of Myrrh-Bearing Women, O Word, and stirred up lamentation: Come, ye friends, weep, crying aloud for the much-suffering Body of the Teacher! Come, behold the voiceless mouth, the motionless lips, the closed eyes of Him in Whose hand is the breath of the living!

*Tone 6. Kontakion:*[^376]

He Who shut the abyss is beheld dead, and wrapped in myrrh and a winding-sheet, the Immortal One is laid in a tomb as a mortal; and women came to anoint Him with myrrh, weeping bitterly and crying aloud: This is the most blessed Sabbath, wherein Christ, having fallen asleep, shall rise again on the third day!

*Ikos:*

He Who upholds all things was lifted up upon the Cross, and all creation laments, beholding Him hanging naked upon the Tree: the sun hid its rays, and the stars withdrew their light; the earth with great fear was shaken, and the sea fled, and the rocks were rent; and many tombs were opened, and the bodies of holy men arose; hades groans below, the Jews take counsel to slander the Resurrection of Christ, and the women cry aloud: This is the most blessed Sabbath, wherein Christ, having fallen asleep, shall rise again on the third day!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

Ineffable wonder! He Who delivered the holy youths from the flame in the furnace is laid dead and breathless in a tomb, for the salvation of us who sing: Blessed art Thou, O God our Redeemer!

*Troparion:*

Bowing down from the heavens, the angelic choirs marveled, beholding Him dead upon the Mother's bosom, Whom they look upon with trembling as they stand before Him sitting in the bosom of the Father, and Who raised the dead from the graves.

*Troparion:*

All the angelic ranks ran trembling above and below to see the breathless Body of Christ laid upon the Mother's bosom, and seized with wonder, they returned with fear and cried out in awe: Blessed art Thou, O God our Redeemer!

*Glory:*

Addressing the revealer of the mysteries of Thy Nativity, O Master, the Virgin wept bitterly: Woe is me, O Gabriel! Where is the Annunciation? Where for me is "Rejoice"? Where is "Blessed art thou"? For the Light from my womb hath been extinguished upon the Cross.

*Both now:*

How did the lawless people have no pity, O sweetest Child, beholding Thee hanging naked upon the Tree, but like savage wild beasts pierced Thy divine ribs? How was shaken creation not dissolved? Yet manifestly all things were held together by Thy hand, O Word!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

Be astonished and afraid, O heaven, and let the foundations of the earth be shaken! For lo, He that dwells in the highest is numbered among the dead, and is lodged as a stranger in a small tomb: Him do ye children bless, ye priests praise, ye people exalt supremely unto all ages!

*Troparion:*

Be astonished, O heaven and earth, and hide the beam of thy light, O sun! For lo, He that placed light within thee, the only God Who shone forth from my womb as a blossom, hath set upon the Tree, desiring to raise up Adam; yet holding Him breathless, I cannot endure the anguish.

*Troparion:*

How shall I cover Thy most pure Body in a dark tomb, O my Son and God, Who didst empty the graves? And shall I return with empty hands, not beholding Thee, O Child? I cannot bear to depart from Thee, O my Son; rather will I die and descend into hades!

*Glory:*

Give ear, O heaven and earth, and lend an ear to my maternal lamentations! For lo, your Maker was crucified by lawless hands; and lo, the Creator is seen without form and without breath upon His Mother's bosom, having been slaughtered by them that crucified Him.

*Both now:*

While the Pure One thus cried aloud: Would that I might hear the voice of sweetest Jesus, and the lips divinely moving, and see Thine eyes shining brightly, beautiful in splendor; that I might see Thee risen again, O my Son, and be filled with joy instead of bitterness!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*

Weep not for Me, O Mother, beholding in the tomb the Son Whom thou didst conceive in the womb without seed; for I shall arise and be glorified, and as God I shall unceasingly exalt in glory them that magnify thee with faith and love.

*Troparion:*

Instead of swaddling bands, O my Son, I wrap Thee in a winding-sheet; instead of a manger, O my Light, I lay Thee in a dark tomb; instead of milk, I sprinkle Thy most pure lips with tears; and instead of living and beautiful words from Thee, I converse with One Who is dead.

*Troparion:*

Weep not for Me, O Mother, as a mortal in the tomb, Whom in swaddling bands thou didst behold as God, devoutly worshipped by the Persian Magi, while the army of angels cried aloud: Glory to the Risen One! for unto thee first will I appear.

*Glory:*

Go, O Child, into hades, to raise up as God with Adam those sprung from Adam who have fallen by corruption; but I, shedding maternal tears, sit directly before the gates of Thy tomb, O my God, until Thou dost arise!

*Both now:*

O all-blessed Mother! weeping before the gates of the tomb, shed tears no longer; for I shall arise and be glorified, and having radiantly proclaimed "Rejoice!" unto thee, I will bestow ineffable joy, having ascended into the heavens.

*Then in place of "It is truly meet": Heirmos: Weep not for Me, O Mother:*

*Then the Trisagion; and after "Our Father": Kontakion: He Who shut the abyss; and the rest as usual. And the Dismissal.*

*After the dismissal of Compline (if it be celebrated) there takes place the Reading from the Acts of the Holy Apostles[^377] from the beginning. The reader says the beginning, and the priest the verse: "Through the prayers of the holy Apostles, O Lord Jesus Christ..." And the reader begins to read, while all the brethren listen with diligence. And the readers relieve one another, reading even until the fourth hour of the night, or as far as they attain; but let the whole Acts be read unto the end.*

*Thereafter the Paraecclesiarch lights all the lamps in their places, and having taken a blessing from the superior, he goes forth and strikes the klepalo, and the reader concludes the reading.*

*The Midnight Office.*[^378]

*And straightway, the priest having given the blessing, we say the Trisagion; and after "Our Father": "Lord, have mercy" (12 times).*
*Glory, both now: "Come, let us worship" (thrice).*
*Then Psalm 50 [51], and the Canon of Great Saturday Matins in Tone 6: "He Who in ancient times concealed..." The Heirmos twice, and the troparia to 12. And at the end, the Heirmos by both choirs together.*
*After Ode 3, the Sessional Hymn: "Thy tomb, O Savior..." Glory, both now: the same.*
*And the homily of Saint Epiphanius of Cyprus[^379] is read, whose beginning is: "What is this great silence today?"*
*After Ode 6, the Kontakion and Ikos of Great Saturday.*
*Then a reading from the words of Chrysostom, from Matthew, at the end of the commentary on the Gospel, or the sermon on the Resurrection from John.*
*Then Ode 7.*
*After Ode 9, at the end, the Heirmos by both choirs together, and a prostration to the ground.*
*And after the conclusion of the Canon, the Trisagion; and after "Our Father": Troparion: "When Thou didst descend unto death..."*

*Then the Litany:*[^380]

**Deacon [or Priest]:** Have mercy on us, O God, according to Thy great mercy, we pray Thee, hear and have mercy.

**Choir:** Lord, have mercy (thrice).

**Deacon [or Priest]:** Again we pray for the preservation of this city (or: this village, or: this holy monastery), and every city, village, and countryside, from famine, plague, earthquake, flood, hail, fire, the sword, invasion of foreign enemies, and civil strife; and that our good and man-befriending God may be merciful, gracious, and easily entreated, and turn away all wrath stirred up against us, and deliver us from His impending righteous chastisement, and have mercy on us.

*If there be a drought, say:*

And that He may send down upon the earth a good rain unto the multiplication of the fruits of the earth, let us all say.

*If there be excessive rain, add this:*

And that He may still the excessive and untimely pouring of rain, and grant us temperate weather, and cause the sun's rays to shine upon the earth, unto the gathering [or: unto the multiplication] of the fruits of the earth, let us all say.

**Choir:** Lord, have mercy (40 times).

*Then the Priest makes the exclamation:*

**Priest:** Hear us, O God our Savior, the hope of all the ends of the earth, and of them that are far off upon the sea; and be merciful, be merciful, O Master, unto us sinners, and have mercy on us: for a merciful God and the Lover of mankind art Thou, and unto Thee do we send up glory, to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages.

**Choir:** Amen.

*Then the Priest:*

**Priest:** Glory to Thee, O Christ God, our hope, glory to Thee.

*And the Dismissal:*[^381]

**Priest:** May He Who for the sake of us men and for our salvation voluntarily was laid in the grave in the Flesh and trod down hades, Christ our true God, through the prayers of His most pure Mother, of our venerable and God-bearing fathers, and of all the saints, have mercy on us and save us, as He is good and the Lover of mankind.

**Choir:** Amen.

[Full-page plate engraving: The Resurrection of our Lord Jesus Christ.][^382]
[The plate depicts the Risen Christ the Savior in the radiant light of the Resurrection, ascending above the tomb and the blossoms of the earth with hands uplifted in blessing. Above Him is an assembly of celestial bodiless hosts and cherubim glorifying His radiance. To the left is depicted a tree and the rolled-away stone of the open sepulchre. In the lower right corner is inscribed: "After a sketch and under the direction of Ev. Voronets. 1901."] 
[At the foot of the leaf within ornamental flourishes is inscribed:]
Truly the Lord is risen... (Luke 24:34)

# PART I: GREAT AND HOLY PASCHA AND BRIGHT WEEK

*Beginning with God the Holy.*

*The Pentecostarion, that is, the Festal Fifty Days:*[^383]

*Beginning from the Holy and Great Sunday of Pascha at Matins, and concluding even unto the Sunday of All Saints: containing abundantly the entire order of services for every day.*

## The Holy and Great Sunday of Pascha

*At Matins:*[^384]

*At the hour of Matins, the paraecclesiarch, having taken a blessing from the superior, goes forth and strikes the great bell, and beats the klepalo sufficiently. And entering into the temple, he lights all the tapers and the oil lamps; and he prepares two vessels with burning coals, and puts therein much fragrant incense, and sets one vessel in the midst of the church, and the other in the holy sanctuary, so that the whole church may be filled with fragrance. Then the superior, entering into the holy sanctuary with the priests and deacons, vests in all his most radiant vestments. And he distributes tapers unto the brethren; and taking the precious Cross, while the deacon takes the censer, a priest the Holy Gospel, and another priest the Icon of the Resurrection of Christ, they stand with their faces toward the west. And they close the church doors that face toward the west. And the superior goes forth with the priests into the narthex through the northern door, while deacons go before them with two tapers, and both choirs, chanting the Sticheron:*

*Tone 6:*

Thy Resurrection, O Christ Savior, the angels sing in the heavens; enable us also on earth to glorify Thee with a pure heart.

*Then they peal all the bells and the heavy bell, and strike the klepalo sufficiently. And entering into the narthex, they stand with the Gospel and with the Icon, with their faces toward the west, as was indicated aforetime. Then the superior takes the censer from the deacon in his right hand, and the Cross in his left, and censes the icons, the choirs, and the brethren according to custom, the deacon carrying before him a burning taper. And all the brethren stand holding their tapers, praying within themselves with attentiveness, and giving thanks unto Christ our God Who suffered and is risen for our sake.*

*And at the conclusion of the censing, the superior comes before the great doors of the church, and censes the deacon who stands before him with a taper. Then the deacon, taking the censer from the superior's hands, censes the superior himself. And the superior, receiving back the censer and standing before the church doors looking toward the east, makes the sign of the Cross over the great doors of the church [which are closed] with the censer thrice, holding the precious Cross in his left hand, the taper-bearers standing on either side. And with a loud voice he exclaims:*

**Priest:** Glory to the holy, and consubstantial, and life-creating, and undivided Trinity, always, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages.

**Choir:** Amen.

*And the superior begins with the other ministers, with a loud voice, the present troparion in Tone 5:*[^385]

*Troparion, Tone 5:*

Christ is risen from the dead, trampling down death by death, and upon those in the tombs bestowing life.

*And we likewise chant the same sweetly. And this troparion is chanted thrice by the superior, and thrice by us.*

*Then the superior pronounces the verses:*[^386]

*Verse 1:*

Let God arise, and let His enemies be scattered, and let them that hate Him flee from before His face.

*And at each verse we sing the troparion:*

Christ is risen... *in full, thrice.*

*Verse 2:*

As smoke vanishes, so let them vanish; as wax melts before the face of fire.

Christ is risen... *(thrice).*

*Verse 3:*

So let the sinners perish from the face of God, but let the righteous be glad.

Christ is risen... *(thrice).*

*Verse 4:*

This is the day which the Lord hath made, let us rejoice and be glad therein.

Christ is risen... *(thrice).*

*Glory:* Christ is risen... *(once).*

*Both now:* Christ is risen... *(once).*

*Then the superior chants in a higher voice:*

Christ is risen from the dead, trampling down death by death;

*And he opens the doors: the superior enters with the precious Cross, while they go before him with two lanterns; and the brethren chant:*

And upon those in the tombs bestowing life.

*Then they peal all the bells, and strike the klepalo sufficiently, with three chimes.*

*And the superior having entered with the priests into the holy sanctuary, the deacon says the Great Litany:*

**Deacon:** In peace let us pray to the Lord.

*Exclamation:*

**Priest:** For unto Thee is due all glory, honor, and worship, to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages.

**Choir:** Amen.

*And the superior begins the Canon, the creation of master John of Damascus. Tone 1:*[^387]

*Heirmos: It is the day of Resurrection. The heirmoi to 8, and the troparia to 12, with the refrain: "Christ is risen from the dead." And again at the end each choir chants the Heirmos. And at the end, at the coming together, the katavasia, the same Heirmos: It is the day of Resurrection; and after it: Christ is risen... in full, thrice. And let the superior always make the beginning of the Canon at each ode, whether the right or left choir happens to begin. And at the beginning of the Canon he censes the holy icons, and both choirs, and the brethren according to order. And after each ode there is a Little Litany outside the sanctuary, as we said, on this holy day. The exclamation within the sanctuary from the priest. After Ode 1 the right choir chants; after Ode 3 the left choir chants. In this manner we chant also at the other odes.*

*The Canon, Tone 1. Ode 1. Heirmos:*

It is the day of Resurrection! Let us be illumined, O ye people! Pascha, the Pascha of the Lord! For from death unto life, and from earth unto heaven, hath Christ God brought us over, as we sing the song of victory!

*Refrain: Christ is risen from the dead.*

*Troparion:*

Let us purify our senses, and we shall behold Christ radiant in the unapproachable light of the Resurrection, and we shall clearly hear Him saying, "Rejoice!" as we sing the song of victory!

*Troparion:*

Let the heavens worthily rejoice, and let the earth be glad, and let the whole world, both visible and invisible, keep festival: for Christ is risen, our eternal joy!

*Then the Little Litany. And the Exclamation:*

**Priest:** For Thine is the majesty, and Thine is the kingdom, and the power, and the glory, of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages.

**Choir:** Amen.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

Come, let us drink a new drink, not miraculously brought forth from a barren rock, but the Fountain of incorruption springing from the tomb of Christ, in Whom we are established.

*Troparion:*

Now are all things filled with light, heaven and earth and the underworld: let all creation therefore celebrate the Resurrection of Christ, in Whom it is established.

*Troparion:*

Yesterday was I buried with Thee, O Christ; today I arise with Thee in Thy Resurrection; yesterday was I crucified with Thee; do Thou Thyself glorify me with Thee, O Savior, in Thy Kingdom!

*Then the Little Litany, and the Exclamation:*

**Priest:** For Thou art our God, and unto Thee do we send up glory, to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages.

**Choir:** Amen.

*Hypakoe, Tone 4:*[^388]

When they that were with Mary came before the dawn and found the stone rolled away from the tomb, they heard from the angel: Why seek ye among the dead as a man Him Who abides in everlasting light? Behold the winding-sheets of the tomb; run and proclaim unto the world that the Lord is risen, slaying death; for He is the Son of God, Who saves mankind!

*And a reading in Gregory the Theologian, whose beginning is: "Upon my watch will I stand."[^389] And after the reading, the brethren light their tapers again.*

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*

Let Habakkuk the divine speaker stand with us upon divine watch, and show forth the light-bearing angel who clearly saith: Today salvation is come to the world, for Christ is risen as almighty!

*Troparion:*

Christ appeared as a male child opening the Virgin's womb; and as a mortal He was called a Lamb; without blemish also, as tasting of no defilement, is our Pascha; and as true God He is proclaimed perfect.

*Troparion:*

As a yearling lamb, Christ, our blessed crown, was willingly slaughtered for all as a cleansing Pascha; and once again from the tomb hath the beautiful Sun of Righteousness shone forth upon us.

*Troparion:*

David the forefather of God leaped, dancing before the symbolic ark; and beholding the fulfillment of the figures, let us the holy people of God rejoice divinely: for Christ is risen as almighty!

*Little Litany, and Exclamation:*

**Priest:** For a good God and the Lover of mankind art Thou, and unto Thee do we send up glory, to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages.

**Choir:** Amen.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

Let us awake early in the deep dawn, and instead of myrrh let us bring a hymn unto the Master; and we shall behold Christ, the Sun of Righteousness, causing life to dawn for all!

*Troparion:*

Beholding Thy boundless compassion, O Christ, they that were held in the bonds of hades hastened unto the light with joyful feet, praising the everlasting Pascha.

*Troparion:*

Bearing tapers, let us go forth to meet Christ proceeding from the tomb as a Bridegroom; and with feast-loving ranks let us celebrate together the saving Pascha of God!

*Little Litany, and Exclamation:*

**Priest:** For sanctified and glorified is Thy most honored and majestic name, of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages.

**Choir:** Amen.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

Thou didst descend into the deepest parts of the earth, and didst shatter the eternal bars that held the fettered, O Christ; and on the third day, as Jonah from the whale, Thou didst arise from the tomb!

*Troparion:*

Preserving the seals intact, O Christ, Thou didst arise from the tomb, Who in Thy Nativity didst not break the Virgin's keys; and Thou hast opened unto us the gates of Paradise.

*Troparion:*

O my Savior, the living and unslain Sacrifice, as God having willingly offered Thyself unto the Father, Thou didst raise up with Thyself the forefather Adam, when Thou didst arise from the tomb!

*Little Litany, and Exclamation:*

**Priest:** For Thou art the King of peace, and the Savior of our souls, and unto Thee do we send up glory, to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages.

**Choir:** Amen.

*Tone 8. Kontakion:*[^390]

Though Thou didst descend into the grave, O Immortal One, yet didst Thou destroy the power of hades, and didst arise as Victor, O Christ God, saying unto the Myrrh-Bearing Women, "Rejoice!" and granting peace unto Thine Apostles, O Thou Who dost bestow resurrection upon the fallen!

*Ikos:*

The myrrh-bearing maidens, anticipating the dawn, sought as day the Sun that was before the sun, which had set of old in the tomb, and they cried one unto another: O friends! Come, let us anoint with sweet spices the life-bearing and buried Body, the Flesh that raises up fallen Adam, lying in the tomb! Let us go, let us make haste like the Magi, and let us worship and bring myrrh as gifts unto Him Who is wrapped not in swaddling clothes, but in a winding-sheet; and let us weep and cry aloud: O Master, arise, Who dost bestow resurrection upon the fallen!

*The Synaxarion for the Holy and Great Sunday of Pascha.*[^391]

*Verses:*
*Christ, having descended alone unto the wrestling with hades,*  
*Came forth, bearing many spoils of victory.*

On the Holy and Great Sunday of Pascha, we celebrate the life-bearing Resurrection itself of our Lord, God, and Savior Jesus Christ, which we also call Pascha, which in the Hebrew tongue is interpreted as "passage" [transitus].

Now the Resurrection of the Lord came to pass in this wise: While the soldiers were guarding the sepulchre, an earthquake occurred; for an angel, having descended, rolled away the stone from the doors of the tomb, and the guards fled in terror. And the arrival of the women here took place late on the Sabbath, as it were at midnight. And first of all the Resurrection was made known unto the Mother of God, who sat opposite the tomb with Magdalene, as Matthew saith. But lest the Resurrection should be doubted because of her maternal relationship, the Evangelists say: He appears first unto Mary Magdalene. And she also saw the angel upon the stone, and stooping down she again beheld those who were within, who indeed proclaimed the Resurrection of the Lord: For He is risen, they say, He is not here; behold the place where they laid Him. Having heard these things, she runs and comes unto the most fervent of the disciples, unto Peter and John, and brings them the glad tidings of the Resurrection. And as she was returning with the other Mary, Christ met them, saying: Rejoice! For it was fitting that the sex which first heard, "In sorrow shalt thou bring forth children," should be the first to hear of joy. And they, overcome with love, approached and touched His most pure feet, wishing to know more assuredly. And the two ran to the tomb: John indeed, having only stooped down at the tomb, departed; but Peter enters within, and beholds more searchingly, and touches the winding-sheet and the napkin. Again Magdalene comes unto the sepulchre in the early morning with the other women, desiring carefully to cense it with incense. And when those had departed, she stood weeping; and stooping down into the tomb, she beholds two angels shining with brightness, as it were reproving her and saying: Woman, why weepest thou? and Whom seekest thou? Seek ye Jesus of Nazareth, Who was crucified? He is risen, He is not here. And immediately they stood up in terror, having seen the Lord. Therefore she, turning back, sees Christ standing, and supposing Him to be the gardener (for the tomb was in a garden), saith: Sir, if Thou hast taken Him away, tell me where Thou hast laid Him, and I will take Him away. Again the Savior saith unto Magdalene: Mary! And she, having heard the sweet and customary voice of Christ answering her, wished to touch Him. But He saith: Touch Me not, for I have not yet ascended unto My Father, as thou supposest, imagining Me to be yet a mortal man; but go unto My brethren, and say unto them all that thou hast seen and heard. And this indeed Magdalene doth. And as day began to dawn, she is again at the sepulchre with the rest. And they that were with Joanna and Salome came when the sun had risen.

Now it is uncertain at what hour the Lord arose: some indeed say at the first cockcrow, others when the earthquake occurred, and others variously. When these things had so occurred, behold, certain of the guard went and announced unto the chief priests all that had taken place. And they, having bribed them with pieces of silver, persuaded them to say that His disciples came by night and stole Him away.

On the evening of this present day, when the disciples were gathered together for fear of the Jews, and the doors were fast shut, Jesus enters unto them, being in an incorruptible Body, and customarily announcs peace unto them. And having seen Him, they rejoiced exceedingly, and by His breathing upon them they received the most perfect operation of the All-Holy Spirit.

And how is the three-day Resurrection of the Lord computed? Each day thus: Thursday evening and the day of Friday: for thus do the Hebrews reckon a day-and-night [nychthemeron], as one day. Friday night again and all Saturday, another day-and-night: lo, the second day. Again Saturday night and the day of Sunday, for from a part of the beginning the whole is reckoned, the third day-and-night: lo, the third day. Or thus: at the third hour of Friday Christ was crucified. Then from the sixth hour unto the ninth hour there was darkness: consider this separately as night; lo, from the third even unto the ninth hour is one day-and-night. Then again after the darkness, the day and the night of Friday: lo, two days-and-nights. The day of Saturday and its night: again, lo, three days-and-nights, according to those that speak in the Gospel: "as it began to dawn toward the first day of the week." But if the Savior promised to do good unto us on the third day, He wrought the beneficence even more swiftly: and thus unto Him be thanksgiving and glory, unto ages of ages. Amen.

*Then we say:*[^392]

Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ, let us worship the holy Lord Jesus, the only Sinless One. We venerate Thy Cross, O Christ, and Thy holy Resurrection we hymn and glorify; for Thou art our God, we know none other beside Thee, we call upon Thy name. Come, all ye faithful, ...let us worship the holy Resurrection of Christ: for behold, through the Cross joy hath come into all the world! Ever blessing the Lord, let us sing His Resurrection: for having endured the Crucifixion, by death He hath destroyed death! *(thrice)*

And: Jesus is risen from the tomb, as He foretold, and hath given unto us eternal life, and great mercy. *(thrice)*

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*[^393]

He Who delivered the youths from the furnace, having become Man, suffers as a mortal, and through suffering doth clothe mortality with the beauty of incorruption, the only blessed God of our fathers, and supremely glorified!

*Troparion:*

The women with myrrh, divinely wise, hastened after Thee; and Him Whom with tears they sought as dead, they worshipped rejoicing as the living God, and the mystical Pascha they announced unto Thy disciples, O Christ!

*Troparion:*

We celebrate the death of death, the destruction of hades, the beginning of another, eternal life; and leaping for joy we praise the Author, the only blessed God of our fathers, and supremely glorified!

*Troparion:*

How truly sacred and all-festive is this saving night, radiant with light, being the herald of the light-bearing day of the Resurrection, wherein the timeless Light in the flesh shone forth from the tomb unto all!

*Litany, and Exclamation:*

**Priest:** Blessed and supremely glorified be the majesty of Thy Kingdom, of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages.

**Choir:** Amen.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^394]

This chosen and holy day is the one of the Sabbaths, the King and Lord of days, the Feast of feasts, and the Triumph of triumphs: on which let us bless Christ unto the ages!

*Troparion:*

Come, on the auspicious day of the Resurrection, let us partake of the fruit of the new vine, of divine gladness, of the Kingdom of Christ, praising Him as God unto the ages!

*Troparion:*

Lift up thine eyes around thee, O Zion, and behold! For lo, thy children have come unto thee, shining like divinely radiant luminaries, from the west, and the north, and the sea, and the east, blessing Christ within thee unto the ages!

*Troparion to the Trinity:*

O Father Almighty, and Word, and Spirit, one Nature united in three Hypostases, super-essential and supremely divine! In Thee have we been baptized, and Thee will we bless unto all ages!

*Then the Litany, and Exclamation:*

**Priest:** For blessed is Thy name, and glorified is Thy Kingdom, of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages.

**Choir:** Amen.

*Ode 9. Refrain:*[^395]

The angel cried aloud unto Her that is full of grace: O Pure Virgin, rejoice! and again I say, Rejoice! for Thy Son is risen on the third day from the tomb, and hath raised up the dead: O ye people, be glad!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^396]

Shine, shine, O New Jerusalem! for the glory of the Lord hath risen upon thee! Dance now and be glad, O Zion! and do thou, O Pure One, magnify thyself, O Mother of God, in the arising of Thy Child!

*Troparion:*

O divine! O beloved! O sweetest voice of Thine! For Thou hast truly promised to be with us unto the end of the age, O Christ: which we the faithful possessing as the anchor of hope, do rejoice!

*Troparion:*

O great and most sacred Pascha, O Christ! O Wisdom, and Word of God, and Power! Grant us more perfectly to partake of Thee in the unwaning day of Thy Kingdom!

*Litany, and Exclamation:*

**Priest:** For all the heavenly powers praise Thee, Father, Son, and Holy Spirit, and unto Thee do they send up glory, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages.

**Choir:** Amen.

*Exapostilarion, Automelon (Пло́тїю оу҆сну́въ):*[^397]

Having fallen asleep in the flesh as a mortal, O King and Lord, on the third day Thou didst arise, raising Adam from corruption and abolishing death: O Pascha of incorruption, salvation of the world! *(thrice)*

*At the Praises, Let every breath praise the Lord, in Tone 1:*

*We set 4 verses, and we sing the resurrectional stichera:*

*Verse: Praise Him for His mighty acts, praise Him according to the multitude of His greatness! (Ps. 150:2)*

We sing Thy saving Passion, O Christ, and we glorify Thy Resurrection!

*Verse: Praise Him with the sound of the trumpet, praise Him with the psaltery and harp! (Ps. 150:3)*

Thou Who didst endure the Cross, and abolish death, and arise from the dead: grant peace unto our life, O Lord, as Thou alone art almighty!

*Verse: Praise Him with the timbrel and dance, praise Him with strings and the organ! (Ps. 150:4)*

Thou Who didst despoil hades, and raise up man by Thy Resurrection, O Christ: make us worthy with pure hearts to hymn and glorify Thee!

*Verse: Praise Him upon the well-tuned cymbals, praise Him upon the cymbals of jubilation: let every breath praise the Lord! (Ps. 150:5-6)*

Glorifying Thy divinely fitting condescension, we sing unto Thee, O Christ: Thou wast born of a Virgin, yet wast not separated from the Father; Thou didst suffer as man, and of Thine own will didst endure the Cross; Thou didst arise from the tomb, coming forth as from a bridal chamber, that Thou mightest save the world: O Lord, glory to Thee!

*Then, both choirs having come together, they chant:*

*The Stichera of Pascha, Tone 5:*[^398]

*Verse: Let God arise, and let His enemies be scattered! (Ps. 67:2a [68:1a])*

A sacred Pascha is revealed unto us today: a new and holy Pascha, a mystical Pascha, an all-honored Pascha, Pascha Christ the Redeemer, an unblemished Pascha, a great Pascha, the Pascha of the faithful, a Pascha that opens unto us the gates of Paradise, a Pascha that sanctifies all the faithful!

*Verse: As smoke vanishes, so let them vanish! (Ps. 67:3a [68:2a])*

Come from that vision, ye women, bearers of good tidings, and say unto Zion: Receive from us the glad tidings of joy, of the Resurrection of Christ! Make merry, dance, and rejoice, O Jerusalem, beholding Christ the King proceeding from the tomb as a Bridegroom!

*Verse: So let the sinners perish from the face of God, but let the righteous be glad! (Ps. 67:3b-4a [68:2b-3a])*

The myrrh-bearing women, in the deep dawn, standing before the tomb of the Giver of life, found an angel sitting upon the stone; and he, addressing them, spake thus: Why seek ye the Living among the dead? Why weep ye for the Incorruptible One in corruption? Go, proclaim unto His disciples!

*Verse: This is the day which the Lord hath made: let us rejoice and be glad therein! (Ps. 117:24 [118:24])*

A delightful Pascha, Pascha, the Lord's Pascha, an all-honored Pascha hath shone forth upon us! Pascha! With joy let us embrace one another! O Pascha, deliverance from sorrow! For today Christ, having shone forth from the tomb as from a bridal chamber, hath filled the women with joy, saying: Proclaim unto the Apostles!

*Glory, both now, Tone 5:*

It is the day of Resurrection! Let us be radiant for the festival, and let us embrace one another! Let us say: Brethren! and unto them that hate us, let us forgive all things by the Resurrection, and so let us cry aloud:

Christ is risen from the dead, trampling down death by death, and upon those in the tombs bestowing life! *(thrice)*

*And we sing this many times, until the brethren kiss one another.*

*And the Kiss of Peace by the superior with the other priests and deacons in the holy sanctuary takes place in this wise: The one approaching says: "Christ is risen!" and the other answers: "Truly He is risen!"*

*After this, the superior takes the precious Cross, and stands outside before the holy church doors. Likewise also all the priests, as many as there be, and the deacons: each of them taking the Holy Gospel and the precious icons according to their rank. And they all stand in a line on the right side of the superior. And first come the more learned counselors and elders, one by one. And they bow slightly before the superior. And each of them kisses the precious Cross, and the Holy Gospel which a priest holds, and the other icons. Then they kiss the superior himself on the mouth, saying: "Christ is risen!" and he answers: "Truly He is risen!" In like manner also the priests kiss, they also among themselves according to their rank. And they stand from the priests in a line on the left side.*

*Then after them the laity likewise make the kiss. And the monastery workmen stand in like rank, kissing one another. And in the midst of all there is a great silence.*[^399]

*And after the kiss, the Catechetical Homily of Chrysostom is read by the superior or the ecclesiarch: and we do not sit during this reading, but all listen standing.*

*The Catechetical Homily of our father among the saints, John Chrysostom, Archbishop of Constantinople, on the holy and light-bearing day of the most glorious and saving Resurrection of Christ our God.*[^400]

If any be pious and a lover of God, let him enjoy this good and radiant triumph! If any be a wise servant, let him enter rejoicing into the joy of his Lord! If any have labored in fasting, let him now receive his denarius! If any have worked from the first hour, let him receive today his righteous due! If any have come after the third...

...hour, let him celebrate with thanksgiving! If any have arrived after the sixth hour, let him not doubt, for he loss nothing! If any have tarried until the ninth hour, let him approach without hesitation, fearing nothing! If any have arrived only at the eleventh hour, let him not be afraid of his delay: for the Master is generous, and accepts the last even as the first; He gives rest unto him who came at the eleventh hour, even as unto him who worked from the first hour; and He shows mercy unto the last, and servs the first; and unto the one He gives, and unto the other He bestows a gift; and He accepts the works, and welcoms the intention; and He honors the deed, and praises the purpose! Enter ye all therefore into the joy of your Lord: and ye first and ye second, receive the reward; ye rich and ye poor, dance together with one another; ye abstinent and ye slothful, honor the day; ye that have fasted and ye that have not fasted, be glad today! The table is full-laden: feast ye all! The calf is fatted: let no one go away hungry! Enjoy ye all the banquet of faith; receive ye all the riches of goodness! Let no one lament poverty, for the universal Kingdom hath appeared! Let no one weep for transgressions, for forgiveness hath dawned from the tomb! Let no one fear death, for the death of the Savior hath set us free: He hath extinguished it, Who was held by it! He hath despoiled hades, Who descended into hades! He embittered it, when it tasted of His Flesh! And Isaiah, foreseeing this, cried aloud: Hades, he saith, was embittered, meeting Thee below: it was embittered, for it was abolished; it was embittered, for it was mocked; it was embittered, for it was slain; it was embittered, for it was overthrown; it was embittered, for it was bound in chains! It received a body, and encountered God; it received earth, and met heaven; it received that which it saw, and fell into that which it saw not! Where, O death, is thy sting? Where, O hades, is thy victory? Christ is risen, and thou art cast down! Christ is risen, and the demons are fallen! Christ is risen, and the angels rejoice! Christ is risen, and life reigns! Christ is risen, and not one dead remains in the tomb! For Christ, being risen from the dead, is become the First-fruits of them that have fallen asleep! Unto Him be glory and dominion, unto ages of ages. Amen.

*Then the troparion of the saint is chanted.*

*Tone 8:*[^401]

The grace shining forth from thy mouth like a torch hath illumined the universe: it hath amassed for the world treasures not of avarice, and hath shown us the height of humility; but instructing us by thy words, O Father John Chrysostom, intercede with the Word, Christ God, that our souls may be saved!

*Then the Deacon says the litanies: "Have mercy on us, O God," and "Let us complete our morning prayer unto the Lord." And after the exclamation, the Deacon: "Wisdom." We say: "Bless." The Superior: "Blessed is Christ our God, always, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages." And we: "Amen. Establish, O God..."*

*Then the superior, holding the Cross, censes: "Glory to Thee, O Christ God..." and sings: "Christ is risen from the dead, trampling down death by death." And we sing: "And upon those in the tombs bestowing life."*

*And straightway the superior pronounces the Dismissal:*

**Priest:** May Christ Who is risen from the dead, trampling down death by death, and upon those in the tombs bestowing life, our true God, through the prayers of His most pure Mother and of all the saints, have mercy on us and save us, as He is good and the Lover of mankind.

*Then, lifting up the Cross, he says:*

**Priest:** Christ is risen! *(thrice)*

*And we answer:*

**Choir:** Truly He is risen! *(thrice) Then we sing the conclusion: "Christ is risen" thrice, the whole troparion. And after it we conclude by singing:*

And unto us He hath given eternal life: we worship His third-day Resurrection!

*We sing the Many Years. And we kiss the precious Cross held in the hand of the superior.*

*And the First Hour is said: "Christ is risen" thrice. And the rest, as is indicated below concerning the Hours of Bright Week.*

*And a Litiya on this day does not take place.*

*Concerning the Hours of Holy Pascha and All Bright Week.*[^402]

*It is fitting to know: That from this day of the Holy and Great Sunday of Pascha, even unto Saturday, the Hours and Compline are chanted in this wise. The Priest having begun:*

**Priest:** Blessed is our God...

*Christ is risen from the dead... thrice, the whole troparion.*

*Then we say thrice:*

Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ, let us worship the holy Lord Jesus, the only Sinless One. We venerate Thy Cross, O Christ, and Thy holy Resurrection we hymn and glorify; for Thou art our God, we know none other beside Thee, we call upon Thy name. Come, all ye faithful, let us worship the holy Resurrection of Christ: for behold, through the Cross joy hath come into all the world! Ever blessing the Lord, let us sing His Resurrection: for having endured the Crucifixion, by death He hath destroyed death!

*Then the Hypakoe, once:*

When they that were with Mary came before the dawn and found the stone rolled away from the tomb, they heard from the angel: Why seek ye among the dead as a man Him Who abides in everlasting light? Behold the winding-sheets of the tomb; run and proclaim unto the world that the Lord is risen, slaying death; for He is the Son of God, Who saves mankind!

*Then the Kontakion, once:*

Though Thou didst descend into the grave, O Immortal One, yet didst Thou destroy the power of hades, and didst arise as Victor, O Christ God, saying unto the Myrrh-Bearing Women, "Rejoice!" and granting peace unto Thine Apostles, O Thou Who dost bestow resurrection upon the fallen!

*Then also this troparion, once:*

In the tomb bodily, and in hades with the soul as God, in Paradise with the thief, and on the throne wast Thou, O Christ, with the Father and the Spirit, filling all things, Thou Who art uncircumscribed!

*Glory:*

As life-bearing, as more delightful than Paradise, and truly more radiant than any royal chamber hath Thy tomb appeared, O Christ, the fountain of our resurrection!

*Both now, Theotokion:*

O consecrated and divine habitation of the Most High, rejoice! For through thee joy hath been given unto them that cry aloud: Blessed art thou among women, O all-immaculate Lady!

*Then: Lord, have mercy (40 times). Glory, both now: More honorable than the Cherubim. In the name of the Lord, bless, father. Priest, verse: "Through the prayers of our holy fathers..." And we say: "Amen." And again we say in like manner: "Christ is risen" (thrice). Glory, both now: "Lord, have mercy" (thrice). "Bless."*

*And the Dismissal of the First Hour.*

*In this same manner it is chanted for the Third and Sixth Hours before the Liturgy. Likewise also before Vespers, for the Ninth Hour; and for Compline, once. Likewise also for the Midnight Office.*

*If there be no Liturgy, on account of necessity, in place of the Typika we say thus:*[^403]

*"Christ is risen" (thrice). "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ" (once). Then: "When they that were with Mary came before the dawn." Glory, Kontakion: "Though Thou didst descend into the grave." Both now: "Only-begotten Son."*

*Then the Beatitudes, from the Canon, Odes 3 and 6. Priest: "Wisdom." The reader, the title of the Epistle, and the reading itself.*

*Then the Priest, the Gospel. Then: "Remember us, O Lord." "The heavenly choir." And we sing: "I believe in one God." "Pardon, remit." And after "Our Father": Kontakion: "Though Thou didst descend into the grave." Glory, both now: Theotokion: "O consecrated and divine habitation..." "Lord, have mercy" (40 times). Then: "One is holy, one is the Lord, Jesus Christ, unto the glory of God the Father, amen."*

*And we sing in place of "Blessed be the name of the Lord": "Christ is risen" (thrice). Then Psalm 33 [34]: "I will bless the Lord at all times," ending: "they shall not lack any good thing."*

*And we sing the Dismissal. And we go forth unto the refectory.*

*It is fitting to know: How the Moleben is chanted on Holy Pascha.*[^404]

*The Priest says: "Blessed is our God." And we: "Amen." Then the Priest: "Christ is risen" (thrice). Then: "Christ is risen" (thrice) by the choirs, as was indicated at the beginning of Matins.*

*After "Glory, both now," the Priest says: "Christ is risen from the dead, trampling down death by death." And we: "And upon those in the tombs bestowing life."*

*Then: Glory, both now: Hypakoe: "When they that were with Mary came before the dawn." But if thou desirest to append the canon of any saint: say first the troparion of the saint, once; Glory, both now: "When they that were with Mary came before the dawn."*

*After this, the Canon of Pascha, with the Heirmos to 6. Refrain: "Christ is risen." And of the saint, if there be one, to 4. Katavasia:*

*After Odes 3 and 6: "Save from perils..."*

*After Ode 6, the Kontakion and Ikos of Pascha.*

*Then the Prokeimenon, Tone 8: "This is the day which the Lord hath made: let us rejoice and be glad therein." Verse: "Confess ye unto the Lord, for He is good, for His mercy endures for ever." Then the prokeimenon of the saint.*

*The Gospel according to Luke, pericope 113 [24:12-35]; thereafter of the saint.*

*After Ode 9, in place of "It is truly meet": Heirmos: "Shine, shine..."*

*Then: "Christ is risen" (thrice). After this, the Hypakoe: "When they that were with Mary came before the dawn." Glory: Troparion of the saint. Both now: Kontakion: "Though Thou didst descend into the grave." Then the Litany: "Have mercy on us, O God."*

*After this, the Priest says: "Wisdom." And we: "Christ is risen" (thrice). Then the Priest, in place of "Glory to Thee, O Christ God": "Christ is risen from the dead, trampling down death by death." And we: "And upon those in the tombs bestowing life."*

*And he pronounces the Dismissal with the Cross in this wise: "May Christ Who is risen from the dead..."*

*And the rest, as was prescribed at the end of Matins. Then he proclaims: "Christ is risen!" (thrice). And we answer: "Truly He is risen!" (thrice). And again we sing: "Christ is risen" (thrice).*

*Then: "Lord, have mercy" (thrice), with singing.*

## The Order of the Divine Liturgy[^405]
*Celebrated early in the morning:*

*The Deacon having begun:*

**Deacon:** Bless, Master.

*And the Superior having exclaimed:*

**Priest:** Blessed is the Kingdom of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages.

**Choir:** Amen.

*The Superior sings with the other ministers in the holy sanctuary:*

**Priest:** Christ is risen from the dead, trampling down death by death, and upon those in the tombs bestowing life *(thrice)*.

*And the choirs sing the same, thrice.*

*After this, the Deacon says the Great Litany.*

*And after the litany and after the exclamation:*

*The First Antiphon, Psalm 65 [66], Tone 2:*[^406]

*Verse 1: Shout with jubilation unto the Lord, all the earth! (Ps. 65:1a [66:1a])*

*Refrain:* Through the prayers of the Mother of God, O Savior, save us!

*And the other choir sings the same verse:*

Shout with jubilation unto the Lord, all the earth! Chant now unto His name, give glory to His praise! (Ps. 65:1-2 [66:1-2])

Through the prayers of the Mother of God, O Savior, save us!

*Verse 2: Say unto God: How awesome are Thy works! In the multitude of Thy power shall Thine enemies lie unto Thee! (Ps. 65:3 [66:3])*

Through the prayers of the Mother of God, O Savior, save us!

*Verse 3: Let all the earth worship Thee, and sing unto Thee: let them sing now unto Thy name, O Most High! (Ps. 65:4 [66:4])*

Through the prayers of the Mother of God, O Savior, save us!

*Glory, both now, together by both choirs in a louder voice:*

Through the prayers of the Mother of God, O Savior, save us!

*Then the Little Litany.*

*The Second Antiphon, Psalm 66 [67], the same Tone:*

*Verse 1: God be gracious unto us, and bless us! (Ps. 66:1a [67:1a])*

*Refrain:* Save us, O Son of God, Who art risen from the dead, who sing unto Thee: Alleluia! *(once)*

*The other side, the same verse:*

God be gracious unto us, and bless us: make His face to shine upon us, and have mercy on us! (Ps. 66:1 [67:1])

Save us, O Son of God...

*Verse 2: That we may know Thy way upon the earth, Thy salvation among all the nations! (Ps. 66:2 [67:2])*

Save us, O Son of God...

*Verse 3: Let the peoples give thanks unto Thee, O God, let all the peoples give thanks unto Thee! (Ps. 66:3 [67:3])*

Save us, O Son of God...

*Glory, both now, by both choirs together:*

Only-begotten Son and Word of God...

*Then the Little Litany.*

*The Third Antiphon, Psalm 67 [68], Tone 5:*

*Verse 1: Let God arise, and let His enemies be scattered! (Ps. 67:2a [68:1a])*

*Troparion:* Christ is risen from the dead...

*The other side, the same verse:*

Let God arise, and let His enemies be scattered, and let them that hate Him flee from before His face! (Ps. 67:2 [68:1])

Christ is risen...

*Verse 2: As smoke vanishes, so let them vanish: as wax melts before the face of fire! (Ps. 67:3 [68:2])*

Christ is risen...

*Verse 3: So let the sinners perish from the face of God, but let the righteous be glad: let them rejoice before God! (Ps. 67:3b-4a [68:2b-3a])*

Christ is risen...

*And the Introit takes place: and the Deacon, if there be one, or if not, the Priest proclaims: "Wisdom, let us attend!" And we sing the Introit:*[^407]

In the assemblies bless ye God, the Lord from the fountains of Israel! (Ps. 67:27 [68:26])

*And we sing the Troparion: "Christ is risen"; the Hypakoe: "When they that were with Mary came before the dawn"; Glory, both now: Kontakion: "Though Thou didst descend into the grave."*

*In place of the Trisagion:*

As many of you as have been baptized into Christ have put on Christ: Alleluia! (Gal. 3:27)

*It is fitting to know: That this beginning of the Liturgy, and the Antiphons, and "As many as have been baptized into Christ," we sing throughout all Bright Week, even unto New Sunday [Thomas Sunday]: likewise also the Kinonikon [Communion Hymn].*

*Prokeimenon, Tone 8:*

This is the day which the Lord hath made: let us rejoice and be glad therein! (Ps. 117:24 [118:24])

*Verse: Confess ye unto the Lord, for He is good, for His mercy endures for ever! (Ps. 117:1 [118:1])*

*The Reading from the Acts of the Apostles, pericope 1 (Acts 1:1-8):*[^408]

The first account I made, O Theophilus...

*Alleluia, Tone 4:*

*Verse: Thou, having arisen, shalt have mercy upon Zion! (Ps. 101:14a [102:13a])*

*Verse: The Lord from heaven hath looked down upon the earth! (Ps. 101:20b [102:19b]) At the Liturgy:*

*The Reading from the Holy Gospel according to John, pericope 1 (John 1:1-17):*[^409]

*Now when the time for reading the Holy Gospel has arrived, the leading Deacon, desiring to read the Gospel, says:*

**Deacon:** Bless, master, the proclaimer of good tidings.

*Then the Superior says:*

**Priest:** May God, through the intercessions of the holy, glorious...

*and the rest, as is indicated in the Liturgicon [Sluzhebnik]. And the Deacon goes forth, and in the customary place opposite the holy doors, faces the east, the Superior having first exclaimed before the holy table:*

**Priest:** Wisdom, arise! Let us hear the Holy Gospel.

*The same also all the concelebrating Priests say, and after them the Deacons, having stationed themselves in various places, from the holy table unto the western doors of the church, one after another. And behind all stands the Archdeacon; and all read after the Superior, one after another, in sections [statiai] as the Superior arranges. And the Superior begins:*

**Priest:** The Reading from the Holy Gospel according to John.

*The others the same.*

**Priest:** Let us attend.

*The others the same.*

*The Superior, standing before the holy table, facing east, reads:*

In the beginning was the Word...

*and the rest. The others the same. And at each exclamation, or section [statia] of the Gospel, they strike the candelabrum once in the church. The paraecclesiarch outside the church strikes the great semantron [bilo] and the great bell; and at the last exclamation they strike all the bells and the great semantron. And the Divine Liturgy of Saint John Chrysostom takes place according to its order.*

*In place of "It is truly meet," we sing:*

*The angel cried aloud unto Her that is full of grace: O Pure Virgin, rejoice! and again I say, Rejoice! for Thy Son is risen on the third day from the tomb, and hath raised up the dead: O ye people, be glad!*

*Then the Heirmos:*

Shine, shine, O New Jerusalem! for the glory of the Lord hath risen upon thee! Dance now and be glad, O Zion! and do thou, O Pure One, magnify thyself, O Mother of God, in the arising of Thy Child!

*Kinonikon [Communion Hymn]:*

Receive ye the Body of Christ; taste of the immortal fountain: Alleluia. *(thrice)*

*And when the Superior says:*

**Priest:** With fear of God and with faith draw near.

*We, in place of "Blessed is He that comes in the name of the Lord," sing:*

Christ is risen from the dead... *(once)*

*The Superior says:*

**Priest:** O God, save Thy people...

*And we:*

Christ is risen from the dead... *(once)*

*And when the Priest exclaims:*

**Priest:** Always, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages.

*We sing:*

Christ is risen from the dead... *(once)*

*After this, the Litany.*

*And in place of "Blessed be the name of the Lord," and in place of the psalm, "I will bless the Lord," we sing:*

Christ is risen from the dead... *(twelve times, and more: until the antidoron [anaphora] is distributed by the Hegumen).*

*Then the Superior:*

**Priest:** The blessing of the Lord be upon you...

**Choir:** Amen.

*After this, the Superior, in place of "Glory to Thee, O Christ God," sings:*

**Priest:** Christ is risen from the dead!

*And the Choir:*

**Choir:** and upon those in the tombs bestowing life.

*After this the Superior pronounces the dismissal with the Cross according to custom, as was indicated at Matins.*

*Let it also be known: That the gates of the sanctuary of the church, both the great [Royal Doors] and the small side doors, in all churches throughout all Bright Week, are not closed, even during the time of communion.*[^410]

*On the Holy and Great Sunday of Pascha:*

*Prayer for the Blessing of Flesh-Meats on the Holy and Great Sunday of Pascha.*[^411]

*The Priest does: "Blessed is our God..." "Christ is risen from the dead..." thrice.*
*Then: "Let us pray to the Lord." "Lord, have mercy."*

**Priest:** Look down, O Lord Jesus Christ our God, upon the flesh-meats, and sanctify them, even as Thou didst sanctify the ram which faithful Abraham brought unto Thee, and the lamb which Abel offered unto Thee as a whole burnt offering; likewise also the fatted calf, which Thou didst command to be slain for Thy son that had gone astray and returned again unto Thee; that even as he was accounted worthy to enjoy Thy goodness, so also may we enjoy these things sanctified and blessed by Thee, for food unto us all. For Thou art the true food and the Giver of good things, and unto Thee do we send up glory, with Thine unoriginate Father, and Thine all-holy, good, and life-creating Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.

*Prayer for the Blessing of Cheese and Eggs.*

*The eggs and cheese that have been brought are placed in the narthex; for it is by no means fitting to bring them into the church.*
*The Priest, having gone forth, says: "Let us pray to the Lord."*

**Priest:** O Master, Lord our God, Creator and Fashioner of all things: Bless the curdled milk [cheese], and with it also the eggs, and preserve us in Thy goodness; that partaking of them, we may be filled with Thy bountifully bestowed gifts and Thine ineffable goodness: For Thine is the dominion, and Thine is the kingdom, and the power, and the glory, of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.

*Be it known: That such an offering of meats and bacon is not the Pascha, nor a lamb, as some say and receive, and partake thereof with all reverence as of some sacred thing; but it is a simple offering, not as though it were to be offered as a sacrifice unto God, but only that for those who begin to eat meat and cheese after the fast, it may be blessed for food through the prayers offered by the Priest. For the Pascha is Christ Himself and the Lamb that takes away the sins of the world, upon the altar in the unbloody Sacrifice, in the most pure Mysteries of His precious Body and life-creating Blood, offered by the Priest unto God and the Father; and they that worthily partake thereof eat the true Pascha. And for this reason it is not fitting to bring those offerings into the sanctuary or into the church, as they are not the Pascha, but to bless them outside the church or in the narthex with prayer, according to the third Canon of the Holy Apostles, which says: "If any Bishop or Presbyter, contrary to the Lord's ordinance concerning the sacrifice, shall bring any other things unto the altar, honey, or milk, or in place of wine strong drink [sikera], that is, beer [olovina], that is, fermented drink, or mead, or birds, or any animals, or legumes, except new ears of grain or grapes in season, and oil for the lamps and incense at the time of the Liturgy, let him be deposed." And according to the Sixth Council, which was in Trullo, in its 94th Canon, which says: "We have also learned that this occurs in the land of the Armenians: that certain persons, boiling portions of meat within the holy sanctuaries, bring pieces to the priests, dividing them in Jewish fashion; therefore, preserving the church blameless, we have decreed that it is not proper for any priest to accept various portions of meat from those who bring them, but let them be content with whatever the bringer sees fit to give them, such offering being made outside the church; and if anyone do not this, let him be excommunicated."*[^412]

*The Order of the Artos on the Day of Holy Pascha:*[^413]

*After the dismissal of the Divine Liturgy, the Priest having taken the icon of the Resurrection of Christ, and the Deacon having received the Artos from another through the church doors, if it be in the sanctuary. [In certain monasteries it is set upon an analogion in the church prepared for this purpose, together with the icon of the Resurrection of the Lord, and as they go to the antidoron they first kiss the feast, then the Artos.] And the Deacon takes it from its place with the panagiarion, and carries it in his hands, one or two lanterns preceding him. And the Superior and all the brethren according to their rank go forth with the Artos (that is, the whole prosphora bread prepared for this purpose with the cross depicted upon it) from the church unto the refectory. In place of the psalm, "I will exalt Thee, my God," we sing: "Christ is risen from the dead..." The paraecclesiarch and the other brethren strike all the bells. And having entered the refectory, they place the feast and the Artos in their place. And the Superior says: "Christ is risen from the dead..." thrice; and "Our Father..."; "Glory, both now"; "Lord, have mercy" thrice; "Bless, master." Superior: "O Christ God, bless the food..." and the rest. And we: "Amen." We sit down, each in his own place, in all silence and reverence. And there is great consolation for all, and reading according to custom. After the customary meal, the brethren having arisen, they set the Artos upon the table, singing: "Christ is risen from the dead..." thrice; and "Lord, have mercy" thrice; "Bless, master." Then the cellarer [kelar], having received the customary forgiveness from the Superior and the brethren: "Bless me, holy fathers, and forgive me, a sinner." And we say: "May God forgive and bless."
